3^{i} | \nabla \g_{i}| \bigr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{m+1+j})}
 \,,
\end{equation*}
and set~$X_{m,i}=0$ if~$m<0$ or~$i<0$. We shall apply Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales} to this extension to all indices in~$\Z$. We have
\begin{equation*}
\sup_{j \geq 0}
3^{-sj}
\sum_{i = (m-j)\vee0}^{m+j}
\bigl\|  | \g_i |
+
3^{i} | \nabla \g_{i}| \bigr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{m+1+j})}
\leq
C_2^{-1} s \ep  \sum_{i \in \N_0}
3^{-\nf{1}{2} s| m-i|} X_{m,i}
 \,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.max.scaling.O.Gamma.Two},~\eqref{e.same.law.shift}, and~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity},
\begin{equation*}
\bigl\|  | \g_i |
+
3^{i} | \nabla \g_{i}| \bigr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{m+1+j})}
\leq
\O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C\gmcdelta (|m- i| + j + 1)^{\nf 12} \bigr)
 \,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.moments.OGamma2}, there exists a constant~$C_1(d)$ such that, for every~$p \in [1,\infty)$,
\begin{equation*}
\E\bigl[ X_{m,i}^p  \bigr]^{\nf 1p} \leq C_1 C_2 s^{-\nf 52} \ep^{-1} \gmcdelta p^{\nf 12}
 \,.
\end{equation*}
Moreover, for~$m,m',i,i' \in \N_0$ with~$|i-i'| \geq 1$, the variables~$X_{m,i}$ and~$X_{m',i'}$ are independent. We fix~$p \coloneqq C_1^{-2} C_2^{-2} s^5 \ep^2 \gmcdelta^{-2}$.  With this choice of~$p$, if~$C(d)$ is large enough in terms of~$C_1$ and~$C_2$, the assumption~$\theta \geq C s^{-6} \ep^{-2} \gmcdelta^{2}$, which since~$\theta\leq1$ also gives~$\gmcdelta \leq C^{-\nf12} s^{3} \ep$, implies that~$\theta \geq p^{-1}$ and~$s \geq 2 p^{-1}$. By the inequality displayed after the definition of~$X_{m,i}$, we then have
\begin{equation*}
\biggl\{
\sup_{j \geq 0}
3^{-sj}
\sum_{i = (m-j)\vee0}^{m+j}
\bigl\|  | \g_i |
+
3^{i} | \nabla \g_{i}| \bigr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{m+1+j})} >  \ep
\biggr\}
\subseteq
\biggl\{
\sum_{i \in \N_0} 3^{-\nf{1}{2} s|m-i|} X_{m,i}  >  C_2 s^{-1} \biggr\}
 \,.
\end{equation*}
For~$C_2$ large enough, Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales} gives~\eqref{e.bad.scales.for.nabla.gj}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~\ref{l.exp.goodscales}]
We apply Propositions~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales.exp.sequence} and~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales.exp.field}. For sufficiently large~$C(d)$, set~$X_n\coloneqq C3^n\|\nabla\g_n\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{n+1})}$ for~$n\in\N_0$ and~$X_n\coloneqq0$ for~$n<0$. On~$\cu_{k+1+j}$,
\begin{align*}
\prod_{i=k+j}^{\infty} \exp\bigl(4 | \g_i - \g_i(0) | \bigr)
&
\leq
\prod_{i=k+j}^{\infty} \exp\Bigl( 3^{k+j-i}
\bigl( C 3^i \| \nabla \g_i \|_{L^\infty(\cu_{i+1})} \bigr)\Bigr)
\leq
\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}
\exp( 3^{-i} X_{k+i+j})
\,.
\end{align*}
The sequence~$(X_n)$ satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales.exp.sequence} with~$\delta_0\coloneqq C\gmcdelta$. If~$\gmcdelta\leq C^{-1}s\theta^{\nf12}$, then that proposition gives
\begin{equation*}
\P \Biggl[
\avsum_{m=m_0}^{m_0+M}
\indc\biggl\{
\sup_{j \geq 0}  3^{- s j}
\biggl\|
\prod_{i=m+j}^{\infty} \exp\bigl(4 | \g_i - \g_i(0) | \bigr)
\, \biggr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{m+1+j})}
>
3
\biggr\}
\geq
\theta
\Biggr]
\leq
\exp\biggl( - \frac{s^2\theta}{C \gmcdelta^{2}} (M+1)  \biggr)  \,.
\end{equation*}
If~$\gmcdelta \leq C^{-1}s \theta^{\nf12}$ for sufficiently large~$C(d)$, then Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales.exp.field}, applied to~$X_i\coloneqq\g_i$ for~$i\in\N_0$ and~$X_i\coloneqq0$ for~$i<0$, yields
\begin{equation*}
\P \Biggl[ \avsum_{k=m_0}^{m_0+M} \! \! \! \indc{\biggl\{ \sup_{j \geq 0}
3^{- s j }
\biggl\| \, \prod_{i=(k-j)\vee0}^{k+j} \exp\bigl(| \g_i |\bigr) \, \biggr\|_{L^\infty(\cu_{k+1+j})}  > 3 \biggr\}} \geq \theta \Biggr]
\leq
 \exp \biggl( - \frac{s^2 \theta}{C \gmcdelta^2} (M+1) \biggr)
 \,.
\end{equation*}
Apply the previous two displays with~$\theta/2$ in place of~$\theta$. If the sum of the two products exceeds six, then at least one exceeds three, so a union bound gives~\eqref{e.amaj.good.scales.one}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~\ref{l.discounted.J.local}]
First,
\begin{align*}
\lefteqn{
\sup_{0\leq j \leq m} \sup_{0\leq n \leq j-2} 3^{-s(m-n)}
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
\sum_{j = 0}^m 3^{-s(m-j)}
\sum_{n = 0}^{j-2} 3^{-s(j-n)}
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})
\,.
\end{align*}
We set, for~$K \geq 1$ to be fixed,
\begin{equation*}
X_{m,j}
\coloneqq
K s^{-1} \ep^{-2}
\sum_{n = 0}^{j-2} 3^{-s(j-n)}
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})
\indc_{\{ j \leq m\}}
  \,.
\end{equation*}
Then
\begin{multline*}
\biggl\{ \sup_{0\leq j \leq m} \sup_{0\leq n \leq j-2} 3^{-s(m-n)}
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})  \geq \ep^2 \biggr\}
\\
\subseteq
\biggl\{
\sum_{j = 0}^m 3^{-s(m-j)}
X_{m,j}
\geq K s^{-1} \biggr\}
 \,.
\end{multline*}
There is an integer~$r(d)<\infty$ such that the columns~$(X_{m,j})_m$, indexed by~$j$, are $r(d)$-dependent in the sense of~\eqref{e.independent.columns.twosided}. To verify this, fix one of the fields~$\g_i$. Column~$j$ depends on~$\g_i$ only if~$i\leq j-2$, and then only through its values on the annulus~$\cu_j\setminus\cu_{j-1}$. If~$j'>j$ and~$j'-j\geq r(d)$, then the distance between these annuli is at least $(3^{j'-1}-3^j)/2>\sqrt d\,3^{j-2}\geq\sqrt d\,3^i$, provided that~$r(d)$ is large enough. Thus, by Assumption~\ref{a.g1}, the restrictions of~$\g_i$ to the regions used by two sets of columns at distance at least~$r(d)$ are independent. Since the fields are mutually independent, so are the two sets of columns. Moreover, by the triangle inequality, stationarity, and Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, there is a constant~$C_1(d)<\infty$ such that, if~$p$ satisfies~$2ds^{-1} \leq p \leq C_1^{-1} \gmcdelta^{-2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|^{-1}$, then
\begin{align*}
\E\bigl[ X_{m,j}^p \bigr]^{\nf 1p}
&
\leq
K s^{-1} \ep^{-2}
\sum_{n = 0}^{j-2} 3^{-s(j-n)} \E\biggl[
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})^p  \biggr]^{\nf 1p}
\notag \\ &
\leq
K s^{-1} \ep^{-2}
\sum_{n = 0}^{j-2} 3^{-(s- \nf{d}{p})(j-n)}
\E\Bigl[
\max_{|e| = 1} J(\cu_n,e,e\,; \ahom_{n}^{-1} \a_{n})^p  \Bigr]^{\nf 1p}
\leq
C_1 K s^{-2} \ep^{-2}\gmcdelta^2  \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|  p
\,.
\end{align*}
Taking~$p \coloneqq C_1^{-1} K^{-1} s^{2} \ep^{2} \gmcdelta^{-2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|^{-1} $ and assuming that~$\gmcdelta$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
\gmcdelta^{2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|
\leq
\frac{s^3 \ep^2}{2d C_1 K}
\quad \implies \quad
2ds^{-1} \leq p = C_1^{-1} K^{-1} s^{2} \ep^{2} \gmcdelta^{-2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|^{-1}
\,,
\end{equation*}
we deduce that~$\E[ X_{m,j}^p ]^{\nf 1p} \leq 1$. Assuming also~$\theta \geq p^{-1}$ and applying Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales} with~$r=r(d)$, we obtain~\eqref{e.discounted.J.local.lemma} for a sufficiently large universal constant~$K$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Excess decay on good scales}
\label{ss.good.scale.estimates}

Throughout this subsection, we denote, for~$m,n \in \Z$ with~$n\leq m$ and~$x \in \cu_m$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.U.m.n.def}
U_{m,n}(x) \coloneqq (x+\cu_{n}) \cap \cu_{m} \,.
\end{equation}

The first lemma adapts~\citet[Lemma~4.10]{ASD}.
\begin{lemma}[{Harmonic approximation on good scales}]
\label{l.harmonic.approximation.good.scales.GMC}
There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, for every~$s\in [512\gmcdelta^2,\nf 14]$, every~$L,m,n\in\N_0$ with~$L\geq m\geq n+5$, every~$z \in \cu_m$ and~$x \in U_{m,n-3}(z)$, and every~$u\in H^1(\cu_m)$,~$\g \in H^{s}(\cu_m;\Rd)$, and~$h \in H^{1+s}(\cu_m)$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
& -\nabla \cdot \a_L \nabla u = \nabla \cdot \g  & \text{in} & \ \cu_m\,, \\
& u = h & \text{on} & \ \partial \cu_m\,,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation*}
the following holds. Let~$y \in \cu_m$ be such that~$U_{m,n-4}(x) \subset y+\cu_{n-2} \subset U_{m,n-1}(x)$. Let~$v$ be the unique solution of the Dirichlet problem
\begin{equation}
\label{e.harmonic.approx.v.def}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
& -\Delta v = 0
& \text{in} & \ y+\cu_{n-2} \,,
\\
& v = u & \text{on} & \ \partial ( y+\cu_{n-2} ) \,.
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{align}
\lefteqn{
\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-4}(x))}
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)}
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
Cs^{-\nf32}
\mathcal{E}_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2} ; \a_L , (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}})
\notag \\ &  \qquad  \qquad
\times
\Bigl( \| u - (u)_{U_{m,n}(x)} \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n}(x))}
+ s^{-\nf32}  3^{n}\bigl| (\nabla h) _{U_{m,n}(x)} \bigr| \indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset \}}
\Bigr)
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
Cs^{-\nf{15}2} (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}}^{-1} 3^{(1+s)n} [ \g ]_{\underline{H}^s(U_{m,n}(x))}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
Cs^{-\nf72} 3^{(1+s) n}  [ \nabla h ]_{\underline{H}^{s}(U_{m,n}(x))} \indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset \}}
\,.
\label{e.good.scale.homog.GMC}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Set
\begin{equation*}
	D\coloneqq y+\cu_{n-2},\qquad
	\a_0\coloneqq(\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}}\,.
\end{equation*}
The stability estimates in Lemma~\ref{l.lambdas.stability},~\eqref{e.mathcalE.one.vs.mathcalE.two}, and Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E} give, on~$\mathcal G_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)$,
\begin{align}
	\mathcal E_{\nf s3,\infty,1}(D;\a_L,\a_0)
	+\mathcal E_{\nf s6,\infty,2}(D;\a_L,\a_0)
	&\leq C\mathcal E_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2};\a_L,\a_0)\leq C,
	\notag\\
	\a_0^{-1}\Lambda_{\nf s6,q}(Q;\a_L)
	+\a_0\lambda_{\nf s6,q}^{-1}(Q;\a_L)
	&\leq C,\qquad q\in\{1,2\}\,,
	\label{e.harmonic.approximation.local.control}
\end{align}
for every cube~$Q$ of side between~$3^{n-3}$ and~$3^{n-1}$ which is contained in~$U_{m,n}(x)$ and meets~$D$.

Let~$v_{\g}$ solve
\begin{equation*}
	-\a_0\Delta v_{\g}=\nabla\cdot\g\quad\text{in }D,
	\qquad v_{\g}=u\quad\text{on }\partial D\,.
\end{equation*}
Since constants in~$\g$ have zero divergence, the energy estimate and the fractional Poincar\'e inequality yield
\begin{equation*}
	\a_0\|\nabla(v_{\g}-v)\|_{\underline L^2(D)}
	\leq Cs^{-\nf12}3^{sn}[\g]_{\underline H^s(D)}\,.
\end{equation*}
Apply Proposition~\ref{p.general.coarse.graining} to~$u$ and~$v_{\g}$ on the cube~$D$, of side~$3^{n-2}$, with the sums in that proposition stopping at the index~$n-3$, and with
\begin{equation*}
	p=2,\qquad (s_1,s,s_2)=\left(\nf s3,\nf s2,s\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
The sums over scales defining the errors in that proposition, and the energy sum over~$k$ in~\eqref{e.general.coarse.graining.estimate.ASD}, end at~$n-3$. Extending these sums to their full ranges only increases them. Using~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.local.control}, the preceding display, and the triangle inequality, we obtain
\begin{align}
	3^{-\nf{s}{2}n}
	\|\nabla u-\nabla v\|_{\Wminusul{-\nf s2}{2}(D)}
	\indc_{\mathcal G_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)}
	\leq{}&
	Cs^{-\nf32}\mathcal E_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2};\a_L,\a_0)\,\a_0^{-\nf12}
	\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(D)}
	\notag\\
	&+Cs^{-\nf{11}2}\a_0^{-1}3^{sn}
	[\g]_{\underline H^s(D)}\,.
	\label{e.harmonic.approximation.coarse.graining}
\end{align}

It remains to estimate the energy on~$D$.  Partition~$D$ into its $9^d$ subcubes~$q+\cu_{n-4}$, with centers~$q$.  For each center~$q$, let~$P_q$ be the cube obtained from~$q+\cu_{n-2}$ by the smallest coordinatewise translation that places it inside~$\cu_m$.  The inclusions in the statement imply
\begin{equation*}
	P_q\subset U_{m,n}(x),\qquad
	q+\cu_{n-4}\subset P_q,
	\qquad
	(q+\cu_{n-3})\cap
	\bigl(\partial P_q\setminus\partial\cu_m\bigr)=\varnothing\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, the first assertion follows from
\begin{equation*}
	|\zeta-x|_\infty
	\leq \nf12 3^{n-2}+\nf12 3^{n-2}+\nf12 3^{n-1}
	<\nf12 3^n,\qquad \zeta\in P_q\,,
\end{equation*}
and the other two follow directly from the construction of~$P_q$.

If~$q+\cu_{n-3}$ does not meet~$\partial\cu_m$, we apply Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.caccioppoli} in $q+\cu_{n-3}$.  Otherwise we apply Lemma~\ref{l.coarse.grained.Caccioppoli.RHS.ASD} in~$P_q$ at~$q$; since~$q+\cu_{n-3}$ is separated from~$\partial P_q\setminus\partial\cu_m$, the only boundary condition used by the lemma is~$u=h$ on $\partial\cu_m$.  We split~$h$ into the affine function with slope~$(\nabla h)_{U_{m,n}(x)}$ and a remainder.  By the Dirichlet principle for the~$\a_L$-harmonic extension of this affine function, the affine part contributes~$C\a_0^{\nf12}|(\nabla h)_{U_{m,n}(x)}|$, and the remainder contributes at most $Cs^{-\nf12}3^{sn}[\nabla h]_{\underline H^s(U_{m,n}(x))}$. Using~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.local.control} and summing over the~$9^d$ subcubes, we obtain
\begin{align}
	\a_0^{-\nf12}
	\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(D)}
	\indc_{\mathcal G_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)}
	\leq{}&
	C3^{-n}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,n}(x)}\|_{\underline L^2(U_{m,n}(x))}
	+C|(\nabla h)_{U_{m,n}(x)}|\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)}\cap\partial\cu_m\ne\varnothing\}}
	\notag\\
	&+Cs^{-6}\a_0^{-1}3^{sn}
	[\g]_{\underline H^s(U_{m,n}(x))}
	\notag\\
	&+Cs^{-2}3^{sn}
	[\nabla h]_{\underline H^s(U_{m,n}(x))}
	\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)}\cap\partial\cu_m\ne\varnothing\}}\,.
	\label{e.harmonic.approximation.energy}
\end{align}

Finally, since~$u-v\in H_0^1(D)$ and $U_{m,n-4}(x)\subset D$,
\begin{equation*}
	\|u-v\|_{\underline L^2(U_{m,n-4}(x))}
	\leq
	C3^{(1-\nf{s}{2})n}
	\|\nabla u-\nabla v\|_{\Wminusul{-\nf s2}{2}(D)}\,.
\end{equation*}
We insert~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.energy} into~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.coarse.graining} and use the previous display.  The term of~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.coarse.graining} involving~$\g$ is bounded by the~$s^{-\nf{15}{2}}$ term in~\eqref{e.good.scale.homog.GMC}, and the factors of~$\mathcal E_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2};\a_L,\a_0)$ multiplying the forcing and boundary terms coming from~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.energy} are absorbed using~$\mathcal E_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2};\a_L,\a_0)\indc_{\mathcal G_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)}\leq C$, which is the first line of~\eqref{e.harmonic.approximation.local.control}. This gives~\eqref{e.good.scale.homog.GMC}.
\end{proof}

The next lemma is a version of~\citet[Lemma~4.11]{ASD}.

\begin{lemma}[{Excess decay on good scales}]
\label{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC}
There exists a constant~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, for every~$s\in [512\gmcdelta^2,\nf14]$ and~$\ep \in [8s^{-1}\gmcdelta^2, 1]$, every~$k\in\N$ and~$L,m,n\in\N_0$ with~$k\leq n$ and~$L\geq m \geq n+5$, every~$x,z \in \cu_m$ such that~$x \in U_{m,n-3}(z)$, and every~$u\in H^1(\cu_m)$,~$\g \in H^{s}(\cu_m;\Rd)$, and~$h \in C^{1,\nf12}(\cu_m)$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
& -\nabla \cdot \a_L \nabla u = \nabla \cdot \g  & \text{in} & \ \cu_m\,, \\
& u = h & \text{on} & \ \partial \cu_m\,,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation*}
we have the estimate
\begin{align}
\lefteqn{
E(u,U_{m,n-k}(x))
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)}
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
C \bigl( 3^{-\nf{1}{2}k} +
3^{(1+\nf{d}{2}) k}
s^{-\nf32}\ep \bigr)
E(u,U_{m,n}(x))
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
C3^{(1+\nf{d}{2}) k}
s^{-\nf32}
\mathcal{E}_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2} ; \a_L , (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}})
\notag \\ & \qquad \qquad
\times
\Bigl( \bigl| \nabla \ell (u,U_{m,n}(x)) \bigr|
+ s^{-\nf32} \bigl| (\nabla h) _{U_{m,n}(x)} \bigr| \indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset \}}
\Bigr)
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
Cs^{-\nf{15}2} 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2}) k} (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}}^{-1} 3^{sn} [ \g ]_{\underline{H}^s(U_{m,n}(x))}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
Cs^{-3} 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2}) k} 3^{\nf{1}{2} n} [ \nabla h ]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(U_{m,n}(x))}
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset\}}
\,.
\label{e.excess.decay.one.step}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Fix~$x,z\in\cu_m$ as in the statement. We may assume~$k\geq6$, since the case~$k<6$ follows by increasing~$C(d)$. Choose~$y\in\cu_m$ so that
\begin{equation*}
	U_{m,n-4}(x)\subseteq y+\cu_{n-2}\subseteq U_{m,n-1}(x)\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We approximate~$u$ by a harmonic function. Let~$v$ be the harmonic function in~$y+\cu_{n-2}$ with boundary data~$u$:
\begin{equation*}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
& -\Delta v = 0  & \text{in} & \ y+\cu_{n-2}\,,
\\
&
v = u & \text{on} & \ \partial (y+\cu_{n-2})\,.
\end{aligned}
\right.
\end{equation*}
Since~$\ep \leq 1$, the event~$\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)$ implies~$\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)$. Lemma~\ref{l.harmonic.approximation.good.scales.GMC}, applied in~$y + \cu_{n-2}$ on the good event~$\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)$, therefore yields~\eqref{e.good.scale.homog.GMC} with~$\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)}$ in place of~$\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(1,\nf s8)}$ on the left side.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We use the excess decay of harmonic functions. The function~$v$ is harmonic in~$U_{m,n-4}(x)$. If~$\overline{U_{m,n-4}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset$, then moreover~$v = u = h$ on~$\partial (y+\cu_{n-2}) \cap \partial \cu_m$, with~$h \in C^{1,\nf12}$. The Schauder estimates for harmonic functions, combined with odd reflection across the flat parts of the boundary, therefore give~$v \in C^{1,\nf12}(U_{m,n-5}(x))$ with the estimate, where~$[\,\cdot\,]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U)}$ denotes the H\"older seminorm of order~$\nf12$ on~$U$,
\begin{equation*}
[\nabla v]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U_{m,n-5}(x))}
\leq
C 3^{-\nf{1}{2} n} E(v,U_{m,n-4}(x))  + C[\nabla h]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U_{m,n-4}(x))} \indc_{\{ \overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{equation*}
It follows that, for every~$k \geq 6$,
\begin{equation*}
E(v,U_{m,n-k}(x))
\leq
C 3^{-\nf{1}{2} k} E(v,U_{m,n-4}(x))  + C 3^{\nf{1}{2}(n-k)} [\nabla h]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U_{m,n-2}(x))} \indc_{\{ \overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We conclude. By the definition of the excess~\eqref{e.excess.def} and the triangle inequality, for~$k \geq 6$,
\begin{align*}
E(u,U_{m,n-k}(x))
&
\leq
E(v,U_{m,n-k}(x))
+
3^{-(n-k)}\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-k}(x))}
\notag \\ &
\leq
C 3^{-\nf{1}{2} k} E(v,U_{m,n-4}(x))
+
3^{-(n-k)}\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-k}(x))}
\notag \\ & \quad
+
C 3^{\nf{1}{2}(n-k)} [\nabla h]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U_{m,n}(x))} \indc_{\{ \overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{align*}
For the~$\| u - v \|$ term, since~$U_{m,n-k}(x) \subset U_{m,n-4}(x)$ and~$|U_{m,n}(x)|/|U_{m,n-k}(x)| \leq C 3^{dk}$, we have
\begin{equation*}
3^{-(n-k)}\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-k}(x))} \leq C 3^{(\nf{d}{2} + 1)k} 3^{-n}\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-4}(x))}
\,.
\end{equation*}
The excess of~$v$ also satisfies~$E(v,U_{m,n-4}(x))\leq CE(u,U_{m,n}(x)) +C3^{-n}\|u-v\|_{\underline L^2(U_{m,n-4}(x))}$. Thus
\begin{align*}
E(u,U_{m,n-k}(x))
&
\leq
C 3^{-\nf{1}{2} k} E(u,U_{m,n}(x)) + C 3^{(\nf{d}{2} + 1)k} 3^{-n}\| u - v \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n-4}(x))}
\notag \\ & \quad
+
C 3^{\nf{1}{2}(n-k)} [\nabla h]_{C^{0,\nf12}(U_{m,n}(x))}
\indc_{\{ \overline{U_{m,n}(x)} \cap \partial \cu_m \neq \emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{align*}
To estimate the middle term, we use the bound from Step~1 together with the inequality
\begin{equation*}
3^{-n} \| u - (u)_{U_{m,n}(x)} \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n}(x))}
\leq
E(u,U_{m,n}(x)) + C \bigl| \nabla \ell (u,U_{m,n}(x)) \bigr|
\,.
\end{equation*}
We multiply the resulting inequality by~$\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)}$. By~\eqref{e.mathcalE.bound.for.good} and the definition of~$\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)$, we have~$\mathcal{E}_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2} ; \a_L, (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}}) \indc_{\mathcal{G}_{n+2,z}(\ep,\nf s8)} \leq C\ep$, so the part of the bound on~$\| u - v \|$ involving~$E(u,U_{m,n}(x))$ contributes~$C 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}s^{-\nf32}\ep \, E(u,U_{m,n}(x))$, the second term in the coefficient of~$E(u,U_{m,n}(x))$ in~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step}. In the part involving~$|\nabla \ell(u,U_{m,n}(x))|$, we keep the factor~$\mathcal{E}_{\nf s8,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{n+2} ; \a_L, (\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}})$ instead of replacing it by~$\ep$; this produces the second and third lines of~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step}. Finally, the averaged seminorm satisfies
\begin{equation*}
3^{sn}[\nabla h]_{\underline H^s(U_{m,n}(x))}
\leq Cs^{\nf12}3^{\nf{1}{2}n}
[\nabla h]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(U_{m,n}(x))}\,.
\end{equation*}
The forcing and boundary terms from Step~1 therefore give the last two lines of~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step}.
\end{proof}

The following lemma iterates the excess decay over a range of scales.

\begin{lemma}
\label{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}
Let~$k \in \N$ and~$\theta \in (0,1)$ with~$\theta^k \in (0, \nf 35)$. Let~$n,m \in \Z$ with~$n < m$. Let~$\{ U_j \}_{j \in \Z}$ be bounded measurable sets such that, for every~$j\leq m$, we have~$U_{j-1} \subset U_j$ and~$U_j$ contains a translate of~$\cu_{j-2}$ and is contained in a translate of~$\cu_j$. Let~$u \in L^2(U_m)$. Assume that~$\mathcal{B} \subseteq \Z \cap [n,m]$ and that~$\{\ep_j\}_{j \in \Z \cap [n,m]}$ and~$\{\delta_j\}_{j \in \Z \cap [n,m]}$ are sequences of nonnegative numbers such that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Ej.decay.assumption}
E(u,U_{j-k})
\leq \theta^k E(u,U_{j}) + \ep_j |\nabla \ell(u,U_j)| + \delta_j\,, \qquad \forall j \in \Z \cap [n,m] \setminus \mathcal{B}\,.
\end{equation}
Then there exists a constant~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, with
\begin{equation*}
A\coloneqq C(k+1)(|\mathcal B|+1)+C\sum_{j=n}^m\ep_j\,,
\end{equation*}
we have
\begin{equation}
\label{e.iteration.slope.lemma}
3^{-n} \| u - (u)_{U_n} \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_n)}
\leq
e^A \biggl(3^{-m} \| u - (u)_{U_m} \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m})}
+ \sum_{j=n}^m \delta_j\biggr)\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every~$M\geq0$ such that~$\ep_j\leq M$ for every~$j\in[n,m]\cap\Z$,
\begin{multline}
\label{e.excess.decay.lemma}
E(u,U_n)
\leq
\theta^{-C(k+1)(|\mathcal{B}|+1)}e^A
\biggl(
\theta^{m-n} E(u,U_m)
\\
+M3^{-m} \| u - (u)_{U_m} \|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m})}
+(1+M)\sum_{j=n}^m \delta_j
\biggr)\,.
\end{multline}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write~$E_j\coloneqq E(u,U_j)$ and~$\ell_j\coloneqq\ell(u,U_j)$. The inclusions assumed for~$U_{j-1}$ and~$U_j$, together with the definition of~$\ell_j$, give, for every~$j\leq m$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.iteration.gradient.stability.GMC}
|\nabla\ell_j-\nabla\ell_{j-1}|
\leq C(E_j+E_{j-1})\,.
\end{equation}
Let~$[a,b]\cap\Z$ be a maximal integer interval contained in~$([n,m]\cap\Z)\setminus\mathcal B$, and partition it into residue classes modulo~$k$. Summing~\eqref{e.Ej.decay.assumption} in each class and using~$\theta^k<3/5$ gives, for~$i\in[a,b]\cap\Z$,
\begin{align}
\sum_{j=i}^{b}E_j
&\leq C3^{(3+\nf{3d}{2})k}E_b
+C\sum_{j=i}^{b}\ep_j|\nabla\ell_j|
+C\sum_{j=i}^{b}\delta_j,
\label{e.iteration.excess.sum.GMC}\\
E_i+|\nabla\ell_i|
&\leq
\exp\biggl(C(k+1)+C\sum_{j=i}^{b}\ep_j\biggr)
\biggl(E_b+|\nabla\ell_b|+\sum_{j=i}^{b}\delta_j\biggr)\,.
\label{e.iteration.interval.GMC}
\end{align}
Indeed, the first inequality uses only that~$U_j$ contains a translate of~$\cu_{j-2}$ and is contained in a translate of~$\cu_j$, in order to bound the at most~$k$ terms with the largest indices by multiples of~$E_b$. Combining the first inequality with~\eqref{e.iteration.gradient.stability.GMC} gives a recursive bound on~$|\nabla\ell_i-\nabla\ell_b|$, from which the discrete Gronwall inequality yields the second inequality.

For every~$j\leq m$, inequality~\eqref{e.iteration.gradient.stability.GMC} and the definition of the excess imply
\begin{equation*}
E_{j-1}+|\nabla\ell_{j-1}|
\leq C(E_j+|\nabla\ell_j|)\,.
\end{equation*}
There are at most~$|\mathcal B|+1$ maximal integer intervals contained in~$([n,m]\cap\Z)\setminus\mathcal B$, and each interval has at most~$k$ incomplete residue classes. Iterating~\eqref{e.iteration.interval.GMC} over these intervals and across the indices in~$\mathcal B$ yields
\begin{equation}
\label{e.iteration.combined.GMC}
E_n+|\nabla\ell_n|
\leq e^A
\biggl(E_m+|\nabla\ell_m|+\sum_{j=n}^{m}\delta_j\biggr)\,.
\end{equation}
The definition of the best affine approximation gives
\begin{equation*}
E_m+|\nabla\ell_m|
\leq C3^{-m}\|u-(u)_{U_m}\|_{\underline L^2(U_m)}
\quad\text{and}\quad
3^{-n}\|u-(u)_{U_n}\|_{\underline L^2(U_n)}
\leq C(E_n+|\nabla\ell_n|)\,.
\end{equation*}
Together with~\eqref{e.iteration.combined.GMC}, these inequalities prove~\eqref{e.iteration.slope.lemma}.

To prove~\eqref{e.excess.decay.lemma}, we again sum~\eqref{e.Ej.decay.assumption} separately over the residue classes modulo~$k$, but now keep track of the powers of~$\theta$. For every maximal integer interval~$[a,b]\cap\Z$ contained in~$([n,m]\cap\Z)\setminus\mathcal B$ and every~$i\in[a,b]\cap\Z$,
\begin{equation*}
E_i
\leq
e^{C(k+1)}\theta^{-C(k+1)}
\biggl(
\theta^{b-i}E_b
+M\max_{i\leq j\leq b}|\nabla\ell_j|
+\sum_{j=i}^{b}\delta_j
\biggr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Iterating this inequality over the maximal intervals and the indices in~$\mathcal B$, and then using~\eqref{e.iteration.combined.GMC} to bound the gradients, gives~\eqref{e.excess.decay.lemma}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The large-scale H\"older estimate}
\label{ss.regularity.iteration}

In this subsection we prove Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}. Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} iterates the excess decay estimate~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step} of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC} from scale~$m$ down to scale~$n$, at the cost of a factor controlled by the number of bad scales between~$n$ and~$m$ and by the sum of the coarse-graining errors on the good scales. The minimal scale~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ is built from the random variables~$M$ and~$N$ of Lemmas~\ref{l.sum.the.errors} and~\ref{l.min.scale.good.scale}. For every~$n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$, both the number of bad scales and the sum of the coarse-graining errors are at most~$C$ plus a small multiple of~$(1-\gamma)(m-n)$, and hence the factor is at most~$C3^{\nf14(1-\gamma)(m-n)}$.

\subsubsection{The minimal scale}
\label{ss.minimal.scale.sep}

The iteration requires bounds for
\begin{equation}
\label{e.sum.of.eps.k}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\mathcal{E}_{\nf1{16},\infty,2}(\cu_k ; \a_L,(\b_{L,k})_{\cu_k}) \indc_{\mathcal{G}_k(\ep,\nf1{16}) }
\end{equation}
and for the number of bad scales between~$n$ and~$m$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.number.bad.scales}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\bigl( 1 - \indc_{\mathcal{G}_k(\ep,\nf1{16}) }\bigr) \,.
\end{equation}
Propositions~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E} and~\ref{p.density.of.good.scales} control~\eqref{e.sum.of.eps.k} and~\eqref{e.number.bad.scales}, respectively.

\smallskip

For~$k\in\N_0$,~$z\in\Rd$, and~$s\in(0,\nf12]$, define
\begin{align}
\label{e.def.bfG.mhq}
\mathbf{G}_{k,z}(s)
&
\coloneqq
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k} \sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{2}s (k-l)}
\max_{z' \in z+\bigl(3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})\bigr)}
\min\!\left\{
\max_{|e| = 1}J(z'+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_l^{-1}\a_l),1
\right\}^{\nf12}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k}
3^{-\nf{1}{8}s(k-j)} \| \g_{k,j} \|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_{k})}
+
3^{-\nf{1}{8}sk}\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_k)}
+
\sum_{j=k}^\infty
3^{k}
\| \nabla \g_j \|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_k)}
\,.
\end{align}
Here and below, a supremum over an empty set of integer indices is zero.

\begin{lemma}
\label{l.sum.the.errors}
Let~$s \in (0,\nf 12]$. There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, if~$s \geq C \gmcdelta^2 \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|$, then, for every~$m,n \in\N_0$ with~$n\leq m$ and~$z \in \Rd$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.after.good.scales.uniform}
\avsum_{k=n}^{m}
\mathbf{G}_{k,z}(s)
\leq
Cs^{-\nf 72}   \gmcdelta + \O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C s^{-\nf 72}  \gmcdelta \left| \log \gmcdelta \right| ^{\nf 12}  (m-n+1)^{-\nf12} \bigr)
 \,.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for every~$\lambda > 0$ satisfying~$\lambda \geq Cs^{-\nf 72}  \gmcdelta \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} $ and every~$m\in\N_0$, there exists a random variable~$M(\lambda,s,m)\in\{-1,0,\ldots,m\}$ such that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.M.lambda.m.bound}
0\leq m-M(\lambda,s,m)
\leq
1+\O_{\Gamma_1}\bigl(
Cs^{-7}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|\lambda^{-2}
\bigr)
\end{equation}
and, for every~$n \in \N_0$ satisfying~$n \leq M(\lambda,s,m)$, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{e.after.good.scales}
\max_{z \in 3^n \Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\mathbf{G}_{j,z}(s)
\leq
\lambda (m-n) \,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We estimate the terms of~$\mathbf G_{k,z}(s)$ involving the fields~$\g_j$. By stationarity it suffices to prove~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform} for~$z=0$. The triangle inequality gives
\begin{align*}
&
\sum_{k=n}^m
\left(
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k}
3^{-\nf{1}{8}s(k-j)} \| \g_{k,j} \|_{L^\infty(\cu_{k})}
+3^{-\nf{1}{8}sk}\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(\cu_k)}
\right)
\notag \\ & \qquad
\leq
2\sum_{k=n}^m
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k}
3^{-\nf{1}{8} s(k-j)}
\sum_{i=j}^k
\|  \mathbf{g}_i  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\notag \\ & \qquad
\leq
2\sum_{k=n}^m
\sum_{i=0}^k
\sup_{0\leq j \leq i}
3^{-\nf{1}{8} s(k-j)}
\|  \mathbf{g}_i  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\notag \\ & \qquad
=
2\sum_{k=n}^m
\sum_{i=0}^k
3^{-\nf{1}{8} s(k-i)}
\|  \mathbf{g}_i  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\notag \\ & \qquad
=
2\biggl(
\sum_{i=n}^m
\sum_{k=i}^m
+
\sum_{k=n}^m \sum_{i=0}^{n-1}  \biggr)
3^{-\nf{1}{8} s(k-i)}
\|  \mathbf{g}_i  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\,.
\end{align*}
By~\eqref{e.same.law.shift} and~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, together with~\eqref{e.max.scaling.O.Gamma.Two}, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
\|  \mathbf{g}_i  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)} \leq \O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C \gmcdelta (k-i+1)^{\nf 12} \bigr)
\end{equation*}
and therefore, by independence, also
\begin{align*}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\left(
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k}
3^{-\nf{1}{8}s(k-j)} \| \g_{k,j} \|_{L^\infty(\cu_{k})}
+3^{-\nf{1}{8}sk}\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(\cu_k)}
\right)
&\leq
C s^{-\nf 32} \gmcdelta(m-n+1)
\\ &\quad+
\O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C s^{-\nf 32}  \gmcdelta (m-n+1)^{\nf 12} \bigr)
 \,.
\end{align*}
For the gradient term,
\begin{align*}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\sum_{l=k}^\infty
3^{k}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
&
=
\sum_{l=n}^\infty
\sum_{k=n}^{m\wedge l}
3^{k}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\notag \\ &
\leq
\frac 32
\sum_{l=n}^\infty
3^{m\wedge l }
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_l)}
\notag \\ &
=
\frac 32
\sum_{l=n}^{m-1}
3^{l}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_l)}
+
\frac 32
\sum_{l=m}^\infty
3^{m}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_l)}
\,.
\end{align*}
Again by~\eqref{e.same.law.shift},~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, and independence,
\begin{equation*}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\sum_{l=k}^\infty
3^{k}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
\leq
C \gmcdelta(m-n+1) + \O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl(C \gmcdelta (m-n+1)^{\nf12}\bigr)
 \,.
\end{equation*}
Thus
\begin{multline}
\label{e.sum.the.errors.fields}
\sum_{k=n}^m
\sum_{l=k}^\infty
3^{k}
\|  \nabla \g_l  \|_{L^{\infty}(\cu_k)}
+
\sum_{k=n}^m
\left(
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k}
3^{-\nf{1}{8}s(k-j)} \| \g_{k,j} \|_{L^\infty(\cu_{k})}
+3^{-\nf{1}{8}sk}\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(\cu_k)}
\right)
\\
\leq
C s^{-\nf 32} \gmcdelta(m-n+1) +
\O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C s^{-\nf 32}  \gmcdelta (m-n+1)^{\nf 12} \bigr)
 \,.
\end{multline}

\emph{Step 2.} We estimate the term of~$\mathbf G_{k,z}(s)$ involving~$J$. Bounding the supremum over~$j$ by a sum, interchanging the sums, and summing the geometric series in~$k$, we obtain
\begin{align}
\lefteqn{
\sum_{k=n}^m
\sup_{0\leq j \leq k} \sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{2}s (k-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\!\left\{\max_{|e|=1}J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,;\ahom_l^{-1}\a_l),1\right\}^{\nf12}
} \quad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
\sum_{j=0}^{m}
\sum_{k=n \vee j}^m
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (k-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\notag \\ &
\leq
4s^{-1}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (j-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+
4s^{-1}
\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (n-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\,.
\label{e.split.J.for.minimal.scale}
\end{align}
By Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, for~$\xi  \in [1, C^{-1} \gmcdelta^{-2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|^{-1}]$,
\begin{equation*}
\E \Bigl[  \max_{|e| = 1} \bigl( J(\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})
\bigr) ^{\nf \xi2}
\Bigr]^{\nf1\xi}
\leq
C\gmcdelta  \log(2+\xi)^{\nf 12} \xi^{\nf 12}
\,.
\end{equation*}
Hence, for every~$\xi \in [1,\infty)$,
\begin{equation*}
\E\biggl[
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf \xi2}  \biggr]^{\nf 1\xi}
\leq
C\gmcdelta  \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} \xi^{\nf 12} \,,
\end{equation*}
which yields
\begin{equation*}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\leq \O_{\Gamma_2} \bigl( C\gmcdelta  \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12}  \bigr)
 \,.
\end{equation*}
Moreover, by H\"older's inequality, the condition~$s \geq C \gmcdelta^{2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|$, a union bound, and stationarity,
\begin{align*}
\lefteqn{
\E\biggl[ \sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (j-l)}  \max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12} \biggr]
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
\E\biggl[ \sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-2d (j-l)}  \max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf {4d}{s} } \biggr]^{\nf {s}{8d}}
\notag \\ &
\leq
\biggl( \sum_{l=0}^{j-2} 3^{-d (j-l)}
\E\Bigl[  \max_{|e| = 1} J(\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})^{\nf {4d}{s} } \Bigr]\biggr)^{\! \nf {s}{8d}}
\notag \\ &
\leq
C s^{-\nf 12} \left| \log s \right|^{\nf 12}
\gmcdelta
 \,,
\end{align*}
and, using~\eqref{e.max.scaling.O.Gamma.Two},
\begin{equation*}
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (j-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\leq
\O_{\Gamma_2} \bigl( Cs^{-\nf 32} \gmcdelta  \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} \bigr) \,.
\end{equation*}
\emph{Step 3.} We prove~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform}. We split the indices~$j$ into the finitely many residue classes modulo the integer~$r(d)$ from the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.discounted.J.local}; as shown there, the summands with indices in the same class are independent. In each class, we apply~\eqref{e.CLT.scaling.O.Gamma.Two} to the centered summands, which are~$\O_{\Gamma_2}(Cs^{-\nf{3}{2}}\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf{1}{2}})$ by the last display, and combining the result with the preceding estimates we obtain
\begin{align*}
\lefteqn{
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (j-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
}  \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
C s^{-\nf 12} \left| \log s \right|^{\nf 12}  \gmcdelta (m-n+1)+ \O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C s^{-\nf 32} \gmcdelta \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} (m-n+1)^{\nf 12} \bigr)
 \,.
\end{align*}
Applying the last display before Step~3 to the annuli with~$j<n$ and summing the resulting geometric series gives
\begin{multline*}
\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}
\sup_{0\leq l \leq j-2} 3^{-\nf{1}{4}s (n-l)}
\max_{z \in 3^l \Zd \cap (\cu_j \setminus \cu_{j-1})}
\min\Bigl\{  \max_{|e| = 1} J(z+\cu_l,e,e\,; \ahom_{l}^{-1} \a_{l})\, ,  1 \Bigr\}^{\nf 12}
\\
\leq
\O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl( C s^{-\nf 52}  \gmcdelta  \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} \bigr)
\,.
\end{multline*}
The last two displays, together with~\eqref{e.split.J.for.minimal.scale} and~\eqref{e.sum.the.errors.fields}, give~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We define~$M(\lambda,s,m)$ and prove~\eqref{e.M.lambda.m.bound}. Define
\begin{align*}
M(\lambda,s,m)
 \coloneqq
\min\!\left(
\biggl\{ n \in \N_0 \; : \;  n \leq m-1\,, \ \max_{z\in3^{n} \Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\mathbf{G}_{j,z}(s)
>
\lambda (m-n)
\biggr\}\cup\{m\}\right)-1
\,.
\end{align*}
The averaged estimate~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform}, stationarity, and the sub-Gaussian tail in that estimate show that, for every~$0\leq k\leq m-1$,
\begin{equation*}
\P\!\left[
\max_{z\in3^k\Zd\cap\cu_m}
\sum_{j=k}^m\mathbf G_{j,z}(s)>\lambda(m-k)
\right]
\leq
\exp\!\left(
C(m-k)-\frac{s^7\lambda^2(m-k)}{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\right)\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, there are at most~$C3^{d(m-k)}$ centers, and we apply~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform}, translated, at each of them. The same bound is valid for~$k=m$ because its right side is one. Consequently, a union bound gives, for every~$\lambda \geq 2 Cs^{-\nf 72}   \gmcdelta \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|^{\nf 12} $ and~$0\leq n\leq m-1$,
\begin{align}
\label{e.M.lambda.m.tail}
\P \bigl[ M(\lambda,s,m) \leq n \bigr]
&
\leq
\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}
\P \biggl[
\max_{z\in3^k \Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=k}^{m}
\mathbf{G}_{j,z}(s)
>
\lambda (m-k)
\biggr]
\notag \\ &
\leq
\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}
\exp \biggl( C (m-k)
-\frac{s^7 \lambda^2  (m-k) }{C \gmcdelta^{2} \left| \log \gmcdelta \right| }
\biggr)
\leq
C\exp \biggl(
-\frac{s^7 \lambda^2  (m-n-1) }{C\gmcdelta^{2} \left| \log \gmcdelta \right|}
\biggr)\,.
\end{align}
This implies~\eqref{e.M.lambda.m.bound} and completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}
\label{l.min.scale.good.scale}
There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, for every~$\lambda,\ep ,s \in (0,1]$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{e.holder.induction.smallness}
\lambda \geq C s^{-8}   \ep^{-2} \gmcdelta^{2} \left| \log \gmcdelta\right|
\end{equation}
and for every~$m \in \N_0$, there exists a random variable~$N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)\in\{-1,0,\ldots,m\}$ such that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.N.theta.m.bound.goodscales}
0\leq m-N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)
\leq
1+\O_{\Gamma_1}\bigl(
Cs^{-8}\ep^{-2}\lambda^{-1}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|
\bigr)
\end{equation}
and, for every~$n \in \N_0$ with~$n \leq N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.goodscales.minscale}
\min_{z\in 3^n\Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\indc{\bigl\{ \mathcal{G}_{j,z}(\ep,s) \bigr\}}
>
(1-\lambda) (m-n)  \,.
\end{equation}
Equivalently, every such~$n$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.bad.scale.count.from.N}
\max_{z\in3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^m
\bigl(1-\indc{\{\mathcal G_{j,z}(\ep,s)\}}\bigr)
<1+\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Define the random variable~$N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)$ by
\begin{align*}
N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)
&\coloneqq
\min\!\Biggl(
\Biggl\{n\in\N_0:n\leq m,\quad
\min_{z\in3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}\indc{\{\mathcal G_{j,z}(\ep,s)\}}
\\
&\hspace{47mm}\leq(1-\lambda)(m-n)
\Biggr\}\cup\{m+1\}\Biggr)-1
\,.
\end{align*}
The definition of~$N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)$ gives~\eqref{e.goodscales.minscale}. On the event in the next display, the proportion of bad scales among the~$m-n+1$ scales from~$n$ to~$m$ is at least~$\lambda/2$. Proposition~\ref{p.density.of.good.scales}, applied with~$\lambda/2$ in place of~$\theta$, therefore gives
\begin{equation}
\P \Biggl[
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
(1-\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j,z}(\ep,s)})
>
\lambda (m-n)  \Biggr]
\leq
\exp \biggl(
-\frac{s^8\ep^2\lambda(m-n+1)}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\biggr)
\,.
\end{equation}
By a union bound, stationarity, and the condition~\eqref{e.holder.induction.smallness} with sufficiently large~$C$, we obtain
\begin{align}
\P \Biggl[
\max_{z\in 3^n\Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
(1-\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j,z}(\ep,s)})
>
\lambda (m-n)  \Biggr]
&
\leq
3^{d(m-n)}
\P \Biggl[
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
(1-\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j}(\ep,s)})
>
\lambda (m-n)  \Biggr]
\notag \\ &
\leq
\exp \biggl(
2d(m-n)
-\frac{s^8\ep^2\lambda(m-n+1)}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\biggr)
\notag \\ &
\leq
\exp \biggl(
-\frac{s^8\ep^2\lambda(m-n+1)}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\biggr)
\,.
\end{align}
By another union bound, for~$0\leq n\leq m-1$,
\begin{align}
\label{e.N.lambda.m.tail}
\P \bigl[ N(\lambda,\ep,s,m) \leq n \bigr]
&
\leq
\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}
\P \Biggl[
\min_{z\in 3^k\Zd \cap \cu_m}
\sum_{j=k}^{m}
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j,z}(\ep,s) }
\leq
(1-\lambda) (m-k)  \Biggr]
\leq
C\exp \biggl(
-\frac{s^8\ep^2\lambda(m-n)}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\biggr)
\,.
\end{align}
This implies~\eqref{e.N.theta.m.bound.goodscales}, completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[{Proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}}]
\emph{Steps~1--2.} We fix the parameters and the minimal scale. Fix~$\gamma$ in the range allowed by the statement. Choose the dimensional constants in the order indicated below and set
\begin{align}
s_0&\coloneqq \nf{1}{32}, \notag\\
\lambda&\coloneqq C_1^{-1}(1-\gamma),
\label{e.choices.lambda.Holder.proof}\\
\ep&\coloneqq C_2^{-1}(1-\gamma)^{\nf12},
\label{e.choices.hepk.Holder.proof}\\
k&\coloneqq\left\lceil4\log_3\!\left(4s_0^{-\nf32}
C_3\right)\right\rceil\,, \notag
\end{align}
where~$C_3(d)$ is the constant of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC}. Choose~$C_1(d)\geq2$ sufficiently large for both~\eqref{e.exp.lambda.bound} below and the absorption in the proof of~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} in Step~6, and then choose~$C_2$ so that~$C_1C_2^{-8}\leq1$ and
\begin{equation*}
C_3
\left(3^{-\nf{k}{2}}+3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}s_0^{-\nf32}C_2^{-1}\right)
\leq\frac12\,3^{-\nf{k}{4}}\,.
\end{equation*}
Finally, choose the constant~$C$ of the statement large enough, depending on~$C_1$ and~$C_2$. The lower bound on~$1-\gamma$ then ensures, for all sufficiently small~$\gmcdelta$, that the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC}, Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E}, Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors}, and Lemma~\ref{l.min.scale.good.scale} hold.

For~$m\in\N_0$, define
\begin{equation}
\label{e.L.def.reg}
\mathcal{L}_L(\gamma,m) \coloneqq
\max\bigl\{ m - M(\lambda,s_0,m) \,,\, m - N(\lambda,\ep,s_0,m) \bigr\} + k + 5\,,
\end{equation}
where~$M$ and~$N$ are supplied by Lemmas~\ref{l.sum.the.errors} and~\ref{l.min.scale.good.scale}. They do not involve~$L$, so~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ is the same random variable for every~$L\geq m$.  The summand~$k+5$ ensures that every scale used in Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC} is at most~$m-5$. Estimates~\eqref{e.M.lambda.m.bound} and~\eqref{e.N.theta.m.bound.goodscales} yield
\begin{align}
\label{e.mathcal.L.bound.inproof}
\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)
&\leq C+\O_{\Gamma_1}\bigl( Cs_0^{-7}\gmcdelta^2\left|\log\gmcdelta\right|\lambda^{-2}
  + Cs_0^{-8}\ep^{-2}\lambda^{-1}\gmcdelta^2\left|\log\gmcdelta\right| \bigr)
\notag \\ &
\leq C+\O_{\Gamma_1}\bigl( CC_1^2(1-\gamma)^{-2}\gmcdelta^2\left|\log\gmcdelta\right| \bigr)\,.
\end{align}
This is~\eqref{e.mathcal.L.bound}. Finally, \eqref{e.after.good.scales} and~\eqref{e.goodscales.minscale} imply, whenever~$n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.after.good.scales.applied}
\max_{z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m} \sum_{j=n}^{m} \mathbf{G}_{j,z}(s_0) \leq \lambda(m-n)
\qand
\max_{z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m} \sum_{j=n}^{m}
\bigl(1 - \indc{\bigl\{\mathcal{G}_{j,z}(\ep,s_0)\bigr\}}\bigr)
<1+\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}

\smallskip

\emph{Step~3.} We prove the excess decay estimate~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step} on good scales. Fix~$m\in\N_0$ with~$L\geq m$, and let~$u\in H^1(\cu_m)$ be a solution of~\eqref{e.eq.for.u.holder.estimate}. Fix~$n\in\N_0$ with~$n \leq m-\mathcal{L}_L(\gamma,m)$ and~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$. We apply Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC} with~$s = 8s_0 = \nf14$ at the scales~$j\in\N_0\cap[n+k,m-5]$, for which its hypotheses hold. For each such~$j$, the conclusion~\eqref{e.excess.decay.one.step}, with~$j$ in place of~$n$ and with~$x=z$, gives
\begin{align}
E(u,U_{m,j-k}(z))\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)}
&\leq
C_3\bigl(3^{-\nf{1}{2} k}
+ 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}s_0^{-\nf32}\ep\bigr) E(u,U_{m,j}(z))
\notag \\ & \quad
+ C_3 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k} s_0^{-\nf32}
\mathcal{E}_{s_0,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{j+2};\a_L,(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}})
\notag \\ & \quad\quad
\times \Bigl(\bigl|\nabla\ell(u,U_{m,j}(z))\bigr|
+ s_0^{-\nf32}\bigl|(\nabla h)_{U_{m,j}(z)}\bigr|
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,j}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}\Bigr)
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)}
\notag \\ & \quad
+ Cs_0^{-\nf{15}2} 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}
(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}^{-1} 3^{8s_0 j}[\g]_{\underline{H}^{8s_0}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\notag \\ & \quad
+ Cs_0^{-3} 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}
3^{\nf{1}{2} j}[\nabla h]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,j}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}\,.
\label{e.one.step.expanded}
\end{align}
By our choice of~$k$ and~$C_2$, we have~$C_3(3^{-\nf{k}{2}} + 3^{(1+\nf{d}{2})k}s_0^{-\nf32}\ep) \leq \nf{1}{2}3^{-\nf{k}{4}}$. Definition~\ref{d.bLm} and the fact that an average lies between the infimum and the supremum give
\begin{align*}
&\max\!\left\{
\frac{(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}}{(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}},
\frac{(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}}{(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}}
\right\}
\\
&\quad\leq
\max\!\left\{
\frac{\ahom_{j+2}}{\ahom_m},
\frac{\ahom_m}{\ahom_{j+2}}
\right\}
\exp\!\left(
\sum_{i=j+3}^m\|\g_i-\tau^2\|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_{j+2})}
+\osc_{\cu_m\cup(z+\cu_{j+2})}\sum_{i=m+1}^L\g_i
\right)\,.
\end{align*}
Equations~\eqref{e.g.m.j} and~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq} give
\begin{equation*}
\sum_{i=j+3}^m\|\g_i-\tau^2\|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_{j+2})}
\leq \tau^2(m-j-2)+C\sum_{i=j+2}^m\mathbf G_{i,z}(s_0)\,.
\end{equation*}
By the mean-value theorem and~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq},
\begin{align*}
\osc_{\cu_m\cup(z+\cu_{j+2})}\sum_{i=m+1}^L\g_i
&\leq C\sum_{i=m+1}^\infty 3^m
\left(
\|\nabla\g_i\|_{L^\infty(z+\cu_m)}
+\|\nabla\g_i\|_{L^\infty(\cu_m)}
\right)
\leq C\bigl(\mathbf G_{m,z}(s_0)+\mathbf G_{m,0}(s_0)\bigr)\,.
\end{align*}
Applying~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.applied} at the centers~$z$ and~$0$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
\sum_{i=j+2}^m\mathbf G_{i,z}(s_0)+\mathbf G_{m,0}(s_0)
\leq C\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation*}
The parameter choice in Steps~1--2 gives~$\tau^2\leq C\gmcdelta^2\leq C\lambda$. Together with~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, the preceding estimates show that, for every~$j\in\N_0\cap[n,m-5]$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.ratio.of.bs}
C^{-1}\exp\bigl(-C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\leq (\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1}
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover,
\begin{equation*}
3^{8s_0 j}[\g]_{\underline{H}^{8s_0}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\leq C3^{\nf{1}{2} j}[\g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\leq C3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)}\bigl(3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}\bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
so, using~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs},
\begin{equation}
\label{e.forcing.j.bound}
(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}^{-1} 3^{8s_0 j}[\g]_{\underline{H}^{8s_0}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr) 3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)}
\bigl((\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
The boundary datum satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.boundary.j.bound}
3^{\nf{1}{2} j}[\nabla h]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(U_{m,j}(z))}
\leq C3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)}\bigl(3^{\nf{1}{2} m}\|\nabla h\|_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
Furthermore, we use the following elementary inequality: if~$D\geq0$ and~$0\leq x\leq\ep 3^{s_0D/16}$, then
\begin{equation*}
	3^{-s_0D/2}x
	\leq 3^{-s_0D/2}\min\{x,1\}+\ep^8\,.
\end{equation*}
Indeed, the second term is needed only when~$x>1$, in which case~$3^{-s_0D/16}<\ep$. By~\eqref{e.good.sum.J}, on~$\mathcal G_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)$ it applies to the square root~$x$ of every quantity~$J$ on the right side of~\eqref{e.mathcalE.bound.for.good}, with~$D$ the difference between the scale~$j+2$ and the scale of that quantity.  Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E} and~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq} therefore give
\begin{equation}
\label{e.mathcalE.bound.applied}
\mathcal{E}_{s_0,\infty,2}(z+\cu_{j+2};\a_L,(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}})
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)}
\leq C\min\bigl\{\ep\,,\,
s_0^{-1}\gmcdelta^2+\ep^8+\mathbf{G}_{j+2,z}(s_0)\bigr\}\,.
\end{equation}
Inserting these bounds into~\eqref{e.one.step.expanded} yields, for every~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$ and~$j\in\N_0\cap[n+k,m-5]$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.ready.for.iteration.lemma}
E(u,U_{m,j-k}(z))\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)}
\leq 3^{-\nf{k}{4}} E(u,U_{m,j}(z))
+ \ep_{j,z}\bigl|\nabla\ell(u,U_{m,j}(z))\bigr|
+ \delta_{j,z}\,,
\end{equation}
where we define
\begin{equation}
\label{e.ep.j.z.def}
\ep_{j,z} \coloneqq
Cs_0^{-\nf32}\min\bigl\{\ep\,,\,\gmcdelta^2+\ep^8+\mathbf{G}_{j+2,z}(s_0)\bigr\}
\indc_{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{align}
\label{e.delta.j.z.def}
\delta_{j,z} &\coloneqq
C3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)}\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+ C\Bigl(\ep_{j,z}\|\nabla h\|_{L^\infty(\cu_m)}
+ 3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}\|\nabla h\|_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\Bigr)\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,j}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}} \,.
\end{align}
The geometric factor~$3^{-\nf{1}{2}(m-j)}$ in the last term comes from~\eqref{e.boundary.j.bound}. Since the sets~$U_{m,j}(z)$ are nested, for~$j\leq m'$,
\begin{equation*}
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,j}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}
\leq
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,m'}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.applied}, we have
\begin{equation}
\label{e.ep.j.z.sum}
\sum_{j=n}^{m-2}\ep_{j,z}
\leq Cs_0^{-\nf32}\biggl((\gmcdelta^2+\ep^8)(m-n)
+ \sum_{j=n}^{m-2}\mathbf{G}_{j+2,z}(s_0)\biggr)
\leq C\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}
Therefore, we also obtain, for every~$m',n'\in\N_0$ with~$n\leq n'\leq m'\leq m-3$,
\begin{align}
\label{e.delta.j.z.sum}
\sum_{j=n'}^{m'}\delta_{j,z}
&\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1}
3^{\nf{1}{2} m'}[\g]_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\notag \\ & \qquad
+ C\Bigl(\lambda(m-n)\|\nabla h\|_{L^\infty(\cu_m)}
+ 3^{\nf{1}{2} m'}\|\nabla h\|_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\Bigr)\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,m'}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{align}

\smallskip

\emph{Step~4.} We set up the iteration. The inequality~\eqref{e.ready.for.iteration.lemma} is the key hypothesis~\eqref{e.Ej.decay.assumption} of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} (with step~$k$ and~$\theta=3^{-\nf14}$). For~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$, we define the set~$\mathcal B$ of indices by
\begin{equation}
\label{e.bad.bad.z}
\mathcal{B} \coloneqq
\bigl\{j\in\N_0\cap[n,m-2] : j<n+k
\ \text{or}\ \mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0) \text{ fails}\bigr\}\,.
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.applied} and the definition~\eqref{e.bad.bad.z},
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Bz.bound}
|\mathcal B|
\leq k+\sum_{j=n+k}^{m-2}\bigl(1-\indc{\bigl\{\mathcal{G}_{j+2,z}(\ep,s_0)\bigr\}}\bigr)
\leq k+1+\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}
This and~\eqref{e.ep.j.z.sum} imply, for the constant~$A$ in Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}, that
\begin{equation*}
e^A
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since~$k$ is a dimensional constant,~\eqref{e.Bz.bound} shows that~$3^{C(k+1)(|\mathcal B|+1)/4}e^A$ satisfies the same bound, with a larger~$C$. We apply~\eqref{e.excess.decay.lemma} with~$M=C\ep$, which is admissible because~$\ep_{j,z}\leq C\ep$ by~\eqref{e.ep.j.z.def}. For every~$n',m'\in\N_0$ with~$n\leq n'\leq m'\leq m-5$, Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} gives the following two inequalities (for~$n'=m'$ they are immediate). The first inequality, from~\eqref{e.iteration.slope.lemma}, is
\begin{equation}
\label{e.iteration.slope.applied}
3^{-n'}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,n'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n'}(z))}
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\biggl(3^{-m'}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,m'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,m'}(z))}
+ \sum_{j=n'}^{m'}\delta_{j,z}\biggr)
\end{equation}
and the second inequality, the excess decay estimate, is
\begin{equation}
\label{e.excess.decay.applied}
E(u,U_{m,n'}(z))
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\biggl(3^{-\nf{m'-n'}{4}} E(u,U_{m,m'}(z))
+ \ep\, 3^{-m'}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,m'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,m'}(z))}
+ \sum_{j=n'}^{m'}\delta_{j,z}\biggr)\,.
\end{equation}

\smallskip

\emph{Step~5.} We prove the large-scale H\"older estimate~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain}. We show that, for every~$n,n',m'\in\N_0$ with~$n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ and~$n\leq n'\leq m'\leq m-5$ and every~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$, we have the estimate
\begin{align}
\label{e.Campanato.full.domain}
\lefteqn{3^{-n'}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,n'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n'}(z))}} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq C3^{\nf{1}{2}(1-\gamma)(m-n)}
\Bigl(3^{-m'}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,m'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,m'}(z))}
+ (\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}\Bigr)
\notag \\ & \qquad
+ C3^{\nf{1}{2}(1-\gamma)(m-n)} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}\|\nabla h\|_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\indc_{\{\overline{U_{m,m'}(z)}\cap\partial\cu_m\neq\emptyset\}}
\,.
\end{align}
By the choice of~$C_1$ and the identity~$\lambda=C_1^{-1}(1-\gamma)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.exp.lambda.bound}
(1+\lambda(m-n))\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\leq C\exp\bigl(2CC_1^{-1}(1-\gamma)(m-n)\bigr)
\leq C3^{\nf{1}{4}(1-\gamma)(m-n)}\,.
\end{equation}
Combining~\eqref{e.iteration.slope.applied},~\eqref{e.delta.j.z.sum}, and~\eqref{e.exp.lambda.bound} gives~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain}. For each~$j\in\N_0$ with~$j\leq n$ and each~$y\in3^j\Zd\cap((x+\cu_n)\cap\cu_m)$, we apply this estimate with~$j$ in place of~$n$, with~$n'=j$,~$m'=m-5$, and~$z=y$. If~$x\in\cu_{m-1}$, then~$y\in x+\cu_n$ and~$n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)\leq m-k-5$, so~$\overline{U_{m,m-5}(y)}\subset\cu_m$ and the boundary indicator in~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain} vanishes; hence this indicator is always bounded by~$\indc_{\{x\notin\cu_{m-1}\}}$. Together with
\begin{equation*}
3^{-\nf{1}{2}(1+\gamma)(m-j)}
\leq 3^{-\gamma(m-j)}\,,
\end{equation*}
this proves~\eqref{e.Holder.estimate.boxes.local}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step~6.} We prove the energy estimate~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}. We use the estimates of Lemma~\ref{l.coarse.graining.RHS} and the Caccioppoli inequality of Lemma~\ref{l.coarse.grained.Caccioppoli.RHS.ASD} to convert the large-scale H\"older estimate~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain} into a gradient bound; this is possible only at good scales, where we control the coarse-grained ellipticities.

\smallskip

Fix~$x\in\cu_m$. If~$m-n\leq2k+14$, then~$|(x+\cu_n)\cap\cu_m|\geq c3^{nd}\geq c|\cu_m|$, and~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} follows directly from the energy in~$\cu_m$. We may therefore assume that~$m-n>2k+14$. Then, since~$\lambda\leq\nf12$, the interval~$[n+2,m-5]$ contains more than~$k+1+\lambda(m-n)$ integers, so by~\eqref{e.Bz.bound} it contains a scale outside the set~$\mathcal B$ of Step~4, for the center~$z$ chosen below. Choose~$y\in\cu_m$ such that
\begin{equation*}
(x+\cu_{n+1})\cap\cu_m \subseteq y+\cu_{n+1} \subseteq \cu_m
\quad\text{and}\quad
(x+\cu_n)\cap\partial(y+\cu_{n+1})\setminus\partial\cu_m = \emptyset\,.
\end{equation*}
When~$x\in\cu_{m-1}$, take~$y=x$. Let~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$ be a nearest neighbor of~$y$ in~$3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$. Then~$y \in z+\cu_{n+1}$ and~$y+\cu_{n+1}\subseteq U_{m,n+2}(z)$. We define the elements of~$[n+2,m-5]\setminus\mathcal B$ nearest to~$n$ and to~$m$ by
\begin{equation*}
n' \coloneqq \min\bigl\{j\in\N_0 : j\in[n+2,m-5]\setminus\mathcal B\bigr\}
\quad\text{and}\quad
m' \coloneqq \max\bigl\{j\in\N_0 : j\in[n',m-5]\setminus\mathcal B\bigr\}\,,
\end{equation*}
respectively. Since~$|\mathcal B|\leq k+1+\lambda(m-n)$ by~\eqref{e.Bz.bound}, we have~$\max\{n'-n,m-m'\}\leq C+\lambda(m-n)$. As in Step~5, the boundary indicator in~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain} vanishes if~$x\in\cu_{m-1}$, so it is bounded by~$\indc_{\{x\notin\cu_{m-1}\}}$. We also fix~$y'\in\cu_m\cap(z+\cu_{m'+1})$ such that~$U_{m,m'}(z)\subseteq y'+\cu_{m'}\subseteq\cu_m$. By~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} and the last assertion of Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E}, for every~$j\in[n,m-5]\setminus \mathcal B$ we have
\begin{equation}
\label{e.ellipticity.good.scale.bounds}
C^{-1}(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}
\leq \lambda_{s_0,2}(z+\cu_{j+2};\a_L)
\leq \Lambda_{s_0,2}(z+\cu_{j+2};\a_L)
\leq C(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}\,.
\end{equation}
Thus, by Lemma~\ref{l.lambdas.stability} and~\eqref{e.big.Lambda.stability}, together with~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs}, we have, for~$q\in\{1,2\}$,
\begin{align*}
\lambda_{\nf 18,q}^{-1}(y+\cu_{n+1};\a_L)
\leq C\lambda_{s_0,2}^{-1}(z+\cu_{n+2};\a_L)
& \leq C3^{2s_0(n'-n)}\lambda_{s_0,2}^{-1}(z+\cu_{n'+2};\a_L)
\notag \\ &
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1}\,.
\end{align*}
The remaining ellipticity quantities satisfy
\begin{multline}
\Lambda_{\nf 14,q}(y+\cu_{n+1};\a_L)(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1}
+ \Lambda_{\nf 14,q}(y'+\cu_{m'};\a_L)(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1}
+ \lambda_{\nf 18,q}^{-1}(y'+\cu_{m'};\a_L)(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}
\\
\leq
C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\label{e.Lambdas.in.reg.bounds}
\end{multline}
Moreover,
\begin{equation*}
\left(\frac{|U_{m,m}(z)|}{|U_{m,m'}(z)|}\right)^{\nf12}
\leq C3^{d(m-m')/2}
\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
We now apply the coarse-grained Caccioppoli inequality of Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.caccioppoli} with~$s=\nf14$ and~$t=\nf18$, or Lemma~\ref{l.coarse.grained.Caccioppoli.RHS.ASD} near the boundary, together with the scaled embedding
\begin{equation*}
	[f]_{\underline H^{\nf14}(Q)}
	\leq C|Q|^{\nf{1}{4d}}[f]_{W^{\nf{1}{2},\infty}(Q)}
\end{equation*}
and obtain
\begin{align*}
\lefteqn{
\exp\bigl(-C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline{L}^2((x+\cu_n)\cap\cu_m)}
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
C(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{\nf12} 3^{-n}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,n+2}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n+2}(z))}
+ C (\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-\nf 12} 3^{\nf14 n}[\g]_{\underline{H}^{\nf14}(U_{m,n+2}(z))}
\notag \\& \qquad
+ C(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{\nf12}
\Bigl(|(\nabla h)_{U_{m,n+2}(z)}|
+ 3^{\nf14 n}[\nabla h]_{\underline{H}^{\nf14}(U_{m,n+2}(z))}\Bigr)
\indc_{\{x\notin\cu_{m-1}\}}
\notag \\ &
\leq
C(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{\nf12}
3^{-n}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,n+2}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,n+2}(z))}
+ C (\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-\nf 12} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\notag \\& \qquad
+ C(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{\nf12}
3^{\nf{1}{2} m}\| \nabla h\|_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}
\indc_{\{x\notin\cu_{m-1}\}}
\,.
\end{align*}
Furthermore,~\eqref{e.nabla.u.detach} and the fractional Poincar\'e inequality give
\begin{equation*}
3^{-m}\|u-(u)_{U_{m,m'}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,m'}(z))}
\leq C3^{-m}\|u-(u)_{y'+\cu_{m'}}\|_{\underline{L}^2(y'+\cu_{m'})}
\leq C3^{-\nf14 m}\|\nabla u\|_{\Besov{-\nf14}{2}{2}(y'+\cu_{m'})}\,.
\end{equation*}
We apply Lemma~\ref{l.coarse.graining.RHS} and~\eqref{e.Lambdas.in.reg.bounds} to obtain
\begin{align*}
\lefteqn{
3^{-\nf14 m}\|\nabla u\|_{\Besov{-\nf14}{2}{2}(y'+\cu_{m'})}
} \qquad &
\notag \\ &
\leq
C\lambda_{\nf 18,2}^{-\nf12}(y'+\cu_{m'};\a_L)
\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline{L}^2(y'+\cu_{m'})}
+ C\lambda_{\nf 18,2}^{-1}(y'+\cu_{m'};\a_L)
3^{\nf14 m}[\g]_{\underline{H}^{\nf14}(y'+\cu_{m'})}
\notag \\
&\leq C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\Bigl((\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-\nf12}\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline{L}^2(\cu_m)}
+ (\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}^{-1} 3^{\nf{1}{2} m}[\g]_{\underline{W}^{\nf12,\infty}(\cu_m)}\Bigr)\,.
\end{align*}
Combining the previous four displays with~\eqref{e.Campanato.full.domain} and~\eqref{e.exp.lambda.bound}, and using the choice of~$C_1$, proves~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step~7.} We prove the excess decay estimate~\eqref{e.excess.decay.estimate}. Fix~$m,n,l$ satisfying~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)\leq m-n\leq(1-\gamma)^{-1}$ and~$n\leq l\leq m-5$, and fix~$x\in\cu_m$. We may assume~$l>n+2$, since otherwise~\eqref{e.excess.decay.estimate} follows, after enlarging~$C$, by comparing the volumes of the sets involved. Choose~$z\in 3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$ such that
\begin{equation*}
(x+\cu_n)\cap\cu_m \subseteq U_{m,n+1}(z) \subseteq U_{m,l-1}(z) \subseteq (x+\cu_l)\cap\cu_m\,.
\end{equation*}
As in Step~5, the boundary indicator in~\eqref{e.delta.j.z.sum} vanishes when~$x\in\cu_{m-1}$, so it is bounded by~$\indc_{\{x\notin\cu_{m-1}\}}$. Using
\begin{equation*}
E(u,(x+\cu_n)\cap\cu_m) \leq CE(u,U_{m,n+1}(z))
\quad\text{and}\quad
E(u,U_{m,l-1}(z)) \leq CE(u,(x+\cu_l)\cap\cu_m)
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
\|u-(u)_{U_{m,l-1}(z)}\|_{\underline{L}^2(U_{m,l-1}(z))}
\leq C\|u-(u)_{(x+\cu_l)\cap\cu_m}\|_{\underline{L}^2((x+\cu_l)\cap\cu_m)}\,,
\end{equation*}
we obtain~\eqref{e.excess.decay.estimate} from~\eqref{e.excess.decay.applied}, \eqref{e.delta.j.z.sum}, and the assumption~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)\leq(1-\gamma)^{-1}$, using that
\begin{equation*}
\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr) \leq C\,,
\end{equation*}
which follows from the condition~$m-n\leq(1-\gamma)^{-1} = C_1\lambda^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Regularity uniformly in the cutoff}
\label{ss.cutoff.uniform.regularity}

We remove the restriction~$L\geq m$ from Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}. Recall from Definition~\ref{d.bLm} that~$\b_{L,n}=\ahom_L$ for~$n\geq L$.  For~$n\leq m$, we shall use the identity
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.centered.ratio}
\frac{\a_{m\wedge L}}{\a_{n\wedge L}}
=
\exp\left(
\sum_{i=n+1}^{m}
(\g_i-\tau^2)\indc_{\{i\leq L\}}
\right)
\end{equation}
and, by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering},
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.D.ordering}
1
\leq
\frac{\ahom_{n\wedge L}}{\ahom_{m\wedge L}}
\leq
\exp\bigl(2\tau^2((m\wedge L)-(n\wedge L))\bigr)
\leq
\exp\bigl(2\tau^2(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}

In this subsection, for the cutoff~$L$, we modify the good event~$\mathcal G_m(\ep,s)$ of Definition~\ref{d.good.events} by replacing only the quantity~$J$ in~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} by
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.response}
J\left(
z+\cu_n,e,e\,;
\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
The conditions~\eqref{e.good.gj} and~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} are left unchanged, and translated events are defined as before.  Likewise, we modify the quantity~$\mathbf G_{k,z}(s)$ in~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq}, for the cutoff~$L$, by replacing its term involving~$J$ with
\begin{equation*}
\min\!\left\{
\max_{|e|=1}J\left(
z'+\cu_l,e,e\,;
\ahom_{l\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{l\wedge L}
\right),1
\right\}^{\nf12}\,.
\end{equation*}
The terms involving the fields~$\g_j$ are unchanged. We retain the original notation. The modified event agrees with the original event when~$L\geq m$, and the modified quantity agrees with the original quantity when~$L\geq k$. Thus the arguments in the previous subsections still apply.

\begin{lemma}[Moments of~$J$ uniformly in the cutoff]
\label{l.cutoff.regularity.response}
There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, for every
\begin{equation*}
1\leq p\leq C^{-1}\gmcdelta^{-2}|\log\gmcdelta|^{-1}\,,
\end{equation*}
and every~$L,n\in\N_0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.response.moment}
\left\|
\max_{|e|=1}
J\left(
\cu_n,e,e\,;
\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L}
\right)
\right\|_{L^p(\P)}
\leq
Cp\log(2+p)\gmcdelta^2\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For~$n\leq L$, the estimate follows from Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} and the homogeneity identity
\begin{equation*}
J(U,e,e\,;\ahom_n^{-1}\a_n)
=
J(U,\ahom_n^{-\nf12}e,\ahom_n^{\nf12}e\,;\a_n)\,.
\end{equation*}
If~$n>L$, partition~$\cu_n$ into cubes~$P$ of side~$3^L$.  By~\eqref{e.subadditivity.J},
\begin{equation*}
J(\cu_n,e,e\,;\ahom_L^{-1}\a_L)
\leq
\avsum_PJ(P,e,e\,;\ahom_L^{-1}\a_L)\,.
\end{equation*}
By Minkowski's inequality and stationarity, the~$L^p$ norm of the right side is at most that of~$\max_{|e|=1}J(\cu_L,e,e\,;\ahom_L^{-1}\a_L)$, which is covered by the case~$n\leq L$.  The maximum over~$e$ is handled as in Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Good-scale estimates uniformly in the cutoff]
\label{p.cutoff.regularity.good.scales}
There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, for every~$L\in\N_0$, the following assertions hold, with~$\mathcal G_m$ and~$\mathbf G_{k,z}$ modified as above for the cutoff~$L$.

First, if~$s,\ep,\theta\in(0,1]$ and
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.density.parameters}
\theta
\geq
Cs^{-6}\ep^{-2}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|\,,
\end{equation}
then, for every~$m_0,M\in\N_0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.density}
\P\left[
\avsum_{m=m_0}^{m_0+M}
\indc_{\mathcal G_m(\ep,s)}
\leq
1-\theta
\right]
\leq
\exp\left(
-\frac{s^6\ep^2\theta}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}(M+1)
\right)\,.
\end{equation}

Second, if~$s\in(0,\nf12]$ and~$s\geq C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|$, then, for every~$0\leq n\leq m$ and~$z\in\Rd$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.error.average}
\avsum_{k=n}^{m}\mathbf G_{k,z}(s)
\leq
Cs^{-\nf72}\gmcdelta
+
\O_{\Gamma_2}\left(
Cs^{-\nf72}\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}
(m-n+1)^{-\nf12}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
Consequently, if
\begin{equation*}
\lambda\in(0,1]
\quad\text{and}\quad
\lambda
\geq
Cs^{-\nf72}\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}\,,
\end{equation*}
there exists~$M(\lambda,s,m)\in\{-1,0,\ldots,m\}$ such that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping.tail}
0\leq m-M(\lambda,s,m)
\leq
1+\O_{\Gamma_1}\left(
Cs^{-7}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|\lambda^{-2}
\right)
\end{equation}
and, for every~$n\in\N_0$ with~$n\leq M(\lambda,s,m)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping}
\max_{z\in3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}\mathbf G_{j,z}(s)
\leq
\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}
If, in addition,
\begin{equation*}
\ep\in(0,1]
\quad\text{and}\quad
\lambda
\geq
Cs^{-8}\ep^{-2}\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|\,,
\end{equation*}
there is~$N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)\in\{-1,0,\ldots,m\}$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping.tail}
0\leq m-N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)
\leq
1+\O_{\Gamma_1}\left(
Cs^{-8}\ep^{-2}\lambda^{-1}
\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|
\right)
\end{equation}
and, for every~$n\in\N_0$ with~$n\leq N(\lambda,\ep,s,m)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping}
\max_{z\in3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m}
\sum_{j=n}^{m}
\left(1-\indc_{\mathcal G_{j,z}(\ep,s)}\right)
<
1+\lambda(m-n)\,.
\end{equation}

Finally, for~$s\in[64\gmcdelta^2,\nf12]$,~$\ep\in[s^{-1}\gmcdelta^2,1]$, every~$m\in\N_0$, and every~$z\in\Rd$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E}
\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}\left(
z+\cu_m\,;\a_L,
(\b_{L,m})_{z+\cu_m}
\right)
\indc_{\mathcal G_{m,z}(\ep,s)}
\leq
C\min\left\{\ep,
s^{-1}\gmcdelta^2+\ep^8+\mathbf G_{m,z}(s)
\right\}\,.
\end{equation}
In particular,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E.epsilon}
\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}\left(
z+\cu_m\,;\a_L,
(\b_{L,m})_{z+\cu_m}
\right)
\indc_{\mathcal G_{m,z}(\ep,s)}
\leq
C\ep\,.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The conditions~\eqref{e.good.gj} and~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} in~$\mathcal G_m$ are those of Definition~\ref{d.good.events}. For the modified condition~\eqref{e.good.sum.J}, the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.discounted.J.local} applies, with the moments of~$J$ bounded by~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.response.moment}. Indeed, the coefficient~$\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L}$ has range of dependence at most~$C3^{n\wedge L}\leq C3^n$, so~$J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,;\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L})$ depends only on the fields in the~$C3^n$-neighborhood of~$z+\cu_n$.  Since the quantities~$J$ enter only with~$n\leq j-2$, splitting the indices~$j$ into finitely many residue classes, as in that proof, gives the finite-range hypothesis of Proposition~\ref{p.concentration.for.scales}.  The moment exponent in that proof is at least~$Cd/s$, and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.density.parameters} ensures both the smallness condition required there and~$\theta\geq p^{-1}$.  This proves~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.density}.

For~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.average}, the terms involving the fields~$\g_j$ are the same as those in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors}.  For the term involving~$J$, we estimate the maximum over~$z$ as in Step~2 of that proof, using the moment of order~$8d/s$ of~$\min\{\max_{|e|=1}J(z+\cu_n,e,e\,;\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L}),1\}^{\nf12}$, that is, the moment of order~$4d/s$ of the same quantity without the square root.  If the dimensional constant in the lower bound on~$s$ is large enough, this order lies in the range allowed by~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.response.moment}. With the same residue classes as above, the sum of these terms is then bounded exactly as in Step~3 of that proof, and the rearrangement~\eqref{e.split.J.for.minimal.scale} and the bound for the annuli below scale~$n$ are unchanged.  This proves~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.average}.  The union bound over translated centers used to prove~\eqref{e.M.lambda.m.tail} gives~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping.tail} and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping}.  Applying the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.min.scale.good.scale} to~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.density} gives~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping.tail} and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping}.

It remains to prove~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E}.  By~\eqref{e.good.sum.J}, on~$\mathcal G_{m,z}(\ep,s)$ the elementary inequality of Step~3 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}, with~$s$ in place of~$s_0$, applies to the square root of every quantity~$J$ in the annular decomposition.  For~$m\leq L$, that inequality and~\eqref{e.mathcalE.bound.for.good} give~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E}.  Suppose that~$m>L$.  We use the annular decomposition~\eqref{e.mathcalE.annular.decomp.pre} again and, at each nonnegative scale~$n$, compare~$\ahom_L^{-1}\a_L$ with~$\ahom_{n\wedge L}^{-1}\a_{n\wedge L}$.  The two coefficients agree if~$n\geq L$. If~$n<L$, we apply Lemma~\ref{l.J.sensitivity}, with the ratios of the two coefficients given by~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.centered.ratio}; the conditions~\eqref{e.good.gj} and~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} in~$\mathcal G_m$ bound these ratios exactly as in~\eqref{e.J.sensitivity.applied.reg}--\eqref{e.J.localization.reg}, and the ratios of effective diffusivities are bounded by~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.D.ordering}.  The contribution of the negative scales is bounded by~\eqref{e.good.scale.subunit.tail}, with the same proof. Combining these estimates with the preceding inequality for these quantities, as in Step~3 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E}, gives~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E}.  The definition of~$\mathcal G_m(\ep,s)$ and~\eqref{e.what.e.good.gj.gives} give~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E.epsilon}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regularity at a good scale, uniformly in the cutoff]
\label{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}
Let~$L\in\N_0$.  Then the following hold.
\begin{itemize}
\item \underline{\emph{Harmonic approximation}}: in the setting of Lemma~\ref{l.harmonic.approximation.good.scales.GMC}, with its hypothesis~$L\geq m$ removed, its conclusion holds with the good event~$\mathcal G_{n+2,z}$ modified as above for the cutoff~$L$.

\item \underline{\emph{Excess decay}}: in the setting of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC}, with its hypothesis~$L\geq m$ removed, its conclusion holds with the same modification.
\end{itemize}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
In the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.harmonic.approximation.good.scales.GMC}, we take for~$\a_0$ the positive scalar~$(\b_{L,n+2})_{z+\cu_{n+2}}$ and use Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.regularity.good.scales} in place of Proposition~\ref{p.good.scale.mathcal.E}.  The general coarse-graining estimate, its stability estimates, the interior and boundary Caccioppoli inequalities, and the estimates for the right side~$\nabla\cdot\g$ are deterministic statements about~$\a_L$ and~$\a_0$.  They therefore give the same estimate, with the same powers of~$\a_0$.  The bounds on~$\a_0^{-1}\Lambda_{s,q}$ and~$\a_0\lambda_{s,q}^{-1}$ used there follow from~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E.epsilon} and~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es}.  Finally, multiplying the Laplacian by the positive scalar~$\a_0$ does not change its harmonic functions.  The interior and reflected boundary estimates in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC} are consequently unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cutoff-uniform large-scale regularity]
\label{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}
There exists~$C(d)<\infty$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq C^{-1}$, then for every
\begin{equation*}
\gamma
\in
\left[
\nf12,\,
1-C\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}
\right]
\end{equation*}
and every~$L,m\in\N_0$, there is an~$\N$-valued random variable~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ satisfying, for every~$k\in\N$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale}
\P\left[
\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)>k
\right]
\leq
C\exp\left(
-\frac{(1-\gamma)^2(k-C)_+}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
For every~$\g\in C^{0,\nf12}(\cu_m;\Rd)$,~$h\in C^{1,\nf12}(\cu_m)$, and~$u\in H^1(\cu_m)$ satisfying~\eqref{e.eq.for.u.holder.estimate}, all three conclusions~\eqref{e.Holder.estimate.boxes.local},~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}, and~\eqref{e.excess.decay.estimate} hold with~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ as the minimal scale. If~$L\geq m$, one may take
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.Holder.replacements}
\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)=\mathcal L_m(\gamma,m)\,.
\end{equation}
The same statement holds on every translate~$y+\cu_m$, with the tail in~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale} uniform in~$y$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
If~$L\geq m$, then this is Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}.  We may therefore suppose~$L<m$.  Choose~$s_0,\lambda,\ep$, and the step size~$k$ exactly as in Steps~1--2 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}.  Define
\begin{equation}
\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)
\coloneqq
\max\left\{
m-M(\lambda,s_0,m),\,
m-N(\lambda,\ep,s_0,m)
\right\}
+k+5\,.
\end{equation}
Here~$M$ and~$N$ are the random variables of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.regularity.good.scales} for the cutoff~$L$, not those of Lemmas~\ref{l.sum.the.errors} and~\ref{l.min.scale.good.scale}. Equations~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping.tail} and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping.tail}, with the parameter choices in that proof, give~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale}. Equations~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping} and~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.stopping} give the two conclusions in~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.applied} for the modified~$\mathbf G$ and~$\mathcal G$.

For~$n\leq j\leq m-5$ and~$z\in3^n\Zd\cap\cu_m$, the calculation leading to~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs}, with all sums over the fields and all deterministic sums stopped at~$L$, gives
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.Holder.b.ratio}
C^{-1}\exp\bigl(-C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)
\leq
\frac{
(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}
}{
(\b_{L,m})_{\cu_m}
}
\leq
C\exp\bigl(C\lambda(m-n)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
Indeed, if~$j+2\geq L$, the numerator and the denominator both equal~$\ahom_L$.  If~$j+2<L<m$, only the fields~$\g_{j+3},\ldots,\g_L$ occur; they are bounded by~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.error.stopping}, while~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.D.ordering} controls the ratio of effective diffusivities.

We use Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates} in place of Lemma~\ref{l.excess.decay.good.scales.GMC}.  With~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.b.ratio} in place of~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs}, the proofs of~\eqref{e.forcing.j.bound},~\eqref{e.delta.j.z.sum}, and~\eqref{e.ellipticity.good.scale.bounds}--\eqref{e.Lambdas.in.reg.bounds}, and of the coarse-grained Caccioppoli and Besov estimates in Step~6 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}, apply without change.  Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.regularity.good.scales} gives the analogue of~\eqref{e.mathcalE.bound.applied}.  Thus Steps~3--7 of that proof apply verbatim with~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ as the minimal scale; this proves the three asserted estimates.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Stability under multiplication by a nearly constant function]
\label{p.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier}
There are~$\ep_*(d)>0$,~$c(d)>0$, and~$C(d)<\infty$ such that the following holds if~$\gmcdelta\leq c$. Let~$L\in\N_0$,~$m\in\Z$,~$z\in\Rd$, and~$j\in\N$. Put~$B=z+\cu_m$, and let~$B'$ be a cube of side~$3^{m-j}$ contained in the concentric cube~$B/2$ of half the side of~$B$. Outside an event of probability at most
\begin{equation}
 C\exp\left(-\frac{c}{\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|^2}\right)\,,
 \label{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier.probability}
\end{equation}
the following estimates hold for every positive~$\vartheta\in C(B)$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
 \inf_{b>0}\|b^{-1}\vartheta-1\|_{L^\infty(B)}\leq\ep_*
 \label{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier.assumption}
\end{equation}
and every weakly~$\a_L\vartheta$-harmonic function~$h\in H^1(B)$:
\begin{equation}
 \osc_{B'}h\leq C3^{-cj}\osc_Bh
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 \sup_{B'}|h-(h)_B|
 \leq C\|h-(h)_B\|_{\underline L^2(B)}\,.
 \label{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Multiplying~$\vartheta$ by a positive constant does not change the set of~$\a_L\vartheta$-harmonic functions. We may therefore assume that~$\|\vartheta-1\|_\infty\leq\ep_*$. Put~$s_0=1/32$ and suppose first that~$m\geq0$. We run the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} for each of~$4^d$ translates of the triadic grids~$3^n\Zd$, on the correspondingly translated cubes (that proposition holds on every translate of~$\cu_m$), with exponent~$3/4$, and show that the conclusion survives with exponent~$1/2$. On each cube~$Q$ on which that proof estimates the coarse-graining error, Lemma~\ref{l.J.sensitivity}, applied with perturbation parameter~$\ep_*$ and with the scalar~$\a_0$ of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}, gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal E_{s_0,\infty,2}(Q;\a_L\vartheta,\a_0)
 \leq
 (1+C\ep_*)\mathcal E_{s_0,\infty,2}(Q;\a_L,\a_0)
 +C\ep_*^{\nf{1}{2}}\,,
 \label{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier.error}
\end{equation}
Indeed, it suffices to apply the sensitivity estimate on every subcube in the definition of~$\mathcal E$ and to use Minkowski's inequality in the geometrically weighted sum. Consequently, on each good scale, the factor~$\theta^k$ and the coefficient~$\ep_j$ of~$|\nabla\ell(u,U_j)|$ in~\eqref{e.Ej.decay.assumption} change by at most~$C\ep_*^{\nf{1}{2}}$, while the set of good scales does not depend on~$\vartheta$. Choose~$\ep_*$ so that~$C\ep_*^{\nf{1}{2}}<\nf{1}{8}\log3$. Then the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}, run with exponent~$3/4$, still yields exponent at least~$1/2$. Thus the translated large-scale H\"older estimate of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} holds with exponent~$1/2$, uniformly for every~$\vartheta$ satisfying~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier.assumption}. This argument is deterministic whenever the good-scale conditions of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} hold, so no union bound over~$\vartheta$ is needed.

It remains to pass from averages to point values. We use these~$4^d$ grids. Campanato's argument gives the first estimate down to scale one. Since~$B'$ lies in the concentric cube~$B/2$, we can choose unit cubes~$Q$ with~$2Q\subseteq B$ such that their concentric subcubes~$Q/4$, of one quarter the side length, cover~$B'$. When~$m$ is bounded, we use the corresponding rescaled interior cubes instead. Moser iteration gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|h-(h)_Q\|_{L^\infty(Q/4)}
 \leq
 C\left(\frac{\sup_Q\a_L\vartheta}
 {\inf_Q\a_L\vartheta}\right)^C
 \|h-(h)_Q\|_{\underline L^2(Q)}\,.
\end{equation*}
By Assumption~\ref{a.g2} and a union bound over the~$O(3^{d(m-j)_+})$ cubes, outside an event whose probability is bounded by~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.bounded.multiplier.probability}, the logarithm of the largest of these coefficient ratios is at most~$C\gmcdelta\sqrt{m+1}+C\gmcdelta^2(m+1)+C$. The resulting factor is absorbed by the Campanato decay after reducing~$c(d)$.

It remains to treat cubes of side less than one. Let~$P$ be such a cube, with side~$r$. If~$r\|\nabla\log\a_L\|_{L^\infty(P)}\leq\ep_*$, then, after dividing by the value of~$\a_L$ at the center of~$P$, the ellipticity ratio of~$\a_L\vartheta$ on~$P$ is bounded by a dimensional constant.
