The De Giorgi oscillation estimate therefore reduces the oscillation by a fixed factor from~$P$ to the concentric triadic cube of one third its side. Iterating, we obtain decay of the oscillation like a fixed power of the side length below the scale~$\ep_*/(1+\|\nabla\log\a_L\|_\infty)$. The estimates between this scale and scale one introduce only a fixed power of~$1+\|\nabla\log\a_L\|_\infty$, and the bound on~$\|\nabla\log\a_L\|_{L^\infty(B)}$ that holds outside the exceptional event, by Assumption~\ref{a.g2} and a union bound, absorbs this factor into the decay from~$B$. This proves both estimates when~$B'$ has side below one, and also proves the case~$m<0$ directly.

Fix~$j_0$ larger than the additive constant in~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale}. If~$m>j_0$, we restrict to the event that~$\mathcal L_L(3/4,m)\leq j_0$ for each of these~$4^d$ grids; if~$m\leq j_0$, we use the preceding local estimate. For~$j\leq j_0$, the oscillation bound follows from the maximum principle after increasing~$C$, while for~$j>j_0$, this event gives the Campanato estimate down to the scale of~$B'$. By~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale}, the probability of its complement is at most~$Ce^{-c/(\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|^2)}$, uniformly in~$L,m,z$, and~$j$.

\end{proof}

\subsection{Dirichlet homogenization}

The estimates above compare a solution with an affine function.  We now compare it instead with the solution of the homogenized Dirichlet problem in the unit cube, after rescaling the cutoff coefficient~$\a_L$ from a cube at least as large as the cutoff scale.

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative homogenization of the Dirichlet problem]
\label{t.cutoff.Dirichlet.homogenization}
For~$L,M\in\N_0$ with~$L\leq M$, recall the rescaled coefficient of the introduction,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.rescaled.cutoff.coefficient.Dirichlet}
A_{L,M}(x)
\coloneqq
\ahom_L^{-1}\a_L(3^Mx),
\qquad x\in \cu_0\,.
\end{equation}
For every~$\vartheta\in(0,1)$ and~$q\in[1,\infty)$, there are~$\gmcdelta_0(\vartheta,q,d)>0$ and~$C(\vartheta,q,d)<\infty$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random variables~$\mathcal Z_{L,M}\geq1$, $0\leq L\leq M$, satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Dirichlet.random.factor.moment}
\sup_{M\in\N_0}\sup_{0\leq L\leq M}
\|\mathcal Z_{L,M}\|_{L^q(\P)}
\leq C\,.
\end{equation}
For~$f\in L^2(\cu_0)$ and~$h\in H^2(\cu_0)$, recall that~$u_{L,M}$ and~$u_{\mathrm{hom}}$ are the unique weak solutions in~$H^1(\cu_0)$ of
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.Dirichlet.problems}
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
-\nabla\cdot(A_{L,M}\nabla u_{L,M})&=f
&&\text{in }\cu_0,\\
u_{L,M}&=h
&&\text{on }\partial \cu_0,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\qquad
\left\{
\begin{aligned}
-\Delta u_{\mathrm{hom}}&=f
&&\text{in }\cu_0,\\
u_{\mathrm{hom}}&=h
&&\text{on }\partial \cu_0,
\end{aligned}
\right.
\,.
\end{equation}
Then, almost surely, for every such~$f$ and~$h$, the homogenization error~$\mathcal D_{L,M}$ of the introduction satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.cutoff.Dirichlet.homogenization}
\mathcal D_{L,M}
\leq
\mathcal Z_{L,M}\gmcdelta^\vartheta
\left(
\|f\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}+\|h\|_{H^2(\cu_0)}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
If~$f=0$, then the factor~$\gmcdelta^\vartheta$ on the right side may be replaced by~$C\gmcdelta$, with the same random variable~$\mathcal Z_{L,M}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix~$s_2\in(0,1)$, and then choose~$0<s_1<s<\min\{s_2,1/2\}$ so that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Dirichlet.parameter.balance}
\frac{s_2}{s_1+s_2}>\vartheta\,.
\end{equation}
All stochastic estimates below are in~$L^r(\P)$, for a fixed exponent~$r$ that exceeds~$q$ by a dimensional factor.  Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} and Lemma~\ref{l.multiscale.response.large.cubes} give, uniformly in~$0\leq L\leq M$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Dirichlet.full.response.moments}
\left\|
\mathcal E_{s_1,\infty,1}
(\cu_M;\a_L,\ahom_L)
\right\|_{L^r(\P)}
+
\left\|
\mathcal E_{\nf{s_1}{2},\infty,2}
(\cu_M;\a_L,\ahom_L)
\right\|_{L^r(\P)}
\leq C\gmcdelta\,.
\end{equation}
The admissible moment range in those estimates contains every fixed~$r<\infty$ after decreasing~$\gmcdelta_0(\vartheta,q,d)$.

Write~$f$ as the divergence of a field in~$H^1(\cu_0;\Rd)$ of norm at most~$C\|f\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}$, for instance minus the gradient of the solution of the Dirichlet problem for the Laplacian in~$\cu_0$ with source~$f$. Rescale to~$\cu_M$ by setting~$U(y)\coloneqq u_{L,M}(3^{-M}y)$ and~$V(y)\coloneqq u_{\mathrm{hom}}(3^{-M}y)$, and let~$G(y)$ be~$\ahom_L3^{-M}$ times the field above evaluated at~$3^{-M}y$. Then
\begin{equation*}
-\nabla\cdot(\a_L\nabla U)=\nabla\cdot G,
\qquad
-\ahom_L\Delta V=\nabla\cdot G,
\qquad
U-V\in H_0^1(\cu_M)\,.
\end{equation*}

Let~$k\in\N$ and apply Proposition~\ref{p.general.coarse.graining}, with~$p=2$ and~$n=M-k$. Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E} gives
\begin{align*}
\mathcal E_{s_1,\infty,1}
(\cu_M,M-k;\a_L,\ahom_L)
&\leq
3^{s_1k}
\mathcal E_{s_1,\infty,1}
(\cu_M;\a_L,\ahom_L),\\
\mathcal E_{\nf{s_1}{2},\infty,2}
(\cu_M,M-k;\a_L,\ahom_L)^2
&\leq
3^{s_1k}
\mathcal E_{\nf{s_1}{2},\infty,2}
(\cu_M;\a_L,\ahom_L)^2\,.
\end{align*}
The geometric weights in the sum over~$k$ in~\eqref{e.general.coarse.graining.estimate.ASD}, together with~\eqref{e.average.normalized.norm.partition}, bound that sum by~$C\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla U\|_{\underline L^2(\cu_M)}$. Moreover,~\eqref{e.cg.RHS} and~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} give a random variable~$Y_{L,M}\geq1$, with every fixed moment bounded uniformly in~$L\leq M$, such that
\begin{equation}
\label{e.Dirichlet.energy.factor}
\|A_{L,M}^{\nf12}\nabla u_{L,M}\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}
\leq
Y_{L,M}
\left(
\|f\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}+\|h\|_{H^2(\cu_0)}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
Rescaling~\eqref{e.general.coarse.graining.estimate.ASD} back to~$\cu_0$ therefore yields
\begin{align}
\label{e.Dirichlet.prebalance}
\lefteqn{
\|\nabla u_{L,M}-\nabla u_{\mathrm{hom}}\|_{H^{-s}(\cu_0)}
+
\|A_{L,M}\nabla u_{L,M}
-\nabla u_{\mathrm{hom}}\|_{H^{-s}(\cu_0)}
} \qquad & \notag\\
&\leq
C\left(
3^{s_1k}\mathcal E_1Y_{L,M}
+
3^{-s_2k}
\bigl(1+3^{s_1k}\mathcal E_2^2\bigr)
\right)
\left(
\|f\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}+\|h\|_{H^2(\cu_0)}
\right)\,,
\end{align}
where~$\mathcal E_1$ and~$\mathcal E_2$ denote the two random variables in~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.full.response.moments}.

Take
\begin{equation*}
k
\coloneqq
\left\lceil
\frac{\log_3(\gmcdelta^{-1})}{s_1+s_2}
\right\rceil\,.
\end{equation*}
With~$\mathcal E_1,\mathcal E_2$, and~$Y_{L,M}$ as above, define
\begin{align*}
\mathcal Z_{L,M}
\coloneqq 1
&+C\gmcdelta^{-\vartheta}
\left(
3^{s_1k}\mathcal E_1Y_{L,M}
+3^{-s_2k}\bigl(1+3^{s_1k}\mathcal E_2^2\bigr)
\right)\\
&+C\gmcdelta^{-1}\mathcal E_1Y_{L,M}\,.
\end{align*}
Equations~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.parameter.balance},~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.full.response.moments}, and~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.energy.factor}, followed by H\"older's inequality, give~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.random.factor.moment}.  Substituting this choice of~$k$ and the definition of~$\mathcal Z_{L,M}$ into~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.prebalance} proves~\eqref{e.cutoff.Dirichlet.homogenization} for the two~$H^{-1}$ terms of~$\mathcal D_{L,M}$.  If~$f=0$, we apply~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.prebalance} again, with~$k=1$.  The~$3^{-s_2k}$ term is then absent, and the last term in the definition of~$\mathcal Z_{L,M}$ gives the factor~$C\gmcdelta$.

Finally, the difference~$u_{L,M}-u_{\mathrm{hom}}$ belongs to~$H_0^1(\cu_0)$. For every~$w\in H_0^1(\cu_0)$, duality and the spectral theorem for the Dirichlet Laplacian on~$\cu_0$ give~$\|w\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}\leq C\|\nabla w\|_{H^{-s}(\cu_0)}$: test with the image of~$w$ under the inverse Dirichlet Laplacian, whose gradient is bounded in~$H^s(\cu_0)$ by~$C\|w\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}$. Applying this to the difference, and using that~$s<1$ and the embedding~$H^{-s}(\cu_0)\hookrightarrow H^{-1}(\cu_0)$, completes the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative homogenization at a fixed cutoff]
\label{t.fixed.cutoff.Dirichlet.algebraic}
There exists~$\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(d)>0$ with the following property. Fix~$L\in\N_0$. For~$M\in\N_0$ with~$M\geq L$, let~$A_{L,M}$ be as in~\eqref{e.rescaled.cutoff.coefficient.Dirichlet}. For every~$q\in[1,\infty)$, there is a random variable~$\mathcal Y_{L,q}\geq1$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{e.fixed.cutoff.Dirichlet.random.factor}
\|\mathcal Y_{L,q}\|_{L^q(\P)}
\leq C(L,q,d,\gmcdelta)
\end{equation}
such that, almost surely, for every~$M\geq L$,~$f\in L^2(\cu_0)$ and~$h\in H^2(\cu_0)$, the homogenization error~$\mathcal D_{L,M}$ of the unique weak solutions~$u_{L,M}$ and~$u_{\mathrm{hom}}$ of~\eqref{e.cutoff.Dirichlet.problems} satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.fixed.cutoff.Dirichlet.algebraic}
\mathcal D_{L,M}
\leq
\mathcal Y_{L,q}\,
3^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M-L)}
\left(
\|f\|_{L^2(\cu_0)}+\|h\|_{H^2(\cu_0)}
\right)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Consider the coefficient~$A_{L,L}$. For fixed~$L$, this is a stationary positive scalar coefficient with range of dependence at most~$\sqrt d$, and its homogenized matrix is the identity.  It satisfies the logarithmic coefficient bound
\begin{equation}
\label{e.fixed.cutoff.logarithmic.input}
\|\log A_{L,L}\|_{L^\infty(\cu_0)}
\leq\O_{\Gamma_2}\bigl(C\gmcdelta(L+1)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
Indeed, this follows from~\eqref{e.infrared.approx.cutoffs.field}, Assumption~\ref{a.g2}, and the bound~$|\log \ahom_L|\leq C\gmcdelta^2(L+1)$ from Theorem~\ref{t.renormalized.diffusivities}.

We apply the results of~\citet[Theorem~B, Theorem~4.1, and Proposition~D.3]{AK.HC} to~$A_{L,L}$.  Stationarity and finite range give the mixing assumption of that paper with~$\beta=0$ and~$\nu=d/2$, and with Gaussian concentration.  Proposition~D.3 of that paper, applied to~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.logarithmic.input} with exponent~$\nf14$, gives the coarse-grained ellipticity assumption of that paper above a random scale. The probability that this scale exceeds~$\sqrt d\,t$ is at most~$\exp(C_L-c_L\log^2t)$, with positive constants~$C_L$ and~$c_L$ that may depend on~$L$; this tail bound implies that all polynomial moments of this scale are finite.

\citet[Corollary~4.3]{AK.HC}, with exponent~$5/16$, gives an estimate uniform over all subcubes. Together with Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E}, it yields a number~$\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(d)>0$ and, for every fixed~$q<\infty$, a random variable~$\mathcal W_{L,q}$ with finite~$q$th moment such that, for all~$N\in\N_0$,~$r\in\{1,2\}$, and~$s\in\{\nf{3}{16},\nf38\}$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.fixed.cutoff.response.algebraic}
\mathcal E_{s,\infty,r}(\cu_N;A_{L,L},1)
\leq
\mathcal W_{L,q}\,3^{-2\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}N}\,.
\end{equation}
Indeed, by the estimate of~\citet[Corollary~4.3]{AK.HC} for subcubes, the quantity~$\max_{|e|=1}J(\,\cdot\,,e,e\,;A_{L,L})$ on a subcube at scale~$k\leq N$ is bounded by a random multiple of~$3^{-4\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}N}3^{\nf{5}{16}(N-k)}$. After taking square roots, the weighted sum over scales in Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E} converges, since~$s>\nf5{32}$ for both values of~$s$ above. Below that random scale, we use~\eqref{e.ord.bounds.truncated} and the extrema of~$A_{L,L}^{\pm1}$ instead. By a union bound over unit cubes and~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.logarithmic.input}, the moments of these extrema on~$\cu_N$ grow at most like~$C_{L,q}\exp(C_{L,q}\sqrt{N+1})$. H\"older's inequality, the Gaussian-type tail bound for that scale, and the analogous tail for the scale in~\citet[Corollary~4.3]{AK.HC} therefore allow us to absorb these scales into~$\mathcal W_{L,q}$ while keeping its moments finite.

We use the estimate leading to~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.prebalance}, after rescaling~$\cu_M$ first by~$3^L$ and then by~$3^{M-L}$.  Apply Proposition~\ref{p.general.coarse.graining} with
\begin{equation*}
p=2,\qquad s_1=\frac38,\qquad s=\frac7{16},\qquad s_2=\frac34\,,
\end{equation*}
so that $sp^{\prime}=7/8<1$ and both $s_1$ and $s_1/2$ are covered by~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.response.algebraic}, and choose
\begin{equation*}
	k
	\coloneqq
	\max\left\{1,\left\lfloor
	\frac{2\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M-L)}{s_1+s_2}
	\right\rfloor\right\}\,.
\end{equation*}
In~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.prebalance}, we use~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.response.algebraic}, applied at the scale~$M-L$, in place of~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.full.response.moments}, and we construct~$Y_{L,M}$ in~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.energy.factor} from~\eqref{e.cg.RHS},~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es}, and now~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.response.algebraic}. These give~$\sup_{M\geq L}\|Y_{L,M}\|_{L^r}<\infty$ for every fixed~$r$, with constants allowed to depend on~$L,r,d,\gmcdelta$. Substituting~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.response.algebraic} and the choice of~$k$ in~\eqref{e.Dirichlet.prebalance}, and using the bound~$Y_{L,M}\leq3^{\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M-L)/25}\sup_{M'\geq L}3^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M'-L)/25}Y_{L,M'}$, we obtain the negative-Sobolev part of~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.Dirichlet.algebraic} with the random factor
\begin{equation*}
	\mathcal Y_{L,q}
	\coloneqq
	C(1+\mathcal W_{L,4q})^2\Bigl(1+\sup_{M\geq L}3^{-\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M-L)/25}\,Y_{L,M}\Bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since~$Y_{L,M}$ is constructed with a moment larger than~$4q$, the supremum has a finite~$4q$th moment, because its~$4q$th power is at most the sum over~$M\geq L$ of~$3^{-4q\alpha_{\mathrm{hom}}(M-L)/25}Y_{L,M}^{4q}$. H\"older's inequality shows that~$\mathcal Y_{L,q}$ has a finite~$q$th moment, which gives~\eqref{e.fixed.cutoff.Dirichlet.random.factor}. The duality argument with the Dirichlet Laplacian at the end of the proof of Theorem~\ref{t.cutoff.Dirichlet.homogenization} deduces the~$L^2$ estimate from the estimate in a negative Sobolev norm.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Limiting coefficient and regularity}

The limiting coefficient field is the almost-sure local limit
\begin{equation}
	\widetilde\a_L(x)
	\coloneqq
	\frac{\a_L(x)}{\a_L(0)},
	\qquad
	\a(x)
	\coloneqq
	\lim_{L\to\infty}\widetilde\a_L(x)
	=
	\exp\Bigl(\sum_{k=0}^\infty(\g_k(x)-\g_k(0))\Bigr)\,.
	\label{e.fixed.clock.coefficient}
\end{equation}
Dividing by~$\a_L(0)$ is necessary, since the strong law gives~$(L+1)^{-1}\log\a_L(0)\to-\tau^2$.  This division does not affect the generator of~$X^{(L)}$, and the reversible measures~$(\a_L/\a_L(0))\,dx$ converge locally to~$\a\,dx$. By Assumption~\ref{a.g2} and scaling, the coefficient ratios converge locally in~$C^{1,1}$. The following lemma gives bounds uniform in~$L$.

\begin{lemma}[The coefficients divided by their value at the origin]
	\label{l.finite.cutoff.coefficient.convergence}
	There is~$\gmcdelta_0(d)>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0(d)$, then there are~$\kappa\in(0,1)$ and an almost surely finite random variable~$C_\omega$ such that, almost surely, for every~$L\in\N_0$ and~$x\in\Rd$,
	\begin{equation}
		\sup_{\b\in\{\a\}\cup\{\widetilde\a_L\}_{L\in\N_0}}
		\left(\b(x)+\b(x)^{-1}+|\nabla\b(x)|\right)
		\leq C_\omega(1+|x|)^\kappa\,.
		\label{e.finite.cutoff.coefficient.polynomial.growth}
	\end{equation}
	Moreover,
	\begin{equation}
		\widetilde\a_L\longrightarrow\a
		\quad\text{locally in }C^{1,1},
		\qquad
		\nabla\log\widetilde\a_L
		\longrightarrow\nabla\log\a
		\quad\text{locally in }C^{0,1}\,,
		\label{e.finite.cutoff.coefficient.C11.convergence}
	\end{equation}
	and
	\begin{equation}
		\sup_{L\in\N_0}
		\left(
		|\nabla\log\widetilde\a_L(x)|
		+|\nabla^2\log\widetilde\a_L(x)|
		\right)
		\leq
		C_\omega\left(1+\sqrt{\log(2+|x|)}\right)\,.
		\label{e.finite.cutoff.log.derivative.growth}
	\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
	Fix a sufficiently large dimensional constant~$C$, and cover each ball~$B_{C2^n}$ by cubes of side~$3^k$. Stationarity,~\eqref{e.same.law.shift},~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, and the Borel--Cantelli lemma give a finite random variable~$C_\omega$ such that, for all~$n,k\in\N_0$ and~$r\in\{1,2\}$,
	\begin{equation}
		\|\nabla^r\g_k\|_{L^\infty(B_{C2^n})}
		\leq
		C_\omega3^{-rk}\sqrt{n+k+1}\,.
		\label{e.finite.cutoff.derivative.Borel.Cantelli}
	\end{equation}
	Summing over~$k$ proves the local convergence~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.coefficient.C11.convergence} and the bound~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.log.derivative.growth}.

	It remains to obtain the bound on the coefficient itself uniformly in the cutoff~$L$. For each~$z\in\mathbb Z^d\cap B_{C2^n}$, the variables
	\begin{equation*}
		\g_k(z)-\g_k(0)\qquad(0\leq k,\ 3^k\leq C2^n)
	\end{equation*}
	are independent, centered, and sub-Gaussian with scale~$C\gmcdelta$.  The maximal sub-Gaussian inequality, followed by a union bound over these~$O(2^{dn})$ lattice points and Borel--Cantelli, yields
	\begin{equation}
		\max_{\substack{z\in\mathbb Z^d\cap B_{C2^n}\\
		0\leq j,\ 3^j\leq C2^n}}
		\left|
		\sum_{k=0}^j(\g_k(z)-\g_k(0))
		\right|
		\leq
		C\gmcdelta n+C_\omega\,.
		\label{e.finite.cutoff.value.maximal.bound}
	\end{equation}
	For~$x\in B_{2^n}$, we apply~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.value.maximal.bound} at a nearest lattice point and control the difference using~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.derivative.Borel.Cantelli}; for the scales~$k$ with~$3^k\leq C2^n$, this difference costs at most~$C_\omega\sqrt{n+1}$. For the other scales, the mean-value theorem along the segment from~$0$ to~$x$ gives
	\begin{equation*}
		\sum_{3^k>C2^n}|\g_k(x)-\g_k(0)|
		\leq
		C_\omega2^n
		\sum_{3^k>C2^n}3^{-k}\sqrt{n+k+1}
		\leq
		C_\omega\sqrt{n+1}\,.
	\end{equation*}
	Both estimates hold for every partial sum of the series. Consequently,
	\begin{equation*}
		\sup_{L\in\N_0}\sup_{x\in B_{2^n}}
		|\log\widetilde\a_L(x)|
		\leq
		C\gmcdelta n+C_\omega\sqrt{n+1}+C_\omega\,.
	\end{equation*}
	Choosing~$\gmcdelta_0(d)$ small, bounding the square-root term by an arbitrarily small multiple of~$n$ plus a random constant, and exponentiating, we obtain~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.coefficient.polynomial.growth} for~$\widetilde\a_L$ and its reciprocal.  Since
	\begin{equation*}
		\nabla\widetilde\a_L
		=
		\widetilde\a_L\nabla\log\widetilde\a_L\,,
	\end{equation*}
	\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.log.derivative.growth} gives the gradient bound after a final arbitrarily small increase of~$\kappa$.  Passing to the local limit proves the assertions for~$\a$.
\end{proof}

Recall that, for a large~$C_0(d)<\infty$ and~$\gmcdelta$ small, the regularity exponent
\begin{equation}
\label{e.large.scale.Holder.gamma.reg}
\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}
\coloneqq
1-C_0\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}
\end{equation}
of the introduction lies in~$(\nf12,1)$.

\begin{theorem}[Large-scale H\"older regularity and a Liouville theorem]
\label{t.large.scale.Holder.multifractal}
There are~$\gmcdelta_0(d)>0$ and~$C(d)<\infty$ such that the following holds if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0(d)$. For~$L=\infty$, the coefficient~$\a_L$ stands for the limiting coefficient~$\a$.
\begin{itemize}
\item \underline{\emph{Large-scale H\"older estimate}}: Let~$\gamma\in[\nf12,\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}]$. For every~$L\in\N_0\cup\{\infty\}$ and~$m\in\N$, there is an~$\N$-valued random variable~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ satisfying
\begin{equation}
\label{e.limiting.Holder.minimal.scale}
\P\left[\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)>k\right]
\leq
C\exp\left(
-\frac{(1-\gamma)^2(k-C)_+}
{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}
	\right)
,	\qquad k\in\N\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover, almost surely, for every~$L$ and~$m$, if~$u\in H^1(\cu_m)$ is~$\a_L$-harmonic in~$\cu_m$, then, for every~$n\in\N_0$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)
\end{equation*}
and every~$z\in3^n\Zd$ such that~$z+\cu_n\subseteq\cu_{m-1}$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.large.scale.Holder.multifractal}
\left\|
u-(u)_{z+\cu_n}
\right\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_n)}
\leq
C3^{-\gamma(m-n)}
\left\|
u-(u)_{\cu_m}
\right\|_{\underline L^2(\cu_m)}
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}
\label{e.large.scale.energy.multifractal}
\left\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\right\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_n)}
\leq
C3^{(1-\gamma)(m-n)}
\left\|\a_L^{\nf12}\nabla u\right\|_{\underline L^2(\cu_m)}\,.
\end{equation}

\item \underline{\emph{Liouville theorem}}: Almost surely, for every~$L\in\N_0\cup\{\infty\}$, if~$u\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Rd)$ satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.multifractal.Liouville.power.growth}
-\nabla\cdot(\a_L\nabla u)=0
\quad\text{in }\Rd,
\qquad
\liminf_{R\to\infty}
R^{-\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}}
\inf_{c\in\mathbb R}
\|u-c\|_{\underline L^2(B_R)}=0\,,
\end{equation}
then~$u$ is constant.
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix~$\gamma\in[\nf12,\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}]$. For finite~$L$, the two estimates are special cases of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}, for~$\a_L$-harmonic functions and interior cubes: we take~$\g=0$,~$x=z$, and~$j=n$ in~\eqref{e.Holder.estimate.boxes.local}, and use~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}. Approximating the trace in~$H^{\nf{1}{2}}(\partial\cu_m)$ by smooth functions extends the conclusion from smooth to arbitrary~$H^1$ boundary data.

For~$L=\infty$, fix~$m$ and, for~$N\geq m$, let~$u_N\in u+H_0^1(\cu_m)$ be~$\widetilde\a_N$-harmonic. Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} supplies the same random variable~$\mathcal L_N(\gamma,m)$ for every~$N\geq m$, and multiplication of the coefficient by~$\a_N(0)^{-1}$ changes neither the equation nor the estimate~\eqref{e.large.scale.energy.multifractal}. Lemma~\ref{l.finite.cutoff.coefficient.convergence} and the energy identity for~$u_N-u$ give
\begin{equation*}
\|\nabla u_N-\nabla u\|_{L^2(\cu_m)}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
The large-scale H\"older and energy estimates therefore pass to the limit. Taking~$\mathcal L_\infty(\gamma,m)=\mathcal L_N(\gamma,m)$, for any~$N\geq m$, also gives~\eqref{e.limiting.Holder.minimal.scale}.

For the Liouville assertion, fix~$L\in\N_0\cup\{\infty\}$ and suppose that~$u$ satisfies~\eqref{e.multifractal.Liouville.power.growth}. By~\eqref{e.limiting.Holder.minimal.scale} and Borel--Cantelli,~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}},m)\leq m$ for all sufficiently large~$m$. Choose a sequence of radii~$R\to\infty$ and constants~$c=c(R)\in\mathbb R$ such that
\begin{equation*}
	R^{-\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}}
	\|u-c\|_{\underline L^2(B_{R})}\longrightarrow0\,,
\end{equation*}
and let~$m$ be the largest integer such that~$\cu_{m}\subseteq B_{R}$. Since~$3^{m}$ is comparable to~$R$ and~$(u)_{\cu_{m}}$ minimizes~$c\mapsto\|u-c\|_{\underline L^2(\cu_{m})}$,
\begin{equation*}
3^{-\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}m}
\|u-(u)_{\cu_{m}}\|_{\underline L^2(\cu_{m})}\longrightarrow0\,.
\end{equation*}
For every fixed~$z\in\Zd$, we apply~\eqref{e.large.scale.Holder.multifractal} with~$\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{reg}}$ and~$n=0$ on~$z+\cu_0$, which is contained in~$\cu_{m-1}$ once~$R$ is large enough, and let~$R\to\infty$. Thus~$\nabla u=0$ almost everywhere in~$\Rd$, so~$u$ is constant.
\end{proof}

\section{The scaling limit}
\label{s.scaling.limit}

In this section we state Theorem~\ref{t.A} in the precise form in which we prove it. Sections~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} and~\ref{sec:mfd-process-convergence} prove part~(i) of Theorem~\ref{t.scaling.limit} and the convergence of the measures in part~(ii), and Section~\ref{lim:sec-process-measure} proves the remaining assertions.

To prove almost-sure convergence, we realize all rescaled processes using one family of independent fields~$(\g_j)_{j\in\Z}$, with~$\g_j$ having the law of~$\g_0(3^{-j}\,\cdot)$. Rescaling space by the factor~$3^{-N}$ turns the field with scale~$3^k$ into a field with scale~$3^{k-N}$. Accordingly, we take the rescaled potential at cutoff~$N$ to be~$H+\sum_{j=0}^N\g_{-j}$, where
\begin{equation}
\label{e.intro.common.scale.coupling}
 H(x)\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^\infty(\g_j(x)-\g_j(0))
\end{equation}
collects the fields with scales larger than one; the series converges locally in~$C^{1,1}$. Increasing~$N$ adds the field with scale~$3^{-N}$ and leaves the others unchanged. Let~$K^\omega_{N,x}$ be the law on continuous paths of the diffusion started at~$x$ and constructed from the elliptic resolvents of the generator
$\ahom_N^{-1}\bigl(\Delta+\nabla(H+\sum_{j=0}^N\g_{-j})\cdot\nabla\bigr)$.
Its stochastic differential description is
\begin{equation}
\label{e.intro.rescaled.generator}
 dY_t=\ahom_N^{-1}\nabla\Bigl(H+\sum_{j=0}^N\g_{-j}\Bigr)(Y_t)\,dt+\sqrt{2/\ahom_N}\,dW_t,
 \qquad Y_0=x\,.
\end{equation}
For each~$N$, the fields~$\g_{k-N}$ with~$k\in\N_0$ have the same joint law as the fields~$\g_k(3^N\,\cdot)$. Thus~$K^\omega_{N,x}$, viewed as a random probability measure, has the same distribution as the law of~$(3^{-N}X_{3^{2N}t/\ahom_N})_{t\geq0}$ conditional on the environment, with~$X_0=3^Nx$. The diffusion~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator} is reversible with respect to
\begin{equation*}
 \mu_N(dx)\coloneqq\exp\Bigl(H(x)+\sum_{j=0}^N\bigl(\g_{-j}(x)-\tau^2\bigr)\Bigr)\,dx\,.
\end{equation*}
The measure~$\mu_N^0\coloneqq e^{-H}\mu_N$ is stationary and has the law of~$\a_N(3^Nx)\,dx$. For Gaussian fields, it is the standard approximation of Gaussian multiplicative chaos: its density is obtained by exponentiating~$\sum_{j=0}^N\g_{-j}$ and dividing by the expectation of that exponential. We write~$d_{\mathrm{weak}}$ for a bounded complete metric on the probability measures on~$C([0,\infty),\Rd)$ that induces weak convergence.

\begin{theorem}[Scaling limit]
\label{t.scaling.limit}
Assume~\ref{a.g1}--\ref{a.g4}. There exists~$\gmcdelta_0(d)>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there exists a continuous probability kernel~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$ such that the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Convergence of the path laws}}: Almost surely, for every compact set~$B\subseteq\Rd$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-main-as}
 \lim_{N\to\infty}\sup_{x\in B}
 d_{\mathrm{weak}}(K^\omega_{N,x},K^\omega_x)=0\,.
\end{equation}
Almost surely, the laws~$(K^\omega_x)_{x\in\Rd}$ define a continuous, conservative, strong Markov process~$Z$, started at~$x$ under~$K_x^\omega$. On the same event, its semigroup maps bounded continuous functions to continuous functions, and, for every bounded open set~$U$, the mean exit time of~$Z$ from~$U$ under~$K_x^\omega$ is bounded uniformly in~$x\in U$. For every~$x\in\Rd$, the annealed law of~$(3^{-N}X_{3^{2N}t/\ahom_N})_{t\geq0}$, with~$X_0=3^Nx$, converges to~$\E[K^\omega_x]$.

\item \underline{\emph{The reversible measure}}: Almost surely, the measures~$\mu_N^0$ and~$\mu_N$ converge locally weakly to measures~$\mu^0$ and~$\mu=e^H\mu^0$, respectively. Both limits are locally finite, nonatomic, of full support, and singular with respect to Lebesgue measure. The measure~$\mu^0$ is stationary and not deterministic, and it satisfies~$\E[\mu^0(dx)]=dx$. Almost surely, the process~$Z$ is reversible with respect to~$\mu$, which is invariant: for every nonnegative Borel function~$f$ and every~$t\geq0$,
\begin{equation*}
 \int\biggl(\int f(Z_t)\,dK_x^\omega\biggr)\mu(dx)=\int f\,d\mu\,.
\end{equation*}

\item \underline{\emph{Anomalous scaling}}: There exist constants~$C\geq1$ and~$\eta>0$, depending only on~$d$ and the law of~$\g_0$, such that, for all~$\ell,m\in\N_0$ with~$\ell\leq m$,
\begin{equation}
 \frac{\ahom_m}{\ahom_\ell}\leq C3^{-\eta(m-\ell)}\,.
\end{equation}
Let~$\sigma_k\coloneqq\inf\{t\geq0:Z_t-Z_0\notin3^{-k}\cu_0\}$. For every~$x\in\Rd$ and every integer~$k\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{e.intro.limit.exit}
 \E\left[\int\sigma_k\,dK_x^\omega\right]\leq C3^{-(2+\eta)k}\,.
\end{equation}
For every~$x\in\Rd$, almost surely in~$\omega$, the law~$K_x^\omega$ is mutually singular with every mixture of Brownian laws with arbitrary covariance matrices, and~$K_x^\omega$-almost every path satisfies
\begin{equation}
\label{e.intro.limit.Holder}
 \sup\{\gamma\geq0:|Z_t-Z_0|=O(t^\gamma)\text{ as }t\downarrow0\}
 \leq\frac1{2+\eta}\,.
\end{equation}
In dimension two, one can take~$\eta=\tau^2/\log3$.

\item \underline{\emph{Non-Gaussian marginals}}: Almost surely, for every~$x\in\Rd$ and every~$t>0$, the law of~$Z_t$ under~$K_x^\omega$ is absolutely continuous with respect to~$\mu$, and it is not a Gaussian law.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

Theorem~\ref{t.A} follows, with~$\mathbf P_x=K^\omega_x$: for its exit-time bound, apply~\eqref{e.intro.limit.exit} with~$3^{-k-1}<r\leq3^{-k}$.

\section{Uniqueness of the limit form}
\label{sec:mfd-uniqueness}

In this section we prove that the energies~$\int A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$ have a unique limit. We show that all subsequential limits of the Dirichlet forms on cubes coincide (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-c1}). Section~\ref{sec:mfd-process-convergence} pairs this limit with the limit of the reversible measures~$\mu_N$ to construct the limiting diffusion. The argument has four parts.
\begin{enumerate}
\item We bound, uniformly in~$N$, the energy that solutions carry in small balls; this includes solutions of Neumann problems whose datum is approximated by a volume source (Sections~\ref{mfd:sec-environment}--\ref{mfd:sec-local}). These bounds control the change in the energy on a fixed cube when a fine-scale field is resampled. We use this control to prove compactness of the finite-volume energies (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-resampling}).
\item Along subsequences, the Dirichlet Green operators converge, and their limits define Dirichlet forms. These limit forms are local and consistent under restriction to subcubes, and multiplying the coefficient by a positive continuous function multiplies their energy measures by the same function (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}).
\item On a single event that does not depend on the solution, we bound the number of cubes on which the regularity, extension and harmonic-approximation estimates of Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic} fail along every chain of nested cubes (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}). We integrate this bound against the energy measure of a solution. This selects cubes carrying a fixed fraction of its energy, on which these estimates hold and the solution is close to affine.
\item Any two subsequential limit forms are comparable: the second lies between two deterministic multiples of the first. The optimal comparison constants agree, since otherwise replacing a solution by harmonic functions on the selected cubes would improve one of them (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}). Both the energy saved by a replacement and the errors it must absorb are proportional to the gap between the optimal comparison constants. To obtain errors of this size, we apply the Efron--Stein bound of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-resampling} to the difference between the minimal energy of the second limit form and a multiple of the minimal energy of the first, rather than to each energy separately (Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-conc}). Hence the two limits are proportional. The factor is one because the coarse-grained matrices of~$e^{-H}A_N$ on large cubes approach the identity uniformly in~$N$, and both limits inherit this normalization (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-scalar}).
\end{enumerate}

Throughout, Assumptions~\ref{a.g1}--\ref{a.g4} hold. Before reducing~$\gmcdelta$, we fix all exponents and tolerances and a finite list of moment orders. Bounds in~$L^p(\P)$ for the orders in this list are uniform in~$N$.

\subsection{Rescaled coefficients and energies}
\label{mfd:sec-environment}

At fixed~$N$, relabel the fields by replacing~$\g_i$ with~$\g_{i-N}(3^{-N}\,\cdot)$, and let~$\a_N$ be the cutoff coefficient of~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.coefficients} formed from the relabelled fields. The diffusion with law~$K^\omega_{N,x}$ has Dirichlet form~$\int A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$ on~$L^2(\mu_N)$, where~$A_N\coloneqq\ahom_N^{-1}\,d\mu_N/dx$ (see~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}). In terms of the relabelled fields,
\begin{equation}
 \label{eq:mfd-normalization}
 A_N(x)=e^{H(x)}\frac{\a_N(3^Nx)}{\ahom_N},
 \qquad A_N^0(x)=e^{-H(x)}A_N(x).
\end{equation}
If~$H$ is replaced by one of its partial sums, then~$A_N$ becomes a constant multiple of a stationary cutoff coefficient, so the results of Sections~\ref{s.self.similar} and~\ref{s.Holder.regularity} apply.

\begin{lemma}[Moments of the field~$H$]\label{mfd:lem-infrared}
The field~$H$ and its partial sums $\sum_{j=1}^{L'}(\g_j-\g_j(0))$ satisfy, on every compact cube~$Q$,
\begin{equation*}
 \bigl\|\|H\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_Q\gmcdelta\sqrt p,
 \qquad
 \E e^{\lambda\|H\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}}\leq2e^{C_Q\lambda^2\gmcdelta^2}
 \quad(p\geq2,\ \lambda\geq0),
\end{equation*}
with the same bounds for the partial sums, uniformly in the number~$L'$ of terms. Moreover, the partial sums converge to~$H$ locally in~$C^{1,1}$ almost surely.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
As in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.finite.cutoff.coefficient.convergence}, scaling, the mean value theorem and~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity} give $\|\|\g_j-\g_j(0)\|_{C^{1,1}(Q)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_Q\gmcdelta\sqrt p\,3^{-j}$ for $j\geq1$. Sum over~$j$ and expand the exponential. Since the sum of these norms has finite mean, the partial sums also converge in~$C^{1,1}$ almost surely.
\end{proof}

On a bounded cube~$Q$, let
\begin{equation}
 E_N^Q(u,v)=\int_QA_N\nabla u\cdot\nabla v\,dx,
 \qquad \Gamma_N(u)=A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx.
 \label{eq:mfd-energy}
\end{equation}
The form~$E_N^Q$ has domain~$H^1_0(Q)$, and we use the same integral for functions in~$H^1(Q)$ when prescribing boundary values. Let~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$ be the minimum of~$E_N^Q(u)$ over~$u\in H^1(Q)$ with~$u-g\in H^1_0(Q)$. Let~$G_N^Q$ be the Dirichlet Green operator, the inverse of~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla)$ on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ with zero Dirichlet boundary values, so that
\begin{equation}
 \langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle
 =\sup_{v\in H^1_0(Q)}\left(2\int_Qfv\,dx-E_N^Q(v)\right).
 \label{eq:mfd-quadratic-response}
\end{equation}
For the constant flux~$p$, the quantity $|Q|\,p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ is the energy~$E_N^Q(v)$ of a solution~$v$ of~$-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla v)=0$ in~$Q$ with boundary flux~$A_N\nabla v\cdot n=p\cdot n$, and it has the variational formula~\eqref{e.variational.astar}. Since $d\mu_N/dx=\ahom_N A_N$, the same energy, regarded as a form on~$L^2(\mu_N)$, has generator~$u\mapsto\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla u)/(\ahom_NA_N)$, the generator of the diffusion~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}; its resolvent is studied in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-speed}.

\subsection{Local estimates and approximation of the Neumann datum}
\label{mfd:sec-local}

We derive the local estimates from the coarse-grained inequalities of Section~\ref{s.coarse.graining}, the moments of Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, and the regularity estimates of Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}. The constants in these estimates have the required moments, uniformly in~$N$. These estimates also give the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}, the coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs} on cutoff functions, which are used in Section~\ref{s.tightness}.

\smallskip

At radii above the smallest field scale~$3^{-N}$, we apply Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} after replacing~$H$ by a partial sum. At smaller radii, elliptic estimates for coefficients with small oscillation complete the bounds. At these small radii we use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} for the coefficient and its logarithmic gradient. The moments of these bounds grow with~$N$, and positive powers of~$3^{-N}$ absorb this growth.

\smallskip

\subsubsection{Coercivity and extension}

The coarse-grained bounds of Section~\ref{s.self.similar} also cover scales below the smallest field scale. We first apply them on a rescaled cube. We call a cube $z+3^{-k}\cu_0$ of a triadic mesh a \textbf{cell}.

\begin{lemma}[Ellipticity moments on a cell]
\label{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}
Fix $s\in(0,1]$, $q\in\{1,2\}$ and a bounded cube~$Q$. For every $p<\infty$ and all sufficiently small~$\gmcdelta$, uniformly for $0\leq k\leq N$ and cubes $z+3^{-k}\cu_0\subset Q$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-cell-moment}
 \bigl\|\Lambda_{s,q}(z+3^{-k}\cu_0;A_N)+\lambda_{s,q}^{-1}(z+3^{-k}\cu_0;A_N)\bigr\|_{L^p}
 \leq C_{p,s,Q}\exp\bigl(C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2k\bigr)\,.
\end{equation}
In particular, on a fixed cell~$U$ these moments are bounded uniformly in~$N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a finite moment order~$\xi\geq4p$ large enough for Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} at exponent~$s/2$, then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that its remaining hypotheses hold. The estimate~\eqref{e.homogenize.at.scale.m.E}, the comparison of the weighted sums at exponents~$s/2$ and~$s$, and~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} give
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_m\bigl\|\Lambda_{s,q}(\cu_0;A_m^0)
                 +\lambda_{s,q}^{-1}(\cu_0;A_m^0)\bigr\|_{L^{2p}}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}

By definition of~$A_N$, the coefficient~$A_N(z+3^{-k}y)$ is the product of three factors: the scalar $\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}\exp\bigl(H(z)+\sum_{j<k}(\g_{-j}(z)-\tau^2)\bigr)$, the ratio of $\exp\bigl(H+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j}\bigr)$ at~$z+3^{-k}y$ to its value at~$z$, and a function of~$y$ with the law of~$A_{N-k}^0$. Summing the bounds on the gradients of the fields as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared} bounds the oscillation of $H+\sum_{j<k}\g_{-j}$ over the cube~$z+3^{-k}\cu_0$ by a sub-Gaussian variable of size~$C_Q\gmcdelta$. The scalar involves the values of $k$ independent fields. By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio}, for every fixed~$v<\infty$ the sum of the scalar and its inverse, multiplied by the exponential of this oscillation, has $L^v$ norm at most $C_{v,Q}e^{C(v+v^2)\gmcdelta^2k}$. Monotonicity and homogeneity of the coarse-grained ellipticity constants, followed by H\"older's inequality, prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-cell-moment}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Coercivity of the form~$E_N^Q$]\label{mfd:lem-coercivity}
Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of side at most one. For every $N\geq0$ there is a random variable $K_N$ such that, for every $v\in H^1_0(Q)$ and every $w\in H^1(Q)$,
\begin{equation}
 \|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq K_NE_N^Q(v)
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 \|w-(w)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq K_NE_N^Q(w)\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-1}
\end{equation}
For every $p<\infty$ there is $\gmcdelta_0(d,p)>0$ such that $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ if $\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On $\cu_0$, take $s=\nf18$. By the functional inequalities~\eqref{e.besov.grad.poincare},~\eqref{e.nabla.u.detach} and~\eqref{e.CG.Poincare.trace.zero}, followed by $B^{\nf{7}{8}}_{2,\infty}\subset H^{\nf34}$, we obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} with $K_N=C\lambda_{1/8,1}^{-1}(\cu_0;A_N)$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} bounds its~$L^p$ norms uniformly in~$N$. For a fixed smaller cube, rescale the same inequalities and use~\eqref{eq:mfd-cell-moment}. If its side is not triadic, then the remaining dilation lies in $[1,3]$. Apply the same argument after replacing~$\gmcdelta$ by~$C_d\gmcdelta$, with constants depending on~$Q$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Boundary extension and bounds on grid cubes]\label{mfd:lem-extension}
Let $\beta\in(\nf12,1)$ and set $\sigma=\nf12(\beta-\nf12)$. The following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There is $C(d,\beta)<\infty$ such that, for every cube $q=z+r\cu_0$ with $r\leq1$ and every $g\in C^\beta(\partial q)$,
\begin{equation}
 \Lambda_{N,q}(g)\leq C\,\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\,r^{d-2}\,\|g(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)/\mathbb R}^2\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-2}
\end{equation}
\item Let~$Q$ be a bounded cube, and fix finitely many grids, each consisting of the cubes $z+r\,\cu_0$ with $r=3^{-k}$, $k\geq0$, and $z$ in~$z_0+r\mathbb Z^d$ for a point~$z_0$ fixed for each grid; we call their cubes contained in~$Q$ grid cubes. For every $\eta>0$ and $p<\infty$ there is $\gmcdelta_0(d,\eta,p)>0$ such that, if $\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random variables~$K_N$ with $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ such that, for every~$N$ and every grid cube~$q$ of side $r\geq3^{-N}$,
\begin{equation}
 \Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)+\lambda_{\sigma/2,2}^{-1}(q;A_N)\leq K_Nr^{-\eta}\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-3}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}. The change of variables $x=z+ry$ multiplies the unnormalized energy by~$r^{d-2}$ and leaves the volume-normalized constant~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}$ unchanged, so we may take $q=\cu_0$. The space $C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$ embeds into $H^{\nf12+\sigma}(\partial \cu_0)$, and the trace operator has a bounded right inverse $H^{\nf12+\sigma}(\partial \cu_0)\to H^{1+\sigma}(\cu_0)$; let~$h$ be the resulting extension of~$g$. Applying~\eqref{e.cg.RHS} with zero right side, $s=\sigma$ and boundary datum~$h$, we find that the $A_N$-harmonic function~$v$ with $v=h$ on~$\partial \cu_0$ satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{N,\cu_0}(g)=\|A_N^{\nf12}\nabla v\|_{L^2}^2
 \leq C\sigma^{-3}\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(\cu_0;A_N)
       \|\nabla h\|_{H^\sigma}^2\,.
\end{equation*}

\emph{Step 2.} We prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. Since $L^p$ norms increase with~$p$, we may assume that $p>1$ and $p\eta>d$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} with exponents $\sigma/2$ and~$2$. There are at most $C3^{dk}$ grid cubes at depth~$k$, so taking the maximum over them costs a factor $C3^{\nf{dk}{p}}$ in~$L^p$. Reduce~$\gmcdelta$ so that $C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2<\nf12(\eta-d/p)\log3$. For $K_N$ the supremum, over $k\leq N$ and grid cubes~$q$ of depth~$k$, of $3^{-\eta k}\bigl(\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)+\lambda^{-1}_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\bigr)$, Minkowski's inequality gives
\begin{equation*}
\|K_N\|_{L^p}
  \leq C_p\sum_{k\geq0}3^{-(\eta-\nf{d}{p})k}
  e^{C(p+p^2)\gmcdelta^2k}\leq C_p\,.
\end{equation*}

\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Morrey bounds for the Dirichlet problem}

To apply the regularity estimates with a bounded volume source, we first write that source as a divergence.

\begin{lemma}[The gradient of the Newtonian potential]\label{mfd:lem-primitive}
Let $f\in L^\infty$ be supported in a cube of side~$R$, and let $g$ be the gradient of the Newtonian potential of~$f$, that is, the convolution of~$f$ with the gradient of the fundamental solution of the Laplacian. Then $\nabla\cdot g=f$ and, for all~$x$ and~$y$ with $|x-y|\leq R$,
\begin{equation}
 |g(x)-g(y)|\leq C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|\bigl(1+\log(R/|x-y|)\bigr)\leq CR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty|x-y|^{\nf12}\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-primitive}
\end{equation}
In particular, $[g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}}\leq CR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$ on that cube.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The kernel of the convolution and its gradient are bounded by $C|z|^{1-d}$ and $C|z|^{-d}$. If~$x$ is farther than~$2\sqrt dR$ from the supporting cube, the derivative bound gives~$|g(x)-g(y)|\leq C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|$ directly. Otherwise the support lies in~$B_{CR}(x)$. We split the convolution according to whether the integration variable~$z$ satisfies $|z-x|\leq2|x-y|$. The part with $|z-x|\leq2|x-y|$ is bounded by $C\|f\|_\infty|x-y|$, both at~$x$ and at~$y$. For $|z-x|>2|x-y|$, the mean value theorem bounds the difference of the kernel at~$x-z$ and at~$y-z$ by $C|x-y||x-z|^{-d}$. The integral over $2|x-y|<|z-x|<CR$ is at most $C|x-y|\|f\|_\infty\log(CR/|x-y|)$. The second inequality in~\eqref{eq:mfd-primitive} follows from $(|x-y|/R)^{\nf12}(1+\log(R/|x-y|))\leq C$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Pointwise extremes of the coefficient]\label{mfd:lem-extremes}
Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube, and set
\begin{equation*}
 D_N=3^{-N}\|\nabla\log A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\,.
\end{equation*}
For every~$p$ there are $C_p<\infty$, $C_d<\infty$ and $c_d>0$ such that, if $\gmcdelta\leq c_d/p$, then
\begin{align}
 \|D_N\|_{L^p}&\leq C_p(1+N)^{\nf12},\notag\\
 \bigl\|\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}+\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}
 &\leq C_pe^{(C_d\gmcdelta+C_p\gmcdelta^2)N}.
 \label{eq:mfd-6}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}

The linear term $C_d\gmcdelta N$ in the exponent arises from taking the maximum over the cubes of side~$3^{-N}$ in~$Q$.

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We bound~$D_N$. We have $\nabla\log A_N=\nabla H+\sum_{j=0}^N\nabla\g_{-j}$ and, in law, $\|\nabla\g_{-j}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ is at most $3^j$ times the maximum, over at most $C3^{dj}$ cells, of the regularity norms on unit cells from~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}. The maximum of $n$ sub-Gaussian variables with parameter~$\gmcdelta$ has $L^p$ norm at most $C\gmcdelta(\sqrt{\log n}+\sqrt p)$. Therefore
\begin{equation*}
 \|D_N\|_{L^p}\leq3^{-N}\bigl\|\|\nabla H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr\|_{L^p}+\sum_{j\leq N}3^{j-N}C_p\gmcdelta\sqrt{1+j}\leq C_p\sqrt{1+N}\,.
\end{equation*}

\emph{Step 2.} We bound~$\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ and~$\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$. The deterministic factor~$\ahom_N^{-1}e^{-(N+1)\tau^2}$ of~$A_N$ satisfies~$1\leq e^{(N+1)\tau^2}\ahom_N\leq e^{C\gmcdelta^2(N+1)}$ by~\eqref{e.ord.bounds.truncated}, Proposition~\ref{p.homogenized.coefficient.reciprocal.lower} and~\eqref{e.tau.bound}, and $e^{\|H\|_\infty}$ has moments of every order by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. By H\"older's inequality, it therefore suffices to bound the moments of $\exp(\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)$. Cover~$Q$ by at most $C3^{dN}$ cubes~$Q_i$ of side~$3^{-N}$ with centers~$x_i$. On each of them, $\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|\leq|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}(x_i)|+\sum_{j\leq N}\operatorname{osc}_{Q_i}\g_{-j}$, where the first term is sub-Gaussian with parameter $C\gmcdelta\sqrt{N+1}$ and the second is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$. H\"older's inequality gives $\E\exp(q\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)\leq Ce^{Cq^2\gmcdelta^2(N+1)}$. For $q\geq p$, the inequality $\exp(p\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)\leq(\sum_i\exp(q\sup_{Q_i}|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|))^{\nf pq}$ and Jensen's inequality bound $\E\exp(p\sup_Q|\sum_{j\leq N}\g_{-j}|)$ by $(C3^{dN}e^{Cq^2\gmcdelta^2(N+1)})^{\nf pq}$. We choose $q=\gmcdelta^{-1}\sqrt{d\log3/C}$, which is admissible when $\gmcdelta\leq c_d/p$; the exponent is then at most $C_dp\gmcdelta(N+1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Morrey bounds at all radii]\label{mfd:prop-growth}
Let $t\in(d-1,d)$ and $\alpha\in(0,1)$, fix a finite list of moment orders, and let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of side at most one. There is $\gmcdelta_0(d,t,\alpha,\text{list})>0$ such that, if $\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there are random variables~$K_N$ with $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, such that, almost surely, the following estimates hold for every~$f\in L^\infty(Q)$ and~$\varphi\in C^2(\overline Q)$. Here~$u_N\in H^1(Q)$ solves $-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla u_N)=f$ in~$Q$ with $u_N=\varphi$ on~$\partial Q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Morrey bound}}: for every $x\in Q$ and $r\in(0,1]$,
\begin{equation}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq K_N\bigl(\|f\|_\infty+\|\varphi\|_{C^2}\bigr)^2r^t\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-4}
\end{equation}
\item \underline{\emph{H\"older bound}}: $\|u_N\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N(\|f\|_\infty+\|\varphi\|_{C^2})$ and, if $\varphi=0$,
\begin{equation}
 \|G_N^Qf\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-5}
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Rescale so that $Q=y_0+\cu_0$. In the coordinates $y=3^Nx$ of the relabelled fields, the domain is $3^Ny_0+\cu_N$, a deterministic translate of~$\cu_N$. Throughout the proof, $K_N$ denotes a random variable with $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, which may change from line to line. Choose the exponents as follows. Take $\alpha_M\in(\nf12,1)$ with $\alpha_M>\max\{\alpha,(t+2-d)/2\}$, and set $t_0=d-2+2\alpha_M>t$. Choose~$t_1$ with $\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\}<t_1<t_0$, and require~$\gmcdelta$ to be so small that $\alpha_M\leq1-C\gmcdelta|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12}$.

\emph{Step 1.} We replace~$H$ by a partial sum $\sum_{j=1}^{L'}(\g_j-\g_j(0))$ of its series, which makes the coefficient a constant multiple of a cutoff coefficient. In Steps~1--5 we keep the notation~$H$, $A_N$, $u_N$ and~$\Gamma_N$ for the truncated objects; the truncation is removed in Step~6. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization} and~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.coefficients},
\begin{equation*}
 A_N(3^{-N}y)=\ahom_N^{-1}e^{L'\tau^2}\exp\Bigl(-\sum_{i=N+1}^{N+L'}\g_i(0)\Bigr)\a_{N+L'}(y)\,,
\end{equation*}
so $A_N(3^{-N}\cdot)$ is a random scalar multiple of the cutoff coefficient~$\a_{N+L'}$. In the coordinates~$y$, the rescaled function~$u_N(3^{-N}\cdot)$ solves $-\nabla\cdot(\a_{N+L'}\nabla u_N(3^{-N}\cdot))=\nabla\cdot g$, where~$g$ is the vector field constructed in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive} for the source $f(3^{-N}\cdot)$, divided by~$3^{2N}$ and by this random scalar and extended by zero. Moreover, $u_N(3^{-N}\cdot)=\varphi(3^{-N}\cdot)\in C^{1,\nf12}$ on the boundary. Since $N+L'\geq N$, the estimate~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} applies with $m=N$; we write $\mathcal L=\mathcal L_{N+L'}(\alpha_M,N)$ for the minimal scale in that estimate.

\emph{Step 2.} We prove the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} at the radii $3^{-N}\leq r\leq3^{-\mathcal L}$. For $x\in Q$ and $0\leq n\leq N-\mathcal L$, we have $B_{3^{n-N}/2}(x)\cap Q\subset3^{-N}\bigl((3^Nx+\cu_n)\cap(3^Ny_0+\cu_N)\bigr)$. Substituting the definitions of the random scalar and of~$\b_{N+L',N}$, the constants~$L'\tau^2$ cancel, and the random scalar times the average $(\b_{N+L',N})_{3^Ny_0+\cu_N}$ equals $\fint_Qe^H\,dx$. We square~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate}, multiply it by the volume, which is at most~$3^{dn}$, and return to the $x$-coordinates. Using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive} with $R=3^N$ and bounding $\fint_Qe^H\,dx$ and its inverse by $e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}$, we obtain, for $r=3^{n-N}/2$,
\begin{equation}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq Cr^{t_0}\Bigl(\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)
 +e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}\bigl(\|f\|_\infty^2+\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^2\bigr)\Bigr)\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-macro}
\end{equation}
The factor $e^{\|H\|_{L^\infty(Q)}}$ has moments bounded uniformly in~$N$ and~$L'$ by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. The random variable~$\mathcal L$ does not depend on~$x$.

\emph{Step 3.} We treat the remaining large radii $3^{-\mathcal L}<r\leq1$ and bound the total energy. For these radii, $\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)\leq\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)\leq3^{t_1\mathcal L}r^{t_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)$. Together with Step~2, this gives a bound $K_Nr^{t_1}$ at every radius $r\geq3^{-N}$, also when $\mathcal L>N$. For an order~$p$ in the list and for the larger orders used in H\"older's inequality,~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale} gives $\E3^{pt_1\mathcal L}<\infty$ as soon as
\begin{equation}
 \frac{(1-\alpha_M)^2}{C\gmcdelta^2|\log\gmcdelta|}>pt_1\log3\,,
 \label{eq:mfd-tail-rate}
\end{equation}
which is a condition on~$\gmcdelta$ alone once~$\alpha_M$, $p$ and~$t_1$ are fixed. The global energy has moments: by~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response},~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2},
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(Q)\leq2\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)+2\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle\leq2\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)+2K_N\|f\|_{L^2}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
uniformly in the truncation, since the truncated coefficient satisfies the same estimates.

\emph{Step 4.} We prove the Morrey bound for $r\leq3^{-N}$ on balls away from~$\partial Q$. Let $p_1<\infty$ satisfy $d-2d/p_1>t_1$ and $1-d/p_1>\alpha$, and let~$\ell_N$ be $3^{-N}(1+D_N)^{-1}$ times a small constant depending on~$d$ and~$p_1$, chosen below. For $x_0\in Q$ with $B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)\subset Q$, the oscillation of $\log A_N$ on $B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)$ is at most $2\ell_ND_N/3^{-N}$, hence at most twice the small constant. Dividing the equation by $A_N(x_0)$ gives an equation with coefficient $A_N/A_N(x_0)$, whose logarithm is bounded by twice the small constant on the ball, and source $f/A_N(x_0)$. By~\citet[Theorem~2, equation~(48)]{Meyers1963}, the admissible gradient exponent tends to infinity as the ellipticity ratio tends to one. Choose the small constant so that the admissible exponent exceeds~$p_1$, subtract the mean of~$u_N$, and apply that estimate with initial gradient exponent~$2$ and a finite source exponent greater than~$d$. After scaling to the unit ball, this gives
\begin{equation*}
 \Bigl(\fint_{B_{\ell_N}(x_0)}|\nabla u_N|^{p_1}\Bigr)^{\nf1{p_1}}\leq C\Bigl(\fint_{B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)}|\nabla u_N|^2\Bigr)^{\nf12}+C\ell_NA_N(x_0)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
H\"older's inequality on~$B_r(x_0)$, $r\leq\ell_N$, and the fact that~$A_N$ is within a fixed factor of~$A_N(x_0)$ on~$B_{2\ell_N}(x_0)$ yield, for every $r\leq\ell_N$,
\begin{equation}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq C\Bigl(\frac r{\ell_N}\Bigr)^{d-2d/p_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{2\ell_N})
 +C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^2\ell_N^{2+\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-micro}
\end{equation}
We divide by~$r^t$. Since $d-2d/p_1>t_1>t$, the right side is largest at $r=\ell_N$, so
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)r^{-t}\leq C(1+D_N)^t3^{tN}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})+C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^23^{-(d+2-t)N}\,.
\end{equation*}
For $\ell_N\leq r\leq3^{-N}$, we use $\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})\leq(3^{-N}/\ell_N)^tr^t3^{tN}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}})$. Together with Step~3 at $r=C3^{-N}$, this gives, for every $r\leq3^{-N}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r)\leq C(1+D_N)^t3^{-(t_1-t)N}K_Nr^t
 +C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|^2_\infty3^{-(d+2-t)N}r^t\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-6}, the positive powers~$3^{-(t_1-t)N}$ and~$3^{-(d+2-t)N}$ absorb the polynomially growing moments of~$D_N$ and the moments of~$\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$, once~$\gmcdelta$ is small enough for the finitely many larger moment orders used in H\"older's inequality.

\emph{Step 5.} We prove the Morrey bound for $r\leq3^{-N}$ near the faces of~$Q$, where the Dirichlet condition holds. Let~$x_0$ be within distance~$2\ell_N$ of a face, say $\{x_d=0\}$. The function $u_N-\varphi$ vanishes on the face and solves $-\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla(u_N-\varphi))=f+\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla\varphi)$, where $\|\nabla\cdot(A_N\nabla\varphi)\|_\infty\leq CA_N(x_0)(\|D^2\varphi\|_\infty+\ell_N^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi\|_\infty)$ on the ball around~$x_0$ on which~$\log A_N$ has small oscillation, as in Step~4. We reflect $u_N-\varphi$ oddly, $A_N$ evenly and the source oddly across the face; the reflected function solves the reflected equation, with no additional source concentrated on the face. Indeed, for a compactly supported test function on the doubled ball, the difference of the test function and its reflection, restricted to~$Q$, lies in~$H^1$, has zero trace on the face, and vanishes near the other faces, so it is an admissible test function for the original equation. The map that folds the reflected domain back onto~$Q$ is $1$-Lipschitz, so the oscillation of the logarithm of the reflected coefficient on a ball~$B$ centered on the face is at most that of $\log A_N$ on~$B\cap\overline Q$. Hence~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} applies to the reflected function and coefficient, since the proof of~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} uses only the boundedness and the small oscillation of the coefficient, not its differentiability across the face. Since the energy measure of the reflected function on a symmetric set~$B$ is twice the energy measure of $u_N-\varphi$ on $B\cap Q$, and
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N-\varphi)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
 \leq2\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
 +C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_\infty^23^{-dN}\,,
\end{equation*}
we obtain
\begin{align*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)
 &\leq C(r/\ell_N)^{d-2d/p_1}\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_{C3^{-N}}\cap Q)
 +C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty^2\ell_N^{2+\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\\
 &\qquad+C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^2(1+D_N)^d\ell_N^{\nf{2d}{p_1}}r^{d-2d/p_1}\,.
\end{align*}
Dividing the last term by~$r^t$ and maximizing at $r=\ell_N$ gives $C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|\varphi\|_{C^2}^23^{-(d-t)N}(1+D_N)^t$. The positive power~$3^{-(d-t)N}$ absorbs its moments as in Step~4. Near an edge or a corner of~$Q$, we reflect across each face meeting the neighborhood of~$x_0$. On a set invariant under these reflections, the energy of the reflected solution is two to the power of the number of these faces times the energy of the solution in the part of the set inside~$Q$. Divide by this factor to obtain the estimates on the original cube.

\emph{Step 6.} We remove the truncation. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}, the truncated coefficients converge uniformly on~$\overline Q$ to the untruncated~$A_N$ almost surely. For fixed~$N$, they are therefore bounded away from zero once the truncation is sufficiently large. Testing the difference of the two weak equations with the difference of the solutions, which lies in~$H^1_0(Q)$, bounds its energy by the uniform distance between the coefficients times the product of the~$L^2$ norms of the gradient of the untruncated solution and of the gradient of the difference. The lower bound on the coefficients then shows that the gradients converge in~$L^2(Q)$. Consequently, for every~$x$ and~$r$, the truncated energies converge to~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)$. By Steps~2--5, these energies satisfy~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} with random constants whose~$L^p$ norms are bounded uniformly in~$N$ and in the truncation, for every order~$p$ in the list. Their limit inferior gives a constant for the untruncated estimate, and Fatou's lemma preserves these moment bounds.

\emph{Step 7.} We prove the H\"older bounds. At radii above~$3^{-N}$,~\eqref{e.Holder.estimate.boxes.local} is a Campanato bound at every $x\in Q$, with exponent $\alpha_M>\alpha$ and the same source norm; the large radii $3^{-\mathcal L}<r\leq1$ are handled as in Step~3, and the $L^2$ oscillation on~$Q$ is bounded by the energy through~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}. At radii below~$3^{-N}$, the $W^{1,p_1}$ bound of Step~4 and Morrey's inequality bound the oscillation on~$B_{\ell_N}$. After division by~$r^\alpha$, the energy on the ball of radius~$C3^{-N}$, the volume source and the boundary datum contribute, respectively,
\begin{align*}
 &CK_N^{\nf12}\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}^{\nf12}
       3^{-((t_1-d+2)/2-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,,\\
 &C\|A_N^{-1}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\|f\|_\infty
       3^{-(2-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,,\\
 &C\|\varphi\|_{C^2}
       3^{-(1-\alpha)N}(1+D_N)^C\,.
\end{align*}
All three powers of~$3^{-N}$ are positive by the choice of the parameters. Chaining balls of radius~$\ell_N$ inside a cube of side~$3^{-N}$ costs another fixed power of~$1+D_N$. Above the smallest field scale, telescoping the cube averages costs the geometric sum $\sum_{s\leq R}s^{\alpha_M}\leq CR^{\alpha_M}$. With the same absorption of moments as in Step~4, this proves the H\"older bounds. Approximate a general bounded source by smooth sources. Remove the truncation as in Step~6: the equicontinuity just proved and the convergence in~$L^2$ give uniform convergence of the truncated solutions.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Approximating the Neumann datum by a volume source}

We approximate the functional $v\mapsto\int_Qp\cdot\nabla v$, which encodes the Neumann datum~$p\cdot n$, by a bounded volume source near the faces.

Let $Q=(0,1)^d$, let $p\in\Rd$ with $|p|=1$, and let
\begin{equation*}
 L_p(v)=\int_Qp\cdot\nabla v\,dx=\int_{\partial Q}(p\cdot n)\operatorname{Tr}v\,d\mathcal H^{d-1}\,,
\end{equation*}
where~$n$ is the outer normal. For $0<\epsilon<\nf18$, let~$f_\epsilon$ be the sum over $i=1,\dots,d$ of~$p_i$ times the difference of two smooth bumps of integral one, the first supported in the layer $\{\epsilon<1-x_i<2\epsilon\}$ near the face $\{x_i=1\}$ and the second in the layer $\{\epsilon<x_i<2\epsilon\}$ near the face $\{x_i=0\}$, both obtained from one fixed profile by dilation by~$\epsilon$, and let
\begin{equation}
 L_{p,\epsilon}(v)=\int_Qf_\epsilon v\,dx\,.
 \label{eq:mfd-bump}
\end{equation}
Each summand of~$f_\epsilon$ has integral zero, $f_\epsilon\in C^\infty(\overline Q)$, $\|f_\epsilon\|_\infty\leq C\epsilon^{-1}$, and $L_{p,\epsilon}(1)=0$.

\begin{lemma}[Error of the volume-source approximation of the Neumann datum]\label{mfd:lem-neumann-error}
Let~$v_N,v_{N,\epsilon}$ be the mean-zero Riesz representers of~$L_p,L_{p,\epsilon}$ with respect to~$E_N^Q$, so that~$E_N^Q(v_N)=p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$. For every~$v\in H^1(Q)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-7}
 |L_p(v)-L_{p,\epsilon}(v)|
 \leq C\epsilon^{\nf14}\|v-(v)_Q\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}.
\end{equation}
With~$K_N$ from~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}, this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-8}
 E_N^Q(v_N-v_{N,\epsilon})\leq CK_N\epsilon^{\nf12}.
\end{equation}
For every moment order~$q$ in the list, if~$\gmcdelta$ is small enough,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-9}
 \|E_N^Q(v_N)-E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})\|_{L^q}\leq C_q\epsilon^{\nf14},
 \qquad \sup_{N,\epsilon}\|E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})\|_{L^q}<\infty.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Subtract~$(v)_Q$ and extend to~$H^{\nf{3}{4}}(\Rd)$. Fourier transformation in the normal variable and Cauchy--Schwarz give, on each face,
\begin{equation*}
 \|v(\tau,\cdot)-v(0,\cdot)\|_{L^2(\mathbb R^{d-1})}^2
 \leq\|v\|_{H^{\nf{3}{4}}(\Rd)}^2
       \int_{\mathbb R}\frac{\min\{4,\tau^2\xi^2\}}{(1+\xi^2)^{\nf{3}{4}}}\,d\xi
 \leq C\tau^{\nf12}\|v\|_{H^{\nf{3}{4}}(\Rd)}^2.
\end{equation*}
Integrate against the bump profile and sum over the faces to get~\eqref{eq:mfd-7}. Test the difference of the two weak equations with~$v_N-v_{N,\epsilon}$ and use coercivity to obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-8}. The reverse triangle inequality for the energy norm then gives
\begin{equation*}
 |E_N^Q(v_N)-E_N^Q(v_{N,\epsilon})|
 \leq CK_N^{\nf12}\epsilon^{\nf14}E_N^Q(v_N)^{\nf12}
       +CK_N\epsilon^{\nf12}.
\end{equation*}
Here~$E_N^Q(v_N)=p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ has moments of the orders in the list bounded uniformly in~$N$ by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}. H\"older's inequality proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-9}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Reflection across the faces}

Let~$a$ be a scalar coefficient, and let~$u$ be a solution with zero conormal derivative and bounded source~$f$. Reflect~$u$ and~$f$ evenly across a set of faces. This gives a solution~$u\circ T$ of the equation with coefficient~$a\circ T$ and source~$f\circ T$, where~$T$ folds the reflected domain onto the original cube. The reflected equation has no additional source concentrated on the folding planes. On a set invariant under these reflections, the energy of~$u\circ T$ is two to the power of the number of reflected faces times the energy of~$u$ in the part of the set inside the original cube.

\begin{lemma}[Effect of folding on~$\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}$]\label{mfd:thm-fold}
Let~$T$ fold the cube $z+\cu_M$ across coordinate planes through~$z$, as above. For every continuous positive coefficient~$a$ on its closure, scalar~$a_0>0$, $s\in(0,\nf12)$ and $n\leq M$,
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(z+\cu_M,n;a\circ T,a_0)^2
 \leq\left(1+\frac{3d}{3^{1-2s}-1}\right)
       \mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}(z+\cu_M,n;a,a_0)^2.
 \label{eq:mfd-11}
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $\kappa_n(a)$ for the maximum, over grid cubes~$U\subset z+\cu_M$ of side~$3^n$, of $\sup_{|e|=1}J(U,e,e;a/a_0)$. On a cube meeting no folding plane, $T$ reflects some of the coordinates, so changing variables by~$T$ shows that the energy for $a\circ T$ is at most~$\kappa_n(a)$. Subdivide each cube that meets a folding plane triadically, and repeat the subdivision on the subcubes that still meet one. After~$j$ subdivisions, the subcubes that still meet a folding plane fill at most a fraction~$d3^{-j}$ of the cube. By subadditivity~\eqref{e.subadditivity.J}, $\kappa_n(a\circ T)\leq\kappa_n(a)+\sum_{j\geq1}3d\,3^{-j}\kappa_{n-j}(a)$. Insert this inequality into Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E} and sum the geometric series $\sum_{j\geq1}3^{-(1-2s)j}$ to obtain~\eqref{eq:mfd-11}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Energy decay for the folded coefficient]\label{mfd:prop-folded-iteration}
Let~$\gamma$ be an exponent as in Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}, let $m,L\in\N_0$ with~$m\leq L$, and let~$T$ fold the cube~$z+\cu_m$ across coordinate planes through~$z$. Let~$\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$ be the minimal scale of Section~\ref{ss.regularity.iteration} for the cube~$z+\cu_m$, defined with the parameter choices~\eqref{e.choices.hepk.Holder.proof} of the proof of Proposition~\ref{p.Holder.regularity}, but with the tolerance in the good events reduced by a fixed dimensional factor. If $u\in H^1(z+\cu_m)$ solves $-\nabla\cdot((\a_L\circ T)\nabla u)=\nabla\cdot\tilde g$, with $\tilde g\in C^{0,\nf12}(z+\cu_m;\Rd)$, then, for $0\leq n\leq m-\mathcal L_L(\gamma,m)$,
\begin{equation}
 \|(\a_L\circ T)^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_n)}
 \leq C3^{(1-\gamma)(m-n)}
 \left(\|(\a_L\circ T)^{\nf12}\nabla u\|_{\underline L^2(z+\cu_m)}
 +(\b_{L,m})_{z+\cu_m}^{-\nf12}3^{\nf m2}[\tilde g]_{W^{\nf12,\infty}(z+\cu_m)}\right).
 \label{eq:mfd-folded-energy}
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Apply the argument proving~\eqref{e.energy.density.estimate} at~$z$, using the averages~$(\b_{L,j+2})_{z+\cu_{j+2}}$ of the original field. The harmonic approximation and excess decay use the good event only through the quantity~$\mathcal E_{s,\infty,2}$ of Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E}, as explained in the proof of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-11}, folding multiplies this quantity by at most a dimensional constant. The bounds with~$q=1$ at larger exponents follow from the bound with~$q=2$ at~$s_0$ by a weighted Cauchy--Schwarz inequality. The reduced tolerance absorbs the dimensional constant. The sum of the errors over the good scales is still bounded by~\eqref{e.ep.j.z.sum}. The bound~\eqref{e.Bz.bound} on the number of bad scales and the ratios~\eqref{e.ratio.of.bs} of these averages involve the original field and are unchanged. These bounds give the inputs to the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}. Finally,~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} supplies the coarse-grained ellipticity constants used in Step~6 of that proof. All cubes in the iteration are centered at~$z$, hence invariant under the reflections.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Bounded Neumann sources]\label{mfd:cor-neumann-source}
Let~$\alpha\in(0,1)$ and~$t\in(d-1,d)$, and fix a finite list of moment orders. There is~$\gmcdelta_0(d,\alpha,t,\text{list})>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then on every fixed cube~$Q$ there are random variables~$K_N$ with $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list, such that the following holds for every~$f\in L^\infty(Q)$ with~$\int_Qf=0$. If~$u_N\in H^1(Q)$ is the mean-zero solution of
\begin{equation*}
 \int_QA_N\nabla u_N\cdot\nabla v=\int_Qfv
 \qquad(v\in H^1(Q)),
\end{equation*}
then, for every~$x\in Q$ and~$0<r\leq1$,
\begin{equation*}
 \|u_N\|_{C^\alpha(\overline Q)}\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty,
 \qquad \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty^2r^t.
\end{equation*}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Rescale to the unit cube. Choose~$t_0\in(\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\},d)$ and~$\eta>0$ such that~$t_1=t_0-\eta>\max\{t,d-2+2\alpha\}$. Replace~$H$ by a partial sum as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}; the identity relating the random scalar and the averages of~$\b_{N+L',\cdot}$ over cubes to~$e^H$ gives the same averages and the same scaling of the source as in that proposition. The coercivity bound for mean-zero functions gives~$E_N^Q(u_N)+\|u_N\|_2^2\leq CK_N\|f\|_\infty^2$.

\emph{Step 1.} We control the energy near the boundary by repeated reflections. At radii above~$3^{-N}$, we bound the energy in a target cube of side~$r$ by the energy in a cube of fixed size, through at most~$d+1$ applications of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-folded-iteration} with energy exponent~$t_0$. Fix a small radius of the form~$3^j/2$, with~$j$ an integer. Starting at~$x$, take~$\rho$ comparable to~$r$, namely the radius of the smallest ball around the current center that contains the target cube. Whenever a face lies within a fixed multiple of~$\rho$ and the domain has not yet been reflected across that face, reflect across it and enlarge~$\rho$ by a dimensional factor. At most one reflection is needed in each coordinate. We stop when~$\rho$ reaches a fixed small fraction of the chosen radius. Between consecutive reflections, apply the energy decay estimate for the reflected coefficient down to the current value of~$\rho$. The initial radius is the largest radius of the form~$3^j/2$ that is at most the chosen radius and at most a fixed small fraction of the distance to the nearest face across which the domain has not been reflected. Each application contributes the~$t_0$th power of the ratio of its final to its initial radius. The product of these factors is at most~$Cr^{t_0}$, where~$C$ also absorbs the bounded factors from the new reflections and the final step.

If, in the $i$th application of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-folded-iteration}, $B_i$ is the number of bad scales, $b_i$ the average of~$\b_{L,m}$ over the cube of the initial radius and~$R_i$ the initial radius, this gives
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r(x)\cap Q)
 \leq C e^{C\sum_iB_i}r^{t_0}
 \left[E_N(u_N)+\|f\|_\infty^2
       \sum_i b_i^{-1}R_i^{d+2-t_0}\right].
\end{equation*}
The source term follows from~\eqref{eq:mfd-primitive}.

\emph{Step 2.} For each~$N$, we find one random constant that serves all radii and centers. At depth~$n$, we run the construction of Step~1 from the points of a mesh of spacing proportional to~$3^{-n}$; every ball of radius comparable to~$3^{-n}$ lies in a target ball centered at a mesh point, once the radius of the latter is enlarged by a fixed factor. There are at most~$C(n+1)^{C_d}3^{dn}$ such constructions. The exponential tail~\eqref{e.cutoff.Holder.minimal.scale} and H\"older's inequality over the at most~$d+1$ numbers~$B_i$ of bad scales give uniformly bounded moments of~$e^{C\sum_iB_i}$ of any order larger than~$d/\eta$, after the moment order and then~$\gmcdelta$ are chosen. Summing over the mesh, as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}(ii), bounds all these factors by~$K_Nr^{-\eta}$, where~$K_N$ satisfies $\sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^p}<\infty$ for every order~$p$ in the list.

The averages~$b_i$ are treated in the same way: each is taken at the initial radius of its own application, and we sum over meshes of spacing proportional to that radius. Their positive and negative moments are bounded as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}. The same moment summation shows that, after the moment order and then~$\gmcdelta$ are chosen, each~$b_i^{-1}$ is at most~$K_N$ times a negative power of~$R_i$ whose exponent is smaller than~$d+2-t_0$. Since there are at most~$d+1$ applications, Step~1 gives~$\Gamma_N(u_N)(B_r\cap Q)\leq K_N\|f\|_\infty^2r^{t_1}$ for~$r\geq3^{-N}$.

\emph{Step 3.} We extend the estimates below the smallest field scale and deduce H\"older continuity. At radii below~$3^{-N}$, even reflection does not increase the Lipschitz constant of~$\log A_N$. Apply the Meyers estimate for coefficients with small oscillation~\eqref{eq:mfd-micro} at the radius~$\ell_N$ of Step~4 of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}, with~$p_1$ large enough that~$d-2d/p_1>t_1$ and~$1-d/p_1>\alpha$. Matching this bound at radius~$3^{-N}$ with the bound of Step~2 gives exactly the energy estimate of Step~4 of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}, with~$t_1$ as chosen here. The positive powers~$3^{-(t_1-t)N}$ and~$3^{-(d+2-t)N}$ in that estimate absorb the moments of the coefficient bounds in~\eqref{eq:mfd-6}. At radii above~$3^{-N}$ the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} for the reflected coefficient also bounds oscillations; at smaller radii Morrey's inequality applies. The powers of~$3^{-N}$ in the first two terms of Step~7 of that proposition are positive because~$t_1>d-2+2\alpha$. The same chaining proves the~$C^\alpha$ bound. Finally, remove the truncation of~$H$ as in Step~6 of that proposition, by comparing the weak equations on the space of mean-zero functions and applying Fatou's lemma.
\end{proof}

Applying Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} to the mean-zero source~$f_\epsilon$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-bump}, with~$\|f_\epsilon\|_\infty\leq C\epsilon^{-1}$, gives, with one random constant for all~$\epsilon$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-13}
 \Gamma_N(v_{N,\epsilon})(B_r(x)\cap Q)
 \leq K_N\epsilon^{-2}r^t.
\end{equation}

\subsection{Compactness of the finite-volume energies}
\label{mfd:sec-resampling}

We approximate each finite-volume energy by its conditional expectation given finitely many fields, and we bound the error by resampling one field at a time. These conditional expectations form a compact family in~$L^p(\P)$.

\smallskip

On a fixed cube~$Q$, write~$E_N=E_N^Q$. By a \emph{finite-volume energy}~$\mathcal R_N$ we mean either the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)$ with a nonconstant smooth boundary datum~$\varphi$, or the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of the Dirichlet Green operator for a nonzero smooth source~$f$. Write~$\mathcal R(g)$ for the same energy computed with the coefficient whose logarithm is~$g$, so that~$\mathcal R_N=\mathcal R(\log A_N)$.

\subsubsection{Local perturbations of the coefficient}

\begin{lemma}[Multiplicative perturbations]\label{mfd:cor-14}
Let~$A$ be bounded above and below by positive constants, let~$g$ be bounded and supported in~$B\subset Q$. Write~$E^g(v)=\int_Qe^gA|\nabla v|^2$ and~$E=E^0$. Let~$u$ and~$u^g$ be the minimizers for the coefficients~$A$ and~$e^gA$ with the same Dirichlet boundary values, or the solutions for these coefficients with the same linear right-hand side and zero Dirichlet or zero conormal boundary condition; in the conormal case, the right-hand side vanishes on constants and the solutions have mean zero. Denote their energies by~$\mathcal R,\mathcal R^g$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-14}
\begin{aligned}
 &E^g(u^g-u)\leq\|g\|_\infty^2e^{3\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B),\qquad
 \Gamma_{E^g}(u^g)(B)\leq4e^{\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B),\\
 &|\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R|\leq2\|g\|_\infty e^{4\|g\|_\infty}\Gamma_E(u)(B).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
For two log coefficients~$g,h$ and any of these energies,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-mult}
 e^{-\|g-h\|_\infty}\mathcal R(h)\leq\mathcal R(g)
 \leq e^{\|g-h\|_\infty}\mathcal R(h).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Set~$v=u^g-u$. Subtracting the Euler equations gives $E^g(v,\phi)=-\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\Gamma_E(u,\phi)$. Test with~$v$ and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-perturbation-sharp}
 E^g(v)^{\nf12}\leq
 (e^{\|g\|_\infty/2}-e^{-\|g\|_\infty/2})\Gamma_E(u)(B)^{\nf12}.
\end{equation}
This proves the first bound in~\eqref{eq:mfd-14}; the triangle inequality in~$L^2(B,e^gA\,dx)$ gives the second estimate. The Euler equations also give
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal R^g-\mathcal R
 =\pm\left[\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\Gamma_E(u)-E^g(v)\right],
\end{equation*}
with plus sign when~$\mathcal R$ is a minimal energy with given boundary values and minus sign when it is the energy of a solution with given right-hand side. Using~$|e^g-1|\leq\|g\|_\infty e^{\|g\|_\infty}$ proves the third bound. Monotonicity in the coefficient and scaling by constants give~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult}. These arguments use only the Euler equations and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality for energy measures, so they also apply to the regular strongly local forms constructed below.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Resampling one random field}

We bound the change in a finite-volume energy when a field~$\g_{-j}$ with~$j\geq0$ is replaced by an independent copy. The field has scale~$r=3^{-j}$ and range of dependence~$\sqrt d\,r$, so its restrictions to boxes at mutual distance larger than~$\sqrt d\,r$ are independent. We partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell$ with~$r\ll\ell\ll1$. Delete the field on strips of width of order~$r$ around the faces of the boxes, and apply the Efron--Stein inequality to the independent restrictions of the field to the remaining interiors of the boxes. The energy in the strips controls the error caused by deleting the field there. The largest energy in one box controls the differences~$X-X^{(i)}$ in the Efron--Stein inequality~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES} below. By the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}, both are bounded by a positive power of the scale~$r$. Exponential moments of the sup norm of the field enter through Lemma~\ref{mfd:cor-14}; they grow more slowly than any negative power of the scale~$r$, so this decay absorbs them.

\begin{lemma}[Mass near the box faces and in a single box]
\label{mfd:lem-strips}
Let~$t>d-1$ and~$K<\infty$, and let~$\nu$ be a finite positive measure on a fixed cube~$Q$ such that~$\nu(B_\rho(x)\cap Q)\leq K\rho^t$ for every~$x\in Q$ and~$0<\rho\leq1$. For~$4\sqrt d\,r<\ell\leq1$, partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell$, and call \textbf{strips} the points within distance~$4\sqrt d\,r$ of a box face. There is~$C(d,t,Q)<\infty$ such that
\begin{equation*}
 \nu(\text{strips})\leq CK\ell^{-1}r^{t-d+1},
 \qquad \max_{B\ \mathrm{box}}\nu(B)\leq CK\ell^t.
\end{equation*}
With~$\ell=r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$ and sufficiently small~$r$, both bounds are~$CKr^b$, where~$b=t(t-d+1)/(t+1)>0$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
There are at most~$C_Q\ell^{-1}$ face hyperplanes. Each strip is covered by~$C_Qr^{1-d}$ balls of radius~$4\sqrt d\,r$, and each box by a dimensional number of balls of radius~$\ell$. Apply the bound~$\nu(B_\rho(x)\cap Q)\leq K\rho^t$ and use~$\ell^{-1}r^{t-d+1}=\ell^t=r^b$ for the stated choice of~$\ell$.
\end{proof}

The sup norm on~$Q$ of a fine-scale field grows only logarithmically in the inverse of its scale, and its exponential moments grow more slowly than any power of the inverse scale. By~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, the variable~$\|\g_0\|_{L^\infty(\cu_0)}$ is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$. Let~$j\geq0$ and~$r=3^{-j}$, and let~$S=\|\g_{-j}\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$. The field~$\g_{-j}$ has the law of~$\g_0(3^j\,\cdot)$. Cover~$3^jQ$ by at most~$Cr^{-d}$ unit cubes to obtain~$\P[S>s]\leq2Cr^{-d}\exp(-s^2/C\gmcdelta^2)$. The union bound and~$(s+a)^2\geq s^2+a^2$ show that the excess of~$S$ over~$\gmcdelta\sqrt{C(1+|\log r|)}$ has the tail~$\exp(-s^2/C\gmcdelta^2)$. Thus the excess is sub-Gaussian with parameter~$C\gmcdelta$, uniformly in~$r$. Consequently, for all~$k\geq0$,~$\lambda\geq0$,~$p<\infty$ and~$\gamma>0$, and every~$0<\gmcdelta\leq1$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-layernorm}
 \|S^ke^{\lambda S}\|_{L^p}
 \leq C_{p,k,\lambda}\,\gmcdelta^{k}(1+|\log r|)^{\nf{k}{2}}
 \exp\bigl(C\lambda\gmcdelta\sqrt{1+|\log r|}+C_p\lambda^2\gmcdelta^2\bigr)
 \leq C_{p,k,\lambda,\gamma}\,\gmcdelta^{k}r^{-\gamma}\,.
\end{equation}
The splitting of~$S$ into a deterministic logarithmic part and a sub-Gaussian excess is needed: a union bound applied directly to the exponential moment would produce the factor~$Cr^{-d}$.

\smallskip

The fluctuations are estimated with the following moment form of the Efron--Stein inequality. Let~$\xi_1,\dots,\xi_m$ be independent random elements, let~$p\geq2$, and let~$X=X(\xi_1,\dots,\xi_m)\in L^p$ be measurable. Write~$X^{(i)}$ for the variable obtained by replacing~$\xi_i$ with an independent copy, and~$\E_i$ for the integral over this copy alone. Then
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-ES}
 \|X-\E X\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\Bigl\|\Bigl(\sum_i\E_i\bigl[(X-X^{(i)})^2\bigr]\Bigr)^{\nf12}\Bigr\|_{L^p}\,.
\end{equation}
This is a consequence of~\citet*[Theorem~2]{BBLM2005}, who proved two one-sided bounds in terms of variance proxies; both proxies are dominated by the sum on the right side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES}. The inequality also holds conditionally on every~$\sigma$-field independent of the random elements and their copies.

\begin{lemma}[Resampling one random field]
\label{mfd:lem-15}
Let~$0<\gmcdelta\leq1$,~$t\in(d-1,d)$ and~$p\geq2$, and let~$\mathcal R_N$ be a finite-volume energy. Assume that, for every~$N$, the function~$u_N$ defining~$\mathcal R_N$ (the minimizer with the given boundary values, or the solution with zero Dirichlet boundary values) satisfies~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} on~$Q$ with the constant~$K_N$, and that
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_N\|K_N\|_{L^{3p}}+\sup_N\|\mathcal R_N\|_{L^{3p}}<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
For~$0\leq j\leq N$, let~$\mathcal R_N^{(j)}$ be the finite-volume energy obtained after replacing the field~$\g_{-j}$ by an independent copy. Let~$b$ be the exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}. Then there exists~$C_p<\infty$, depending on~$p$,~$d$,~$t$,~$Q$, on the boundary datum or source defining~$\mathcal R_N$ and on the two suprema in the previous display, such that, for all integers~$0\leq j\leq N$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-15}
 \|\mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N^{(j)}\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\,\gmcdelta\,3^{-bj/8}\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
