Put~$r=3^{-j}$ and let~$S=\|\g_{-j}\|_\infty$ on~$Q$, as in~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}. A prime denotes the corresponding quantity after this field is resampled. Throughout, expectations over this field are conditional on the other fields. For the finitely many scales at which the strip construction of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips} does not apply, the bound $|\mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N'|\leq(S+S')e^{S+S'}\mathcal R_N$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm} suffice.

At the remaining scales, partition~$Q$ into boxes of side~$\ell=r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$. Choose smooth cutoff functions~$\chi_i$, one for each box, supported on interior subboxes that are separated by more than~$\sqrt d\,r$, such that~$\chi=\sum_i\chi_i$ equals one outside the strips of width~$4\sqrt d\,r$. Let~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ be the energy after deleting~$(1-\chi)\g_{-j}$. Let~$\widehat\nu$ be the energy measure of the corresponding solution. The restrictions of the field to these subboxes are independent. Lemmas~\ref{mfd:cor-14} and~\ref{mfd:lem-strips} give
\begin{equation*}
 |\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N|\leq CSe^{CS}K_Nr^b,
 \qquad \max_B\widehat\nu(B)\leq Ce^{CS}K_Nr^b,
 \qquad \widehat\nu(Q)\leq e^S\mathcal R_N,
\end{equation*}
where the maximum is over the interior subboxes~$B$. For the middle bound, apply~\eqref{eq:mfd-perturbation-sharp} on the strips and the inequality that $\widehat\nu(B)$ is at most $2e^S\Gamma_N(u_N)(B)$ plus twice the energy, for the form with the deleted field, of the difference between the minimizers before and after the deletion. The second term bounds the change in the minimizer when replacing~$\g_{-j}$ by~$\chi\g_{-j}$.

Resampling the field on an interior subbox~$B$ changes~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ by at most $C(S+S')e^{C(S+S')}\widehat\nu(B)$. Since $\sum_B\widehat\nu(B)^2\leq\max_B\widehat\nu(B)\widehat\nu(Q)$, the conditional Efron--Stein inequality~\eqref{eq:mfd-ES} and conditional Jensen give
\begin{equation*}
 \|\widehat{\mathcal R}_N-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N\|_p
 \leq C_pr^{\nf{b}{2}}
 \bigl\|(S+S')e^{C(S+S')}K_N^{\nf{1}{2}}\mathcal R_N^{\nf{1}{2}}\bigr\|_p
 \leq C_p\gmcdelta r^{\nf{3b}{8}}.
\end{equation*}
The last step uses H\"older's inequality with three factors and~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}, at a total loss of~$r^{-\nf{b}{8}}$. The errors~$\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$ and~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\mathcal R_N'$ from deleting the field on the strips are at most~$C_p\gmcdelta r^{\nf{7b}{8}}$ in~$L^p$. The energy~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'$, computed with the cutoff function applied to the resampled field, has the same conditional mean as~$\widehat{\mathcal R}_N$, so
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-fourterm}
 \mathcal R_N-\mathcal R_N'=(\mathcal R_N-\widehat{\mathcal R}_N)+(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N)
      -(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\E_j\widehat{\mathcal R}_N')+(\widehat{\mathcal R}_N'-\mathcal R_N').
\end{equation}
Since~$r^{\nf{3b}{8}}\leq3^{-bj/8}$, this proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-15}.
\end{proof}

The following martingale bound adds up the effects of the fields on which we do not condition.

\begin{lemma}[Summing resampling bounds]
\label{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}
Let $(\xi_j)_{j\in I}$ be a countable independent family and let $X\in L^p$, $1\leq p<\infty$, be measurable with respect to this family. Write $X^{(j)}$ for the result of replacing~$\xi_j$ by an independent copy. For every $J\subseteq I$,
\begin{equation*}
 \bigl\|X-\E[X\mid\sigma(\xi_j:j\in J)]\bigr\|_p
 \leq\sum_{j\notin J}\|X-X^{(j)}\|_p\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Condition first on the retained variables, and then reveal the omitted ones one at a time, in some enumeration. At the step that reveals~$\xi_j$, the martingale difference is the conditional expectation of~$X-X^{(j)}$ given the variables revealed so far. Conditional Jensen bounds its~$L^p$ norm by $\|X-X^{(j)}\|_p$. Sum and use~$L^p$ martingale convergence.
\end{proof}

We apply this bound to approximate an energy by conditioning on finitely many fields.

\begin{proposition}[Approximation by conditioning on finitely many fields]
\label{mfd:prop-16}
Let~$\ell\geq0$ be an integer, and let~$b$ be the exponent of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}. Let~$\mathcal R_N$ be a finite-volume energy satisfying the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} for every~$j\geq0$. Then there exists~$C_p<\infty$ such that, for every~$N\geq \ell$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-16}
 \bigl\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\bigr\|_{L^p}
 \leq C_p\,\gmcdelta\,3^{-b\ell/8}\,.
\end{equation}
If instead~$\mathcal R_N=|Q|\,e\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)e$ is the Neumann energy for the constant flux~$e$, a unit vector, assume~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-13} with moments of order~$3p$. Then~\eqref{eq:mfd-16} holds with right side~$C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}3^{-b\ell/256}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For~$j\geq0$,~\eqref{eq:mfd-15} bounds the resampling error by~$C_p\gmcdelta3^{-bj/8}$, and the error is zero for~$j>N$. For~$j<0$, the field~$H$ changes by the difference of the expressions~$\g_{-j}-\g_{-j}(0)$ for the field and for its independent copy. This difference has norm of size~$C\gmcdelta3^j$ and sub-Gaussian tails by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. Equation~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} bounds the resampling error by~$C_p\gmcdelta3^j$. Sum both geometric series using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}.

For the Neumann energy, first use the volume source~$f_\epsilon$. The proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} applies because Lemma~\ref{mfd:cor-14} also covers zero-conormal problems with a given right-hand side. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-13}, the constant in the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for the solution with source~$f_\epsilon$ is~$K_N\epsilon^{-2}$; this factor enters the error from deleting the field on the strips linearly and the Efron--Stein bound through its square root. Together with~\eqref{eq:mfd-9}, this gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_p
 \leq C_p\bigl(\epsilon^{\nf{1}{4}}
       +\gmcdelta\epsilon^{-2}3^{-b\ell/8}+\gmcdelta3^{-\ell}\bigr).
\end{equation*}
Choose~$\epsilon=3^{-\nf{b\ell}{32}}/16$ to get~$C_p3^{-\nf{b\ell}{128}}$. We also bound the left side by~$C_p\gmcdelta$. Remove the field~$H$. The bound~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrix.deviation.by.J} and Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} put the Neumann energy for the constant flux~$e$ within~$C_p\gmcdelta$ of~$1$ in~$L^p$. Restoring~$H$ costs another~$C_p\gmcdelta$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. Thus~$\|\mathcal R_N-\E[\mathcal R_N\mid\g_{-j},\ |j|\leq\ell]\|_p\leq2\|\mathcal R_N-1\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta$. The geometric mean of the two bounds proves the assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Compactness of conditional expectations}

\begin{proposition}[Strong compactness of the finite-volume energies]
\label{mfd:prop-response-compact}
Let~$Q$ be a cube, and let~$\mathcal R_N$ be the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(\varphi)$ with boundary values~$\varphi$, the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of the Dirichlet Green operator with source~$f$, or the Neumann energy~$|Q|\,e\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)e$ for a constant flux~$e\in\Rd$. Assume that~$\mathcal R_N$ satisfies the conclusion of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-16} and that~$\sup_N\|\mathcal R_N\|_{L^q}<\infty$ for some~$q>p$. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The sequence~$(\mathcal R_N)_N$ is relatively compact in~$L^p(\P)$.
\item The same holds for~$\langle f,G_N^Qg\rangle$ with two sources~$f$ and~$g$.
\item For any countable collection of cubes, sources, boundary data and fixed integer shifts~$N\mapsto N+j$ of the cutoff level~$N$, a common subsequence can be chosen along which all the corresponding energies converge.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix~$\ell$ and $N\geq \ell$. The log coefficient on~$\overline Q$ is the sum of the retained potential $\sum_{-\ell\leq j<0}(\g_{-j}-\g_{-j}(0))+\sum_{0\leq j\leq \ell}\g_{-j}$ and a remaining potential, which is independent of the retained fields. Consider the function of a potential~$v\in C(\overline Q)$ given by the expectation of the energy~$\mathcal R$ of the log coefficient~$v$ plus the remaining potential. By independence, the conditional expectation of~$\mathcal R_N$ given the retained fields is the value of this function at the retained potential. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult}, its values at two potentials~$v$ and~$w$ differ at most by the factor~$e^{\|v-w\|_\infty}$. Choose a radius such that the retained potential has sup norm at most this radius with positive probability. Integrate the comparison over the event that the retained potential has sup norm at most this radius to bound the value of the function at~$0$, uniformly in~$N$, by the product of~$\E\mathcal R_N$ and the exponential of this radius, divided by the probability of this event. The resulting functions are finite, uniformly bounded and equicontinuous on every bounded set.

A diagonal extraction on a countable dense subset of~$C(\overline Q)$ gives pointwise convergence on the whole space. Conditional Jensen gives a uniform~$L^q$ bound on the conditional expectations, so this convergence is in~$L^p(\P)$.

For any~$\varepsilon>0$, choose~$\ell$ so that the error in~\eqref{eq:mfd-16} is below~$\varepsilon/2$. A finite~$\varepsilon/2$-net of the conditional expectations is then an~$\varepsilon$-net of the energies with~$N\geq \ell$. This proves~(i). Polarization gives~(ii); a diagonal extraction gives~(iii).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Subsequential limit forms}
\label{mfd:sec-local-form}

We construct limit forms on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ along a subsequence and show that they are local, compatible under restriction to subcubes, and multiplied by a continuous weight when the coefficient is.

\smallskip

Let~$\mathcal C$ be the set of cubes with rational centers and triadic side lengths. It contains the cubes~$\cu_j$, $j\geq0$, and all their triadic subcubes. Fix $1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$, $d-1<t<d$ and $0<\eta<2\alpha-1$. Choose a countable~$\mathbb Q$-linear family~$\mathcal D\subset C_c^\infty(\Rd)$ such that, for each cube, the functions of~$\mathcal D$ supported in the cube are dense in~$L^2$ and in~$C_c^\infty$ on that cube. This also gives density in~$C^\alpha_c$ for the~$C^\beta$ norm. For each finite union of closed rational cubes contained in a finite union of open rational cubes, include in~$\mathcal D$ a smooth cutoff function, that is, a function with values in~$[0,1]$ equal to one near the first set and zero outside the second. Also include the smooth functions used to build the cutoff functions~$\chi_{N,r}$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii).

The \textbf{harmonic interpolant} of a function on a mesh is the function equal, on each cell, to the $A_N$-harmonic extension of the restriction of that function to the cell boundary.

We extract a subsequence jointly for the finite-volume energies for these cubes and data, the solutions, the environment and the random constants in~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5}. Include as single random variables each constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}, which is valid on all grid cubes at once, the constants of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}, which do not depend on the source, and $3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$, whose moments are bounded by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes}. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-response-compact} gives~$L^p$ convergence of the energies along a subsequence. The moment and H\"older bounds give tightness of all the remaining coordinates.

Apply Prokhorov's theorem and Skorokhod's representation theorem~\citep[Theorems~16.3 and~4.30]{Kallenberg} in the countable product of the Polish coordinate spaces. We obtain a further subsequence and a representation on a probability space where all coordinates converge almost surely. The energy limits retain their joint law with the environment and their measurability with respect to the random fields.

We work on the event of probability one on which all coordinates converge. The constants just included are bounded along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. The bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} hold for all meshes, and the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} hold for all sources and boundary data. Fix~$Q\in\mathcal C$ and abbreviate~$E_N=E_N^Q$,~$G_N=G_N^Q$.

\subsubsection{The Dirichlet Green operator and the limit form}

\begin{proposition}[Convergence of the Dirichlet Green operators and the limit form]
\label{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}
Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Norm convergence}}: the operators~$G_N$ converge in operator norm on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ to a compact, positive, self-adjoint, injective operator~$G$.
\item \underline{\emph{Limit form}}: the form
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-18}
 E(u)=\sup_{f\in L^2(Q)}\bigl\{2\langle f,u\rangle-\langle f,Gf\rangle\bigr\},
 \qquad D(E)=\{u:E(u)<\infty\}=\operatorname{Ran}G^{\nf12}\,,
\end{equation}
is a densely defined closed Dirichlet form on~$L^2(Q,dx)$.
\item \underline{\emph{Mosco convergence}}: if~$u_N\rightharpoonup u$ weakly in~$L^2(Q)$, then~$E(u)\leq\liminf E_N(u_N)$ (the liminf inequality). Every~$u\in D(E)$ has a \textbf{recovery sequence}, that is, a sequence~$u_N\in H^1_0(Q)$ with~$u_N\to u$ in~$L^2(Q)$ and~$E_N(u_N)\to E(u)$.
\item \underline{\emph{Density}}: the functions~$Gf$ with~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$ are dense in~$D(E)$ for the form norm.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We show that~$G_N$ converges in operator norm. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the energy identity,
\begin{equation*}
 \|G_Nf\|_{H^{\nf34}}^2\leq K_NE_N(G_Nf)=K_N\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\leq K_N\|f\|_2\|G_Nf\|_2\,,
\end{equation*}
so~$\|G_N\|_{L^2\to H^{\nf34}}\leq\sup_NK_N<\infty$ along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. By Rellich's theorem the images of the unit ball under all~$G_N$ lie in a common precompact subset of~$L^2$. Given~$\varepsilon>0$, choose a finite~$\varepsilon$-net for these images consisting of elements of~$\mathcal D$, and let~$P$ be the orthogonal projection onto its span. Then~$\|(I-P)G_N\|\leq\varepsilon$. By self-adjointness,~$\|G_N(I-P)\|=\|(I-P)G_N\|\leq\varepsilon$. Hence~$\|G_N-G_M\|\leq\|P(G_N-G_M)P\|+4\varepsilon$. The operator~$PG_NP$ is determined by the finite matrix of the bilinear form~$\langle\cdot,G_N\cdot\rangle$ on the elements of the net. Polarization expresses each entry as a real linear combination of quadratic forms~$\langle h,G_Nh\rangle$ with~$h\in\mathcal D$, which converge by the choice of the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Hence~$(G_N)$ is Cauchy in operator norm.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that~$G$ is injective. For~$w\in H^1_0(Q)$ and~$s\in\mathbb R$, the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} gives~$\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\geq2s\langle f,w\rangle-s^2E_N(w)$, and optimizing in~$s$ gives~$|\langle f,w\rangle|^2\leq\langle f,G_Nf\rangle E_N(w)$. Let~$Gf=0$ and~$\phi\in\mathcal D\cap C_c^\infty(Q)$, and let~$w_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant of~$\phi$ on the rational mesh of side~$r<\operatorname{dist}(\operatorname{supp}\phi,\partial Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} with~$\|\phi(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)/\mathbb R}\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$,
\begin{equation*}
 E_N(w_{N,r})=\sum_q\Lambda_{N,q}(\phi|_{\partial q})
 \leq Cr^{-d}\max_q\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\,r^{d-2}\,r^2\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty^2\,,
\end{equation*}
which is bounded along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation at fixed~$r$, since only finitely many cells of~$\mathcal C$ occur. The maximum principle on each cell gives~$\|w_{N,r}-\phi\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$. Hence
\begin{equation*}
 |\langle f,\phi\rangle|\leq|\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle|+Cr\|f\|_1\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
and the first term tends to zero as~$N\to\infty$, since~$\langle f,G_Nf\rangle\to\langle f,Gf\rangle=0$. Letting~$r\downarrow0$ gives~$f\perp C_c^\infty(Q)$, so~$f=0$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} The spectral theorem gives $E(u)=\|G^{-\nf12}u\|_2^2$ with domain~$\operatorname{Ran}G^{\nf12}$, a dense closed form domain. For every~$\lambda>0$, Step~1 gives
\begin{equation*}
 (G_N^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}=G_N(I+\lambda G_N)^{-1}
 \longrightarrow G(I+\lambda G)^{-1}=(G^{-1}+\lambda I)^{-1}
\end{equation*}
in operator norm. The resolvent criterion~\citep[Theorem~2.4]{KuwaeShioya2003} therefore gives Mosco convergence on~$L^2(Q,dx)$. Applying the liminf inequality to normal contractions of recovery sequences proves the Markov property of~$E$. Finally, $E(u,Gf)=\langle u,f\rangle$; if~$u$ is orthogonal in the form norm to~$G\mathcal D$, then $\langle(I+G)u,f\rangle=0$ for every~$f\in\mathcal D$, so~$u=0$. This proves~(ii)--(iv).
\end{proof}

Since~$\mathcal C$ contains countably many cubes, the proposition gives, along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, a limit form~$E^Q$ on every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$; we write~$E=E^Q$ when the cube is fixed.

\smallskip

The comparison arguments of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare} need the quadratic forms~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ to be bounded above and below with positive probability, uniformly in~$N$ and in the extracted limit. Both bounds follow from moment bounds.

\begin{lemma}[Positive and negative moments of the quadratic forms]
\label{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}
Fix a cube~$Q$ of side at most one and a nonzero function~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$. For every~$p<\infty$, if~$\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_N\E\bigl[\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^p+\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^{-p}\bigr]<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
The same bounds hold for every extracted limit, uniformly over the subsequence and over all values of~$\gmcdelta$ in a fixed, sufficiently small interval.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} gives~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle\leq K_N\|f\|_2^2$. For the negative moments, fix a mesh of side~$r$ and let~$w_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant of~$f$ on it, as in Step~2 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}. The maximum principle gives~$\|w_{N,r}-f\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla f\|_\infty$. Fix~$r$ so small that~$\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle\geq\nf{1}{2}\|f\|_2^2$ for every~$N$. The energy of~$w_{N,r}$ has a uniformly bounded~$p$th moment by~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}--\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on this fixed finite mesh. Optimize the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} over the multiples of~$w_{N,r}$ to obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle^{-1}\leq\frac{E_N^Q(w_{N,r})}{\langle f,w_{N,r}\rangle^2}
 \leq4\|f\|_2^{-4}E_N^Q(w_{N,r})\,.
\end{equation*}
Fatou's lemma passes both moment bounds to each extracted limit.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Traces and localization}

We will apply the following trace estimate to the energy measures of the harmonic interpolants of cutoff functions (Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}).

\begin{lemma}[Measure trace estimate]
\label{mfd:lem-19}
Let~$t>d-1$ and~$K<\infty$, and let~$\nu$ be a finite positive Borel measure on~$\Rd$ supported in~$\overline Q$ such that~$\nu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^t$ for every~$x\in\overline Q$ and~$0<r\leq1$. Then the map~$v\mapsto v$, from smooth functions on~$\overline Q$ to~$L^2(\nu)$, extends by completion to~$H^{\nf12}(Q)$, and there exists~$C(d,t,Q)<\infty$ such that, for every~$v\in H^{\nf12}(Q)$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-19}
 \int|v|^2\,d\nu\leq C(d,t,Q)\bigl(K+\nu(Q)\bigr)\|v\|_{H^{\nf12}(Q)}^2\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover, if~$v_N\to v$ in~$L^2(Q)$ and~$\sup_N\|v_N\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}<\infty$, then~$v_N\to v$ in~$H^{\nf12}(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Bessel kernel of order one is bounded by~$C|z|^{1-d}$ for~$|z|\leq1$ and is bounded outside the unit ball. The assumption~$\nu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^t$ and $t>d-1$ therefore give
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{x\in\operatorname{supp}\nu}\int\bigl(|x-y|^{1-d}\wedge1\bigr)\,d\nu(y)
 \leq C\left(\nu(\overline Q)+K\sum_{n\geq0}2^{-n(t-d+1)}\right)
 \leq C\bigl(K+\nu(\overline Q)\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
Regard the Bessel potential of order~$\nf12$ as a map from~$L^2(dx)$ to~$L^2(\nu)$. The composition of this map with its adjoint has the Bessel kernel of order one as its kernel. This kernel is bounded by the integrand above. By Schur's test, the squared norm of the map is at most $C(K+\nu(\overline Q))$. The Bessel-potential description of~$H^{\nf12}(\Rd)$ and a bounded extension operator from the cube give~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} for smooth~$v$.

The boundary of~$Q$ is~$\nu$-null: cover it with~$O(r^{1-d})$ balls. Hence~$\nu(\overline Q)=\nu(Q)$. Density of smooth functions in $H^{\nf12}(Q)$ gives the asserted completion, and convergence in~$L^2(dx)$ identifies it with the ordinary integral when~$\nu$ has a bounded density with respect to Lebesgue measure. Finally, the same extension operator and Sobolev interpolation give
\begin{equation*}
 \|w\|_{H^{\nf12}(Q)}
 \leq C\|w\|_{L^2(Q)}^{\nf13}\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^{\nf23}\,.
\end{equation*}
Weak compactness first puts~$v$ in~$H^{\nf34}(Q)$; applying this inequality to~$w=v_N-v$ proves the last assertion.
\end{proof}

Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, recovery sequences and solutions with zero Dirichlet boundary values are bounded in~$H^{\nf34}$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}, and the energy measures of the cutoff functions of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs} below satisfy~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} with a common constant. Thus~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} controls~$\int(v_N-v)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$ uniformly in~$N$. We use this estimate when localizing recovery sequences in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality}.

\subsubsection{Regularity, locality, and restriction to subcubes}

To obtain energy measures for~$E$, we show that~$E$ is regular and strongly local. Both properties are proved by multiplying recovery sequences by cutoff functions. Smooth cutoff functions are not suitable, since~$A_N$ has no upper bound uniform in~$N$; we use instead the harmonic interpolants of cutoff functions, whose energies, constants in the Morrey bounds and H\"older norms are controlled cell by cell by~\eqref{eq:mfd-2}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5}.

\begin{lemma}[Harmonic cutoff functions]
\label{mfd:lem-cutoffs}
Let~$K\subset O$ be finite unions of closed and open rational cubes, respectively, with~$K$ compact and~$\overline O\subset Q$, and let~$\chi_N$ be the harmonic interpolant, on a mesh of~$\mathcal C$, of a smooth cutoff function from~$\mathcal D$, with values in~$[0,1]$, equal to one near~$K$ and compactly supported in~$O$. The mesh is so fine that every cell meeting the set where this function lies strictly between~$0$ and~$1$ is contained in~$O\setminus K$. Then the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The function~$\chi_N$ belongs to~$H^1_0(Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$, satisfies~$0\leq\chi_N\leq1$, equals one on a neighbourhood of~$K$ and zero outside~$O$, and~$\nabla\chi_N=0$ on every cell on which this function is constant.
\item Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the energy~$E_N(\chi_N)$, the constant in the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for~$\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$ at all radii, and the~$C^\alpha$ norm of~$\chi_N$ are bounded. In particular, along a subsequence,~$\chi_N$ converges uniformly to a continuous function~$\chi$ that agrees with the original cutoff function on every cell where that function is constant.
\item Let~$r>0$, and let~$\chi_{N,r}$ be the harmonic interpolant on the mesh of side~$r$ of a smooth function~$\Rd\to[0,1]$ that vanishes within distance~$r$ of~$\partial Q$, equals one at distance at least~$3r$ from~$\partial Q$ and has gradient at most~$C/r$ on~$\Rd$. Let~$K_N$ be the constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. Then~$0\leq\chi_{N,r}\leq1$ and
\begin{equation*}
 E_N(\chi_{N,r})\leq C\bigl(K_N+3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr)r^{-1-\eta}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On a cell on which the cutoff function is constant the harmonic extension is that constant, by uniqueness, and the maximum principle gives~$0\leq\chi_N\leq1$. Continuity across faces follows from the matching traces, and continuity up to the boundary from the H\"older bound for the cell solutions. At a fixed mesh there are finitely many cells, each with bounded~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)$ and bounded constants in the Morrey and H\"older bounds along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Sum over the cells to obtain~(ii). The Arzel\`a--Ascoli theorem gives uniform convergence.

For~(iii), the maximum principle gives~$0\leq\chi_{N,r}\leq1$ as in~(i). There are~$O(r^{-(d-1)})$ cells on which the function defining~$\chi_{N,r}$ is not constant. On each such cell the rescaled function has~$C^\beta$ norm at most~$C$. If~$r\geq3^{-N}$, then~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} bound the energy on each such cell by~$C\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)r^{d-2}\leq CK_Nr^{d-2-\eta}$. If~$r<3^{-N}$, the smooth function defining~$\chi_{N,r}$ is itself a competitor in the minimization on each cell, which gives
\begin{equation*}
 E_N(\chi_{N,r})\leq C\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}r^{-1}
 \leq C\bigl(3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}\bigr)r^{-1-\eta}\,.
\end{equation*}
Both the factor~$3^{-\eta N}\|A_N\|_{L^\infty(Q)}$ and the constant of~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} were included in the joint extraction, and their sum bounds the energy for all widths~$r$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Regularity]
\label{mfd:prop-regularity}
The Dirichlet form~$E$ of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse} is regular.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For~$f\in\mathcal D$, the H\"older bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} gives $u_N=G_Nf\to Gf$ uniformly on~$\overline Q$, with zero boundary values. Let~$\chi_{N,r}$ be the cutoff function of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii). Covering the boundary layer $\{x\in Q:\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial Q)<3r\}$ by $O(r^{1-d})$ balls and using~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}--\eqref{eq:mfd-5} gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-20}
 E_N(u_N(1-\chi_{N,r}))
 \leq 2\Gamma_N(u_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\partial Q)<3r\})
       +2\sup_{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\partial Q)<3r}|u_N|^2E_N(\chi_{N,r})
 \leq C_{\omega,f}(r^{t-d+1}+r^{2\alpha-1-\eta}).
\end{equation}
At fixed~$r$, pass to a subsequence along which~$\chi_{N,r}\to\chi_r$ uniformly. The liminf inequality shows that~$Gf\chi_r\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ converges to~$Gf$ in the form norm as~$r\downarrow0$. Since the functions~$Gf$ are dense in~$D(E)$ by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}(iv), $D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ is dense in~$D(E)$ for the form norm.

For density in the uniform norm, take~$\phi\in\mathcal D\cap C_c^\infty(Q)$ and its harmonic interpolants on a mesh of side~$r$. Their bounded energies and H\"older norms give a uniform subsequential limit~$w_r\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$. The cell maximum principle gives $\|w_r-\phi\|_\infty\leq Cr\|\nabla\phi\|_\infty$. Since these~$\phi$ are dense in~$C_0(Q)$, the core is uniformly dense as well.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Strong locality on the core]
\label{mfd:prop-locality}
Let~$u,v\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$, and suppose that~$u$ equals a constant~$c$ on a neighbourhood of~$\operatorname{supp}v$. Then~$E(u,v)=0$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Choose two cutoff functions from~$\mathcal D$ and let~$\psi_N,\chi_N$ be their harmonic interpolants from Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}, with~$\psi_N=1$ near~$\operatorname{supp}v$, $\chi_N=1$ near~$\operatorname{supp}\psi_N$, and~$\operatorname{supp}\chi_N\subset\{u=c\}^\circ$. For recovery sequences~$u_N,v_N$ of~$u$ and~$v$, consider the sequences~$c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N$ and~$\psi_Nv_N$.
These are still recovery sequences. Indeed, they converge in~$L^2$ to~$u$ and~$v$, and the only extra gradient terms are controlled by
\begin{equation*}
 \int(u_N-c)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_N)
 +\int v_N^2\,d\Gamma_N(\psi_N)\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation*}
On the support of~$\nabla\chi_N$ we have~$u=c$, and on that of~$\nabla\psi_N$ we have~$v=0$. Apply the trace estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} together with the~$H^{\nf12}$ convergence of the recovery sequences. Young's inequality gives~$\limsup E_N(c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N)\leq(1+\varepsilon)E(u)$ for every~$\varepsilon>0$, and likewise for~$\psi_Nv_N$. The liminf inequality gives equality. Since~$c\chi_N+(1-\chi_N)u_N=c$ near~$\operatorname{supp}\psi_N$, the form~$E_N$ of the two sequences vanishes. Applying the liminf inequality to their sum and difference yields $E(u\pm v)\leq E(u)+E(v)$, hence~$E(u,v)=0$.
\end{proof}

By regularity and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality}, uniqueness in the Beurling--Deny representation~\citep[Section~3.2]{FOT} makes the jump and killing measures vanish. Thus~$E$ is strongly local, and its energy measures~$\Gamma_E(u,v)$ satisfy the chain rule, Leibniz rule, Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and locality on open sets~\citep[Theorem~3.2.2 and Lemma~3.2.5]{FOT}.

We normalize the energy measure by~$\int\phi\,d\Gamma_E(u)=E(u,u\phi)-\nf{1}{2}E(u^2,\phi)$ for bounded~$u\in D(E)$ and~$\phi\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$, so that the corresponding measure at finite~$N$ is~$\Gamma_N(u)=A_N|\nabla u|^2\,dx$, and~$\Gamma_E(u)(Q)=E(u)$ and~$\Gamma_E(u,v)(Q)=E(u,v)$. The measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ is supported in~$\operatorname{supp}u$.

\smallskip

\begin{proposition}[Consistency under restriction to subcubes]
\label{mfd:prop-killed-consistency}
Let~$q\subset Q$ be an open cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$E^q$ and~$E^Q$ be the limit forms on~$q$ and~$Q$ along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Then extension by zero to~$Q$ sends~$D(E^q)$ onto the~$E^Q$-closure of~$\{w\in D(E^Q)\cap C_c(Q):\operatorname{supp}w\subset q\}$, and~$E^q(u)$ equals the~$E^Q$-energy of the extension of~$u$ by zero, for every~$u\in D(E^q)$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Zero extension of a recovery sequence on~$q$ shows that the~$E^Q$-energy of the extension of~$u$ by zero is at most~$E^q(u)$. Conversely, fix~$w\in D(E^Q)\cap C_c(q)$. Localize a recovery sequence on~$Q$ by a harmonic cutoff function equal to one near~$\operatorname{supp}w$ and supported in~$q$. The trace argument in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-locality} shows that this is still a recovery sequence. The liminf inequality on~$q$ gives~$E^q(w|_q)\leq E^Q(w)$. Hence equality holds on this core. Regularity of~$E^q$ and the first inequality extend equality to all of~$D(E^q)$. The resulting isometry has closed image, and this image is exactly the stated closure.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Continuous weights and minimal energies with given boundary values}

For~$\varrho\in C(\overline Q)$ with~$\varrho>0$, let~$E_N^{\varrho}(u)=\int_Q\varrho\,d\Gamma_N(u)$ for~$u\in H^1_0(Q)$, and let~$G_N^{\varrho}$ be its Dirichlet Green operator.

\begin{lemma}[Form order implies measure order]
\label{mfd:lem-sincos}
Let~$E,F$ be regular strongly local Dirichlet forms on~$L^2(Q,dx)$ with~$D(E)=D(F)$, let~$q\subset Q$ be open, and let~$C<\infty$ be such that~$F(w)\leq CE(w)$ for every~$w\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ with~$\operatorname{supp}w\subset q$. Then~$\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\Gamma_E(u)$ on~$q$ for every~$u\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let~$v\in D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ with~$\operatorname{supp}v\subset q$, and let~$k\geq1$. The functions~$k^{-1}v\sin(ku)$ and~$k^{-1}v\cos(ku)=k^{-1}v(\cos(ku)-1)+k^{-1}v$ belong to~$D(E)\cap C_c(Q)$ and they are supported in~$\operatorname{supp}v$. For either form, the chain and Leibniz rules show that the cross terms in the energy measures of these two functions cancel in the sum, so that
\begin{equation*}
 E(k^{-1}v\sin ku)+E(k^{-1}v\cos ku)=\int v^2\,d\Gamma_E(u)+k^{-2}E(v)\,,
\end{equation*}
and likewise for~$F$. Applying~$F\leq CE$ to both functions and letting~$k\to\infty$ gives~$\int v^2\,d\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\int v^2\,d\Gamma_E(u)$. For~$0\leq\phi\in C_c(q)$, choose by regularity a core function with values in~$[0,1]$ that equals one on~$\operatorname{supp}\phi$ and is supported in~$q$. Also choose core functions converging uniformly to~$\phi^{\nf12}$. Then the products of the latter with the former are admissible and their squares converge uniformly to~$\phi$. Hence~$\int\phi\,d\Gamma_F(u)\leq C\int\phi\,d\Gamma_E(u)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Continuous weights]
\label{mfd:prop-21}
Let~$\varrho\in C(\overline Q)$ be positive. Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the forms~$E_N^\varrho$ Mosco-converge to a form~$E^\varrho$ such that~$D(E^\varrho)=D(E)$ and
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-21}
 E^{\varrho}(u,v)=\int_Q\varrho\,d\Gamma_E(u,v),\qquad u,v\in D(E)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The bounds $(\inf\varrho)E_N\leq E_N^\varrho\leq(\sup\varrho)E_N$ show that the forms~$E_N^\varrho$ satisfy the $H^{\nf34}$ coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} up to a constant factor, and that the order reverses for their Green operators:
\begin{equation*}
 (\sup\varrho)^{-1}G_N\leq G_N^\varrho\leq(\inf\varrho)^{-1}G_N.
\end{equation*}
By the finite-rank compactness argument of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}, every subsequence therefore has a further subsequence along which~$G_N^\varrho$ converges in norm on all cubes of~$\mathcal C$. The reversed order makes each limit injective. The same proposition constructs their Mosco limit~$F$, with~$D(F)=D(E)$ and equivalent form norms. The two forms therefore have the same regular core. The proofs of locality and consistency under restriction to smaller cubes also apply: recovery sequences retain their~$H^{\nf{3}{4}}$ bound, and the energy measures of the cutoff functions are at most~$(\sup\varrho)\Gamma_N(\chi_N)$. Thus~$F$ is strongly local and its restrictions to smaller cubes satisfy
\begin{equation*}
 (\inf_q\varrho)E^q\leq F^q\leq(\sup_q\varrho)E^q
 \qquad(q\in\mathcal C).
\end{equation*}
By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos}, the same comparison holds for energy measures on~$q$. For a core function~$u$, take a finite partition of unity near~$\operatorname{supp}u$ subordinate to cubes on which~$\operatorname{osc}\varrho\leq\varepsilon$. Integrate against this partition of unity to obtain~$|F(u)-\int\varrho\,d\Gamma_E(u)|\leq\varepsilon E(u)$. Let~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$, extend by density in the form norm and polarize. Every subsequential limit form is therefore the form in~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}, which proves convergence of the whole sequence in the Skorokhod representation.
\end{proof}

We also obtain convergence in probability of the weighted Dirichlet Green operators along the subsequence extracted above. Indeed, given a subsequence, we represent the weight, the original energy limit and the preceding coordinates jointly on one probability space. The comparison~\eqref{eq:mfd-mult} absorbs the error in the uniform approximation of the weight. The proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21} identifies every limit with~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}. The subsequence criterion for convergence in probability then gives the assertion, also for random positive continuous weights and for countable families of them.

\begin{lemma}[Bounded weights and deletion of a field]
\label{mfd:lem-borel-weights}
Let~$g$ be a bounded Borel function on~$Q$. Then~$E^g(u,v)=\int e^g\,d\Gamma_E(u,v)$ defines a regular strongly local Dirichlet form with domain~$D(E)$, and~$\Gamma_{E^g}(u,v)=e^g\,\Gamma_E(u,v)$. Moreover, for every field~$\g_{-j}$ with~$j\geq0$, the form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$, given by~$(u,v)\mapsto\int e^{-\g_{-j}}\,d\Gamma_E(u,v)$, is measurable with respect to the remaining fields.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The bounds~$e^{-\|g\|_\infty}E\leq E^g\leq e^{\|g\|_\infty}E$ show that~$E^g$ is closed on~$D(E)$ and has the same core as~$E$. The chain rule for energy measures gives the Markov property, and the locality of energy measures gives strong locality. The defining identity of the energy measure and the Leibniz rule give the formula for~$\Gamma_{E^g}$.

For the deletion, observe that~$e^{-\g_{-j}}$ is continuous and bounded above and below on each fixed cube. The coefficient~$e^{-\g_{-j}}A_N$ is independent of the deleted field for all sufficiently large~$N$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21}, applied to random weights as explained after its proof, identifies the limit of the Dirichlet Green operators of~$e^{-\g_{-j}}A_N$ with the Dirichlet Green operator of~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$. Hence each quadratic form~$\langle f,G^Qf\rangle$ of this Dirichlet Green operator, with~$f\in\mathcal D$ and~$Q\in\mathcal C$, is a limit in probability of functions of the remaining fields. It is therefore measurable with respect to the remaining fields. These quadratic forms determine the form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$.
\end{proof}

Therefore, conditionally on the remaining fields, the forms corresponding to coefficients in which the deleted field is multiplied by the cutoff functions of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15} can be defined by weighting the fixed form~$e^{-\g_{-j}}E$ with the independent restrictions of the deleted field. The first part of the lemma allows these weights to be discontinuous.

\smallskip

\begin{corollary}[Energy-measure convergence]
\label{mfd:cor-energy-measures}
Along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, the following statements hold, with weak convergence of finite measures on~$\overline Q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item If~$u_N\in H^1_0(Q)$ is a recovery sequence for~$u\in D(E)$, then~$\Gamma_N(u_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(u)$.
\item If~$u_N\to u$ in~$L^2$ and~$\sup_NE_N(u_N)<\infty$, then every weak subsequential limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N)$ is at least~$\Gamma_E(u)$.
\item If~$(u_N)$ is as in~(b) and~$(v_N)$ is a recovery sequence for~$v\in D(E)$, then~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(u,v)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Let~$0\leq\phi\in C(\overline Q)$. For a recovery sequence, the liminf inequalities for the weighted forms with weights~$1\pm\varepsilon\phi$, together with~$E_N(u_N)\to E(u)$, give both inequalities needed for~(a). For~(b), apply the liminf inequality for the weight~$\phi+\varepsilon$, and let~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$ along a subsequence on which the measures converge weakly.

For~(c), Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the total variations of the mutual energy measures. Pass to a joint subsequential limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N)$ and~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)$. Apply~(b) to~$u_N+sv_N$ and~(a) to~$v_N$. For every rational~$s$, this shows that the measure
\[
 \lim_N\Gamma_N(u_N)-\Gamma_E(u)
 +2s\bigl(\lim_N\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)-\Gamma_E(u,v)\bigr)
\]
is nonnegative. Testing against~$0\leq\phi\in C(\overline Q)$ and letting~$s\to\pm\infty$ identifies the limit of~$\Gamma_N(u_N,v_N)$ with~$\Gamma_E(u,v)$. Every subsequential limit is therefore the same.
\end{proof}

For the regular strongly local form~$E$, the one-dimensional energy-image-density theorem and the absolutely continuous chain rule~\citep[Proposition~1.16 and its proof]{Fitzsimmons2004} give $\Gamma_E(v)(\{v=c\})=0$ and $\Gamma_E(T(v))=T'(v)^2\Gamma_E(v)$ for Lipschitz~$T$ with~$T(0)=0$, using quasi-continuous representatives. The reference normalizes the energy measure as~$2\Gamma_E$, which does not affect either assertion.

\begin{lemma}[Zero-trace differences lie in~$D(E^q)$]
\label{mfd:lem-truncation}
Let~$q\subset Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$v\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ vanish on~$\partial q$. Then~$v_q=v\mathbf 1_{\overline q}$ belongs to the space~$D(E^q)\subset D(E^Q)$ of functions extended by zero, and~$E^Q(v_q)=\Gamma_E(v)(q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since~$v=0$ on~$\partial q$, for every~$\epsilon>0$ the set~$\{|v|\geq\epsilon\}\cap\overline q$ is compact in~$q$. Truncate~$v_q$ at height~$\epsilon$, that is, replace it by~$\operatorname{sgn}(v_q)(|v_q|-\epsilon)_+$, and multiply by a core cutoff function equal to one near this set and supported in~$q$. The result belongs to~$D(E^q)$ by consistency under restriction to smaller cubes. The chain rule and locality show that its energy measure is the restriction of~$\Gamma_E(v)$ to~$q\cap\{|v|>\epsilon\}$. The energy of the difference of the truncations at two heights is the mass of~$\Gamma_E(v)$ on the part of~$q$ where~$|v|$ lies between these heights. Since the level sets of~$v$ carry no energy, the truncations are Cauchy in the form norm and converge uniformly to~$v_q$. Since~$E^q$ is closed, $v_q\in D(E^q)$. Letting~$\epsilon\downarrow0$ in the first identity gives~$E(v_q)=\Gamma_E(v)(q)$, with~$\Gamma_E(v_q)(\partial q)=0$.
\end{proof}

We now identify the limits of the minimal energies~$\Lambda_{N,q}$ with given boundary values on cubes of~$\mathcal C$. The candidate is the energy in~$q$ of the limit~$U$ of the harmonic interpolants of a fixed smooth datum on the~$3^d$ cells around~$q$.

\begin{proposition}[Limits of the minimal energies with given boundary values]
\label{mfd:prop-boundary}
Let~$q=z+r\cu_0\in\mathcal C$, let~$Q=z+3r\cu_0$ be the cube formed by the~$3^d$ congruent cells around~$q$, let~$b$ be a boundary datum on~$\partial q$ from~$\mathcal D$, and fix a smooth extension of~$b$ to a function with compact support in~$Q$. Let~$U_N$ be the function equal, on each of the~$3^d$ cells, to the~$A_N$-harmonic extension of the restriction of this extension to the cell boundary. Then, along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation,~$U_N$ converges uniformly on~$\overline Q$ to a function~$U$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Boundary values}}: the function~$U$ belongs to~$D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$, and~$U=b$ on~$\partial q$.
\item \underline{\emph{No energy on the faces}}: the energy measure of~$U$ satisfies~$\Gamma_E(U)(\partial q)=0$.
\item \underline{\emph{Harmonicity}}: for every~$\phi\in D(E^q)$, extended by zero to~$Q$, we have~$E^Q(U,\phi)=0$.
\item \underline{\emph{Minimality}}: the minimal energies converge, and
\begin{equation*}
 \lim_N\Lambda_{N,q}(b)=\Gamma_E(U)(q)
 =\min\bigl\{\Gamma_E(V)(q):V\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q),\ V=b\text{ on }\partial q\bigr\}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The energy, H\"older norm and constant in the Morrey bound for~$U_N$ are bounded by summing the corresponding cell bounds. Hence every subsequence has a further subsequence with~$U_N\to U$ uniformly, where~$U\in D(E^Q)$ and~$U$ equals the fixed extension of~$b$ on every cell boundary. We show that~$(U_N)$ is a recovery sequence for~$U$.

Let~$W_N$ be a recovery sequence for~$U$, let~$\Sigma$ be the union of the cell boundaries, and take on each cell the cutoff function of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}(iii) with~$r=\rho$. Their sum~$\chi_{N,\rho}$ vanishes near~$\Sigma$ and equals one away from its~$3\rho$-neighborhood. Use~$\chi_{N,\rho}W_N+(1-\chi_{N,\rho})U_N$, which has the same cell-boundary values as~$U_N$, as a competitor. Cellwise minimality and convexity give, for~$\varepsilon>0$,
\begin{align*}
 E_N(U_N)
 &\leq(1+\varepsilon)\bigl[E_N(W_N)
       +\Gamma_N(U_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\Sigma)<3\rho\})\bigr]\\
 &\quad+(1+\varepsilon^{-1})
       \int(W_N-U_N)^2\,d\Gamma_N(\chi_{N,\rho}).
\end{align*}
At fixed~$\rho$, the last term tends to zero by~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} and convergence in~$H^{\nf12}$. The term $\Gamma_N(U_N)(\{\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\Sigma)<3\rho\})$ is at most~$C\rho^{t-d+1}$ by the Morrey bound at every radius and a covering of the finitely many faces. Let first~$N\to\infty$, then~$\rho\downarrow0$ and~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$. The liminf inequality gives~$E_N(U_N)\to E(U)$.

Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}(a) now gives~$\Gamma_N(U_N)\rightharpoonup\Gamma_E(U)$. The common Morrey bound passes to the limit. Hence~$\Gamma_E(U)(\Sigma)=0$ and $\Lambda_{N,q}(b)=\Gamma_N(U_N)(q)\to\Gamma_E(U)(q)$. For~$\phi\in D(E^q)$, a recovery sequence on~$q$, extended by zero, is also a recovery sequence on~$Q$ by consistency under restriction to smaller cubes. The identity~$E_N(U_N,\phi_N)=0$ at finite~$N$ passes to~$E(U,\phi)=0$ by Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}(c).

If~$V=b$ on~$\partial q$, then by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation} the function $w=(V-U)\mathbf1_{\overline q}$ lies in~$D(E^q)$. Its energy measure does not charge~$\partial q$. Harmonicity therefore yields $\Gamma_E(V)(q)=\Gamma_E(U)(q)+E(w)\geq\Gamma_E(U)(q)$. The same argument on each cell shows that the difference of two possible limits has zero energy on every cell. Since the energy measures do not charge the faces, coercivity forces this difference to vanish on~$Q$. Thus the uniform convergence and all the conclusions hold along the full sequence in the Skorokhod representation. The argument applies to every fixed finite partition into cubes with a common smooth datum vanishing on the outer boundary.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[H\"older boundary data, gluing, harmonic replacement]
\label{mfd:prop-gluing}
Let~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$ and set~$\sigma=\nf12(\beta-\nf12)$. Then the following statements hold along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Let~$q=z+r\cu_0$ and~$Q=z+3r\cu_0$ be as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}. For every~$b\in C^\alpha(\partial q)$, the limit~$\Lambda_{E,q}(b)=\lim_N\Lambda_{N,q}(b)$ exists and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{E,q}(b)\leq C\bigl(\sup_N\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)\bigr)r^{d-2}\|b(z+r\,\cdot)\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
and all conclusions of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} hold for~$b$, with a harmonic extension~$U\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$.
\item Let~$Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$, and let~$q_1,\dots,q_m\in\mathcal C$ be disjoint cells such that the closure of each~$q_i$ and the concentric cube of three times its side length lie in~$Q$. Let~$V\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ satisfy~$V|_{\partial q_i}\in C^\alpha$ for every~$i$. Let~$V'$ equal~$V$ outside~$\bigcup_iq_i$ and equal, on each~$q_i$, the harmonic extension~$U_i$ of~$V|_{\partial q_i}$. Then~$V'\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ and
\begin{equation*}
 E^Q(V')=E^Q(V)-\sum_i\bigl[\Gamma_E(V)(q_i)-\Lambda_{E,q_i}(V|_{\partial q_i})\bigr]\leq E^Q(V)\,.
\end{equation*}
\item If moreover~$\Gamma_E(V)$ charges no face~$\partial q_i$ (for instance, if~$V$ is itself a limit of functions that, at finite~$N$, are harmonic in each cell for the cutoff coefficient and share a common smooth datum on the union of the cell boundaries), and the cells~$q_i$ partition a cube~$Q'\subset Q$, then~$\Gamma_E(V')(Q')=\sum_i\Lambda_{E,q_i}(V|_{\partial q_i})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
(i) Extend~$b$ to a function in~$C_c^\alpha(Q)$ and approximate it in~$C^\beta$ by functions in~$\mathcal D$. On the fixed finite partition, apply the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary data and the maximum principle to two approximations. The bounds show that the energy~$E_N$ of the difference of the corresponding interpolants is at most~$C$ times the squared~$C^\beta$ norm of the difference of the approximations. The sup norm of the difference of the interpolants is at most the sup norm of the difference of the approximations. The same bounds hold for the limits by the liminf inequality. Hence the corresponding harmonic extensions converge in energy and uniformly to a limit~$U$ with boundary values~$b$. Harmonicity passes to the limit by energy convergence. The energy on the faces also remains zero, since energy measures depend continuously in total variation on the function in the form norm. Minimality follows again from harmonicity and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation}. Since~$\sqrt{\Lambda_{N,q}}$ is a seminorm of the datum, its reverse triangle inequality and~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} transfer the convergence of~$\Lambda_{N,q}$ and the asserted bound from the approximations to~$b$. The argument holds for each realization and covers random boundary data.

(ii) Consistency under restriction to subcubes extends each~$U_i$ to~$D(E^Q)$, and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-truncation} puts~$(U_i-V)\mathbf1_{\overline{q_i}}$ in~$D(E^{q_i})$ with zero energy on~$\partial q_i$. Adding these differences to~$V$ shows that~$V'$ belongs to the form domain. Locality identifies its energy measure with~$\Gamma_E(U_i)$ inside~$q_i$ and with~$\Gamma_E(V)$ elsewhere, including the faces by Cauchy--Schwarz. Integrating gives the identity. Cell minimality gives the inequality.

(iii) The same identification and the assumption that~$\Gamma_E(V)$ charges no face give the sum over the partition. For a common smooth datum, the energy measure of the limit does not charge the faces by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}; for a common~$C^\alpha$ datum, this follows from the approximation in energy in~(i).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Finite partitions with prescribed boundary values]
\label{mfd:lem-skeleton}
Let~$\alpha>\nf12$, let the cubes~$q_i$ form a finite partition of a cube~$Q$, and let~$b\in C^\alpha(\overline Q)$ vanish on~$\partial Q$. Let~$v$ be the function equal, on each cell~$q_i$, to the limiting harmonic extension of~$b|_{\partial q_i}$. Then~$v\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ and
\begin{equation*}
 E^Q(v)\leq\sum_i\Lambda_{E,q_i}(b|_{\partial q_i})
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 \|v-b\|_\infty\leq\sup_i\operatorname{osc}_{q_i}b\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For smooth data compactly supported in~$Q$, apply the last paragraph of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} to this finite partition. The harmonic interpolants converge uniformly and in energy, and their cell energies add. For general~$b$, choose~$1/2<\beta<\min\{\alpha,1\}$. The zero extension of~$b$ can be approximated in~$C^\beta$ by~$C_c^\infty(Q)$ functions. The bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary data and the maximum principle, as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-gluing}(i), give convergence in energy and uniformly. This proves the energy bound, also for cells touching~$\partial Q$. The cell maximum principle gives~$\|v-b\|_\infty\leq\sup_i\operatorname{osc}_{q_i}b$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Estimates along chains of nested cubes}
\label{mfd:sec-branches}

The energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ depends on the same fields as the regularity, extension and harmonic-approximation estimates of Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic}. To control the energy in the cells where these estimates fail, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} bounds, on a single event that does not depend on~$u$, the number of bad cells, defined below, along every chain of nested cubes. This bound can then be integrated against the energy measure.

Let~$E$ be a subsequential limit along the subsequence of cutoffs of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, realized on the Skorokhod probability space.

\subsubsection{Continuous versions of the conditions}

Fix~$s\in(0,1]$. Let $\mathsf F_{m,z}$, $\mathsf P_{m,z}$ and $\mathsf R_{m,z}$ be the left sides of~\eqref{e.good.gj},~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} and~\eqref{e.good.sum.J}, translated to~$z$. For~$\gmcdelta=0$, they equal~$0$, $2$ and~$0$, respectively. We use the quantity~$\mathbf G_{m,z}(s)$ of~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq}, suppressing its fixed argument~$s$, and the quantity~$\mathcal J_n(P)$ of~\eqref{e.normalized.J.maximum.definition}.

To measure failure of the bounds at center~$z$ and scale~$m$, apply continuous piecewise linear functions to~$\mathsf F_{m,z}$,~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ and~$\mathsf R_{m,z}$, and let~$Z_{m,z}$ be their sum. Each function increases linearly from zero to one between its lower and upper thresholds, is zero below the lower threshold and one above the upper threshold, and lies between the indicators of exceeding these thresholds. The threshold pairs are~$(\epsilon/2,\epsilon)$,~$(6,12)$ and~$(\epsilon^2/4,\epsilon^2)$, respectively. Thus $Z_{m,z}$ takes values in~$[0,3]$; it is a continuous version of the number of the three conditions~\eqref{e.good.gj},~\eqref{e.good.discounted.am.vs.aj} and~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} that fail at center~$z$ and scale~$m$. The probability bounds of Lemmas~\ref{l.bad.scales.for.nabla.gj},~\ref{l.exp.goodscales} and~\ref{l.discounted.J.local} are applied at the lower thresholds. Where $Z_{m,z}<1$, each quantity lies below its upper threshold. Apply the deterministic good-scale estimates at these upper thresholds; in particular, they use the threshold~$12$ for~$\mathsf P_{m,z}$ in place of~$6$. Only the constants change, provided~$\tau^2\leq s\log3/16$: the product bound~$12$ gives $e^{\|\g_{m,n}\|_\infty}\leq12\cdot3^{s(m-n)/8}$, while~\eqref{e.rho.m.n.bound} gives $e^{|\rho_{m,n}|}\leq3^{s(m-n)/16}$. The squared coefficient ratio in~\eqref{e.combined.coefficient.ratio.reg}, multiplied by the bound~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} and its weight, therefore still decays like~$3^{-s(m-n)}$.

\subsubsection{The good event of a cell}

We now define the good event of a cell. It requires two things: the quantities~$Z_{j,w}$ and~$\mathbf G_{j,w}$ must have small averages over the scales between each of the cubes defined below and their triadic descendants; and finitely many conditions on the cell itself must hold.

Fix a rational cube~$Q$ and consider its triadic subdivision. For a cell~$q=z+3^{-k}\cu_0$, evaluate the conditions below on its concentric enlargements~$3^iq$, where~$3^i$ ranges over a fixed finite list of triadic factors. Also use the cubes of the finitely many translated grids needed to cover the concentric cubes used for the harmonic approximation in~(b), and all triadic descendants of these cubes. For an enlargement~$U=3^iq$ and a depth~$D\geq0$, use the centers~$w$ of its depth-$D$ descendants and finitely many additional centers, fixed in advance, needed to compare~$s_E$ at different scales. There are at most~$C_d3^{dD}$ such centers. At each center~$w$, consider the scales between the side of~$U$ and the side of its depth-$D$ descendants, together with a fixed number~$k_1$ of additional scales. Evaluate every quantity at the same cutoff level~$N$.

Fix deterministic $\epsilon\in(0,1)$, $\lambda\in(0,1)$ and an initial depth~$k_0$ at least as large as~$k_1$. The \textbf{good event} $G(q)$ of the cell~$q$ is the event on which, for every cube~$U=3^iq$, every $D\geq k_0$ and every center~$w$ as above at depth~$D$, the sums of~$Z_{j,w}$ and of~$\mathbf G_{j,w}$ over the scales concerned (between~$U$ and its depth-$D$ descendants, with the $k_1$ additional scales) are both smaller than~$\lambda D$, and on which the following three conditions hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*),leftmargin=*]
\item the upper and lower coarse-grained ellipticity bounds~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.ellipticity} hold on~$q$ and on its finitely many concentric enlargements~$3^iq$;
\item the error of Definition~\ref{d.mathcal.E} on the cube concentric with~$q$ used for the harmonic approximation is below a prescribed tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ times the deterministic constant in the hypothesis of the interior harmonic approximation of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates};
\item the ratios of the scalars~$s_E$ of~$q$ and of the cubes concentric with~$q$ used for the harmonic comparison, defined in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare} and, at a finite cutoff, in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, lie in $[\nf12,2]$.
\end{enumerate}
Conditions (a)--(c) are finitely many conditions on scalars or matrices, and each has a continuous version given by a continuous piecewise linear function. The good event is defined with thresholds strictly smaller than those used by the deterministic estimates; the finitely many initial scales are counted as scales at which one of the conditions fails. At depth~$k$, these conditions use the scales~$N-k-D,\ldots,N-k$ of the relabeled fields of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}, with every quantity evaluated at the same cutoff~$N$.

\begin{lemma}[Deterministic consequences of the good event]
\label{mfd:in-deterministic}
Fix~$N$ and a cell~$q$. On~$G(q)$, every weak solution satisfies the following statements.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The local H\"older estimate with source holds at exponent~$\alpha$, with a fixed deterministic constant. More precisely, if the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} is run with a fixed exponent in~$(\alpha,1)$, then the constant in its conclusion after~$D$ scales is at most $3^{Ck_0+C(\lambda+\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8)D}$, where $C(\lambda+\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8)$ is smaller than the difference between this exponent and~$\alpha$.
\item The bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values holds on~$q$ with the factor~$\Lambda_{\sigma/2,2}(q;A_N)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}, which is comparable to the scalar~$s_E(q)$ of~$q$ (see Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}); this scalar is the factor by which the coefficient on~$q$, rescaled to unit size, is a multiple of a copy of~$A^0_{N-k}$, up to the oscillation factor of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity}.
\item The hypothesis of the interior harmonic approximation of Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates} holds.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
On $G(q)$, we apply the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} with the bad set consisting of the $k_1$ additional indices and the indices of the scales at which one of the conditions fails. This set has at most $2k_0+\lambda D$ elements. By~\eqref{e.cutoff.regularity.good.E} the exponent in the constant of the conclusion is at most $C(\gmcdelta^2+\epsilon^8+\lambda)D+Ck_0$. This gives~(i). Condition~(a), the estimate~\eqref{e.cg.RHS} and a trace extension $C^\beta(\partial q)\to H^{1+\sigma}(q)$ with $0<\sigma<\beta-\nf12$ give~(ii). Condition~(b) is the hypothesis in~(iii). These bounds depend only on the coefficient and the deterministic geometry, so the same bounds apply to every weak solution.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Passage to the limit in sums over finitely many scales]
\label{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}
Along the subsequence that defines~$E$, the following statements hold for each fixed finite interval of scales.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The quantities $Z_{m,z}$ and $\mathbf G_{m,z}$ and each of the finitely many coarse-grained quantities in (a)--(c) converge in $L^p$, for each of the finitely many orders~$p$ fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness}. The moment bounds are uniform in the deterministic position of the interval of scales.
\item Let $A,D>0$. If, at every cutoff, the sum of $Z_{m,z}$ or of $\mathbf G_{m,z}$ over the interval of scales exceeds a given threshold with probability at most $e^{-AD}$, then the limit of this sum exceeds any larger threshold with probability at most $e^{-AD}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We first prove~(i). Split the terms that involve only the fields into finite sums at finitely many depths and geometrically weighted tails. By~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity} the tails are bounded in~$L^p$ by their geometric weights times the moments of the fields. The other parts are geometrically weighted maxima of~$J$. Recall that $J$ is a fixed quadratic combination of the minimal energies $\Lambda_{N,q}$ with affine boundary values and of the coarse-grained quantities $|q|\,p\cdot a_*^{-1}(q;A_N)p$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-environment}. These energies converge in~$L^p$ along the subsequence of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, realized in the Skorokhod representation.

We truncate the geometrically weighted sums at a large relative depth. The finitely many retained quantities converge in~$L^p$ because their higher moments are uniformly bounded. At a relative depth~$j$ beyond the truncation there are at most $C3^{dj}$ cells. By Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound} and the bound~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio} on the ratios~$\ahom_m\ahom_n^{-1}$, the quantity~$\mathcal J$ of each cell has $L^p$ norm at most of order $e^{C_p\gmcdelta^2j}$, and taking a spatial maximum costs a factor~$3^{\nf{dj}{p}}$. We choose $p$ with $\nf dp$ below the margin of the geometric weights, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that $C_p\gmcdelta^2$ is below the remaining margin. The tail is then exponentially small in the truncation depth, uniformly in the cutoff.

Below the smallest field scale~$3^{-N}$, the maximum of the coefficient on a cell whose side equals that scale controls every smaller descendant by the direct bounds~\eqref{e.direct.J.bound.subunit}, so the finer depths require no further spatial maximum. The continuous piecewise linear functions and minima are Lipschitz, and square roots satisfy $|\sqrt x-\sqrt y|\leq\sqrt{|x-y|}$. Hence the quantities built from these are Cauchy in~$L^p$.

For~(ii), on the event that the limiting sum exceeds the larger threshold, a subsequence of the approximating sums that converges almost surely eventually exceeds some threshold strictly between the two thresholds, and each approximating sum exceeds that threshold with probability at most $e^{-AD}$. Fatou's lemma applied to the indicators gives the bound for the limit.
\end{proof}

The \textbf{good event} $G_E(q)$ of the cell~$q$ for the limit form~$E$ is the event on which the limiting conditions (a)--(c) hold and the limiting inequalities for the sums over the scales between~$U$ and its depth-$D$ descendants hold for all cubes~$3^iq$, depths and centers, all with strict margins relative to the conditions at finite cutoff. In particular, every ratio of the scalars~$s_E$ in~(c) lies in $(\nf12,2)$. A cell~$q$ is \textbf{good} if $G_E(q)$ holds and \textbf{bad} otherwise. Because the margins are strict, each fixed finite set of these conditions holds at all sufficiently large cutoffs after passing to a further subsequence of the one defining~$E$, in the Skorokhod representation, along which the quantities involved converge almost surely.

We apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-deterministic} at each fixed depth to the already extracted solutions $u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$, pass the finitely many inequalities and the local energy measures to the limit along recovery sequences~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}, and intersect over the depths. This gives, on $G_E(q)$, the local H\"older estimate with source, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values, and the harmonic approximation with tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$. The comparison argument of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare} applies regularity only to the solutions $G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$. For the second limit form~$F$ introduced there, the argument uses only the maximum principle for harmonic extensions.

\subsubsection{Events depending on finitely many fields}

The conditions depend on all the fields. We approximate the quantities in the conditions by functions of the fields with indices in a finite interval around the scale of the cube, and then cover the failure of~$G_E(q)$ by events that depend on such intervals and have exponentially small probabilities.

\begin{lemma}[Approximation by functions of finitely many fields]
\label{mfd:lem-band}
Let $X_{n,z}$ be one of the following: the quantity $\mathbf G_{n,z}$; one of the three summands of~$Z_{n,z}$; or a fixed finite nonnegative combination of the quantities in (a)--(c) that vanish for~$\gmcdelta=0$. There exist $a,c>0$, not depending on the moment order~$p$, such that, for every $p<\infty$, there exists $C_p<\infty$ with the following property. For every $h\geq1$, every deterministic $n$ and~$z$ and every cutoff, there is a random variable~$\widehat X_{n,z}^{(h)}$, measurable with respect to the fields with indices in an interval of length at most $C(h+1)$ about~$n$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-22}
 \bigl\|X_{n,z}-\widehat X_{n,z}^{(h)}\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_p\gmcdelta^c3^{-ah}\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We treat the terms that involve only the fields. Before the geometric weights are applied, the depth-$j$ sum in~$\mathsf F_{n,z}$ has $L^p$ norm at most $C_p\gmcdelta(j+1)^{\nf32}$: we cover $z+\cu_{n+1+j}$ by cubes of the scale of each field, use the sub-Gaussian bound for the maximum from~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}, and sum the $2j+1$ terms. We discard the depths $j>ch$, whose total contribution is at most $C_p\gmcdelta3^{-\nf{sch}{16}}$. The tail terms of~$\mathbf G$ (see~\eqref{e.def.bfG.mhq}) carry the explicit weight~$3^{k-i}$.

\emph{Step 2.} We treat the product term. We retain the depths $j\leq\lfloor h/2\rfloor$ and stop the second product at $n+h$. Both $\mathsf P$ and its approximant are at least~$2$, the approximant is not larger, and the removed depths contribute only their excess over~$2$. Fix~$j$. The first product factor at depth~$j$ is the exponential of $\sup_{x\in z+\cu_{n+1+j}}\sum_{i=(n-j)\vee0}^{n+j}|\g_i(x)|$. We cover the cube by at most $C_d3^{d(2j+1)}$ cubes of side $3^{(n-j)\vee0}$. On each such cube, this sum is at most the sum over~$i$ of the value of~$|\g_i|$ at the center plus $C_d3^{(n-j)\vee0}$ times the gradient norm of~$\g_i$ on the cube; these terms are independent in~$i$ and sub-Gaussian with parameter $C_d\gmcdelta$. We take the exponential moment before the spatial maximum, which bounds the moment of order~$u$ on each cube by $\exp(C_du\gmcdelta(j+1)+C_du^2\gmcdelta^2(j+1))$, and a maximum over the covering cubes, taken in $L^q$ with $q\geq p$, costs a factor equal to their number to the power~$\nf1q$. With $e^x-1\leq xe^x$, choosing $q$ large depending on $d,s,p$ and then $\gmcdelta$ small shows that $3^{-\nf{sj}{8}}$ times the $L^p$ norm of the first product factor minus one is at most $C_{d,s,p}\gmcdelta(j+1)3^{-\nf{sj}{16}}$.

The second product factor is the exponential of $4\sup_x\sum_{i\geq n+j}|\g_i(x)-\g_i(z)|$, which is at most a sum, over $i\geq n+j$, of sub-Gaussian variables multiplied by $C_d3^{n+j-i}$. Hence its excess over one has $L^p$ norm at most $C_p\gmcdelta$, and the part with $i>n+h$ contributes $C_p\gmcdelta3^{j-h}$.

Summing over $j\leq h/2$ gives $C_{s,p}\gmcdelta3^{-\nf h2}$, and the discarded depths $j>h/2$ contribute $\sum_{j>h/2}3^{-\nf{sj}{8}}$ times the $L^p$ norm of the sum of the two excesses at depth~$j$, which is at most $C_p\gmcdelta3^{-ah}$.

\emph{Step 3.} We treat the terms built from~$\mathcal J$. At relative depth~$r$ there are at most $C3^{dr}$ cells. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-16}, each cell's quantity~$\mathcal J$ can be approximated using finitely many fields. The maximum over the cells costs a factor~$3^{\nf{dr}{p}}$, and the ratios $\ahom_m\ahom_n^{-1}$ of effective diffusivities that occur grow at most like $e^{C\gmcdelta^2r}$ by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio}. Choose~$p$ with~$\nf dp$ below a fixed fraction of the exponent~$s/8$ of the geometric weights, and decrease~$\gmcdelta$. Discard the depths~$r>ch$. At each retained depth, use an interval of field indices whose radius is at least a fixed fraction of~$h$ and which is contained in the interval of length~$C(h+1)$ about~$n$. For square roots we use $|\sqrt x-\sqrt y|\leq\sqrt{|x-y|}$, which halves $a$ and~$c$.

\emph{Step 4.} We obtain the factor~$\gmcdelta^c$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-22}. The deviations of the quantities defining the conditions from their values at~$\gmcdelta=0$ satisfy
\begin{equation*}
 \|\mathsf F\|_p+\|\mathsf P-2\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta,
 \qquad \|\mathsf R\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta^2
 \quad\text{and}\quad \|\mathbf G\|_p\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}\,.
\end{equation*}
The first bound follows from Steps~1 and~2. For~$\mathsf R$, group the cubes of~\eqref{e.good.sum.J} over which the supremum is taken by $r=m-n$. There are at most $C_d3^{dr}$ such cubes, the quantity~$\mathcal J$ of each has $L^q$ norm $C_q\gmcdelta^2$ by Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, and $\sum_{r\geq2}3^{-(\nf s8-\nf dq)r}<\infty$ for a fixed $q\geq p$ large enough. For~$\mathbf G$, we take $m=n$ in Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors} and obtain $C_p\gmcdelta(1+|\log\gmcdelta|^{\nf12})\leq C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf12}$. If a truncation gives only $C_p3^{-ah}$, without a positive power of~$\gmcdelta$, we combine it with a bound $C_p\gmcdelta^b$, for some $b>0$, on the distance of~$X$ from its deterministic value at~$\gmcdelta=0$. Conditional expectation onto the fields with indices in the interval of length $C(h+1)$ about~$n$ is a contraction in~$L^p$. Thus the distance of~$X$ from its conditional expectation is at most twice its distance from the approximation. The same bound holds with the value at~$\gmcdelta=0$ in place of the approximation. The minimum of the two resulting bounds is at most their geometric mean $2C_p\gmcdelta^{\nf b2}3^{-\nf{ah}{2}}$. Since $a$ and~$c$ are fixed by the geometric weights and by~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity} before $p$ is chosen, they do not depend on~$p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Sums of $Z$ and $\mathbf G$ over scales at a fixed center]\label{mfd:in-prefix}
Let $\lambda>0$ and $A<\infty$. If $\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then, for every deterministic center~$z$, every interval $n,\ldots,n+k-1$ of scales, in the coordinates of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}, with all quantities evaluated at a single cutoff level, and each $X\in\{Z,\mathbf G\}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \P\Bigl[\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}X_{n+i,z}>\frac{\lambda k}{4}\Bigr]\leq Ce^{-Ak}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For $X=Z$ we apply Lemmas~\ref{l.bad.scales.for.nabla.gj},~\ref{l.exp.goodscales} and~\ref{l.discounted.J.local} at the lower thresholds of the continuous piecewise linear functions. The exponential rates in these lemmas tend to infinity as $\gmcdelta\downarrow0$. For $X=\mathbf G$ we apply Lemma~\ref{l.sum.the.errors}. Its estimate~\eqref{e.after.good.scales.uniform} concerns the average of the approximation errors over the interval, with an $\mathcal O_{\Gamma_2}$ fluctuation of size $(m-n+1)^{-\nf12}$. After multiplication by the length $m-n+1$, the sum exceeds $\lambda(m-n+1)$ with probability at most $\exp(-c\lambda^2(m-n+1)\gmcdelta^{-2}|\log\gmcdelta|^{-1})$. The bounds are uniform in the position of the interval by the stationarity in Assumption~\ref{a.g1} and by Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Covering the failure of the good event by events depending on finitely many fields]\label{mfd:lem-witness}
Let $A<\infty$. If $\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, then, for every cell~$q$ at triadic depth~$n$, there exist events $W_{q,h}$ for $h\geq1$ such that $W_{q,h}$ is measurable with respect to the fields $\g_{-j}$ with $n-h\leq j\leq n+2h$, $\P(W_{q,h})\leq e^{-Ah}$, and
\begin{equation*}
 \{G_E(q)\text{ fails}\}\subseteq\bigcup_{h\geq1}W_{q,h}\,.
\end{equation*}
The same conclusion holds for the failure of one additional scalar or matrix condition whose centered $L^p$ norm is at most $C_p\gmcdelta$ and whose approximation by a function of the fields with indices in an interval of length at most $C(h+1)$ about~$n$ has error at most $C_p\gmcdelta3^{-ah}$ in~$L^p$, for every~$h\geq1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If $G_E(q)$ fails, then either one of finitely many initial conditions fails, or there are a depth $k\geq k_0$, one of at most $Ce^{Ck}$ choices of a cube and a center, and a sum of $Z$ or of $\mathbf G$ over an interval of~$k$ scales that exceeds~$\lambda k$. We approximate this sum by the sums of the approximations of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band} with $h=k,2k,4k,\ldots$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-22} and the triangle inequality, the approximation with $h=2^\ell k$ differs from the sum by at most $C_pk\gmcdelta^c3^{-a2^\ell k}$ in~$L^p$. If the sum exceeds~$\lambda k$, then either the approximation with $h=k$ exceeds $\nf{\lambda k}{2}$ or, for some $\ell\geq1$, the approximations with $h=2^\ell k$ and $h=2^{\ell-1}k$ differ by more than $\lambda k2^{-\ell-2}$. The thresholds $\lambda k2^{-\ell-2}$ for the increments sum to $\nf{\lambda k}{4}<\nf{\lambda k}{2}$, which justifies this alternative.

The first event implies that the sum exceeds $\nf{\lambda k}{4}$ or differs from its approximation with $h=k$ by more than $\nf{\lambda k}{4}$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-prefix}, applied with a rate larger than $3A+C$, and Markov's inequality, the probability of this first event is at most $Ce^{-(3A+C)k}+(4C_p\gmcdelta^c/\lambda)^p3^{-apk}$. By Markov's inequality, the $\ell$-th increment event has probability at most $(C_p\gmcdelta^c)^p2^{p(\ell+C)}3^{-ap2^{\ell-1}k}$.

Take the union over the $Ce^{Ck}$ spatial choices. For each~$h$, combine into one event the events for all pairs $(k,\ell)$ with $2^\ell k\asymp h$. There are polynomially many such pairs. This event is measurable with respect to the fields indexed by an interval of radius at most $Ch$ about~$n$. After relabeling~$h$, this interval is $[n-h,n+2h]$. We choose $p$ with $ap>3A+C$, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ to absorb the factor $(C_p\gmcdelta^c)^p$ and the finitely many initial conditions. For an additional centered condition, we start from its approximation by a function of the fields with indices in an interval of radius~$k_0$. This approximation exceeds its threshold with probability at most $(C_p\gmcdelta/\lambda)^p$, which we make smaller than $e^{-ACk_0}$ by decreasing~$\gmcdelta$. Telescope the increments as above.
\end{proof}

Fix $L=3^{H_1}$ and a bounded cube~$Q$. A chain of length~$J$ is a sequence of~$J$ cubes of the $L$-adic subdivision of~$Q$, each a child of the preceding one and the first a child of~$Q$.

\begin{proposition}[Bad cells along every chain]\label{mfd:prop-allchain}
Let $L=3^{H_1}$, fix a bounded cube~$Q$, and let $\theta\in(0,1)$ and $b_*>0$. There exists $\gmcdelta_0>0$, depending on $L$, $\theta$, $b_*$ and the parameters of the good event, such that, if $\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there is a random variable $B_\omega<\infty$ such that, for every $t>0$,
\begin{equation*}
 \P(B_\omega>t)\leq(1-e^{-b_*})^{-1}\exp\Bigl(-\frac{b_*t}{1-\theta}\Bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
and such that, almost surely, for every $J\geq1$ and every chain~$\pi$ of length~$J$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-23}
 \#\{\text{bad cells on }\pi\}\leq\theta J+B_\omega\,.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} with rate~$A$. On a fixed chain, the depths~$H_1n$, $n\in\mathbb N$, of the $L$-adic subdivision are spaced by~$H_1$. Enlarge the interval of field indices determining~$W_{q,h}$ to the symmetric interval of radius~$2h$ about its depth, set all events at other depths or odd radii to the empty event, and apply Lemma~\ref{l.concentration.rare.intervals}. In that lemma, the rate is~$A/3$ and the density is at least~$\theta/H_1$. Thus, once~$A\theta\geq CH_1$, the probability that this chain has at least~$\theta J$ bad cells is at most~$e^{-cA\theta J}$. There are~$L^{dJ}$ chains. Choose~$A$ with $cA\theta\geq d\log L+b_*$. The probability that some chain of length~$J$ has at least~$\theta J$ bad cells is then at most~$e^{-b_*J}$. These choices precede the moment order and the threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}.

Set $B_\omega=\sup_J\max_{|\pi|=J}(\#\{\text{bad cells on }\pi\}-\theta J)_+$. If~$B_\omega>t$, then for some~$J>t/(1-\theta)$ there is a chain of length~$J$ with more than~$\theta J$ bad cells. Summing~$e^{-b_*J}$ over these~$J$ proves the stated tail bound and~\eqref{eq:mfd-23}.
\end{proof}

Every point of~$Q$ lies in exactly one chain of half-open cells. Integrating~\eqref{eq:mfd-23} over these chains gives, on the same event, for every finite Borel measure~$\mu$ on~$Q$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-24}
 \sum_{j=1}^J\mu(\text{union of the bad cells at $L$-adic level }j)
 \leq(\theta J+B_\omega)\mu(Q).
\end{equation}
In particular this holds for the random energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$.

\subsection{Comparison of two subsequential limits}
\label{mfd:sec-compare}

In this subsection we show that two subsequential limit forms differ only by a deterministic scalar factor (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop}). Let $E$ and~$F$ be two such forms, obtained along two subsequences of cutoffs, realized in the same Skorokhod representation (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}), so that both are functions of the same fields. Their domains are not assumed to be equal. We compare them on the cubes of~$\mathcal C$.

The preliminary two-sided comparison (Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0}) adapts the argument of~\citet[Section~3.4]{DGComparison} on the ratios of the normalizations of the two forms. In that argument, comparisons built from paths around annuli, together with upper and lower bounds on crossing distances, exclude ratios of the two normalizations that are too large or too small. Here harmonic extensions play the role of the paths. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} glues harmonic extensions on cubes at the scales where the ratio~$r_k$ of the two normalizations, defined below, is small, and shows that if this happens on a positive density of scales, then~$F$ is bounded by a small multiple of~$E$. By the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18}, this contradicts the moment bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}. This excludes ratios that are too small. Exchanging~$E$ and~$F$ excludes ratios that are too large. The proof of Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0} then applies the same construction on the remaining scales, where the ratio is bounded above and below, to compare the domains and the energies in both directions.

As in the metric uniqueness proofs of~\citet[Section~6]{GM} and~\citet[Section~4.5]{DGUniqueness}, we consider the optimal upper and lower comparison constants. Those proofs shorten geodesic segments to obtain probability bounds incompatible with a strict difference between the constants. They must control which annuli a field-dependent geodesic visits. The probability comparison and counting argument in~\citet[Section~4.2]{DGUniqueness} provide this control after suitable smooth changes of the Gaussian free field.

Here Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} bounds the number of bad cells along every chain of nested cubes, on a single event that does not depend on the solution. Its integrated form~\eqref{eq:mfd-24} therefore applies to the energy measure of the solution being replaced. We use harmonic extensions on disjoint cubes in place of shorter path segments. Since the solution is close to affine on good cells, the comparison of the minimal energies of~$E$ and~$F$ with affine boundary values transfers to its boundary values. Summing, over these cubes, the amount by which the minimal $F$-energy falls below the upper constant times the minimal $E$-energy improves one of the comparison constants for the forms themselves.

In this subsection a cell~$q$ is called good only when both $G_E(q)$ and~$G_F(q)$ hold. Applying Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} to each form with half the allowed density of bad cells and adding the two counts gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-23} for this notion of bad cell. On a good cell we thus have the regularity and coercivity estimates for~$E$ and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values for~$F$, and the same with the roles of $E$ and~$F$ exchanged.

The comparison is expressed through the scalars~$s_E(q)$ and~$s_F(q)$ of the two forms. At a cell~$q$ of side~$3^{-k}$ and center~$z$, the scalar~$s_E(q)$ of~$E$ is
\begin{equation*}
 s_E(q)=\exp\Bigl(H(z)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}(\g_{-j}(z)-\tau^2)\Bigr)
          \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}\,,
\end{equation*}
the limit being taken along the subsequence that defines~$E$. We define~$s_F(q)$ in the same way, along the subsequence that defines~$F$. After a further diagonal extraction, these limits exist for all~$k$ at once: since $|\ahom_{N}\ahom_{N-k}^{-1}-1|\leq C\gmcdelta^2ke^{C\gmcdelta^2k}$ by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering.ratio}, the ratio $\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ lies in a compact subinterval of $(0,\infty)$ that depends only on~$k$. The ratio
\begin{equation*}
 r_k=\frac{s_F(q)}{s_E(q)}
\end{equation*}
of these two scalars is deterministic and does not depend on~$z$.

Choose the parameters in the following order. First fix the geometric and regularity parameters below, then~$L$, the moment orders, and a first threshold for~$\gmcdelta$, which determines the comparison constant~$C_0$ of Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-C0}. With~$C_0$ fixed, we tighten the tolerance~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ of the harmonic comparison and add the comparison of the minimal energies of~$E$ and~$F$ with affine boundary values, of Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-32}, whose error is proportional to the difference between the optimal comparison constants defined below.
