This requires only a further decrease of~$\gmcdelta$. The thresholds for sums over intervals of scales and~$L$ stay fixed. None of these choices depends on the difference of the optimal constants.

\subsubsection{Comparison from a positive density of small ratios}

We first show that a positive density of scales at which $r_k\leq a$ forces $F\leq C_*aE$. Fix $\eta_0\in(0,\nf13)$ and choose the parameters so that
\begin{equation*}
 D\geq\frac{8d}{\eta_0},\qquad \theta\leq\frac{\eta_0}{8}\quad\text{and}\quad 2(1-\alpha)+\eta<\frac{\eta_0}{8}\,.
\end{equation*}
The last condition is equivalent to $d-2+2\alpha-\eta>d-\nf{\eta_0}{8}$. Then choose $L=3^{H_1}$ so large that $L^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}-\nf{\eta_0}{8}}\geq2C_d^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}}$, where $C_d$ is the constant in the bound below on the number of children whose enlargement is not contained in the parent. We consider the $L$-adic levels $n\in\mathbb N$, which correspond to the triadic depths $k=H_1n$; thus $r_{H_1n}$ is the ratio~$r_k$ defined above at the depth~$k=H_1n$. For a child~$q$ of side~$r$ of a cell~$p$ of side~$Lr$, consider its concentric enlargement of side~$3r$. At most $C_dL^{d-1}$ children of each cell have an enlargement whose closure is not contained in~$p$. A set $S\subseteq\mathbb N$ has \textbf{upper density} at least~$\eta$ if $\limsup_JJ^{-1}\#(S\cap\{1,\ldots,J\})\geq\eta$.

On a good cell whose enlargement has its closure in the parent, the regularity of~$E$ and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values of~$F$ combine as follows.

\begin{lemma}[Extension on good cells]\label{mfd:lem-goodext}
Let $Q$ be a cube of~$\mathcal C$ and $f$ a smooth function, and set $u=G_E^Qf$, where~$G_E^Q$ is the Dirichlet Green operator of~$E$ on~$Q$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item If $q=z+r\cu_0$ is a good child of~$p$ at depth~$k$ whose concentric enlargement of side~$3r$ has its closure in~$p$, then
 \begin{align*}
  \inf_{c\in\R}\|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c\|_{C^\alpha(\cu_0)}
  &\leq Cr^{\nf{2-d}{2}}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(p)}
              +Cr^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,,\\
  \Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})
  &\leq Cr_k\bigl[\Gamma_E(u)(p)+s_E(q)^{-1}r^{d+2}\|f\|_\infty^2\bigr]\,.
 \end{align*}
\item There is a random constant $K<\infty$ such that $\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\leq Kr^{d-2+2\alpha-\eta}$ for every cell $q\subseteq Q$ of side~$r$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
For~(i), the local H\"older estimate with source on the good cell bounds the oscillation on~$q$ by the normalized~$L^2$ oscillation on the concentric cube of side~$3r$ and the source (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}). The coarse-grained Poincar\'e inequality~\eqref{e.CG.Poincare.mean.zero} on that cube bounds its oscillation by its energy. Since its closure lies in~$p$, this energy is at most~$\Gamma_E(u)(p)$, giving the factor $r^{\nf{2-d}{2}}$ in unnormalized units. The source is controlled by~\eqref{eq:mfd-5}: by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-primitive}, convolving~$f$ with the gradient of the Newtonian kernel gives a vector field with $C^{\nf12}$ seminorm at most $Cr^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$ on a cube of side~$r$. Scaling gives the term $r^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty$. For the form~$F$ on~$q$, the coefficient factor in~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} is comparable to~$s_F(q)=r_ks_E(q)$. Inserting the first estimate into that bound gives the second estimate: we square the first bound and multiply by~$s_F(q)r^{d-2}$.

For~(ii), the global bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} for~$u$ gives $\|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c\|_{C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)}\leq r^\alpha[u]_\alpha$, and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes bounds the factor in~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for~$F$ on~$q$ by $Kr^{-\eta}$. Then~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} gives the bound $Kr^{-\eta}r^{d-2}r^{2\alpha}[u]_\alpha^2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Comparison from a positive density of small ratios]
\label{mfd:prop-density}
Assume that the parameters are chosen as above. There exists a deterministic $C_*<\infty$, depending on $d$, $\eta_0$, $L$ and the constants of the good event, such that the following holds for every $a>0$. Let $S$ be a deterministic set of $L$-adic levels of upper density at least~$\eta_0$ with $r_{H_1n}\leq a$ for every $n\in S$. Then, on every cube of~$\mathcal C$, $D(E)\subseteq D(F)$ and, for every $v\in D(E)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-25}
 F(v)\leq C_*aE(v)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Fix a cube $Q$ of~$\mathcal C$. Translate the $L$-adic grid so that its origin is a corner of~$Q$. Since the side of~$Q$ is triadic, every sufficiently fine $L$-adic mesh partitions $Q$ exactly. Fix $u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$, fix $c>0$, and set $\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} with $\eta<2$, the source term in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext} is at most $Kr^{d+2-\eta}\|f\|_\infty^2\leq cr^d=c|q|$ for cells of side below a number~$h_0$ depending on $u$, $c$ and the environment.

Fix an $L$-adic mesh of side at most~$h_0$ that partitions~$Q$; all cells below are subcubes of the finitely many cubes of this mesh, and we apply Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} to each of them.

\emph{Step 1.} We stop the subdivision at good cells that carry a fixed fraction of the mass of their parents, and bound the sum, over the stopped cells~$q$, of the minimal $F$-energy~$\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ with the boundary values of~$u$. Within each cube of the mesh of side at most~$h_0$ we follow the subdivision tree and stop at the first child~$q$ that lies at an $L$-adic level of~$S$, is good, has an enlargement with closure in its parent, and satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 \mu(p)\leq L^D\mu(q)\,.
\end{equation*}
By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext}, a stopped cell satisfies $\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\leq Ca[\mu(p)+c|q|]\leq CaL^D\mu(q)$. The interiors of the stopped cells are disjoint. Hence the sum of $\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over the stopped cells is at most $C_*a\mu(Q)$ with $C_*=CL^D$.

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the sum of $\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over the cells that are never stopped tends to zero. There are arbitrarily large depths~$J$ at which at least $\nf{3\eta_0J}{4}$ of the $L$-adic levels below the mesh of side at most~$h_0$ belong to~$S$. Along a chain that is not stopped by such a depth~$J$, every step to a good child with an enlargement in its parent, at an $L$-adic level of~$S$, fails the condition $\mu(p)\leq L^D\mu(q)$. Since $\mu$ is monotone along the chain and drops by more than the factor $L^D$ at each of these steps, while every leaf satisfies $\mu(\text{leaf})\geq c|\text{leaf}|=cL^{-dJ}$ on the $L$-adic mesh of depth~$J$, the number of these steps is at most
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{d}{D}J+\frac{\log(\mu(Q)/c)}{D\log L}
 \leq\frac{\eta_0}{8}J+O_{Q,c,u,\omega}(1)\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-23}, at most $\theta J+B_\omega\leq\nf{\eta_0J}{8}+B_\omega$ of the steps at $L$-adic levels of~$S$ are bad. For all sufficiently large such~$J$, the chain therefore has at least $\nf{3\eta_0J}{4}-\nf{\eta_0J}{4}-O(1)\geq\nf{\eta_0J}{3}$ steps at $L$-adic levels of~$S$ to a child whose enlargement is not contained in its parent. At a step to such a child there are at most $C_dL^{d-1}$ choices of the child. At every other step there are at most $L^d$. Summing over the at most $2^J$ positions of the steps to such children, we find that the number of leaves that are never stopped is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C_Q2^JL^{dJ}(C_d/L)^{\nf{\eta_0J}{3}}\leq C_{Q,c,u,\omega}L^{(d-\nf{\eta_0}{8})J}\,,
\end{equation*}
where we used $L^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}-\nf{\eta_0}{8}}\geq2C_d^{\nf{\eta_0}{3}}$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext}(ii), the sum of $\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over them is at most $KL^{(d-\nf{\eta_0}{8})J}$ times~$L^{-(d-2+2\alpha-\eta)J}$, which tends to zero because $d-2+2\alpha-\eta>d-\nf{\eta_0}{8}$.

\emph{Step 3.} We show that $u\in D(F)$ and $F(u)\leq C_*aE(u)$. At a finite depth~$J$, the stopped cells and the remaining leaves form a finite partition of~$Q$ into cells, on whose boundaries we take the values of~$u$. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-skeleton}, the $F$-harmonic extensions of these boundary values glue to a function $v_J\in D(F)$ with $F(v_J)\leq C_*a\mu(Q)+o(1)$. By the maximum principle, each extension lies between the extreme boundary values of~$u$ on its cell, so $\|v_J-u\|_\infty$ is at most the modulus of continuity of~$u$ at scale~$h_0$. For fixed~$h_0$ we take $J$ large and then let~$h_0\to0$. Then $v_J\to u$ uniformly, and the closedness of~$F$ gives $F(u)\leq C_*a(E(u)+c|Q|)$. Letting $c\downarrow0$ gives $u\in D(F)$ and $F(u)\leq C_*aE(u)$.

\emph{Step 4.} We extend the inequality to~$D(E)$. Let $v\in D(E)$. By Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-killed-inverse}, there are smooth~$f_n$ with $G_Ef_n\to v$ in the form norm of~$E$. By Step~3, the sequence $(G_Ef_n)$ is Cauchy in the form norm of~$F$, and the closedness of~$F$ identifies its limit with~$v$. This gives $v\in D(F)$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-25}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Two-sided comparison]\label{mfd:thm-C0}
There exists a deterministic $C_0\geq1$ such that, for every pair of subsequential limit forms and every $0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_*$, where~$\gmcdelta_*$ is the initial threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ chosen above, on every cube of~$\mathcal C$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-26}
 D(E)=D(F)\quad\text{and}\quad C_0^{-1}E\leq F\leq C_0E\,.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fix a nonzero smooth source~$f_0$ on the unit cube. By the positive and negative moments of $\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle$ and $\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal}, choose a deterministic $A\geq1$, uniform over the subsequential limit forms and over~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_*$, so that $\P[A^{-1}\leq\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle,\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle\leq A]>0$. It suffices to choose~$A$ so that each of the four tail probabilities is less than~$\nf18$. Set $a_0=(2C_*A^2)^{-1}$ and let $S_-$ be the set of $L$-adic levels~$n$ with $r_{H_1n}\leq a_0$. Since the ratios~$r_k$ are deterministic, so is~$S_-$. If $S_-$ has upper density at least~$\eta_0$, then Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} gives $D(E)\subseteq D(F)$ and $F\leq C_*a_0E$ on~$D(E)$. The variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18} then gives, almost surely,
\begin{equation*}
 \langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle=\sup_{v\in D(F)}\bigl\{2\langle f_0,v\rangle-F(v)\bigr\}
 \geq\sup_{v\in D(E)}\bigl\{2\langle f_0,v\rangle-C_*a_0E(v)\bigr\}
 =(C_*a_0)^{-1}\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle=2A^2\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle\,,
\end{equation*}
which contradicts $\P[A^{-1}\leq\langle f_0,G_Ef_0\rangle,\langle f_0,G_Ff_0\rangle\leq A]>0$. Hence $S_-$ has upper density less than~$\eta_0$. Exchanging $E$ and~$F$ gives the same bound for the set~$S_+$ of $L$-adic levels with $r_{H_1n}\geq a_0^{-1}$. The complement of $S_-\cup S_+$ therefore has lower density greater than $1-2\eta_0>\eta_0$, since $\eta_0<\nf13$, and on this complement $a_0<r_{H_1n}<a_0^{-1}$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density}, applied in both directions with $a=a_0^{-1}$, gives $F\leq C_*a_0^{-1}E$ and $E\leq C_*a_0^{-1}F$ with equal domains. Thus~\eqref{eq:mfd-26} holds with $C_0=C_*/a_0=2C_*^2A^2$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The optimal constants are deterministic}

For $a>0$ we write $aE\leq F$ if $aE^Q\leq F^Q$ on $D(E^Q)=D(F^Q)$ for every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$, and we optimize over all these cubes:
\begin{equation*}
 m=\sup\{a:\ aE\leq F\},\qquad M=\inf\{a:\ F\leq aE\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad
 \Delta=M-m\,.
\end{equation*}
The numbers $m$ and~$M$ are the optimal lower and upper comparison constants, and $\Delta$ is their difference. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-26}, $C_0^{-1}\leq m\leq M\leq C_0$, and $mE\leq F\leq ME$ by monotone limits. The comparison constants are defined for each realization; the next lemma shows that they are deterministic, which Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop} uses to conclude that the proportionality constant is deterministic.

\begin{lemma}[The optimal comparison constants are deterministic]\label{mfd:lem-endpoints}
The optimal comparison constants $m$ and~$M$ are almost surely constant.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The comparison constants are measurable: on each cube of~$\mathcal C$, $aE\leq F$ is equivalent, by the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18}, to $\langle f,G_Ff\rangle\leq a^{-1}\langle f,G_Ef\rangle$ for all sources~$f$ in the countable dense family.

Resampling one field multiplies both energy measures by the same positive continuous weight, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-21}. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos}, an inequality between the two forms is equivalent to the same inequality between their energy measures. Multiplying both measures by a common weight preserves these inequalities in both directions. Thus resampling one field leaves~$m$ and~$M$ unchanged. Since these constants are bounded, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum}, with no fields retained, gives~$\|m-\E m\|_1=\|M-\E M\|_1=0$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Concentration of the difference between the energies}

Assume~$\Delta>0$. We prove a concentration bound for the difference of the two energies, with an error proportional to~$\Delta$.

We first check that the minimal energies of $E$ and~$F$ with given boundary values on a cell are taken over the same class of admissible functions. Let $q$ be a cell and let $Q\supset q$ be a larger cube of~$\mathcal C$. A boundary value~$\ell$ on~$\partial q$, affine or of class~$C^\beta$ with $\beta>\nf12$, is realized by an $E$-harmonic extension through a neighbourhood of~$\partial q$, which belongs to~$D(E^Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-26} this extension lies in~$D(F^Q)$. Its restriction to~$q$, together with the identification of the minimal energies in Propositions~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary} and~\ref{mfd:prop-gluing}, places it in the admissible class of both $\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$ and~$\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)$. Thus both minimal energies below are taken over the same class of functions with the prescribed boundary values, on which $m\Lambda_{E,q}\leq\Lambda_{F,q}\leq M\Lambda_{E,q}$ as quadratic forms.

\begin{lemma}[Difference of two minimizers]\label{mfd:lem-diff}
Let $u_E$ and~$u_F$ be the minimizers of $E$ and~$F$ in~$q$ with the same affine boundary values, let $c\in[m,M]$, and set $D=F-cE$ and $w=u_F-u_E$. Then $F(w,v)=-D(u_E,v)$ for every $v\in D(E^q)$, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-27}
 E(w)\leq\Bigl(\frac{\Delta}{m}\Bigr)^2E(u_E)\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover, for all $u,v$ in the common domain and every Borel set~$B$,
\begin{equation*}
 |\Gamma_D(u,v)|(B)\leq\Delta\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(B)\Gamma_E(v)(B)}\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Subtracting the Euler equations $F(u_F,v)=0=E(u_E,v)$ gives $F(w,v)=F(u_F,v)-F(u_E,v)=-F(u_E,v)+cE(u_E,v)=-D(u_E,v)$. Since $(m-c)E\leq D\leq(M-c)E$ and $|m-c|,|M-c|\leq\Delta$, the symmetric form~$D$ satisfies $|D(u,v)|\leq\Delta\sqrt{E(u)E(v)}$. Taking $v=w$ gives $mE(w)\leq F(w)=-D(u_E,w)\leq\Delta\sqrt{E(u_E)E(w)}$, which is~\eqref{eq:mfd-27}.

For a Borel set~$A$, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-sincos} gives $|\Gamma_D(h)(A)|\leq\Delta\Gamma_E(h)(A)$. Polarization therefore gives, for every~$t>0$,
\begin{equation*}
 4t|\Gamma_D(u,v)(A)|
 \leq2\Delta\bigl[\Gamma_E(u)(A)+t^2\Gamma_E(v)(A)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Optimizing in~$t$ gives $|\Gamma_D(u,v)(A)|\leq\Delta\sqrt{\Gamma_E(u)(A)\Gamma_E(v)(A)}$. Sum this bound over a finite Borel partition of~$B$, apply Cauchy--Schwarz, and take the supremum over the partitions to obtain the total-variation bound.
\end{proof}

We normalize the minimal energies with affine boundary values on a cell. Fix~$c\in[m,M]$. Let~$\ell$ range over the affine boundary values in a fixed finite set that contains the coordinate functions and determines quadratic forms by polarization. Set $D_q=\sum_{i=1}^d\Lambda_{E,q}(x_i)$ and, for one of these boundary values, $\mathcal R=(\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell))/D_q$. By polarization, these quantities give the entries of the matrix~$B_k$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}. Define the normalized measures
\begin{equation*}
 \nu=\frac{1}{D_q}\sum_{\ell}\bigl[\Gamma_E(u_E^{\ell})+\Gamma_E(u_F^{\ell})\bigr],
 \qquad
 \zeta=\frac{1}{D_q}\sum_{\ell}\Gamma_E(w^{\ell})\,,
\end{equation*}
where the sums run over the fixed affine boundary values and $w^\ell=u_F^\ell-u_E^\ell$. Then $\nu(q)\leq C(C_0)$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-26}, and $\zeta(q)\leq C(C_0)\Delta^2$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-27}. The measure~$\nu$ inherits the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} of the energy measures of the minimizers. For a bounded~$g$ supported in~$B$, let $\mathcal R^g$ denote the quantity~$\mathcal R$ computed for the forms $E^g$ and~$F^g$.

\begin{lemma}[Perturbation of the normalized difference~$\mathcal R$]\label{mfd:lem-relvar}
There exists $C(C_0)<\infty$ such that, for every bounded~$g$ supported in~$B$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-30}
 |\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R|\leq C\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}
 \bigl[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}\bigr]\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let~$u_E^g$ and~$u_F^g$ minimize the weighted forms~$E^g$ and~$F^g$ with the same boundary values as~$u_E$ and~$u_F$, respectively. Put $H_q=\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$, $D=F-cE$, $D^g=F^g-cE^g$, $v_E=u_E^g-u_E$ and $v_F=u_F^g-u_F$. Subtract the weighted Euler equations to obtain, for every test function~$\phi$ with zero boundary values on~$\partial q$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-28}
 F^g(v_F-v_E,\phi)=-\int_B(e^g-1)
       [d\Gamma_F(w,\phi)+d\Gamma_D(u_E,\phi)]-D^g(v_E,\phi).
\end{equation}
Test with~$v_F-v_E$ and use Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-diff} and the perturbation bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} for~$v_E$. Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-29}
 E^g(v_F-v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}
       [\Gamma_E(w)(B)+\Delta^2\Gamma_E(u_E)(B)].
\end{equation}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-14},
\begin{equation*}
 H_q^g-H_q
 =\int_B(e^g-1)\,d\bigl[\Gamma_F(u_F)-c\Gamma_E(u_E)\bigr]
   -\bigl[F^g(v_F)-cE^g(v_E)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Use the bilinear identities
\begin{align*}
 \Gamma_F(u_F)-c\Gamma_E(u_E)
 &=\Gamma_D(u_E)+\Gamma_F(w,u_F+u_E)\,,\\
 F^g(v_F)-cE^g(v_E)
 &=D^g(v_E)+F^g(v_F-v_E,v_F+v_E)\,.
\end{align*}
Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-diff} bounds the first line, in total variation on~$B$, by $CD_q[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}]$. For the second,~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} for each form and~\eqref{eq:mfd-29} give
\begin{equation*}
 E^g(v_E)+E^g(v_F+v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q\nu(B),
 \qquad
 E^g(v_F-v_E)\leq C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q[\zeta(B)+\Delta^2\nu(B)]\,.
\end{equation*}
Cauchy--Schwarz and $|D^g(v_E)|\leq\Delta E^g(v_E)$ thus bound the correction by $C\|g\|_\infty^2e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q[\Delta\nu(B)+\sqrt{\nu(B)\zeta(B)}]$. Absorbing $\|g\|_\infty^2$ into $\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}$ proves the required bound for $|H_q^g-H_q|/D_q$.

Finally, $|H_q|\leq C\Delta D_q$, $e^{-\|g\|_\infty}D_q\leq D_q^g\leq e^{\|g\|_\infty}D_q$, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} summed over the coordinate data gives $|D_q^g-D_q|\leq C\|g\|_\infty e^{C\|g\|_\infty}D_q\nu(B)$. Apply these bounds to $\mathcal R^g-\mathcal R=(H_q^g-H_q)/D_q^g +H_q(D_q-D_q^g)/(D_qD_q^g)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Concentration of the normalized difference~$B_k$]\label{mfd:prop-conc}
Let $A_E$ and~$A_F$ be the coarse-grained matrices of $E$ and~$F$ on a cell~$q$ at depth~$k$, defined by $\Lambda_{E,q}(p\cdot x)=|q|\,p\cdot A_Ep$, and similarly for~$F$. Set
\begin{equation*}
 c_k=\E\Bigl[\frac{\operatorname{tr}A_F}{\operatorname{tr}A_E}\Bigr],
 \qquad B_k=\frac{A_F-c_kA_E}{\operatorname{tr}A_E}\,.
\end{equation*}
Then $c_k\in[m,M]$ and $\E B_k=0$. Moreover, there exists $a>0$, depending only on the dimension and on the regularity exponents fixed earlier, and not on $C_0$ or~$p$, such that the following holds for every $1\leq p<\infty$. If $\gmcdelta$ is sufficiently small, depending on $p$ and~$C_0$, then, for every $k$ and every integer~$h\geq0$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-31}
 \|B_k\|_{L^p}\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 \bigl\|B_k-\E[B_k\mid\text{fields with indices within distance }h\text{ of }k]\bigr\|_{L^p}\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta3^{-ah}\,,
\end{equation}
where $C_p(C_0)$ does not depend on~$k$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}

\emph{Step 1.} The form order gives~$c_k\in[m,M]$. By Assumptions~\ref{a.g1} and~\ref{a.g3}, the joint law of~$A_E$ and~$A_F$, after their common scalar factor is removed, is invariant under signed permutations of the coordinates. Thus~$\E B_k$ is a scalar matrix. Its trace is zero by the definition of~$c_k$, so~$\E B_k=0$.

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the moments of the constant in the Morrey bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-4} for~$\nu$, uniformly in~$k$. Rescale a cell to the unit cube and divide $E$ by~$s_E(q)$ and $F$ by~$s_F(q)$. The fields of scale smaller than the cell then have their original law, and the sum of the fields of larger scale, with its value at the cell center subtracted, has the moments of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}, uniformly in~$k$. At finite cutoff, let~$u$ minimize the normalized energy with affine boundary values of slope~$e_1$. Test the Neumann variational formula for boundary flux~$e_1\cdot n$ with scalar multiples of~$u$. The product of the normalized Dirichlet and Neumann energies is then at least~$(\int_{\cu_0}e_1\cdot\nabla u)^2=1$. This inequality passes to the extracted limits. The moment bounds of Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-cell-ellipticity} and~\ref{mfd:lem-response-reciprocal} therefore control each normalized minimal energy and its reciprocal. For some deterministic~$A<\infty$, both normalized minimal energies with slope~$e_1$ lie in~$[A^{-1},A]$ with positive probability. On that event, the order of the original minimal energies and their scaling give that $r_k$ times the ratio of the two normalized energies lies in~$[m,M]$, so that $mA^{-2}\leq r_k\leq MA^2$. The last bounds hold deterministically because~$r_k$ is deterministic. Also the reciprocal of the sum of the normalized minimal energies over the coordinate directions is at most the normalized energy of the Neumann problem for~$E$ with boundary flux~$e_1\cdot n$. H\"older's inequality, the Morrey bounds for the two minimizers and these reciprocal moments now give the moments, of the finitely many orders fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness}, of the constant in the Morrey bound for~$\nu$, uniformly in~$k$.

\emph{Step 3.} Let~$p\geq2$, and consider one of the fields of scale smaller than the cell, of scale~$r$. Use the cutoff functions and strips of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15}, with boxes of side~$r^{(t-d+1)/(t+1)}$ and strips of width~$4\sqrt d\,r$. The restrictions of the field to the separated interior subboxes are independent by~\ref{a.g1}, and by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-borel-weights} the whole field can be removed from the limit forms. With~$b$ from Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-strips}, the original measure satisfies~$\nu(\mathrm{strips})\leq Kr^b$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-30}, the change of~$\mathcal R$ caused by deleting the field on the strips is at most~$C\Delta Se^{CS}(Kr^b+K^{\nf{1}{2}}r^{\nf{b}{2}})$, where~$S$ is the sup norm of the field. As in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15},~\eqref{eq:mfd-14} controls the changed minimizers, so the energy measures after the cutoff function is applied to the field satisfy
\begin{equation*}
 \max_B\widehat\nu(B)\leq Ce^{CS}Kr^b,
 \qquad \widehat\nu(q)\leq C,
 \qquad \widehat\zeta(q)\leq C\Delta^2.
\end{equation*}
The last two bounds follow from the order between the two forms, which is preserved when the same cutoff function is applied to the field in both forms. The new ingredient in the Efron--Stein estimate is the bound on the sum of squares
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_B[\Delta\widehat\nu(B)
             +\sqrt{\widehat\nu(B)\widehat\zeta(B)}]^2
 \leq2\max_B\widehat\nu(B)
       [\Delta^2\widehat\nu(q)+\widehat\zeta(q)]
 \leq C\Delta^2\max_B\widehat\nu(B).
\end{equation*}
Thus the first and last terms of~\eqref{eq:mfd-fourterm} in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-15}, and the conditional Efron--Stein estimate, all keep the factor~$\Delta$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-layernorm}, the norms of the fields are absorbed at the cost of a fixed fraction of the exponent~$b$. For an entry~$X$ of~$B_k$, resampling the field at relative index~$j$ consequently gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|X-X^{(j)}\|_p\leq C_p(C_0)\gmcdelta\Delta
 \begin{cases}3^{-aj},&j\geq0,\\3^j,&j<0.\end{cases}
\end{equation*}
For~$j<0$ this follows directly from~\eqref{eq:mfd-30} and the bound of Step~2 on the sum of the fields of larger scale, with their value at the cell center subtracted. Choose~$a$ below a fixed fraction of~$b$. Apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-resampling-sum} with no fields retained and then with the fields within distance~$h$ of~$k$ retained. Centering, polarization and the two geometric sums give~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}.
\end{proof}

By the concentration bound, we can add to the good event the requirement that the minimal energies of~$F$ and~$c_kE$ with affine boundary values differ by at most a small multiple of~$\Delta$ times the minimal energy of~$E$ with the same boundary values.

\begin{corollary}[Minimal energies with affine boundary values on good cells]\label{mfd:cor-32}
Let $\epsilon>0$. The good event of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} can be strengthened by one additional condition, with a threshold for~$\gmcdelta$ and a bad-cell density~$\theta$ that do not depend on~$\Delta$, such that, on every good cell~$q$ at depth~$k$ and for every affine boundary value~$\ell$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-32}
 |\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)-c_k\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)|\leq\epsilon\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Apply the last assertion of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} to~$X=B_k/\Delta$, with the additional condition~$|X|\leq\epsilon c_0$, where $c_0>0$ is the deterministic constant of the next paragraph. Its hypotheses are precisely~\eqref{eq:mfd-31}. Reserve half the allowed density of bad cells for this condition. Choose the rate~$A$ of the events~$W_{q,h}$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness} larger than the exponential growth rate~$d\log L$ of the number of chains. Then choose the required moment order and decrease~$\gmcdelta$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} gives the strengthened good event, with choices independent of~$\Delta$.

We pass from~$B_k$ to~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}. Condition~(a) of the good event bounds the condition number of the normalized matrix~$A_E$: the coarse-grained ellipticity constants~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.ellipticity} bound $|A_E|$ and~$|A_E^{-1}|$. Hence $\lambda_{\min}(A_E)\geq c_0\operatorname{tr}A_E$ with a deterministic $c_0>0$. For an affine function with slope~$\xi$, we obtain $|\xi\cdot(A_F-c_kA_E)\xi|\leq|B_k|\operatorname{tr}A_E|\xi|^2\leq\epsilon c_0\Delta\operatorname{tr}A_E|\xi|^2\leq\epsilon\Delta\,\xi\cdot A_E\xi$, which is~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Affine approximation of the boundary values}

\begin{lemma}[Affine approximation of the boundary values]\label{mfd:lem-affine}
Let $\nf12<\beta<\alpha<1$, $0<\gamma<1$, $\zeta>0$ and $\varrho>0$, and assume that
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-33}
 \frac{d+\zeta}{2}
 -\gamma\left(\frac{d-2}{2}+\alpha
       +\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+d/2}(2-\alpha)\right)<0.
\end{equation}
Let~$E$ be a subsequential limit form along the subsequence of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, realized in the Skorokhod representation, let~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$, $u=G_E^Qf$, $c>0$ and $\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$. Suppose that $L=3^{H_1}$ is chosen large, with $\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+6<H_1$, that $\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ is then chosen small, and that~$\gmcdelta$ is then chosen small, all independently of~$f$ and~$c$. Then the following holds almost surely for cells of side below a number~$h_0>0$, which may depend on~$f$, $c$ and the environment. Let $q$ be a good child, of side~$r$, of a cell~$p\subset Q$ of side~$Lr$. If the cube concentric with~$q$ of side $3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r$, used in conditions~(b)--(c), has its closure in~$p$, and
\begin{equation*}
 \mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)\,,
\end{equation*}
then there is an affine function~$\ell_q$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-34}
 \Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)\leq\varrho\mu(q)\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Take $L=3^{H_1}$ with $\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+6<H_1$ and put $S=3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r/729$. Condition~(c) of the good event compares $s_E$ at the sides $729S$ and~$r$ within a factor two. The mass bound on the parent and the estimate of the oscillation by the energy bound the oscillation of~$u$ on the cube of side~$S$ concentric with~$q$ by $CS^{\nf{2-d}{2}}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}}\sqrt{\mu(q)}$, together with the source term $CS^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty$. Condition~(b) and Lemma~\ref{l.cutoff.regularity.good.scale.estimates}, with the harmonic comparison on the concentric cube of side~$9S$ and its error measured on the concentric cube of side~$S$, show that the $\underline L^2$ distance on the cube of side~$S$ between~$u$ and the Laplace-harmonic function on the cube of side~$9S$ with the boundary values of~$u$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C\epsilon_{\rm hom}S^{(2-d)/2}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}
                L^{(d+\zeta)/2}\sqrt{\mu(q)}
       +CS^2s_E(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
The estimates at finite cutoff pass to the extracted solutions by uniform convergence and Corollary~\ref{mfd:cor-energy-measures}, since the energy measure~$\Gamma_E(u)$ does not charge the faces of~$p$. Let $\ell_q$ be the affine function that best approximates~$u$ in~$L^2(q)$; we use it for both norms below. Approximating the Laplace-harmonic function by its first-order Taylor polynomial gains a factor $(r/S)^2$ in~$L^2(q)$. Restricting the error of the harmonic approximation from the concentric cube of side~$S$ to~$q$ costs a factor $(S/r)^{\nf d2}$ in volume-normalized~$L^2$. The local H\"older bound on~$q$ gains a factor $(r/S)^\alpha$, since the affine projection is bounded on~$C^\alpha$. Since $L^\gamma r/27<S\leq L^\gamma r/9$, after division by $r^{\nf{2-d}{2}}\sqrt{\mu(q)/s_E(q)}$ and reducing~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ after fixing~$L$, the rescaled $L^2$ norm and $C^\alpha$ norm of $u-\ell_q$ on~$q$ are therefore at most
\begin{equation*}
 CL^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma\nf{d+2}{2}}
 +C\epsilon_{\rm hom}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}+\gamma}
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 CL^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma(\nf{d-2}{2}+\alpha)}\,,
\end{equation*}
respectively. Interpolate between them with the exponent $(\alpha-\beta)/(\alpha+d/2)$ and apply the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values on the good cell, where the factor in it is comparable to~$s_E(q)$. The spatial and coefficient factors cancel, giving
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)
 \leq C\bigl(L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma\nf{d+2}{2}}
          +\epsilon_{\rm hom}L^{\nf{d+\zeta}{2}+\gamma}\bigr)^{\frac{2(\alpha-\beta)}{\alpha+d/2}}
       L^{2(1-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{\alpha+d/2})(\nf{d+\zeta}{2}-\gamma(\nf{d-2}{2}+\alpha))}\mu(q).
\end{equation*}
At~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}=0$, half the exponent of~$L$ is the left side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-33}, which is negative. First choose~$L$ to make this contribution smaller than~$\nf\varrho4$. Next choose $\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ so small that the contribution of the error of the harmonic approximation is comparably small. Then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that the enlarged good event has the required density of bad cells along every chain. After the same normalization, the source terms are at most $C_Lc^{-\nf12}s_E(q)^{-\nf12}r\|f\|_{C^1}\leq K_{L,\omega}c^{-\nf12}r^{1-\nf\eta2}\|f\|_{C^1}$, by $\mu(q)\geq cr^d$ and~\eqref{eq:mfd-3}. They tend to zero because $\eta<2$. A small~$h_0$ makes them smaller than~$\nf\varrho4$. The left side of~\eqref{eq:mfd-33} tends to~$-(1-\beta)/(1+d/2)<0$ as~$\alpha,\gamma\uparrow1$ and~$\zeta\downarrow0$, so these choices are possible in every dimension.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Strict improvement of a comparison constant}

We now combine concentration for affine boundary values with the affine approximation of the solution's boundary values to rule out~$\Delta>0$. Let $\mathcal U=\{n:c_{H_1n}\leq(m+M)/2\}$. Either this set or its complement has upper density at least~$\nf12$. Fix $\epsilon=\nf18$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-32}. On a good cell~$q$ at an $L$-adic level in~$\mathcal U$, for every affine~$\ell$,
\begin{equation*}
 B_q(\ell)\coloneqq M\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)-\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)\geq
 (M-c_k-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\geq\frac38\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since both minimal energies are taken over the same class of admissible functions, $0\leq B_q\leq\Delta\Lambda_{E,q}$ on all admissible boundary values. If $u$ is replaced on~$q$ by the $F$-harmonic function with the same boundary values~$b$, then the new function has $F$-energy $\Lambda_{F,q}(b)=M\Lambda_{E,q}(b)-B_q(b)$ on~$q$, which is at most $M\Gamma_E(u)(q)-B_q(b)$.

Harmonic replacements will give~$F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)$ for~$u=G_Ef$, once the errors they introduce are controlled. Density extends this inequality to the common domain, contradicting the optimality of~$M$. If the complementary set of $L$-adic levels has upper density at least~$\nf12$, then we interchange the forms and improve the optimal upper constant~$1/m$ instead. Both the energy saved by the replacements and the approximation error are proportional to~$\Delta$, so the tolerances can be chosen independently of its size.

Fix~$\beta\in(\nf12,1)$ and choose~$\alpha,\gamma,\zeta$ satisfying~\eqref{eq:mfd-33}. For $L=3^{H_1}$ as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}, set $b=H_1-\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor-4$. Then $b\geq1$, $H_1\geq b+2$ and $3^{-b}Lr$ is the side of the cube concentric with~$q$ used for the harmonic comparison in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}. With the dimensional constant~$C_d\geq1$ of the next lemma, the closure of this cube lies in~$p$ for every child at distance at least $C_d3^{-b}$ times the side of~$p$ from~$\partial p$, while $C_d3^{-b}\leq81C_dL^{\gamma-1}$ tends to zero as~$L$ grows. Averaging over finitely many translated grids makes the mass of the children close to the boundary of their parent small, for every measure.

\begin{lemma}[Shifted rational grids]\label{mfd:lem-shifts}
Let $L=3^{H_1}$ with $H_1\geq b+2$, and set $\mathsf M=3^b+1$. Translate the nested $L$-adic grid by the shifts $\mathcal S=\{0,1/\mathsf M,\ldots,(\mathsf M-1)/\mathsf M\}^d$. Consider the children at depth~$n$ whose closure is within distance $C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent. The dimensional constant in the counting bounds below may be larger than the constant~$C_d$ in this distance. Then, for every~$x$ and every $n\geq1$,
\begin{equation*}
 \mathsf M^{-d}\#\{\sigma\in\mathcal S:\ \text{the depth-$n$ child of }x
 \text{ in grid }\sigma\text{ is within distance }C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}\text{ of the boundary of its parent}\}\leq2d\Bigl(C_d3^{-b}+\frac1L+\frac1{\mathsf M}\Bigr)
 \leq C_d3^{-b}\,.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, for every finite Borel measure~$\mu$ on~$Q$, the average over $\sigma\in\mathcal S$ of the mass of the cells at depth~$n$ within distance $C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent is at most $C_d3^{-b}\mu(Q)$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix a coordinate~$i$. Relative to its parent at depth $n-1$, the point~$x$ has fractional coordinate $\{L^{n-1}(x_i-\sigma_i)\}$. Since $\gcd(L,\mathsf M)=1$, multiplication by~$L^{n-1}$ permutes the residues modulo~$\mathsf M$. As $\sigma_i$ ranges over the $\mathsf M$ shifts, these fractional coordinates form an equally spaced set of $\mathsf M$ points with a common rotation. If the child is within this distance of the boundary of its parent in coordinate~$i$, then $x_i$ is within $(C_d3^{-b}L+1)L^{-n}$ of a face of the parent. Its fractional coordinate then lies in two intervals of total length at most $2C_d3^{-b}+2/L$. A rotated equally spaced set has at most~$2\mathsf M(C_d3^{-b}+1/L)+2$ points in these two intervals. We divide by~$\mathsf M$, sum over the $d$ coordinates, and integrate the pointwise bound against~$\mu$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Good cells carry a fixed fraction of the mass]\label{mfd:lem-mass}
Suppose that~$\mathcal U$ has upper density at least~$\nf12$. Fix~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$ and~$c>0$, put~$u=G_Ef$ and~$\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$, let~$\epsilon_{\rm coll}>0$, and assume that~$\theta+L^{-\zeta}+C_d3^{-b}+\epsilon_{\rm coll}\leq\nf18$. Then, at $L$-adic levels~$n\in\mathcal U$ of arbitrarily large depth, there are shifts~$\sigma\in\mathcal S$ and finite families~$\mathcal Q$ of cells at level~$n$ in the grid~$\sigma$ such that~$\mu(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq\mu(Q)/8$. Every cell~$q\in\mathcal Q$, of side~$r$ and with parent~$p$, is good, satisfies~$\mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)$, and has its concentric enlargement of side~$3^{\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor+4}r$ with closure in~$p$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
At one $L$-adic level, a child that violates $\mu(p)\leq L^{d+\zeta}\mu(q)$ has mass less than $L^{-(d+\zeta)}\mu(p)$, and each parent has at most~$L^d$ children. Thus the total mass of these children is at most $L^{-\zeta}\mu(Q)$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-24} for each of the $\mathsf M^d$ grids, the mass of the bad cells over $J$ $L$-adic levels is at most $(\theta J+B_\omega^\sigma)\mu(Q)$. The maximum over the finitely many grids of the random constants~$B^\sigma_\omega$ is finite on the intersection of the events of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} for these grids. After division by~$J$, this maximum is absorbed in the $o(1)$ below. By Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-shifts}, the mass of the cells within distance $C_d3^{-b}L^{1-n}$ of the boundary of their parent, averaged over~$\sigma$, is at most $C_d3^{-b}\mu(Q)$ at every level.

We omit a neighbourhood of~$\partial Q$ of mass at most $\epsilon_{\rm coll}\mu(Q)$. At $L$-adic levels of large depth, the parents of the remaining points lie inside~$Q$. Along depths~$J$ with $\#(\mathcal U\cap\{1,\ldots,J\})\geq J/2-o(J)$, we sum the mass of the good cells with enlargement in the parent that satisfy the bound on their parents over~$n\in\mathcal U\cap\{1,\ldots,J\}$ and~$\sigma\in\mathcal S$, and divide by~$J|\mathcal S|$. This normalized sum is at least
\begin{equation*}
 \Bigl[\frac12-\epsilon_{\rm coll}-\theta-L^{-\zeta}-C_d3^{-b}
 -o(1)\Bigr]\mu(Q)>\frac{\mu(Q)}{4}
\end{equation*}
for large~$J$. A finite average that exceeds $\mu(Q)/4$ has a summand that exceeds it, and since every fixed finite set of levels contributes~$o(J)$, such levels can be taken of arbitrarily large depth.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Harmonic replacement]\label{mfd:lem-replace}
Let~$u\in D(E^Q)\cap C(\overline Q)$ and let~$\mathcal Q$ be a finite disjoint family of cells of side at most~$h$, whose threefold enlargements lie in~$Q$. Assume that~$u|_{\partial q}\in C^\alpha$ for every~$q\in\mathcal Q$, with~$\alpha>1/2$, and let $u^{\mathcal Q}$ be the function equal to the $E$-harmonic extension of $u|_{\partial q}$ on each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and to~$u$ elsewhere. Then $u-u^{\mathcal Q}$ is the $E$-orthogonal projection of~$u$ onto $\bigoplus_qD(E^q)$, and $E(u)=E(u^{\mathcal Q})+E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})$. If moreover $u=G_Ef$, then $E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})\leq Kh^{\nf32}\|f\|_2^2$. In particular, $\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}[\Gamma_E(u)(q)-\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})] =E(u-u^{\mathcal Q})\to0$ as $h\to0$, uniformly over the choice of~$\mathcal Q$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Euler equations and strong locality identify $w=u-u^{\mathcal Q}$ as the stated orthogonal projection and give both energy identities. Recovery sequences and~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} give $\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2\leq KE(v)$; they also show that $D(E^q)\subset H^{\nf34}(q)$ on each cell and that its functions have zero trace on~$\partial q$. The fractional Poincar\'e inequality for zero-trace functions, rescaled to~$q$, gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|w\|_2^2
 \leq Ch^{\nf32}\sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}[w]_{H^{\nf34}(q)}^2
 \leq Ch^{\nf32}[w]_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}^2
 \leq Kh^{\nf32}E(w)\,.
\end{equation*}
Here the seminorm is the Gagliardo double integral. Finally, for $u=G_Ef$, $E(w)=E(u,w)=\langle f,w\rangle\leq\|f\|_2\|w\|_2$, which proves the estimate.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Proportionality]\label{mfd:thm-prop}
The optimal comparison constants coincide: $\Delta=0$. Hence there exists a deterministic $c\in[C_0^{-1},C_0]$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-36}
 F=cE\,.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Suppose for a contradiction that $\Delta>0$.

\emph{Step 1.} We show that $\mathcal U$ having upper density at least~$\nf12$ contradicts the definition of~$M$. Fix a cube~$Q$ in~$\mathcal C$, $u=G_Ef$ with smooth~$f$, $c>0$, $\mu=\Gamma_E(u)+c\,dx$ and choose $\varrho\leq3/1024$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-affine}. Choose the parameters in the order above, enlarging~$L$ so that $\theta+L^{-\zeta}+C_d3^{-b}+\epsilon_{\rm coll}\leq\nf18$ with $b=H_1-\lfloor\gamma H_1\rfloor-4$, before fixing~$\epsilon_{\rm hom}$ and~$\gmcdelta$. Let $\mathcal Q$ be the family of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-mass} at an $L$-adic level of large depth, with maximal side~$h$. For each cell~$q$ of this family, the triangle inequalities for $\sqrt{B_q}$ and $\sqrt{\Lambda_{E,q}}$, applied to $u|_{\partial q}=\ell_q+(u|_{\partial q}-\ell_q)$, give $B_q(u|_{\partial q})\geq\Delta[\nf{3}{16}\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})-4\varrho\mu(q)]$, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-34}. Summing over $q\in\mathcal Q$ and using Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-replace}, we obtain
\begin{align*}
 \sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}B_q(u|_{\partial q})
 &\geq\Delta\Bigl[\frac{3}{16}\sum_q\Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})-4\varrho\mu(Q)\Bigr]\\
 &\geq\Delta\Bigl[\frac{3}{16}\Gamma_E(u)\bigl(\textstyle\bigcup\mathcal Q\bigr)
 -\frac{3}{16}Kh^{\nf32}\|f\|_2^2-4\varrho(E(u)+c|Q|)\Bigr]\,.
\end{align*}
Since $\mu(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq\mu(Q)/8$, we have $\Gamma_E(u)(\bigcup\mathcal Q)\geq E(u)/8-7c|Q|/8$, and with $\varrho\leq3/1024$ this gives
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{q\in\mathcal Q}B_q(u|_{\partial q})\geq\Delta\bigl[\frac{3}{256}E(u)-Cc|Q|-o_h(1)\bigr]\,.
\end{equation*}
Now $u\in D(F)$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-26}. Let $v$ be the function equal to the $F$-harmonic extension of~$u|_{\partial q}$ on each $q\in\mathcal Q$ and to~$u$ elsewhere. By strong locality and the bound $\Gamma_F\leq M\Gamma_E$ off the cells,
\begin{equation*}
 F(v)\leq M\Gamma_E(u)\bigl(Q\setminus\textstyle\bigcup\mathcal Q\bigr)+\sum_q\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})
 =ME(u)-\sum_q\bigl[M\Gamma_E(u)(q)-\Lambda_{F,q}(u|_{\partial q})\bigr]\leq ME(u)-\sum_qB_q(u|_{\partial q})\,,
\end{equation*}
where the last inequality uses $\Gamma_E(u)(q)\geq \Lambda_{E,q}(u|_{\partial q})$. As $h\to0$ along the $L$-adic levels of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-mass}, the maximum principle bounds $\|v-u\|_\infty$ by the modulus of continuity of~$u$ at scale~$h$, which tends to zero. Thus $v\to u$ in~$L^2$. Lower semicontinuity of~$F$ gives $F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)+C\Delta c|Q|$.

The tolerances and~$\epsilon_{\rm coll}$ do not depend on~$c$. Letting $c\downarrow0$ gives $F(u)\leq(M-3\Delta/256)E(u)$ for every $u=G_E^Qf$ with smooth~$f$, on every cube~$Q$ in~$\mathcal C$. Step~4 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} extends the bound to $D(E^Q)$ for every cube~$Q$ of~$\mathcal C$. This contradicts the definition of~$M$ as an infimum over all these cubes.

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the complement of~$\mathcal U$ having upper density at least~$\nf12$ contradicts the definition of~$m$. On good cells at $L$-adic levels outside~$\mathcal U$, we have $\Lambda_{F,q}(\ell)\geq(c_k-\epsilon\Delta)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)\geq(m+3\Delta/8)\Lambda_{E,q}(\ell)$. We regard $F$ as the first form and $E$ as the second. The comparison constants of~$E$ relative to~$F$ are $1/M\leq1/m$, with difference $1/m-1/M$, and the gain at affine boundary values is at least $(3/8)(1/m-1/M)$, because $m+3\Delta/8\leq M$. The argument of Step~1, with the solutions $G_Ff$ for smooth~$f$, improves the optimal upper comparison constant~$1/m$ of this pair, which contradicts the optimality of~$m$.

Hence $\Delta=0$, so $F=mE$, and $c=m$ is deterministic by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-endpoints}.
\end{proof}

We now show that the proportionality constant~$c$ in Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-prop} is one, using the fact that the homogenized matrix of the stationary coefficients~$A_N^0$ is the identity.

\subsection{Determination of the proportionality constant}
\label{mfd:sec-scalar}

The coefficients~$A_N^0$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization} are stationary, have range of dependence at most~$\sqrt d$, and have homogenized matrix~$I$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}.

\begin{theorem}[Uniform qualitative homogenization]\label{mfd:thm-eta}
For every unit vector~$e$,
\begin{equation*}
 \lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_N\E J(\cu_k,e,e;A_N^0)=0.
\end{equation*}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We obtain uniform moment bounds and choose cutoffs for which the expected energy changes little across a long interval of scales. Fix a sufficiently large even integer~$\xi$. Proposition~\ref{p.coarse.grained.bound}, subadditivity~\eqref{e.subadditivity.J} and stationarity give
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{N,k\geq0}\left\|\sup_{|e|=1}J(\cu_k,e,e;A_N^0)\right\|_{L^\xi}<\infty.
\end{equation*}
Moreover, the family
\begin{equation*}
 \left\{\Lambda_{1/4}(\cu_k;A_N^0)+\lambda_{1/4}(\cu_k;A_N^0)^{-1}:N,k\geq0\right\}
\end{equation*}
is tight. This follows from Lemma~\ref{l.multiscale.response.large.cubes}, with $s=\nf18$, $r=2$ and the initial error $C\xi\log(2+\xi)\gmcdelta^2$, followed by~\eqref{e.bound.Lambdas.by.Es} and weighted Cauchy--Schwarz. Choose~$\xi$ large enough for that lemma and then reduce~$\gmcdelta$.

Write
\begin{equation*}
 f_N(k)=\sum_{j=1}^d\E J(\cu_k,e_j,e_j;A_N^0)
       =\frac12\E\operatorname{tr}\bigl(A_N^0(\cu_k)+(A_N^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_k)-2I\bigr).
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.subadditivity.a} and~\eqref{e.annealed.matrices.def}, $\E A_N^0(\cu_k)$ and $\E(A_N^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_k)$ decrease to~$I$. Thus $f_N(k)\geq0$ decreases in~$k$, and the limit of $\sup_N f_N(k)$ as $k\to\infty$ exists. Choose~$N_k$ with $f_{N_k}(4k)\geq\sup_N f_N(4k)-k^{-1}$. Then
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl(f_{N_k}(n)-f_{N_k}(4k)\bigr)
 \leq\sup_N f_N(k)-\sup_N f_N(4k)+k^{-1}\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation*}

\emph{Step 2.} We show that the coarse-grained matrices~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$ concentrate and that the expectations of the coarse-grained matrices~$A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)$ converge along these scales. By subadditivity, the average of $(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(z+\cu_k)$ over the scale-$k$ subcubes of~$\cu_n$, minus~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$, is positive semidefinite and has expected trace at most $2(f_{N_k}(k)-f_{N_k}(n))$. This difference tends to zero in~$L^1$, uniformly for $k\leq n\leq4k$. The moment bound of Step~1 and~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrix.deviation.by.J} upgrade this to~$L^2$. For $n\geq2k$, the variance of the average is at most $C3^{-d(n-k)}$, because the finite range of dependence splits the subcubes into a dimensional number of classes of independent cubes. Consequently, after extracting a subsequence, there are deterministic matrices $P,R\geq I$ such that
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl\|\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)-P\bigr\|\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad
 \sup_{2k\leq n\leq4k}\bigl\|(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)-R\bigr\|_{L^2}\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation*}
For the first convergence, extract a limit of~$\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_{4k})$ and note that~$\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_n)-\E A_{N_k}^0(\cu_{4k})$ is positive semidefinite with trace at most $2(f_{N_k}(n)-f_{N_k}(4k))$, which tends to zero by Step~1.

\emph{Step 3.} We use the estimates of Section~\ref{s.self.similar} to show that both limiting matrices equal the identity. Fix~$p\in\Rd$ and let $v=v(\cdot,\cu_{4k},p,R^{-1}p;A_{N_k}^0)$. Apply~\eqref{e.Besov.norms.gradient} with $s=\nf14$ and $L_0=2k$. The expected gaps in the subadditivity inequality~\eqref{e.subadditivity.a} tend to zero uniformly over $2k\leq n\leq4k$ by Step~1. The mean-square differences of the coarse-grained matrices~$(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_n)$ tend to zero by Step~2 and stationarity. The weights of the two sums in~\eqref{e.Besov.norms.gradient} are summable, and $(A_{N_k}^0)_*^{-1}(\cu_{4k})R^{-1}p-p\to0$ in~$L^2$. Since the coarse-grained ellipticity constants are tight, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 3^{-4k}\|\nabla v\|_{\Hminusul(\cu_{4k})}\longrightarrow0
 \qquad\text{in probability}.
\end{equation*}
Now~\eqref{e.J.L.bound}, with the same tight constants and the vanishing gaps in the subadditivity inequality, gives
\begin{equation*}
 J(\cu_{4k-1},p,R^{-1}p;A_{N_k}^0)\longrightarrow0
 \qquad\text{in probability}.
\end{equation*}
The moment bounds make these energies uniformly integrable, so their expectations tend to zero. By~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrices}, the limit of these expectations is also $\nf12p\cdot(P-R^{-1})p$. Thus $P=R^{-1}$. Since $P,R\geq I$, both equal~$I$, and $f_{N_k}(4k)\to0$. The choice of~$N_k$ then shows that the limit of $\sup_Nf_N(k)$ is zero.
\end{proof}

Let $\Lambda^0_{N,Q}$ denote the minimal energy with given boundary values for~$A_N^0$. The bound~\eqref{e.coarse.grained.matrix.deviation.by.J} and Cauchy--Schwarz turn Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-eta} into
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-37}
 \lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_N\E\left|
 \frac{\Lambda^0_{N,3^k\cu_0}(p\cdot x)}{3^{dk}}-|p|^2\right|=0
 \qquad(p\in\Rd).
\end{equation}

\begin{theorem}[Identification of the scalar]\label{mfd:thm-c1}
The scalar~$c$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-36} equals one. Consequently, all subsequential limit forms coincide.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $E^0=e^{-H}E$ and $F^0=e^{-H}F$ be the forms obtained by removing the factor~$e^H$ with the rule~\eqref{eq:mfd-21} for continuous weights, applied to the random continuous weight~$e^{-H}$ on a compact cube. They are subsequential limits of the same normalized sequence, whose coefficient is~$A_N^0$. Fix $R$ and $p\neq0$. By Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-boundary}, $\Lambda_{N,R\cu_0}^0(p\cdot x)$ converges along the subsequence that defines~$E$ to $\Lambda_{E^0,R\cu_0}(p\cdot x)$. The uniform moments of these energies give uniform integrability. Hence
\begin{equation*}
 \E\Bigl|\frac{\Lambda_{E^0,R\cu_0}(p\cdot x)}{R^d}-|p|^2\Bigr|
 \leq\sup_N\E\Bigl|\frac{\Lambda^0_{N,R\cu_0}(p\cdot x)}{R^d}-|p|^2\Bigr|\,.
\end{equation*}
The same holds for~$F^0$ along its subsequence. Letting $R\to\infty$ through triadic values,~\eqref{eq:mfd-37} shows that both $\E|\Lambda_{E^0,R\cu_0}(p\cdot x)/R^d-|p|^2|$ and its analogue for~$F^0$ tend to zero. But $F^0=cE^0$ gives $\Lambda_{F^0,R\cu_0}=c\Lambda_{E^0,R\cu_0}$ on every cube, so $c|p|^2=|p|^2$ and $c=1$.

Since all subsequential limit forms are therefore equal, and the energies of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-response-compact} are strongly relatively compact, every subsequence has a further subsequence converging to the same limit. Hence these energies, for the countable family of cubes and data fixed in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, converge along the full sequence of cutoffs.
\end{proof}
\section{Convergence of the process}
\label{sec:mfd-process-convergence}

In this section we prove Theorem~\ref{t.scaling.limit}(i). Combining the unique limit form of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} with the limit of the reversible measures, we prove that the path laws converge in probability, and we then upgrade this convergence to almost-sure convergence.
\begin{enumerate}
\item After constructing the diffusions from every starting point (Section~\ref{s.fixed.coefficient}), we show that the measures~$\mu_N$ converge and that their mass in small balls is bounded uniformly in the cutoff (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-speed}). This bound lets us pass to the limit in the resolvent equations of the processes killed on leaving a cube, even though the sources in these equations are given by the random measures. Mollifying these sources and applying the H\"older estimates of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} gives convergence in probability of the killed resolvents, uniformly in the starting point.
\item Together with tightness of the path laws (Section~\ref{s.tightness}), the convergence of the killed resolvents identifies the limiting path laws and gives convergence in probability (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-paths}).
\item We then compare the energies at two finite cutoffs. On a small cube, rescaling shows that the two energies differ essentially by the ratio of their deterministic normalizations. If these ratios were bounded away from one on a positive proportion of scales, the energy of a fixed affine boundary value would stay a fixed factor away from its limit, contradicting the convergence in probability. Once such scales are excluded, the comparison holds with summable error probabilities, and the Borel--Cantelli lemma gives almost-sure convergence of the finite-volume energies (Section~\ref{mfd:sec-finite-comparison}). Bounds that hold for all cutoffs on a single event of probability one then carry this almost-sure convergence over to the Dirichlet forms, the killed resolvents and the path laws (Sections~\ref{mfd:sec-pathwise-bounds}--\ref{mfd:sec-as-paths}).
\end{enumerate}
The conventions of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} remain in force, except that Sections~\ref{s.fixed.coefficient} and~\ref{s.tightness} work with the diffusions~$X$ and~$X^{(L)}$ and their coefficients~$\a$ and~$\a_L$.

\subsection{Construction of the diffusions}
\label{s.fixed.coefficient}

We construct the diffusions from their elliptic resolvents. By Lemma~\ref{l.finite.cutoff.coefficient.convergence}, the coefficient~$\a$ is positive and locally~$C^{1,1}$. The same holds for~$\a_L$. The logarithmic derivative bound~\eqref{e.finite.cutoff.log.derivative.growth} gives at most linear growth of the drift, uniformly in~$L$. For either coefficient, the weak solutions of
\begin{equation*}
 \lambda\a u-\nabla\cdot(\a\nabla u)=\a f
\end{equation*}
on increasing bounded cubes, with zero boundary values, converge to the whole-space resolvent. Outside large cubes, comparison with supersolutions constructed from the drift growth bound shows that the solution vanishes at infinity for compactly supported data. Contraction extends the construction to~$C_0(\Rd)$. The maximum principle gives positivity and the bound~$\|R_\lambda\|\leq\lambda^{-1}$; uniqueness gives the resolvent identity. For compactly supported smooth data, the elliptic equation gives~$\lambda R_\lambda f\to f$ as~$\lambda\to\infty$, and contraction extends this to~$C_0(\Rd)$. Hille--Yosida~\citep[Theorem~19.11]{Kallenberg} therefore gives a Feller semigroup. Its positive operators have transition kernels by the Riesz representation theorem.

On each bounded cube, local ellipticity and regularity give the short-time bounds needed to realize the transition kernels on continuous paths. Kolmogorov extension, followed by continuous extension from a countable dense set of times, constructs the path laws. We denote the resulting diffusions for~$\a$ and~$\a_L$ by~$X$ and~$X^{(L)}$, respectively, initially defined up to their lifetimes. The Feller property and approximation of stopping times by dyadic times give the strong Markov property. Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness} shows that~$X$ and every~$X^{(L)}$ are conservative from every starting point.

The semigroup is symmetric on~$L^2(\Rd,\a\,dx)$, and its resolvents agree with those of the closure of
\begin{equation}
 f\longmapsto\int_{\Rd}\a|\nabla f|^2\,dx,
 \qquad f\in C_c^\infty(\Rd).
 \label{e.fixed.clock.form}
\end{equation}
This follows by testing the weak equation and exhausting~$\Rd$ by cubes. Its generator is~$\a^{-1}\nabla\cdot(\a\nabla)$. Restricting the form to a cube with zero boundary values identifies its resolvents with those of the diffusion killed on leaving the cube~\citep[Theorems~4.4.2--4.4.3]{FOT}. Thus the killed resolvents are the zero-boundary weak solutions.

These laws also describe the usual SDE construction. Since~$\nabla\log\a$ is locally Lipschitz, there is a unique maximal strong solution from every~$x\in\Rd$ of
\begin{equation}
 dX_t=\sqrt2\,dW_t+\nabla\log\a(X_t)\,dt,
 \label{e.fixed.clock.sde}
\end{equation}
where~$W$ is standard Brownian motion on~$\Rd$. Fix a bounded cube~$Q$,~$\lambda>0$ and~$f\in C_c^\infty(Q)$, and let~$u$
solve~$\lambda\a u-\nabla\cdot(\a\nabla u)=\a f$ in~$Q$, with zero
boundary values. Interior elliptic regularity and It\^o's formula,
stopped first strictly inside~$Q$, give, after exhaustion and removal
of the deterministic time cutoff,
\begin{equation*}
 u(x)=\mathbf E_x^{\mathrm{SDE}}
 \left[\int_0^{\tau_Q}e^{-\lambda t}f(X_t)\,dt\right],
 \qquad x\in Q.
\end{equation*}
Here~$\mathbf E_x^{\mathrm{SDE}}$ denotes expectation for the maximal SDE
solution. Thus its killed resolvents agree with those of the analytic
construction. Uniqueness of Laplace transforms identifies the killed
semigroups, and the Markov property identifies their path laws.
Exhausting~$\Rd$ identifies the laws up to their lifetimes, for every
starting point. The same argument applies to~$\a_L$ and to the space
and time rescalings in~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator};
Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness} makes these lifetimes infinite. For an open set~$U$, write~$\tau_U=\inf\{t\geq0:X_t\notin U\}$ and let~$\tau_U^{(L)}$ be the exit time of~$X^{(L)}$ from~$U$. The diffusion~$X^{(L)}$ has generator~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.generator}.

\subsection{The reversible measure and resolvent convergence in probability}
\label{mfd:sec-speed}

Write~$E$ for the unique limit form; it is measurable with respect to the random fields. Along each subsequence of the Skorokhod representation, the forms~$E_N^Q$ Mosco-converge to~$E^Q$ on~$L^2(Q,dx)$, and their coercivity constants are bounded.

\subsubsection{The chaos martingale and the mass of small balls}

Recall the measures~$\mu_N^0$ and the reversible measures~$\mu_N=e^H\mu_N^0$ from the paragraph following~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}.

\begin{lemma}[Positive moments of the chaos]
\label{mfd:lem-chaos-moments}
Let~$p\geq1$ be an integer. There exist~$C_p<\infty$ and~$c_p>0$ such that, if~$\gmcdelta\leq c_p$, then, for every~$N\geq0$ and every cube~$q$ of side~$r\leq1$ contained in a fixed bounded region,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-38}
\E\bigl[\mu_N(q)^p\bigr]\leq C_p\,r^{dp-C_p\gmcdelta^2}\,.
\end{equation}
Moreover,~$N\mapsto \mu_N^0(q)$ is a nonnegative martingale, and~$\mu_N(q)$ is a nonnegative martingale conditionally on~$H$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since~$\E e^{\g_{-j}(x)}=e^{\tau^2}$ for every~$x$ and the fields are independent, integration against fixed nonnegative test functions shows that~$\mu_N^0$ is a measure-valued martingale. The sum~$H$ of the fields with scales larger than one is independent of the fields at scales at most one, so~$\mu_N=e^H\mu_N^0$ is a martingale given~$H$. On a bounded region the factor~$e^{pH}$ is controlled by~$\E e^{p\sup H}<\infty$, which follows from the exponential moments of the~$C^{1,1}$ norm of~$H$ on compact cubes in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-infrared}. It therefore suffices to prove~\eqref{eq:mfd-38} for~$\mu_N^0$.

For~$p\geq2$ we expand
\begin{equation*}
\E \mu_N^0(q)^p=\int_{q^p}\E\prod_{i=1}^p\prod_{j=0}^N
e^{\g_{-j}(x_i)-\tau^2}\,dx\,.
\end{equation*}
The field~$\g_{-j}$ has range of dependence~$C3^{-j}$ by Assumption~\ref{a.g1}. If all pairwise distances~$|x_i-x_k|$ exceed that range, then the~$p$ factors are independent and the normalized expectation equals one. For each of the remaining fields, H\"older's inequality gives $\E\prod_ie^{\g_{-j}(x_i)-\tau^2}\leq\E e^{p\g_0(0)}e^{-p\tau^2}\leq e^{Cp^2\gmcdelta^2}$ by~\eqref{e.coefficient.field.regularity}. The number of fields for which this separation condition fails is at most $C+\sum_{i<k}\log_3^+(C/|x_i-x_k|)$, so the integrand is bounded by $C\prod_{i<k}(|x_i-x_k|\wedge1)^{-a}$ with~$a=Cp^2\gmcdelta^2$. There are~$p(p-1)/2$ pair factors. H\"older's inequality with exponent~$p(p-1)/2$ and the bound $\int_{q\times q}|x-y|^{-ap(p-1)/2}\,dx\,dy\leq Cr^{2d-ap(p-1)/2}$, valid when~$ap(p-1)/2<d$, give~$\E \mu_N^0(q)^p\leq C_pr^{dp-ap(p-1)/2}$. Since~$ap(p-1)/2\leq Cp^4\gmcdelta^2$, the condition~$ap(p-1)/2<d$ holds once~$\gmcdelta\leq c_p$, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-38} follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Mass of small balls and the limiting measure]
\label{mfd:prop-chaos-growth}
Let~$\epsilon\in(0,1)$, let~$p$ be an integer with~$p\epsilon>d$, and assume that~$\gmcdelta\leq c_p$ and~$C_p\gmcdelta^2<p\epsilon-d$, with~$c_p$ and~$C_p$ as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-chaos-moments}. Then, almost surely,~$\mu_N$ converges locally weakly to a locally finite measure~$\mu$, and there is a random constant~$K_\mu$ with~$\E K_\mu^p<\infty$ such that, for every~$x$ in the fixed bounded region, every~$0<r\leq1$ and every~$N\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-39}
\mu_N(B_r(x))\leq K_\mu r^{d-\epsilon}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\mu(B_r(x))\leq K_\mu r^{d-\epsilon}\,.
\end{equation}
Almost surely, the measure~$\mu$ has full support and no atoms, and~$\mu(\partial Q)=0$ for every cube~$Q$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a dyadic cube~$q$ of side~$2^{-k}$, Doob's inequality~\citep[Proposition~7.16]{Kallenberg} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-38} give $\E\sup_N\mu_N(q)^p\leq(p/(p-1))^p\sup_N\E\mu_N(q)^p \leq C2^{-k(dp-C_p\gmcdelta^2)}$. Let~$C$ be the constant in the covering below and put
\begin{equation*}
 K_\mu^p=C^p\sum_{k\geq0}\ \sum_{q\ \text{of side }2^{-k}}
 2^{kp(d-\epsilon)}\sup_N\mu_N(q)^p\,.
\end{equation*}
There are~$C2^{dk}$ cubes of side~$2^{-k}$ in the region, so $\E K_\mu^p\leq C\sum_k2^{k(d+C_p\gmcdelta^2-p\epsilon)}<\infty$ by the choice of~$p$ and then of~$\gmcdelta$. A ball~$B_r(x)$ with~$2^{-k-1}<r\leq2^{-k}$ is covered by a number of cubes of side~$2^{-k}$ that depends only on~$d$. This gives the first bound in~\eqref{eq:mfd-39} with one random constant for all~$N$, $x$ and~$r\leq1$.

Since~$p>1$, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-38} at this order gives uniform integrability of~$\mu_N(C)$. The martingale convergence theorem~\citep[Theorem~7.18 and Corollary~7.22]{Kallenberg} gives almost-sure and~$L^p$ convergence against every function in a countable dense subset of~$C_c(\Rd)$. The local mass bound above gives vague relative compactness~\citep[Theorem~A2.3]{Kallenberg}, so these limits determine a single locally finite measure~$\mu$, and~$\mu_N\to\mu$ locally weakly almost surely. The bound for~$\mu$ follows from the portmanteau theorem applied to open balls. The measure~$\mu$ has no atoms by~\eqref{eq:mfd-39}. To prove that~$\mu(\partial Q)=0$, cover~$\partial Q$ by~$Cr^{1-d}$ balls of radius~$r$. This gives $\mu(\partial Q)\leq CK_\mu r^{1-\epsilon}\to0$.

It remains to prove that~$\mu$ has full support. Set~$\mu^0=e^{-H}\mu$. Fix an open cube~$Q$ and~$k\geq0$, and write $\mu^0=e^{\sum_{j=0}^k\g_{-j}-(k+1)\tau^2}\mu^{0,(>k)}$, where~$\mu^{0,(>k)}$ is the limiting measure built in the same way from the fields~$\g_{-j}$ with~$j>k$ only. Since $\E \mu^{0,(>k)}(\partial Q)=|\partial Q|=0$ and the factor in front of~$\mu^{0,(>k)}$ is a fixed continuous positive function, weak convergence shows that the two sides of this identity give the same mass to~$Q$. Hence the events~$\{\mu^0(Q)=0\}$ and~$\{\mu^{0,(>k)}(Q)=0\}$ coincide up to null sets for every~$k$, so~$\{\mu^0(Q)=0\}$ is a tail event of the independent fields. Its probability is therefore~$0$ or~$1$, and it is~$0$ because~$\E \mu^0(Q)=|Q|>0$ by uniform integrability. The positive continuous factor~$e^H$ gives the full support of~$\mu$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{Traces on the reversible measures}

We choose~$\epsilon<1$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-39}, so that the trace inequality~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} applies with~$t=d-\epsilon>d-1$ to every~$\mu_N$ and to~$\mu$, with the constant~$C(K_\mu+\mu_N(Q))$. We extend functions in~$H^1_0(Q)$ by zero to~$\Rd$. For such a function~$w$, the zero extension satisfies $\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}(\Rd)}\leq C\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}(Q)}$, so~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-D-zeroext}
\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}(\Rd)}^2\leq K_NE_N^Q(w)\,,
\end{equation}
for every~$w\in H^1_0(Q)$, with a constant that is bounded along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation. Traces on~$\mu$ are defined by density: by~\eqref{eq:mfd-19}, the map sending a smooth function on~$\overline Q$ to its class in~$L^2(\mu)$ is bounded from~$H^{\nf12}$ to~$L^2(\mu)$, and it extends by continuity.

\begin{lemma}[Varying-measure convergence]
\label{mfd:lem-varying-trace}
Let~$u_N\in H^1_0(Q)$ satisfy~$\sup_NE_N^Q(u_N)<\infty$ along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation and~$u_N\to u$ in~$L^2(Q,dx)$. Then, for every bounded continuous function~$f$ on~$\overline Q$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-40}
\int u_N^2\,d\mu_N\longrightarrow\int u^2\,d\mu
\quad\text{and}\quad
\int fu_N\,d\mu_N\longrightarrow\int fu\,d\mu\,.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By~\eqref{eq:mfd-D-zeroext} the zero extensions are bounded in~$H^{\nf34}(\Rd)$, and the interpolation inequality $\|w\|_{H^{\nf12}}\leq\|w\|_{L^2}^{\nf{1}{3}}\|w\|_{H^{\nf34}}^{\nf{2}{3}}$ upgrades the convergence in~$L^2$ to strong convergence in~$H^{\nf12}(\Rd)$. We write
\begin{equation*}
 \int u_N^2\,d\mu_N-\int u^2\,d\mu
 =\int(u_N-u)(u_N+u)\,d\mu_N+\Bigl(\int u^2\,d\mu_N-\int u^2\,d\mu\Bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
The first term tends to zero by Cauchy--Schwarz and~\eqref{eq:mfd-19}. For the second, we approximate~$u$ in~$H^{\nf12}$ by smooth functions~$\varphi$. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-19}, the resulting error is small uniformly over all~$\mu_N$ and~$\mu$, and $\int\varphi^2\,d\mu_N\to\int\varphi^2\,d\mu$ by weak convergence, since~$\mu(\partial Q)=0$. The proof of the second limit is the same.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The killed resolvent as a minimizer}

At finite cutoff the generator is symmetric in~$L^2(\mu_N)$ with form~$E_N^Q$. Since the density of~$\mu_N$ is bounded on~$\overline Q$ at every finite~$N$, it follows that, for~$\lambda>0$ and bounded~$f$, the killed resolvent~$u_N=R^Q_{N,\lambda}f$ is the unique minimizer over~$H^1_0(Q)$ of
\begin{equation*}
 E_N^Q(u)+\lambda\int u^2\,d\mu_N-2\int fu\,d\mu_N\,.
\end{equation*} It satisfies the sub-Markov bound $\|u_N\|_\infty\leq\|f\|_\infty/\lambda$, the weak equation $E_N^Q(u_N,\varphi)=\int(f-\lambda u_N)\varphi\,d\mu_N$, and, by testing with~$u_N$, the energy bound $E_N^Q(u_N)\leq\|f\|_\infty^2\mu_N(Q)/\lambda$.

\begin{proposition}[Passage of the minimization to the limit]
\label{mfd:prop-speed-resolvent}
Let~$f\in C(\overline Q)$ and~$\lambda>0$, and assume that, along the sequence in the Skorokhod representation, $E_N^Q$ Mosco-converges to~$E^Q$ and that~$E^Q$ satisfies the coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} with a finite constant. Then the functional
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-41}
u\longmapsto E^Q(u)+\lambda\int u^2\,d\mu-2\int fu\,d\mu
\end{equation}
has a unique minimizer~$u^*$ in the energy Hilbert space~$(D(E^Q),E^Q)$, and every~$L^2(dx)$ cluster point of the killed resolvents~$u_N=R^Q_{N,\lambda}f$ equals~$u^*$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The energy space is complete because $E^Q(u)\geq\|G^Q\|^{-1}\|u\|_{L^2}^2$ by the variational formula~\eqref{eq:mfd-18}. The trace from~$D(E^Q)$ to~$L^2(\mu)$ is bounded, since $\int u^2\,d\mu\leq C(K_\mu+\mu(Q))\|u\|_{H^{\nf12}}^2\leq CKE^Q(u)$ by~\eqref{eq:mfd-19} and the coercivity of~$E^Q$. The functional~\eqref{eq:mfd-41} is therefore continuous, strictly convex and coercive on the energy space, and the Riesz representation theorem gives a unique minimizer~$u^*$, which satisfies $E^Q(u^*,v)+\lambda\int u^*v\,d\mu=\int fv\,d\mu$ for every~$v\in D(E^Q)$.

To pass to the limit, note that the energies~$E_N^Q(u_N)$ are bounded, so, by~\eqref{eq:mfd-1}, a subsequence converges strongly in~$L^2(dx)$. The liminf inequality of Mosco convergence and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-varying-trace} show that the~$\liminf$ of the finite-cutoff functionals at~$u_N$ is at least the value of~\eqref{eq:mfd-41} at the limit. Since~$u_N$ minimizes the finite-cutoff functional, recovery sequences for every~$v\in D(E^Q)$, together with~\eqref{eq:mfd-40}, show that this~$\liminf$ is at most the value of~\eqref{eq:mfd-41} at~$v$. Hence the limit minimizes~\eqref{eq:mfd-41}, so it equals~$u^*$.
\end{proof}

Write~$R^Q_\lambda f$ for the minimizer~$u^*$.

\subsubsection{Uniform convergence of the killed resolvents}

To obtain uniform convergence we need H\"older bounds on the killed resolvents that are uniform in~$N$. The source in the resolvent equation is the measure~$(f-\lambda u_N)\mu_N$, whose density is not bounded uniformly in~$N$, so we mollify the source before applying the H\"older estimate for bounded sources.

\begin{proposition}[Uniform convergence of killed resolvents]
\label{mfd:prop-uniform-resolvent}
Assume the hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-speed-resolvent}. Assume also that solutions with zero Dirichlet boundary values and bounded sources satisfy the H\"older estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} with~$\alpha=1/2$ up to the boundary, with the supremum norm of the source on the right side and with a constant whose moments of the finitely many orders fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} are finite. Let~$\epsilon<1/[8(d+2)]$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-39}. Then, for every bounded continuous~$f$ and every~$\lambda>0$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:mfd-42}
\sup_{\overline Q}\bigl|R^Q_{N,\lambda}f-R^Q_\lambda f\bigr|\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{in probability}\,,
\end{equation}
and the limit vanishes on~$\partial Q$. There is a random constant~$K_Q$ with finite moments of these orders such that, for all~$\lambda>0$ and~$f\in C(\overline Q)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-uniform-lambda}
 \|R^Q_\lambda f\|_{C^{\nf14}(\overline Q)}\leq K_Q\|f\|_\infty.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Let~$u_N=R^Q_{N,\lambda}f$ and~$\zeta_N=(f-\lambda u_N)\mu_N$, so that $|\zeta_N|\leq2\|f\|_\infty\mu_N$ and $E_N^Q(u_N,\varphi)=\int\varphi\,d\zeta_N$. Fix~$0<r\leq1$, put~$R=r^{d+2}\leq1$, and let~$\eta_R$ be a mollifier at scale~$R$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We bound the mollified source and the error of the mollification. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-39},
\begin{equation*}
|\zeta_N*\eta_R(x)|\leq CR^{-d}|\zeta_N|(B_R(x))\leq CK_\mu R^{-\epsilon}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
For the error, we first use that, by Schur's test, $\|\zeta_N\|_{H^{-\nf14}}^2$ is at most $\|\zeta_N\|_{TV}$ times the supremum over~$x$ of the Bessel potential of order~$1/2$ of~$|\zeta_N|$ at~$x$. Splitting the Bessel potential into dyadic annuli and using~\eqref{eq:mfd-39} bounds this supremum, since~$\epsilon<1/2$. Hence $\|\zeta_N\|_{H^{-\nf14}}\leq K\|f\|_\infty$. Then the bound $\sup_\xi(1+|\xi|^2)^{-\nf12}\min(1,R|\xi|)^2\leq CR$ gives
\begin{equation*}
\|\zeta_N-\zeta_N*\eta_R\|_{H^{-\nf34}}\leq CR^{\nf12}\|\zeta_N\|_{H^{-\nf14}}
\leq KR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We compare~$u_N$ with the solution for the mollified source. Let~$v_{N,R}\in H^1_0(Q)$ solve $E_N^Q(v_{N,R},\varphi)=\int\varphi\,(\zeta_N*\eta_R)\,dx$. Its source is a bounded function of size~$KR^{-\epsilon}\|f\|_\infty$, so~\eqref{eq:mfd-5} with~$\alpha=1/2$ gives $\|v_{N,R}\|_{C^{\nf12}(\overline Q)}\leq KR^{-\epsilon}\|f\|_\infty$. The difference of the two equations and~\eqref{eq:mfd-D-zeroext} give
\begin{equation*}
E_N^Q(u_N-v_{N,R})=\langle\zeta_N-\zeta_N*\eta_R,u_N-v_{N,R}\rangle
\leq KR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty\|u_N-v_{N,R}\|_{H^{\nf34}}\leq KR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty E_N^Q(u_N-v_{N,R})^{\nf12}\,,
\end{equation*}
hence $\|u_N-v_{N,R}\|_{L^2}\leq KR^{\nf12}\|f\|_\infty$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We prove a H\"older bound for~$u_N$. By Steps~1 and~2, the mean-square oscillation of~$u_N$ on~$B_r(x)\cap Q$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
 K\bigl(r^{\nf12}R^{-\epsilon}+r^{-\nf{d}{2}}R^{\nf12}\bigr)\|f\|_\infty
 =K\bigl(r^{\nf{1}{2}-(d+2)\epsilon}+r\bigr)\|f\|_\infty\,,
\end{equation*}
and~$1/2-(d+2)\epsilon>3/8>1/4$. Using zero extensions across~$\partial Q$, Campanato's criterion gives $\|u_N\|_{C^{\nf14}(\overline Q)}\leq K\|f\|_\infty$ and~$u_N=0$ on~$\partial Q$. Here~$K$ is a product of finitely many random constants with finite moments of these orders, and it is therefore tight.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We prove convergence in probability. Fix two cutoff subsequences, and apply the Skorokhod representation of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form} to both of them jointly; the variables represented jointly include the fields~$(\g_j)_{j\in\Z}$, the reversible measures, the quadratic forms~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ with~$f\in\mathcal D$ and~$Q\in\mathcal C$, and the constants in Steps~1--3. The constants have tight laws. The limit forms of the two sequences coincide by Theorem~\ref{mfd:thm-c1}, and their limiting reversible measures are the same. The Arzel\`a--Ascoli theorem and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-speed-resolvent} therefore identify both uniform limits with the unique minimizer of the same functional.

Consequently, every pair of cutoff subsequences has a further pair of subsequences along which the joint laws of the two killed resolvents converge to a law supported on the diagonal of~$C(\overline Q)^2$. Since~$C(\overline Q)$ is Polish, the Gy\"ongy--Krylov criterion~\citep[Lemma~1.1]{GyongyKrylov1996} gives convergence in probability on the original probability space. The joint representation with these quadratic forms and the reversible measure identifies the limit with~$R^Q_\lambda f$. The constant in Step~3 is independent of~$\lambda$ and~$f$, which gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-uniform-lambda} and the zero boundary values.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Tightness}
\label{s.tightness}

In this subsection we prove Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness} (tightness in expectation and in probability). Section~\ref{mfd:sec-paths} uses both forms of tightness, together with the compact containment estimate~\eqref{tight:eq-containment}, to identify the limiting path law from its resolvents. The early-exit bound~\eqref{tight:eq-fast-exit} is used again in Section~\ref{lim:sec-process-measure} to bound mean exit times from below.

We first bound the probability of an early exit from a cube. After the rescaling introduced below, a cube of side~$3^m$ becomes~$\cu_0$ and time is measured in units of~$3^{2m}/\ahom_{m\wedge L}$. The process started in~$\cu_{-2}$ leaves~$\cu_0$ before time~$t$ with probability at most~$K\sqrt t$, where~$K$ has moments bounded uniformly in the cutoff, the cube size, and the position of the cube.

The proof bounds the Laplace transform of the exit time by one minus the solution of a resolvent problem with a cutoff function as source. A local maximum principle for subsolutions, with the Laplace parameter equal to~$1/t$, then gives the exit-probability bound. Since pointwise ellipticity is not uniform in the cutoff and the reversible measure becomes singular in the limit, we prove this maximum principle by Moser iteration with respect to the reversible measure. The iteration uses the mass bounds, the~$H^{\nf34}$ coercivity bound, and cutoff functions of Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static}. The energy measures of these cutoff functions satisfy an upper bound with the same power of the ball radius as the upper mass bound for the reversible measure. The exit-probability bound shows that, with high probability, the paths stay in a large cube up to a fixed time; applied at stopping times, it also controls their modulus of continuity.

\smallskip

Throughout this subsection we assume~\ref{a.g1}--\ref{a.g4} and that~$\gmcdelta$ is small enough, depending on the moment orders fixed below. We recall the diffusion~$X^{(L)}$ from~\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.coefficients}--\eqref{e.intro.cutoff.generator} and we set
\begin{equation*}
 Z_t^{(L)}\coloneqq3^{-L}X_{3^{2L}t/\ahom_L}^{(L)}\,,\qquad t\geq0\,.
\end{equation*}
We use rescaled coordinates for~$Z^{(L)}$ and physical coordinates for~$X^{(L)}$. All statements of this subsection also hold for the rescaled diffusion~$3^{-L}X_{3^{2L}t/\ahom_L}$ without cutoff.

\smallskip

For~$L,m\in\N_0$ and~$z\in\Rd$, consider the cube~$z+\cu_m$ in physical coordinates, and set
\begin{equation*}
 j\coloneqq m\wedge L,\qquad
 c\coloneqq\left(\frac{\a_L}{\a_j}\right)(z),\qquad
 d\mu\coloneqq c^{-1}\a_L(z+3^m x)\,dx,\qquad
 A\coloneqq\ahom_j^{-1}c^{-1}\a_L(z+3^m x)\,.
\end{equation*}
If~$m\geq L$, then~$c=1$. In the local coordinates~$x\mapsto z+3^mx$, which rescale~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$, and in units of time
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-local-time}
 \frac{3^{2m}}{\ahom_{m\wedge L}}\,,
\end{equation}
the generator of~$X^{(L)}$ becomes~$\mathcal A=(d\mu/dx)^{-1}\nabla\cdot(A\nabla)$. It is symmetric on~$L^2(\mu)$, with Dirichlet form and local energy
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal E(v,w)\coloneqq\int A\nabla v\cdot\nabla w
 \qquad\text{and}\qquad
 \mathcal E(v;V)\coloneqq\int_V A|\nabla v|^2\,.
\end{equation*}
The factor~$c$ normalizes the product of the factors contributed by~$\g_i$, with~$j<i\leq L$, to have value one at~$z$. By~\eqref{e.sensitivity.field}, the oscillation of the sum of these fields on the cube has uniformly bounded exponential moments, and therefore the moment bounds for the random constants below are uniform in~$L$,~$m$ and~$z$. For the coefficient~$\a$ without cutoff we use instead~$j=m$ and~$c=\a(z)/\a_m(z)$, so that the density of~$\mu$ is~$c^{-1}\a(z+3^mx)$, and the unit of time is~$3^{2m}/\ahom_m$. This density is then~$\a_m(z+3^mx)$ multiplied by~$\exp(\sum_{i>m}(\g_i(z+3^mx)-\g_i(z)))$, and this exponential factor satisfies the same oscillation bound.

\smallskip

\begin{lemma}[Local mass, coercivity and cutoff-function estimates]
\label{tight:lem-static}
Fix a bounded reference cube and a moment order~$q\in[1,\infty)$. Also fix finitely many concentric pairs $V_0\Subset V_1$ inside the reference cube. For each pair and each~$s\in[0,1]$, let~$V_s$ be the cube with the same center whose side length interpolates affinely between those of~$V_0$ and~$V_1$; the nested families below consist of the cubes~$V_{k2^{-n}}$, for~$n\geq0$ and $0\leq k\leq2^n$. There exists a threshold~$\gmcdelta_0>0$, which may depend on~$q$, such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then there is a deterministic~$B<\infty$, independent of~$q$, such that, for every~$L,m\in\N_0$ and~$z\in\Rd$, there is a random constant~$K=K_{L,m,z}\geq1$ satisfying
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{L,m,z}\E\bigl[K_{L,m,z}^q\bigr]<\infty
\end{equation*}
such that, after rescaling~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ as in~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time}, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Mass bounds}}: for every ball~$B_r(x)$ contained in the reference cube with~$0<r\leq1$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-mass}
 K^{-1}r^{d+\nf12}\leq\mu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^{d-\nf12}\,,
\end{equation}
and the analogous bounds hold for cubes.

\item \underline{\emph{Coercivity in~$H^{\nf34}$}}: for each fixed interior cube~$V$ of the family with~$n=0$ and every~$v\in H^1(V)$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-coercivity}
 \|v\|_{H^{\nf34}(V)}^2
 \leq K\bigl(\mathcal E(v;V)+\|v\|_{L^2(V,dx)}^2\bigr)\,,
\end{equation}
and the last term can be omitted if~$v\in H_0^1(V)$. For $V=V_{k2^{-n}}$, the same assertion holds with~$K2^{Bn}$ in place of~$K$.

\item \underline{\emph{Harmonic cutoff functions}}: between consecutive cubes $V_{k2^{-n}}\Subset V_{(k+1)2^{-n}}$ with gap~$a\asymp2^{-n}$ there is a cutoff function~$\chi$, equal to one on the smaller cube, vanishing near the boundary of the larger cube, and satisfying~$0\leq\chi\leq1$, such that, for every ball~$B_r(x)$ with~$0<r\leq1$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-cutoff-measure}
 \Gamma_\chi(B_r(x))
 \coloneqq\int_{B_r(x)}A|\nabla\chi|^2
 \leq Ka^{-B}r^{d-\nf12}\,.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

When needed, we replace~$K$ by a fixed power of~$K$; this is possible if the lemma is applied from the outset with a correspondingly larger moment order~$q$. For a finite collection of cubes we take the maximum of their constants; the resulting moment bounds remain uniform in~$L$,~$m$ and~$z$.

\begin{proof}
