The three estimates are local versions of estimates proved in Sections~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} and~\ref{mfd:sec-speed} for the rescaled coefficient~$A_N$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}: Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-chaos-moments} bounds the positive moments of the mass of small cubes under the multiplicative chaos, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity} bounds the~$H^{\nf34}$ norm by the energy, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} bounds the energy measure of balls at every radius for solutions of Dirichlet problems, and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs} constructs cutoff functions as harmonic interpolants. We give the additional arguments needed to pass to the rescaling of~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ and to obtain one random constant for all the nested cubes.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We prove the mass bounds~\eqref{tight:eq-mass}. Reindexing the fields in reverse order identifies, in law, the normalized mass of the unit cube at the scale~$j$ with~$\mu_N^0(\cu_0)$ from the paragraph after~\eqref{e.intro.rescaled.generator}. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-chaos-moments}, applied to the martingale~$N\mapsto\mu_N^0(\cu_0)$, bounds the positive moments of~$\mu_N^0(\cu_0)$ uniformly in~$N$, including the field~$\g_0$ at scale one. The bound on the second moment and~$\E \mu_N^0(\cu_0)=1$ give~$\inf_N\P(\mu_N^0(\cu_0)\geq\nf12)>0$, by the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality. Fix~$J\in\N$ and choose two sufficiently separated triadic subcubes of~$\cu_0$ of side~$3^{-J}$. Since the fields are independent and have a finite range of dependence, for every~$N\geq J$ we have
\begin{equation*}
 \mu_N^0(\cu_0)\geq 3^{-dJ}Y\bigl(\mu_{N-J}^{0,(1)}(\cu_0)+\mu_{N-J}^{0,(2)}(\cu_0)\bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
where~$\mu_{N-J}^{0,(1)}$ and~$\mu_{N-J}^{0,(2)}$ are independent copies of~$\mu_{N-J}^0$, independent of the random variable~$Y>0$. Here~$Y$ is the infimum over~$\cu_0$ of the part of the density of~$\mu_N^0$ contributed by the~$J$ coarsest fields. By Assumption~\ref{a.g2}, the variable~$-\log Y$ has a Gaussian upper tail. Consequently, the function~$G(s)\coloneqq\sup_N\P(\mu_N^0(\cu_0)<e^{-s})$ satisfies, for all sufficiently large~$s$,
\begin{equation*}
 G(s)\leq Ce^{-cs^2}+G\Bigl(\frac{3s}{4}\Bigr)^2\,,
\end{equation*}
where the finitely many~$N<J$ are absorbed into the first term. The lower bound on~$\P(\mu_N^0(\cu_0)\geq\nf12)$ gives~$G(s)\leq\rho<1$ for large~$s$. Letting~$s\to\infty$ in the recursion gives~$G(s)\to0$. Iterating the recursion on the intervals~$[(\nf43)^ns_0,(\nf43)^{n+1}s_0]$ then gives~$G(s)\leq C\exp(-cs^{\nf32})$: since~$2(\nf34)^{\nf32}>1$, squaring the bound at~$\nf{3s}4$ gives a stronger bound at~$s$, and the Gaussian term in the recursion is of smaller order. Hence, for every~$p<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-negative-mass}
 \sup_N\E\bigl[\mu_N^0(\cu_0)^{-p}\bigr]<\infty\,.
\end{equation}

We now pass to the triadic subcubes of the reference cube. Let~$P$ be such a subcube, of side~$3^{-n}$, and split the density of~$\mu$ into the factors from fields of scale below and above the physical side of~$P$. The product of the factors from fields of scale between the physical side of~$P$ and~$3^j$, and its reciprocal, have moments of order~$p$ bounded by~$C_p3^{C_p\gmcdelta^2n}$, and the oscillation of the fields above scale~$j$ has a uniformly bounded exponential moment. Combining the positive moments of~$\mu_N^0(\cu_0)$ with~\eqref{tight:eq-negative-mass} therefore gives, for every~$p<\infty$,
\begin{equation*}
 \E\left[
   \left(\frac{\mu(P)}{|P|}\right)^p
   +\left(\frac{\mu(P)}{|P|}\right)^{-p}\right]
 \leq C_p3^{C_p\gmcdelta^2n}\,.
\end{equation*}
The reference cube contains~$O(3^{dn})$ triadic subcubes of side~$3^{-n}$. Choose~$p>q+2d$, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$ so that~$p/2>d+C_p\gmcdelta^2$. Markov's inequality and a summation over~$n$ prove~\eqref{tight:eq-mass} for triadic cubes, with the asserted moments, and a finite collection of translated triadic grids gives the bounds for balls.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove the~$H^{\nf34}$ coercivity bound~\eqref{tight:eq-coercivity}. If~$m\leq L$, it is the estimate of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity}, applied with~$N=m$ and with the value at~$z$ subtracted from each field of scale larger than~$3^m$; the proof of that lemma is uniform in the number of these fields, by~\eqref{e.sensitivity.field}. If~$m>L$, we use Lemma~\ref{l.multiscale.response.large.cubes} to bound the coarse-grained ellipticity of the cubes of physical side at least~$3^L$ in the fractional Poincar\'e inequality~\eqref{e.besov.grad.poincare}; the estimates for the smaller cubes are unchanged. The case~$v\in H^1_0(V)$ uses~\eqref{e.CG.Poincare.trace.zero}.

For triadic subcubes of side~$3^{-n}$ in the local coordinates, the fields of scale between the physical side of the subcube and~$3^j$ contribute scalar factors. Together with~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, they multiply the constant by a random factor with~$L^p$ norm at most~$C_p3^{C_p\gmcdelta^2n}$. For subcubes of physical side less than one, the ellipticity bounds on unit cubes give a fixed power of~$3^n$ instead, with a random factor whose moments are bounded by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes}. Taking the maximum over the~$O(3^{dn})$ triadic cubes of side~$3^{-n}$ and summing over~$n$ with a sufficiently large geometric weight gives one constant~$K$ for all these meshes. The same summation over the~$O(2^n)$ cubes~$V_{k2^{-n}}$ of the nested families gives~$K2^{Bn}$ after enlarging~$B$. The comparison~\eqref{e.lambda.stability} of translated cubes allows fixed enlargements and changes of grid.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We construct the cutoff functions of~\eqref{tight:eq-cutoff-measure}. We use harmonic interpolants as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-cutoffs}. Choose a smooth cutoff function~$f$ that equals one on a neighborhood of the smaller cube and vanishes on a neighborhood of the boundary of the larger cube, and a mesh of side~$h\asymp a$ such that the cells meeting the transition region of~$f$ lie between the two prescribed cubes. Let~$\chi$ be the function which, on each cell, is~$A$-harmonic with boundary values~$f$. The maximum principle gives~$0\leq\chi\leq1$, and~$\chi=f$ on cells where~$f$ is constant.

We apply Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} on each rescaled cell with zero source, smooth boundary datum~$f$ and exponent~$t=d-\nf12$ in~\eqref{eq:mfd-4}. That proposition covers radii below the physical unit scale, as well as boundary faces and corners, so its random constant already accounts for the coefficient at scales below the unit scale. On a physical cell of side~$3^k$ with~$k\geq0$, we apply that proposition at cutoff~$k\wedge L$ and subtract from each remaining field its value at the center of the cell. If~$k>L$, the same proof applies with Lemma~\ref{l.multiscale.response.large.cubes} for the coarse-grained energy and Proposition~\ref{p.cutoff.Holder.regularity} on cubes of side~$3^k$; the contribution of scales below the smallest field scale to the moments is no larger, since~$L\leq k$.

It remains to express the cell estimates after rescaling~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ as in~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time}. Let~$P$ be a cell of side~$h=3^{-n}$ with~$n\leq m$ and physical center~$z_P$, and put~$k=m-n$ and~$j_1=k\wedge L$. In the coordinates of the cell, the coefficient~$A$ is the normalized coefficient of the cell multiplied by two factors: the exponential~$\exp(\sum_{j<i\leq L}(\g_i(z_P)-\g_i(z)))$, which is controlled by the oscillation bound for these fields, and the scalar
\begin{equation*}
 \frac{\ahom_{j_1}}{\ahom_j}\frac{\a_j(z_P)}{\a_{j_1}(z_P)}\,.
\end{equation*}
By~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering} and~\eqref{e.sensitivity.field}, this scalar and its reciprocal have~$L^p$ norms at most~$C_p3^{C_p\gmcdelta^2n}$. After subtracting a constant from~$f$, its rescaled~$C^2$ norm on the cell is at most~$Ch\|\nabla f\|_{C^1}$. The estimate on the cell therefore gives a random constant~$K_P$ with~$\|K_P\|_{L^p}\leq C_ph^{-C_p\gmcdelta^2}$ such that, for every~$0<r\leq h$,
\begin{equation*}
 \Gamma_{\chi}(B_r(x)\cap P)
 \leq K_P\|\nabla f\|_{C^1}^2h^{\nf12}r^{d-\nf12}\,.
\end{equation*}
At each mesh size there are~$O(h^{-d})$ cells. We take the maximum of the constants~$K_P$ over these cells and sum over the meshes with a sufficiently large geometric weight. This proves~\eqref{tight:eq-cutoff-measure} when the cells have physical side at least one, or equivalently~$h\geq3^{-m}$.

If~$h<3^{-m}$, local elliptic estimates and Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} bound the random factor~$R_m$ that controls the coefficient on cells of side less than~$3^{-m}$, with~$\sup_{L,m}\E[(3^{-B_0m}R_m)^q]<\infty$ for a fixed~$B_0$. Since~$h<3^{-m}$, this factor is at most~$(3^{-B_0m}R_m)h^{-B_0}$, which is absorbed by the same summation over meshes. Finally,~$\|\nabla f\|_{C^1}\leq Ca^{-2}$. The estimates for the coefficient~$\a$ follow by locally smooth convergence of the ratios of the fields above scale~$j$ and Fatou's lemma, as in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth}.
\end{proof}

The regularity estimates of Section~\ref{s.Holder.regularity} concern solutions, and the coefficient~$A$ has no ellipticity bounds uniform in~$L$, so we derive the local maximum principle for subsolutions from Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static} alone.

\begin{lemma}[Local bound for subsolutions]
\label{tight:lem-subharmonic}
After rescaling a cube~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ as in~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time}, let~$V'\Subset V$ be fixed cubes. The estimates of Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static} imply that there is a random constant~$K$ such that, for every bounded nonnegative weak subsolution~$w$ of~$\nabla\cdot(A\nabla w)\geq0$ in~$V$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-mean-value}
 \operatorname*{ess\,sup}_{V'}w\leq K\left(\int_V w^2\,d\mu\right)^{\nf12}\,.
\end{equation}
For every fixed~$q<\infty$, there exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then the constant~$K$ has a finite moment of order~$q$.
\end{lemma}

Since~$K$ does not depend on~$w$, the lemma applies with the same constant to every bounded nonnegative solution of~$\nabla\cdot(A\nabla w)=\lambda w\,d\mu/dx$, for every~$\lambda\geq0$. We use this uniformity in~$\lambda$ in Step~1 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness}.

\begin{proof}
By a finite covering it suffices to treat a fixed concentric pair $V'\Subset V$. In both iterations of Step~3 we use the nested family~$V_{k2^{-n}}$ of Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static} associated with this pair. We apply Moser iteration with respect to~$\mu$, in which trace inequalities for measures satisfying the upper bound in~\eqref{tight:eq-mass} replace the Sobolev inequality.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We prove two trace inequalities. Let~$\nu$ be a measure satisfying~$\nu(B_r(x))\leq Kr^{d-\nf12}$ for every ball~$B_r(x)$ with~$0<r\leq1$, and let~$p_0\geq2$. We show that, if~$s>\nf14$ and~$t>\nf d2-\nf{(d-\nf12)}{p_0}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-trace}
 \|v\|_{L^2(\nu)}^2\leq CK\|v\|_{H^s}^2
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 \|v\|_{L^{p_0}(\nu)}^2\leq CK\|v\|_{H^t}^2\,.
\end{equation}
For a Littlewood--Paley decomposition of~$v$, each block~$v_i$ at frequency~$2^i$ satisfies~$\|v_i\|_{L^2(\nu)}^2\leq CK2^{\nf{i}{2}}\|v_i\|_2^2$. To see this, write~$v_i$ as its convolution with a rapidly decaying reproducing kernel, apply the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, and integrate against~$\nu$ using the bound on the mass of balls. Interpolation with Bernstein's inequality for that block gives
\begin{equation*}
 \|v_i\|_{L^{p_0}(\nu)}
 \leq CK^{\nf1{p_0}}2^{i(\nf d2-\nf{(d-\nf12)}{p_0})}\|v_i\|_2\,.
\end{equation*}
Summing over~$i$ with the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality, after extending~$v$ from a cube to the whole space, gives~\eqref{tight:eq-trace} on the fixed cubes. Below we take~$s=\nf12$, $t=\nf34$ and~$p_0=2+\nf1d$, for which~$\nf d2-\nf{(d-\nf12)}{p_0}=\nf d{(2d+1)}<\nf34$; Section~\ref{lim:sec-process-measure} uses other exponents.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the~$L^2(dx)$ norm of~$v$, and the integral of~$v^2$ against the energy measure of a cutoff function, by the energy of~$v$ and its~$L^2(\mu)$ norm. Partition a cube into cubes~$P$ of side~$\rho$, and let~$v_P$ be the average of~$v$ over~$P$ with respect to Lebesgue measure. Apply the first trace inequality, with~$\nf34$ in place of~$\nf12$, to~$v-v_P$. The fractional Poincar\'e inequality and the lower mass bound give
\begin{equation*}
 \int_P|v-v_P|^2\,d\mu
 \leq CK\rho[v]_{H^{\nf34}(P)}^2
\end{equation*}
and
\begin{equation*}
 \|v\|_{L^2(dx)}^2
 \leq CK\rho^{-\nf12}\|v\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2
      +CK^2\rho^{\nf12}\|v\|_{H^{\nf34}}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
We apply~\eqref{tight:eq-coercivity} to the last term and choose~$\rho$ small enough to absorb the resulting multiple of~$\|v\|_{L^2(dx)}^2$ into the left side. For every~$0<\varepsilon\leq1$ this yields
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-two-norms}
 \|v\|_{L^2(dx)}^2
 \leq\varepsilon\mathcal E(v)
     +CK^C\varepsilon^{-C}\|v\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2\,.
\end{equation}
Next let~$\chi$ be a cutoff function across a gap~$a$. We apply the first inequality in~\eqref{tight:eq-trace} to~$\nu=\Gamma_\chi$, which satisfies its hypothesis with~$Ka^{-B}$ in place of~$K$ by~\eqref{tight:eq-cutoff-measure}, interpolate~$H^{\nf12}$ between~$H^{\nf34}$ and~$L^2(dx)$, and then use~\eqref{tight:eq-coercivity} and~\eqref{tight:eq-two-norms}. For every~$0<\varepsilon\leq1$ this gives
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-cutoff-form}
 \int v^2\,d\Gamma_\chi
 \leq\varepsilon\mathcal E(v;V)
      +CK^C\varepsilon^{-B}a^{-B}\int_V v^2\,d\mu\,.
\end{equation}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We iterate. For~$p\geq2$, test the subsolution inequality with~$\chi^2w^{p-1}$ and put~$v=w^{\nf p2}$. The Cauchy--Schwarz inequality and~\eqref{tight:eq-cutoff-form}, with~$\varepsilon$ a small multiple of~$p^{-1}$, give, for nested cubes~$V_r\Subset V_R$,
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal E(v;V_r)
 \leq2^{-B-2}\mathcal E(v;V_R)
       +CK^Cp^B(R-r)^{-B}\int_{V_R}w^p\,d\mu\,.
\end{equation*}
Iterate this inequality over radii increasing from~$r$ to~$R$ with geometrically decreasing gaps. Hence
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal E(w^{\nf p2};V_r)
 \leq CK^Cp^B(R-r)^{-B}\int_{V_R}w^p\,d\mu\,.
\end{equation*}
The second trace inequality, the~$H^{\nf34}$ coercivity bound~\eqref{tight:eq-coercivity} and~\eqref{tight:eq-two-norms} give, since~$p_0/2>1$,
\begin{equation*}
 \|w\|_{L^{p_0p/2}(V_r,\mu)}
 \leq\bigl(CK^Cp^B(R-r)^{-B}\bigr)^{\nf1p}
       \|w\|_{L^p(V_R,\mu)}\,.
\end{equation*}
We iterate with~$p_i=2(p_0/2)^i$ and geometrically decreasing gaps. The product of the resulting constants converges, which proves~\eqref{tight:eq-mean-value}. For continuous functions such as~$1-u$ in the proof of Proposition~\ref{tight:prop-tightness}, the full support of~$\mu$ identifies the essential supremum with the pointwise supremum.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Tightness]
\label{tight:prop-tightness}
Let~$K^\omega_{L,x}$ be the quenched law of either rescaled diffusion defined above, started at~$x$. Equip~$C([0,\infty),\Rd)$ with the topology of locally uniform convergence. Almost surely these processes are conservative and, for every compact set~$B\subset\Rd$, the following statements hold.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item \underline{\emph{Tightness in expectation}}: for every~$\varepsilon>0$, there is a compact set~$\mathcal K\subset C([0,\infty),\Rd)$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-uniform-laws}
 \sup_L\E\left[\sup_{x\in B}K^\omega_{L,x}(\mathcal K^c)\right]
 \leq\varepsilon\,.
\end{equation}
In particular, the annealed laws~$\E[K^\omega_{L,x}]$ with~$L\in\N_0$ and~$x\in B$ are tight.

\item \underline{\emph{Tightness in probability}}: for every~$\eta>0$, there is a compact set~$\mathcal K_\eta$ of probability measures on~$C([0,\infty),\Rd)$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-random-laws}
 \sup_L\P\bigl[K^\omega_{L,x}\notin\mathcal K_\eta\ \text{for some}\ x\in B\bigr]
 \leq\eta\,.
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We show that, after rescaling every cube~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$ as in~\eqref{tight:eq-local-time}, the process started in~$\cu_{-2}$ leaves~$\cu_0$ before time~$t$ with probability at most~$K\sqrt t$. Let~$\chi$ be a cutoff function of Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-static} between~$\cu_{-1}$ and~$\cu_0$, so that~$\chi$ takes values in~$[0,1]$, equals one on~$\cu_{-1}$, vanishes near~$\partial\cu_0$, and satisfies~$\mathcal E(\chi)\leq K$. For~$\lambda>0$, let~$u$ solve
\begin{equation*}
 \lambda u-\mathcal A u=\lambda\chi\quad\text{in }\cu_0,
 \qquad u=0\quad\text{on }\partial\cu_0\,.
\end{equation*}
The maximum principle gives~$0\leq u\leq1$. Since~$u$ minimizes~$\mathcal E(v)+\lambda\|v-\chi\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2$ over~$v\in H^1_0(\cu_0)$, comparison with~$v=\chi$ gives
\begin{equation*}
 \int_{\cu_0}|u-\chi|^2\,d\mu\leq\frac{\mathcal E(\chi)}{\lambda}
 \leq\frac K\lambda\,.
\end{equation*}
On~$\cu_{-1}$, the nonnegative function~$w=1-u$ satisfies~$\nabla\cdot(A\nabla w)=\lambda w\,d\mu/dx\geq0$, and, since~$\chi=1$ on~$\cu_{-1}$, the last display bounds its norm in~$L^2(\cu_{-1},\mu)$. Therefore Lemma~\ref{tight:lem-subharmonic}, whose constant does not depend on~$\lambda$, gives
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{\cu_{-2}}(1-u)\leq K\lambda^{-\nf12}\,.
\end{equation*}
Let~$\widehat X$ be the process obtained by rescaling~$z+\cu_m$ to~$\cu_0$, let~$P_x^\omega$ and~$E_x^\omega$ be its law and expectation started at~$x$, and let~$\tau_{\cu_0}$ be its exit time from~$\cu_0$. The stopped It\^o formula gives, for every~$x\in\cu_0$,
\begin{equation*}
 u(x)=E_x^\omega\int_0^{\tau_{\cu_0}}
             \lambda e^{-\lambda s}\chi(\widehat X_s)\,ds
 \leq1-E_x^\omega e^{-\lambda\tau_{\cu_0}}\,.
\end{equation*}
Taking~$\lambda=\nf1t$, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-fast-exit}
 \sup_{x\in\cu_{-2}}P_x^\omega(\tau_{\cu_0}\leq t)
 \leq e\sup_{x\in\cu_{-2}}E_x^\omega
      \exp\Bigl(-\frac{\tau_{\cu_0}}{t}\Bigr)
 \leq K\sqrt t\,.
\end{equation}
The constant~$K$ does not depend on~$t$, and~$\E K\leq C$. In physical coordinates,~\eqref{tight:eq-fast-exit} says that the probability that~$X^{(L)}$, started at any point of~$z+\cu_{m-2}$, leaves~$z+\cu_m$ before time~$t$ is at most
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-physical-exit}
 K\sqrt{t\ahom_{m\wedge L}}\,3^{-m}\,,
\end{equation}
and the same bound holds for~$X$ with~$m$ in place of~$m\wedge L$. These bounds concern the processes killed on leaving a bounded cube and do not presume that the processes are conservative.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove compact containment and conservativeness. For triadic~$R\geq1$, a cube of side~$R$ in rescaled coordinates has time unit~$R^23^{2L}/\ahom_L$ at finite cutoff, and, by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering}, at least~$R^23^{2L}/\ahom_L$ for the diffusion~$X$ without cutoff. After the cube sizes are adjusted by fixed factors,~\eqref{tight:eq-fast-exit} therefore gives, for every~$T>0$ and every~$R\geq1$ larger than a fixed multiple of~$\max_{x\in B}|x|$,
\begin{equation}\label{tight:eq-containment}
 \sup_L\E\left[\sup_{x\in B}K^\omega_{L,x}\left(\sup_{s\leq T}|Z_s|>R\right)\right]
 \leq C\frac{\sqrt T}{R}\,.
\end{equation}
Letting~$R\to\infty$ shows that, for almost every environment, the process does not explode from any starting point in~$B$. The same estimate then gives compact containment for the laws on continuous path space.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We bound the modulus of continuity and prove~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws}. Fix~$R<\infty$ and~$r>0$, fix~$k\in\N_0$ such that cubes of side~$3^{-k}$ have diameter at most~$r/4$, and cover the ball~$B_R$ by finitely many cubes~$y+\cu_{-k-2}$. For~$L\geq k$, the time unit of each of the cubes~$y+\cu_{-k}$, measured in units of~$3^{2L}/\ahom_L$, satisfies, by~\eqref{e.annealed.ordering},
\begin{equation*}
 3^{-2k}\frac{\ahom_L}{\ahom_{L-k}}
 \geq3^{-(2+\nf{2\tau^2}{\log3})k}>0\,.
\end{equation*}
We apply~\eqref{tight:eq-fast-exit} to each cube of this finite cover and take the maximum of the finitely many random constants. The strong Markov property then gives, for every stopping time~$S\leq T$ and every~$h>0$,
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{L\geq k}\P\left(
    \sup_{0\leq s\leq h}|Z_{S+s}^{(L)}-Z_S^{(L)}|>r/4,
    \ S<\tau_{B_R}\right)
 \leq C_{R,r}\sqrt h\,.
\end{equation*}

To obtain~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws}, in which the supremum over starting points lies inside the expectation, we first restrict to the event on which the maximum of the random constants of this finite cover is at most~$M$. Its complement has probability at most~$\nf{C_{R,r}}M$, uniformly in~$L\geq k$. On this event, for every starting point in~$B_R$, the probability that the path moves by more than~$r/4$ within time~$h$ is at most~$C_{R,r}M\sqrt h$, and, by the strong Markov property, the same bound holds after every stopping time before exit from~$B_R$, conditionally on the path up to that stopping time.

Consider the successive exit times of the path from balls of radius~$\nf r4$, the first centered at the starting point and each later one at the position of the path at the preceding exit time. Choose~$h_0$ so small that this probability bound, with~$h=h_0$, is at most~$\nf1{16}$. If~$n>\nf{2T}{h_0}$ such exits occur before time~$T$, then at least~$\nf n2$ of the gaps between consecutive exit times are shorter than~$h_0$. The strong Markov property in the fixed environment and a union bound over the choices of these gaps bound the probability of this event by~$2^n16^{-\nf n2}$. Hence the number of exits before time~$T$ is tight, uniformly in the starting point.

For a fixed number of exits, the probability that some gap is shorter than~$h$ tends to zero as~$h\to0$. Since the path remains in a ball of radius~$\nf r4$ between consecutive exit times, this gives a uniform bound on the probability that the path oscillates by more than~$r$ on an interval of length~$h$. We choose first~$M$, then the number of exits, then~$h$.

For each of the finitely many~$L<k$, the coefficient is smooth. Nonexplosion and continuous dependence on the starting point show that, almost surely, the laws of these diffusions started in~$B$ are uniformly tight, and dominated convergence gives the corresponding bound for the expected supremum. We apply the preceding construction along a sequence of integer time horizons and of spatial tolerances tending to zero, with summable error probabilities. Compact containment and the Arzel\`a--Ascoli theorem then prove~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We prove~\eqref{tight:eq-random-laws}. By~(i), for every~$j\geq1$ there is a compact set~$\mathcal K_j$ of paths for which the left side of~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws} is at most~$\eta2^{-2j}$. Set
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal K_\eta\coloneqq\bigl\{P:P(\mathcal K_j^c)\leq2^{-j}
 \text{ for every }j\geq1\bigr\}\,.
\end{equation*}
The set~$\mathcal K_\eta$ is closed and uniformly tight, hence compact. Markov's inequality and a union bound over~$j$ prove~\eqref{tight:eq-random-laws}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Convergence in probability of the path laws}
\label{mfd:sec-paths}

We use the killed resolvents to identify the limiting laws on~$C([0,\infty),\Rd)$. The ingredients are compact containment, the Markov identity at finite cutoff, and the fact that discounted integrals of finite-dimensional observations determine a continuous path law.

We record the short-time approximation for the killed resolvents. For~$f\in C_0(Q)$, extend~$f$ by zero. Splitting the occupation integral at~$h$ gives
\begin{multline}\label{eq:mfd-resolvent-identity-approximation}
 \sup_N\E\|\lambda R^Q_{N,\lambda}f-f\|_\infty
 \leq\sup_{|x-y|\leq r}|f(x)-f(y)|+2\|f\|_\infty\Bigl(e^{-\lambda h}\\
 +\sup_N\E\sup_{x\in\overline Q}K^\omega_{N,x}
       \bigl(\sup_{s\leq h}|Z_s-x|>r\bigr)\Bigr).
\end{multline}
The last probability tends to zero as~$h\downarrow0$ by~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws}. If a path is killed before~$h$ without moving farther than~$r$, its starting point is within~$r$ of the boundary, where~$f$ is small. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-uniform-resolvent} passes this estimate to the limiting resolvents. In particular,~$\E\|\lambda R^Q_\lambda f-f\|_\infty\to0$ as~$\lambda\to\infty$.

\begin{proposition}[Convergence in probability]
\label{mfd:prop-quenched-convergence}
Let~$B\subset\Rd$ be compact and~$\varepsilon>0$. Then
\begin{equation*}
 \lim_{N,N'\to\infty}\P\!\left[
 \sup_{x\in B}d_{\mathrm{weak}}(K^\omega_{N,x},K^\omega_{N',x})
 >\varepsilon\right]=0\,.
\end{equation*}
Consequently, the kernels~$x\mapsto K^\omega_{N,x}$ converge in probability, locally uniformly in~$x$, to a continuous random probability kernel~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$ that is measurable with respect to the fields.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the Prokhorov metric~$d_{\mathrm P}$ associated with the complete metric on path space
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{m\geq1}2^{-m}
 \bigl(1\wedge\sup_{t\leq m}|z(t)-z'(t)|\bigr).
\end{equation*}
Write~$R_{N,\lambda}f(x)=\int\!\int_0^\infty e^{-\lambda t}f(Z_t)\,dt\,dK^\omega_{N,x}$ for the whole-space resolvent. If~$Q$ contains~$B$, splitting at the exit time gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-path-localization}
 \sup_{x\in B}|R_{N,\lambda}f(x)-R^Q_{N,\lambda}f(x)|
 \leq\frac{\|f\|_\infty}{\lambda}
 \left(e^{-\lambda T}+\sup_{x\in B}K^\omega_{N,x}(\tau_Q\leq T)\right).
\end{equation}
Choose first~$T$ large and then~$Q$ large. Compact containment~\eqref{tight:eq-containment} makes the expectation of the right side arbitrarily small, uniformly in~$N$. Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-uniform-resolvent} therefore makes the whole-space resolvents Cauchy in probability, locally uniformly in~$x$. Contraction and localization give the same conclusion for measurable random continuous sources~$g_N$ converging in probability locally uniformly to~$g$, provided~$\|g_N\|_\infty$ and~$\|g\|_\infty$ are bounded by one deterministic constant. The intermediate sources in the iterated resolvents below have this bound by contraction.

For bounded continuous~$f_1,\ldots,f_k$ and positive integers~$n_1,\ldots,n_k$, consider the bounded continuous path functional
\begin{equation*}
 \int_{(0,\infty)^k}e^{-\sum_{i=1}^k n_i s_i}
       \prod_{i=1}^k f_i\bigl(z(s_1+\cdots+s_i)\bigr)\,ds.
\end{equation*}
The Markov property and Fubini identify its expectation with
\begin{equation*}
 R_{N,n_1}\bigl(f_1R_{N,n_2}(f_2\cdots R_{N,n_k}f_k)\bigr)(x).
\end{equation*}
Iterating the preceding resolvent convergence makes these expectations Cauchy in probability, uniformly for~$x\in B$. These functionals determine continuous path laws. After the substitution~$u_i=e^{-s_i}$, varying the positive integers~$n_i$ gives the integrals of the finite-dimensional observations against every monomial on~$[0,1]^k$. Polynomial approximation and continuity in time then determine these observations. A separating algebra of spatial test functions determines the finite-dimensional laws.

Tightness~\eqref{tight:eq-uniform-laws} now transfers this Cauchy property to~$d_{\mathrm P}$. Indeed, on a compact set of paths carrying all but a small mass, finitely many continuous tests suffice to distinguish laws at any prescribed positive~$d_{\mathrm P}$ distance; the determining functionals approximate these tests there. Markov's inequality controls the mass outside that set, uniformly over~$x\in B$. A finite union bound proves the asserted Cauchy property. The finite-cutoff kernels are continuous in~$x$, by their Feller property and local path tightness. Completeness of the Prokhorov metric and a subsequence with summable error probabilities on a compact exhaustion of~$\Rd$ give a measurable continuous limiting kernel.

Finally, on the compact set of laws~$\mathcal K_\eta$ in~\eqref{tight:eq-random-laws}, every metric inducing the weak topology is uniformly equivalent to~$d_{\mathrm P}$. With probability at least~$1-2\eta$, both finite-cutoff kernels take values there for every~$x\in B$. Letting~$\eta\downarrow0$ transfers the assertion to~$d_{\mathrm{weak}}$.
\end{proof}

\subsubsection{The Markov property of the limit}

\begin{proposition}[Properties of the limit]
\label{mfd:prop-limit-properties}
Almost surely, the limiting kernel~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$ has the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The map~$x\mapsto K^\omega_x$ is continuous in the weak topology.
\item The laws~$(K^\omega_x)_x$ are the laws of a continuous, conservative, strong Markov process~$Z$.
\item The process is symmetric with respect to~$\mu$: for every~$t\geq0$ and all compactly supported continuous functions~$f$ and~$g$,
\begin{equation*}
 \int g(x)\int f(Z_t)\,dK^\omega_x\,\mu(dx)=\int f(x)\int g(Z_t)\,dK^\omega_x\,\mu(dx)\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Continuity of the kernel and infinite lifetime hold by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-quenched-convergence}: the limiting laws are probability measures on~$C([0,\infty),\Rd)$. The finite-cutoff Markov identity passes to the limit for bounded continuous path functionals, using locally uniform convergence of the kernels and compact containment. A monotone-class argument and approximation of stopping times from above by dyadic times then give the strong Markov property.

At finite cutoff the same identity holds for~$K^\omega_{N,x}$ and~$\mu_N$, by the symmetry of the generator in~$L^2(\mu_N)$. For compactly supported continuous~$f,g$, locally uniform convergence of the kernels and local weak convergence~$\mu_N\to\mu$ pass this identity to the limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Killed resolvents of the limiting process]
\label{mfd:lem-killing}
Almost surely, for every cube~$Q\in\mathcal C$ and every~$\lambda>0$, the resolvent of the process of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-limit-properties} killed on leaving~$Q$ is~$R^Q_\lambda$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a subsequence along which the whole-space kernels converge almost surely, locally uniformly in the starting point, and the killed resolvents converge uniformly on~$\overline Q$, for a countable dense family of data and positive parameters. Fix an environment in this event. For nonnegative data, the discounted occupation functional stopped at~$\tau_Q$ is lower semicontinuous on continuous path space, so the portmanteau theorem gives one inequality between its expectation under~$K^\omega_x$ and~$R^Q_\lambda f(x)$.

For the opposite inequality, first take~$f=1$. Fix~$\varepsilon>0$ and a smaller closed concentric cube~$F\Subset Q$ so that, for all sufficiently large~$N$, $\lambda R^Q_{N,\lambda}1\leq\varepsilon$ on~$Q\setminus F$. Uniform convergence and the continuous zero-boundary representative of~$R^Q_\lambda1$ allow this choice. Let~$\sigma_{h,F}$ be the first time on the grid~$h\N_0$ at which the path is outside~$F$, with~$e^{-\lambda\infty}=0$. The finite-cutoff restart identity gives
\begin{equation*}
 (1-\varepsilon)E^\omega_{N,x}e^{-\lambda\sigma_{h,F}}
 \leq E^\omega_{N,x}e^{-\lambda\tau_Q}
 =1-\lambda R^Q_{N,\lambda}1(x).
\end{equation*}
The grid functional on the left is bounded and lower semicontinuous, since~$F$ is closed. Portmanteau passes the inequality to the limiting law. Continuity of paths gives~$e^{-\lambda\tau_Q}\leq\liminf_{h\downarrow0}e^{-\lambda\sigma_{h,F}}$; Fatou's lemma and then~$\varepsilon\downarrow0$ give~$R^Q_\lambda1(x)\leq E^\omega_x\int_0^{\tau_Q}e^{-\lambda t}\,dt$. Together with the first inequality this proves equality for~$f=1$. Applying the first inequality to~$\|f\|_\infty+f$ and~$\|f\|_\infty-f$ gives equality for every bounded continuous~$f$. Contraction, continuity in~$\lambda$, and a countable intersection give the identity for all~$\lambda>0$ and all cubes on one event of probability one.

To allow~$f\in C(\overline Q)$, we multiply it by bounded compactly supported cutoff functions increasing to one in~$Q$. The resulting data converge in~$L^2(\mu)$, and the variational identity and the trace bound give the convergence of their resolvents in energy. The common~$C^{\nf14}$ bound then gives uniform convergence, and dominated convergence for the path integral weighted by~$e^{-\lambda t}$ proves the identity for~$f$, in particular for~$f=1$.
\end{proof}

The identification of the killed resolvents also gives the finite mean exit times asserted in Theorem~\ref{t.scaling.limit}.

\begin{corollary}[Finite mean exit times]
\label{mfd:cor-finite-exit}
Almost surely, $\sup_{x\in U}\int\tau_U\,dK^\omega_x<\infty$ for every bounded open set~$U$.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Choose~$Q\in\mathcal C$ containing~$\overline U$. Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-killing} and~\eqref{eq:mfd-uniform-lambda}, with~$f=1$, give
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_{x\in Q}\int\!\int_0^{\tau_Q}e^{-\lambda t}\,dt\,dK^\omega_x
 =\|R^Q_\lambda1\|_\infty\leq K_Q
 \qquad(\lambda>0).
\end{equation*}
Monotone convergence as~$\lambda\downarrow0$ and~$\tau_U\leq\tau_Q$ prove the assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Almost-sure convergence by comparison of two cutoffs}
\label{mfd:sec-finite-comparison}

We compare the finite-volume energies at two cutoffs. On a small cube, rescaling to the unit cube writes the ratio of the two energies as a deterministic ratio of normalizations times a ratio of two unit-cube energies. To combine these local comparisons, we join harmonic extensions on disjoint small cubes, using the bound of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain} on the number of bad cells along chains of nested cubes.

Our control of the ratios of the two normalizations follows the argument of~\citet[Section~3.4]{DGComparison}. There, two random metrics are compared by joining paths around annuli at a positive proportion of scales into a single path. Upper and lower bounds on crossing distances then exclude ratios of the two normalizations that are too large or too small. \citet[Lemma~3.11]{Devlin2024} strengthened this argument by using convergence in probability, which rules out ratios bounded away from one on a positive proportion of scales. We apply the argument of~\citet[Lemma~3.11]{Devlin2024} to the minimal energy with a fixed nonconstant affine boundary value. If the ratios were at most a fixed number less than one on a positive proportion of scales, then joining harmonic extensions on cubes of those scales would bound this energy at a finite cutoff by a fixed factor less than one times its limit, which is positive. This would contradict the convergence in probability. Interchanging the two cutoffs excludes, in the same way, ratios at least a fixed number greater than one. On the remaining scales the ratios are close to one, and Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet} turns the comparison on these scales into bounds with summable error probabilities.

We first record how a minimal energy with given boundary values on a small cube rescales. For an integer~$k\geq0$ and~$z\in\Rd$, let~$\Theta_{k,z}$ be the map on environments that translates the fields by~$z$, rescales them by~$3^{-k}$ and subtracts from each field its value at~$z$. We write $\Lambda^{\Theta_{k,z}\omega}_{m,\cu_0}$ for the minimal energy on~$\cu_0$ with given boundary values at cutoff~$m$ in the environment~$\Theta_{k,z}\omega$.

\begin{lemma}[Rescaling of minimal energies on small cubes]\label{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}
For every integer~$k\geq0$, the limit
\begin{equation*}
 e(k)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}
\end{equation*}
exists and is positive. Let~$z\in\Rd$, let~$q=z+3^{-k}\cu_0$, and, for~$N\geq k$, let
\begin{equation*}
 s_N(q)=\frac{\ahom_{N-k}}{\ahom_N}
 \exp\Bigl(H(z)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\bigl(\g_{-j}(z)-\tau^2\bigr)\Bigr)
\end{equation*}
be the value at the center of~$q$ of the factor in~$A_N$ coming from the normalization and the fields whose scales exceed the side length of~$q$. Then, for every~$N\geq k$ and~$g\in H^1(q)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{N,q}(g)
 =3^{-(d-2)k}s_N(q)\,
   \Lambda_{N-k,\cu_0}^{\Theta_{k,z}\omega}(g(z+3^{-k}\,\cdot))\,.
\end{equation*}
For each fixed~$z$, the same identity holds almost surely for the minimal energies of the limit form, with~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ in~$s_N(q)$ replaced by~$e(k)$, for every boundary trace on which these are defined.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The identity at finite cutoff follows from the change of variables~$x=z+3^{-k}y$ in the minimization that defines~$\Lambda_{N,q}(g)$. In the environment~$\Theta_{k,z}\omega$ the fields of scale larger than~$3^{-k}$ enter, at scales larger than one, only through their rescaled increments about~$z$. So all their spatial oscillations are retained, and, by stationarity and the scaling of the fields, the map~$\Theta_{k,z}$ preserves their law.

To prove that the normalizations converge, fix a nonconstant affine datum. Both minimal energies in the identity converge in probability, and their limits are strictly positive. For fixed~$k$ the deterministic ratios~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ lie in a compact subset of~$(0,\infty)$. Two subsequential limits of these ratios must coincide, since otherwise the same positive minimal energy of the limit form would equal one random variable multiplied by two different constants. Finally, we pass to the limit in the identity for the countable family of smooth traces of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local-form}, and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension} gives the identity for every trace on which the minimal energy of the limit form is defined.
\end{proof}

We compare the two cutoffs on a cell~$q$ through the trace on~$\partial q$ of a single function, which is H\"older continuous but otherwise arbitrary. The next lemma reduces this comparison to finitely many smooth traces.

\begin{lemma}[Reduction to finitely many smooth boundary values]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}
Let~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$, $A<\infty$ and~$\eta>0$. There exists a finite family of smooth functions on~$\partial \cu_0$ with the following property. Let~$\Lambda_1$ and~$\Lambda_2$ be minimal energies on~$\cu_0$ with given boundary values such that, for~$i\in\{1,2\}$ and every~$g\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$,
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_i(g)\leq A\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2\,,
\end{equation*}
and such that~$|\Lambda_1(h)-\Lambda_2(h)|\leq\eta/2$ for every function~$h$ of the family. Then, for every~$g\in C^\alpha(\partial \cu_0)$,
\begin{equation*}
 |\Lambda_1(g)-\Lambda_2(g)|
 \leq\eta\|g\|_{C^\alpha/\mathbb R}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since~$\Lambda_i^{\nf12}$ is a seminorm, the triangle inequality gives, for all~$g,h\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$,
\begin{equation*}
 |\Lambda_i(g)-\Lambda_i(h)|
 \leq A\|g-h\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}
       \bigl(\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}+\|h\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
The unit ball of~$C^\alpha/\mathbb R$ is precompact in~$C^\beta/\mathbb R$, so for every tolerance it has a finite net consisting of smooth functions. Take the family to be a sufficiently fine such net, and apply the last display to~$\Lambda_1$ and to~$\Lambda_2$. This gives the assertion for~$g$ in the unit ball, and homogeneity gives it for every~$g$.
\end{proof}

The number of functions in the family and their smooth norms may depend on~$\eta$; the exponents and the moment orders do not. The constant~$A$ is the one in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(iii) below, and it is fixed before the family is chosen.

On a good cell we also need, at a finite cutoff, the local H\"older estimate and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values that Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-goodext} provides for the limit form. The scalar~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} is the finite-cutoff analogue of the scalar~$s_E(q)$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-compare}.

\begin{lemma}[Good cells at finite cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}
Fix~$1/2<\beta<\alpha<1$, a subdivision factor~$L$, a distance factor~$b_0\geq3$ and a rate~$B>0$ for the exponential bounds in~(i). The thresholds for the sums over intervals of scales, the finite moment orders and~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ can be chosen in this order so that the following holds for~$\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$. There are deterministic constants~$C,A<\infty$, independent of the cutoff, the cell, the source and the tolerance~$\eta$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}, and good events~$G_N(q)$ with the following properties. Here~$N\geq k\geq0$, $q=z+r\cu_0$ with~$r=3^{-k}$, and, after~$q$ is rescaled to~$\cu_0$, the coefficient has cutoff~$m=N-k$. The conclusions hold on the same good events both for~$A_N$ and for~$A_N^0=e^{-H}A_N$, with their respective scalars~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} and~$s_N^0(q)=e^{-H(z)}s_N(q)$. Below,~$s(q)$ denotes the scalar of the coefficient under consideration, and~$\Gamma(u)$ denotes the energy measure of~$u$ for that coefficient.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item The complement of~$G_N(q)$ is covered by events indexed by integers~$h\geq1$, the event of index~$h$ being measurable with respect to~$\sigma(\g_{-j}:k-h\leq j\leq k+2h)$ and having probability at most~$e^{-Bh}$.
\item Suppose~$G_N(q)$ holds and~$q$ lies in its parent~$p$ of side~$Lr$ with~$\operatorname{dist}(q,\partial p)\geq b_0r$. For every~$f\in L^\infty(p)$ and every weak solution~$u\in H^1(p)$ of~$-\nabla\cdot(A\nabla u)=f$ on~$p$, there is a constant~$c_q$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder}
 \|u(z+r\,\cdot)-c_q\|_{C^\alpha(\cu_0)}
 \leq C r^{(2-d)/2}s(q)^{-\nf12}\Gamma(u)(p)^{\nf12}
             +Cr^2s(q)^{-1}\|f\|_\infty\,.
\end{equation}
\item On~$G_N(q)$, for every~$g\in C^\beta(\partial \cu_0)$, the normalized minimal energy satisfies
\begin{equation*}
 r^{2-d}s(q)^{-1}\Lambda_{N,q}\bigl(g((\,\cdot-z)/r)\bigr)
 \leq A\|g\|_{C^\beta/\mathbb R}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We define the event~$G_N(q)$. It imposes the conditions of~$G(q)$ on sums over intervals of scales, down to the smallest field scale, with an exponent~$\alpha_1\in(\alpha,1)$ in place of~$\alpha$ in the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC}. We choose the integrability exponent used in Step~3 so that the Sobolev embedding gives a H\"older exponent larger than~$\alpha_1$, then a small~$\sigma>0$, and we add the conditions~$\mathsf F_{0,z}\leq1$ and~$\mathsf P_{0,z}\leq12$ (notation of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}, with~$s=\sigma$, so that the weights are~$3^{-\sigma j/8}$) for~$q$ itself, on a fixed concentric enlargement. Their weighted tails satisfy the bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band} and their values at~$\gmcdelta=0$ are~$0$ and~$2$, so their failure is covered in the same way by events of the form in~(i). For an interval of~$D\leq m$ scales on which a bound fails, we apply, at the single fixed cutoff~$N$, the telescoping argument from the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}, which uses dyadically increasing intervals of field indices. Lemmas~\ref{mfd:in-prefix} and~\ref{mfd:lem-band} are uniform at that cutoff, so the union over the at most~$Ce^{CD}$ spatial choices has the same bound. Choose the moment order used in the approximation so large that the resulting exponential rate exceeds both~$B$ and the exponential growth rate of the number of spatial choices, and then decrease~$\gmcdelta$. This gives~(i) directly at finite cutoff.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the coefficient on the cell. In unit coordinates the smallest field scale is~$3^{-m}$. The term~$j=m$ of the two conditions bounds the product of the exponentials of all fields of scale at most one by~$C3^{\sigma \nf{m}{8}}$, and it bounds their gradients, weighted by their scales, by the same quantity. The increments~$\g_i-\g_i(z)$ of the fields of scale larger than one are bounded by the condition at~$j=0$, and their gradients are summed with the geometric factors of their scales. The deterministic normalization has logarithm at most~$C\tau^2(m+1)$. Denoting by~$\widetilde A$ the coefficient divided by~$s(q)$, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
 \|\widetilde A^{-1}\|_\infty+\|\widetilde A\|_\infty
       +3^{-m}\|\nabla\log\widetilde A\|_\infty
 \leq C3^{C(\sigma+\tau^2)m}\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We prove~(ii) and~(iii). Above the scale~$3^{-m}$, the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} on the event controlling sums over intervals of scales gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} with exponent~$\alpha_1$. Below~$3^{-m}$, we use the interior Sobolev estimate, with this exponent, of Step~4 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} on balls of radius $a/[C(1+3^{-m}\|\nabla\log\widetilde A\|_\infty)]$, on which the oscillation of~$\log\widetilde A$ is small. After matching with the estimate at scale~$3^{-m}$, the energy term carries the factor~$3^{-m(\alpha_1-\alpha)}$ and the source term the factor~$3^{-m(2-\alpha)}$, each multiplied only by fixed powers of the quantities in the last display. We choose~$\sigma$, and then~$\tau^2$, so that $C(\sigma+\tau^2)<\alpha_1-\alpha$, where~$C$ depends only on~$d$ and the integrability exponent. The net exponents of~$3^{-m}$ then remain positive, and~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} holds at all radii with a deterministic constant. Assertion~(iii) is condition~(a) of~$G(q)$ combined with Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}. Finally, we apply the construction separately to the coefficient with and without its factor~$e^H$, and take the intersection of the two good events. When~$H$ is replaced by one of its partial sums, the same bounds hold uniformly in the number of terms, by the summable bounds on the increments of the fields of scale larger than one.
\end{proof}

The comparison of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} on a cell holds when the normalized minimal energies at the two cutoffs are close on the finite family of smooth boundary values of that lemma. By the convergence in probability, this comparison fails only with small probability, but the counting argument of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} requires more: the failure must be covered by events of exponentially small probability, each measurable with respect to the fields with indices in a short interval.

\begin{lemma}[Rare events and field indices]
\label{mfd:lem-rare-tests}
Let~$X_m$, $m\in\N$, be random variables with values in~$[0,1]$ that tend to zero in probability, and assume that, uniformly in~$m$ and~$H$, they are approximated by their conditional expectations given the~$\sigma$-field~$\mathcal B_H$ generated by the fields with indices in the interval of length~$C(H+1)$ about a fixed index~$n$, as in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, in the sense that
\begin{equation*}
 \|X_m-\E[X_m\mid\mathcal B_H]\|_{L^p}
 \leq C_*3^{-aH}\,.
\end{equation*}
There is an absolute constant~$C$ such that, for every~$B$ with~$ap\log3>CB$, there exists~$m_0$ with the following property. For every~$m\geq m_0$, the event~$\{X_m>1/2\}$ is contained in a union of events indexed by integers~$h$; the event with index~$h$ is measurable with respect to the fields indexed by an interval of radius at most~$Ch$ about the same index~$n$, and it has probability at most~$e^{-Bh}$. The smallest index may depend on~$C_*$ and~$B$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let~$H_0$ be large, to be chosen, and put $X_m^{(\ell)}=\E[X_m\mid\mathcal B_{2^\ell H_0}]$ for~$\ell\geq0$. Since the~$L^p$ errors of these approximations are summable in~$\ell$, we have~$X_m^{(\ell)}\to X_m$ almost surely. The allowances~$2^{-\ell-3}$, $\ell\geq1$, sum to~$1/8$. Hence, if~$X_m>1/2$, then either~$X_m^{(0)}>1/4$ or the increment~$X_m^{(\ell)}-X_m^{(\ell-1)}$ exceeds~$2^{-\ell-3}$ for some~$\ell\geq1$. By Markov's inequality, the probability of the first event is at most
\begin{equation*}
 \P(X_m>1/8)+(8C_*)^p3^{-apH_0}\,.
\end{equation*}
We first choose~$H_0$ so large that the second term is smaller than~$e^{-CBH_0}$ with the required margin, and then use the convergence in probability to make the first term smaller than~$e^{-CBH_0}$ for all~$m\geq m_0$. By Markov's inequality, the probability that the~$\ell$th increment exceeds its allowance is at most
\begin{equation*}
 (C C_*)^p2^{p\ell}3^{-ap2^{\ell-1}H_0}\,,
\end{equation*}
and increasing~$H_0$ absorbs the factor~$2^{p\ell}$ uniformly in~$\ell$. Each of these events is measurable with respect to the fields indexed by the larger of the two intervals that determine the two conditional expectations in its definition. We index them by~$h=2^\ell H_0$ and enlarge their intervals to the form used in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-witness}; the assumed margin in~$ap\log3>CB$ absorbs this fixed enlargement.
\end{proof}

We apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests} to a bounded continuous piecewise linear function, as in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}, of the largest difference between the normalized minimal energies at two cutoffs, over the family of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}. The moment bounds and the approximations using finitely many fields of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} pass to the minimal energies of the limit form, so the lemma applies to this difference for every pair of sufficiently large cutoffs, with a constant~$C_*$ that may depend on the tolerance~$\eta$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}. The continuous piecewise linear function tends to zero in probability because the minimal energies converge in probability, and this requires no further decrease of~$\gmcdelta$. The argument is uniform over pairs of cutoffs whose minimum tends to infinity.

\begin{lemma}[Simultaneous good $L$-adic depths for two cutoffs]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-good-levels}
Fix~$0<a<b<1$, $\theta\in(0,1)$, $L=3^{H_1}$ and a bounded initial cube of the subdivision. Choose the finite moment orders and the threshold~$\gmcdelta_0$ as in Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell} and~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests}, with the rate~$B$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i) large enough for the counting estimate in the proof. For every~$\eta>0$ there are~$N_\eta,C_\eta<\infty$ and~$c_\eta>0$ such that, for each pair~$M\geq N\geq N_\eta$, outside an event of probability at most~$C_\eta3^{-c_\eta N}$, every chain in the~$L$-adic subdivision has at most~$\theta N/H_1$ bad $L$-adic depths between~$aN$ and~$bN$.

Here an $L$-adic depth of a chain is good if, on the cell of the chain at that depth, Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell} holds at both cutoffs and the two separately normalized minimal energies satisfy the conclusion of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} for every~$C^\alpha$ trace; otherwise it is bad.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The preceding application of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-rare-tests} and the covers of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i) at the two cutoffs cover each bad depth by events of the form in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-cell}(i), with a prescribed rate~$B$, after increasing that rate by a fixed factor. As in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-allchain}, we apply Lemma~\ref{l.concentration.rare.intervals} to the consecutive $L$-adic depths between~$aN$ and~$bN$. The number of these depths is $(b-a)N/H_1+O(1)$, while the number of chains ending at the last depth of this interval is at most $L^{d(\nf{bN}{H_1}+O(1))}$. Choose~$B$ so that the resulting exponential rate exceeds this growth and take a union bound.
\end{proof}

At depth~$k$ the minimal energies at the cutoffs~$N$ and~$M$ carry the normalizations~$\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N$ and~$\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations}, while the random factor in the scalars~$s_N(q)$ and~$s_M(q)$ is common to both. The ratio of these two normalizations
\begin{equation*}
 r_{N,M}(k)=
 \frac{\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N}{\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M}
\end{equation*}
is therefore, up to the tolerance~$\eta$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests}, the factor relating the two minimal energies on a good cell of depth~$k$. The next lemma adapts the stopping argument of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density} to two finite cutoffs.

\begin{lemma}[Comparison of two cutoffs by stopping]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}
Let~$\vartheta>0$. The geometry, the exponents and the finitely many moment orders can be chosen so that there exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then the following holds with constants~$D$,~$L$ and~$C$ independent of~$\eta$,~$c$,~$N$ and~$M$. Let~$Q$ be a fixed cube of triadic side length and~$g$ a fixed smooth function on~$\partial Q$. For every~$\eta>0$ there exist~$C_\eta<\infty$ and~$\gamma_\eta>0$ with the following property. Let~$N\leq M$ be cutoffs and~$c\leq2$, and assume that a deterministic set~$S$ contains at least a fraction~$\vartheta$ of the $L$-adic depths between~$N/4$ and~$3N/4$, and that~$r_{N,M}(k)\leq c$ for every~$k\in S$. Then, outside an event of probability at most~$C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{N,Q}(g)
 \leq c(1+C\eta L^D)\Lambda_{M,Q}(g)
       +C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}\|g\|_{C^2}^2\,.
\end{equation*}
Under the same assumptions with~$r_{N,M}^{-1}$ in place of~$r_{N,M}$, the reverse inequality holds, with~$\Lambda_{N,Q}$ and~$\Lambda_{M,Q}$ interchanged.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We define where the subdivision stops and estimate the energy on the stopped cells. Let~$u$ be the~$E_M$-harmonic extension of~$g$ to~$Q$, fix a small~$\zeta>0$, and let~$\nu=\Gamma_M(u)+3^{-\zeta N}\,dx$, so that~$\nu(q)\geq3^{-\zeta N}|q|$ for every cell~$q$. Translate the~$L$-adic grid so that, as in Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-density}, its meshes of sufficiently small side partition~$Q$ exactly. Work on the event of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-good-levels} for the interval of depths~$[N/4,3N/4]$, with the fraction of bad $L$-adic depths chosen below. Let~$J=\lfloor N/H_1\rfloor$. We follow this subdivision from $L$-adic generation~$\lfloor J/4\rfloor$ (depth~$H_1\lfloor J/4\rfloor$) to $L$-adic generation~$\lfloor3J/4\rfloor$, and we stop at a child~$q$ with parent~$p$ if the depth of~$q$ belongs to~$S$, the cell~$q$ is good and at distance at least~$b_0$ times its side from~$\partial p$, and~$\nu(p)\leq L^D\nu(q)$. At a stopped cell~$q$ of depth~$k$, the local H\"older estimate~\eqref{eq:mfd-finite-good-holder} and the comparison of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-trace-tests} give
\begin{equation*}
 \Lambda_{N,q}(u|_{\partial q})
 \leq r_{N,M}(k)\Lambda_{M,q}(u|_{\partial q})
       +C\eta r_{N,M}(k)\Gamma_M(u)(p)\,.
\end{equation*}
Since $\Lambda_{M,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ is bounded by~$\Gamma_M(u)(q)$, the stopping rule and the disjointness of the stopped cells show that the sum of $\Lambda_{N,q}(u|_{\partial q})$ over the stopped cells is at most
\begin{equation*}
 c E_M(u)+C\eta c L^D
       \bigl(E_M(u)+3^{-\zeta N}|Q|\bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We count the cells that are never stopped. The moment estimates of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-local} are uniform in both cutoffs, so, outside an event of probability at most~$C3^{-p\epsilon N}$, each of the finitely many global constants used below is at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$. Consider a chain of nested cells along which no cell is stopped. At each of its steps at which the depth belongs to~$S$ and the child is good and at distance at least~$b_0$ times its side from the boundary of its parent, $\nu$ drops by a factor larger than~$L^D$. Since~$\nu(Q)$ is controlled by the global energy and~$\nu(q)\geq3^{-\zeta N}|q|$ for every cell~$q$ of the final depth, the number of such steps is at most
\begin{equation*}
 \frac1{D\log L}\Bigl(\log\bigl(3^{\zeta N}\nu(Q)\bigr)
       +\tfrac34 dN\log3+O(1)\Bigr)\,.
\end{equation*}
We choose~$D$ large in terms of~$d$ and~$\vartheta$, and~$\theta$, $\epsilon$ and~$\zeta$ small. The chain has at least~$\vartheta J/2-O(1)$ steps at depths in~$S$. Discarding the steps counted above and the bad steps at depths in~$S$ leaves at least~$\vartheta J/3-O(1)$ steps at depths in~$S$ at which the child is within distance~$b_0$ times its side of the boundary of its parent. Each cell has at most~$C_dL^{d-1}$ children within that distance of its boundary, so, for~$L$ large, the number of cells at the final depth on chains where the subdivision has not stopped is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C L^{(\nf{3d}{4}-\nf{\vartheta}{4})J}\,.
\end{equation*}

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We bound the energies on the remaining cells at the final depth. On such a cell of side~$r$, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values, together with the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes, bounds the energy by~$K_N[u]_\alpha^2r^{d-2+2\alpha-\eta_0}$. We choose the fixed exponents so that~$2-2\alpha+\eta_0<\vartheta/8$. The sum of the energies on these cells is then at most $C3^{3\epsilon N}L^{-\vartheta \nf{J}{8}}$, which decays exponentially in~$N$ if~$\epsilon$ is small.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We compare the minimal energies with boundary values~$g$. The stopped cells and remaining cells at the final depth form a finite partition of~$Q$. Joining the~$E_N$-harmonic extensions of the traces of~$u$ on the cells of this partition gives a competitor with boundary values~$g$. The Dirichlet principle then gives the asserted upper bound for~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$, and all excluded events have exponentially small probability. The reverse inequality follows from the same proof with the cutoffs interchanged.
\end{proof}

The same construction compares the energies of solutions with a volume source.

\begin{lemma}[Comparison for volume sources]
\label{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}
Assume the hypotheses of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}. There is an event of probability at least~$1-C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}$ on which the following holds. Let~$u_M$ solve~$-\nabla\cdot A_M\nabla u_M=f$ on~$Q$, either with a smooth Dirichlet datum~$g$, in which case we put~$B=\|f\|_\infty+\|g\|_{C^2}$, or with zero conormal derivative and mean zero, in which case we assume that~$\int_Qf=0$ and put~$B=\|f\|_\infty$. Then there is a trial function~$v_N$, with the same Dirichlet datum in the first case and with mean zero in the second, such that
\begin{equation*}
 \|v_N-u_M\|_\infty\leq C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}B
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 E_N^Q(v_N)\leq c(1+C\eta L^D)E_M^Q(u_M)
                  +C_\eta3^{-\gamma_\eta N}B^2\,.
\end{equation*}
The same holds with~$A_N$ and~$A_M$ replaced by~$A_N^0=e^{-H}A_N$ and~$A_M^0=e^{-H}A_M$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We use the partition of the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}, now with~$\nu=\Gamma_M(u_M)+3^{-\zeta N}B^2\,dx$ in place of the measure of that proof. On a good cube~$q$ of side~$r$ whose parent~$p$ satisfies~$\operatorname{dist}(q,\partial p)\geq b_0r$, the deterministic estimate with source adds to the local comparison the term
\begin{equation*}
 C\eta r_{N,M}(k)s_M(q)^{-1}r^{d+2}\|f\|_\infty^2\,.
\end{equation*}
On the event of the preceding proof, the bounds~\eqref{eq:mfd-3} on grid cubes give $s_M(q)^{-1}\leq3^{\epsilon N}r^{-\eta_0}$. Since the side of every cube used in the comparison is at most~$C3^{-\nf{N}{4}}$, this source term is bounded by~$C\eta c3^{-\zeta N}B^2|q|$ if~$\epsilon+\zeta<(2-\eta_0)/4$, so it is absorbed in the lower bound~$3^{-\zeta N}B^2|q|$ for~$\nu(q)$. The global estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} bound the energy by~$3^{\epsilon N}B^2$ and the~$C^\alpha$ norm by~$3^{\epsilon N}B$, after these constants are added to the finite list of constants required to be at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$.

On each element of the partition we take the~$A_N$-harmonic extension of the trace of~$u_M$. By the maximum principle, its distance from~$u_M$ is at most twice the oscillation of~$u_M$ on that element. All elements have side at most~$C3^{-\nf{N}{4}}$, so this distance is at most~$C3^{-(\nf{\alpha}{4}-\epsilon)N}B$. In the Neumann case we first subtract the mean. This proves both inequalities.

For~$A_N^0$ and~$A_M^0$, the estimates with source follow from the same regularity argument. On each cube used in the comparison the common weight~$e^{-H}$ has ratio of supremum to infimum at most~$\exp\bigl(C3^{-\nf{N}{4}}\|H\|_{C^1(Q)}\bigr)$. We add~$e^{\|H\|_{C^1(Q)}}$, which has every finite moment, to the constants required to be at most~$3^{\epsilon N}$; this ratio is then~$1+O(3^{-\nf{N}{5}})$ for large~$N$.

By the variational definition of the minimal energy with given boundary values, the local comparison transfers to the coefficients~$A_N^0=e^{-H}A_N$ and~$A_M^0$ with an exponentially small additional error. The ratio of their scalars~$s_N^0(q)$ and~$s_M^0(q)$ is still~$r_{N,M}(k)$, because the value of the weight at the center of the cell cancels. After summation over the stopped cells, the additional error is at most~$C3^{-\nf{N}{5}}E_M^0(u_M)$, which is absorbed in the exponential remainder on this event. The rest of the construction is unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Almost-sure convergence of minimal energies]
\label{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length and~$g$ a smooth function on~$\partial Q$. Then $\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)\to\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)$ almost surely as~$N\to\infty$ along the full sequence of integers.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1.} We show that the normalizations are close to their limits on most depths~$k$ with~$N/4\leq k\leq3N/4$. Put $r_N(k)=(\ahom_{N-k}/\ahom_N)/e(k)$, and fix~$\varepsilon>0$ and a density~$\vartheta<1/3$. Suppose that, for infinitely many~$N$, at least a fraction~$\vartheta$ of the depths in this interval satisfy~$r_N(k)\leq1-\varepsilon$. For each such~$N$, since $\ahom_{M-k}/\ahom_M\to e(k)$, the same depths satisfy $r_{N,M}(k)\leq1-\varepsilon/2$ for all sufficiently large~$M$. We apply Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping} to a fixed nonconstant affine datum, with~$\eta$ so small that the multiplicative error~$C\eta L^D$ is less than~$\varepsilon/4$, and we let~$M\to\infty$ first. Along these~$N$, the lemma bounds the minimal energy with this boundary value at cutoff~$N$ by a fixed factor strictly smaller than one times its limit, up to an error that tends to zero in probability. This contradicts the convergence in probability of this minimal energy to its limit, which is strictly positive. The reverse comparison excludes in the same way a fraction~$\vartheta$ of depths with~$r_N(k)\geq1+\varepsilon$. Hence, for all sufficiently large~$N$, more than a fraction~$1-2\vartheta$ of the depths in this interval satisfy $1-\varepsilon\leq r_N(k)\leq1+\varepsilon$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We prove an exponential bound and conclude. For each large~$N$, we apply the two comparisons of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-stopping}, with~$S$ the set of depths found in Step~1, and let~$M\to\infty$. Taking~$\eta$ small relative to~$\varepsilon$ gives, with constants depending on~$\varepsilon$ and on the datum,
\begin{equation*}
 \P\left[
 |\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)-\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)|
 >C\varepsilon\Lambda_{E,Q}(g)+C_\varepsilon3^{-\gamma_\varepsilon N}
 \right]\leq C_\varepsilon3^{-\gamma_\varepsilon N}\,.
\end{equation*}
The right side is summable in~$N$, so the Borel--Cantelli lemma for~$\varepsilon=1/j$, $j\in\N$, gives the almost-sure convergence.
\end{proof}

The same argument applies to the quantities~(ii)--(iv) of the next proposition.

\begin{proposition}[Summable error probabilities]
\label{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length, and let~$Z_N$ be one of the following quantities on~$Q$, computed for the coefficient~$A_N$ or for~$A_N^0$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),leftmargin=*]
\item the minimal energy~$\Lambda_{N,Q}(g)$ with a fixed smooth boundary value~$g$;
\item the quadratic form~$\langle f,G_N^Qf\rangle$ of~\eqref{eq:mfd-quadratic-response} for a fixed bounded source~$f$;
\item the quadratic form $\sup_{v\in H^1(Q)}\{2\int_Qfv-E_N^Q(v)\}$ for a fixed bounded source~$f$ with~$\int_Qf=0$;
\item the Neumann energy~$|Q|\,p\cdot a_*^{-1}(Q;A_N)p$ of Section~\ref{mfd:sec-environment}, for the constant flux given by a fixed vector~$p$.
\end{enumerate}
There exists~$\gmcdelta_0>0$ such that, if~$0<\gmcdelta\leq\gmcdelta_0$, then~$Z_N$ converges in probability to a limit~$Z$, and, for every~$\varepsilon>0$, there exist~$C_\varepsilon<\infty$, $c_\varepsilon>0$ and a deterministic~$N_\varepsilon$ such that, for every~$N\geq N_\varepsilon$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mfd-summable-response}
 \P\bigl[|Z_N-Z|>
 C\varepsilon Z+C_\varepsilon3^{-c_\varepsilon N}\bigr]
 \leq C_\varepsilon3^{-c_\varepsilon N}\,.
\end{equation}
The finitely many moment orders and the threshold~$\gmcdelta_0$ do not depend on~$\varepsilon$. For the coefficients~$A_N^0$, the constants are uniform over deterministic translations of~$Q$. In particular, for every countable family of such quantities, all of them converge almost surely along the full sequence of cutoffs.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For the minimal energies of~$A_N$, the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} was proved in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet}. For~$A_N^0$, the convergence in probability also follows from Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-21} on continuous weights, applied with the weight~$e^{-H}$, and the last paragraph of the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison} gives the same comparison between two cutoffs.

Consider next a volume source~$f$, let~$u_M$ be the solution at cutoff~$M$, and write~$Z_M=E_M(u_M)=\int_Qfu_M$. Let~$a$ be the factor multiplying~$E_M^Q(u_M)$ in Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-finite-source-comparison}. Inserting~$a^{-1}v_N$ in the variational formula for~$Z_N$, we obtain from the two bounds of that lemma, when~$1/2\leq a\leq3$,
\begin{equation*}
 Z_N\geq a^{-1}Z_M-C_\varepsilon3^{-c_\varepsilon N}\|f\|_\infty^2\,.
\end{equation*}
The construction with the cutoffs interchanged gives the opposite inequality. Step~1 of the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-dirichlet} has shown that, for every prescribed tolerance and all large~$N$, a positive proportion of the depths~$k$ with~$N/4\leq k\leq3N/4$ have~$r_N(k)$ within that tolerance of one. For fixed large~$N$ the two comparisons therefore hold with~$a=1+C\varepsilon$, uniformly in all sufficiently large~$M$.

In case~(iii), this argument also shows that a limit in probability exists, without using a Neumann limit form. Indeed, we compare two sufficiently large cutoffs~$M$ and~$M'$ with the same fixed~$N$. The two exceptional probabilities tend to zero as~$N\to\infty$, and, since the quantities have uniformly bounded moments, the multiplicative error can then be made arbitrarily small by reducing~$\varepsilon$. Hence~$(Z_M)$ is Cauchy in probability. In case~(ii) the limit is the quadratic form of the Dirichlet Green operator of the unique limit form. Letting~$M\to\infty$ with~$N$ fixed gives~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} in cases~(ii) and~(iii).

For~(iv),~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} and case~(iii) first give the Cauchy property in probability. To obtain the rate, take~$h=3^{-vN}$ with~$0<v<c_\varepsilon/4$ in the two-cutoff comparison. The exceptional events of that comparison do not depend on the source, and the factor~$\|f_h\|_\infty^2\leq C3^{2vN}$ is absorbed by the remainder. Fix a moment order~$q\geq1$ before decreasing~$\gmcdelta$. Markov's inequality and~\eqref{eq:mfd-9} show that, uniformly for~$M\geq N$, the energy at cutoff~$M$ and the corresponding energy for the smoothed source~$f_h$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-neumann-error} differ by more than~$3^{-\nf{vN}{8}}$ with probability at most~$C_q3^{-\nf{qvN}{8}}$. The same bound holds at cutoff~$N$. Let~$M\to\infty$ with~$N$ fixed in the two comparisons, decreasing~$c_\varepsilon$ if necessary. This proves~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response}.

All the choices that depend on~$\varepsilon$ are spatial cutoffs, finite nets, lengths of the initial intervals of field indices and the exponent~$v$ of the smoothing width; none of them requires a further moment order or a further decrease of~$\gmcdelta$. The last assertion follows from the Borel--Cantelli lemma.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Almost-sure bounds uniform in the cutoff}
\label{mfd:sec-pathwise-bounds}

We now make the local estimates uniform over all cutoffs on one event of probability one. On cells of depth at most~$\theta N$, Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank} compares the finite-cutoff energies with their limits, with error probabilities summable over the cells and over~$N$. For the deeper cells, the moment bounds imply that, almost surely, the random constants in the estimates are smaller than any fixed exponential in~$N$ once~$N$ is large. The margins in the spatial exponents absorb this growth.

\begin{lemma}[Coarse-grained ellipticity bounds uniform in the cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-as-coarse}
Let~$Q$ be a cube of triadic side length and let~$s$ be one of the positive fractional orders used above. Then, almost surely, for~$q\in\{1,2\}$,
\begin{equation*}
 \sup_N\bigl(\Lambda_{s,q}(Q;A_N)
                 +\lambda_{s,q}(Q;A_N)^{-1}\bigr)<\infty\,.
\end{equation*}
In particular, the coercivity bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-1} and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values on~$Q$ hold with one finite random constant for all~$N$. The same conclusions hold with~$A_N^0$ in place of~$A_N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
It suffices to consider a unit cube.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We bound the finite-cutoff energies of the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$ by their limits. Denote by~$Z_m(z)$ the finite family consisting of the minimal energies with the affine boundary values~$x\mapsto e\cdot x$ and the Neumann energies for the fluxes~$e$, for~$e\in\{e_i,e_i+e_j\}$, computed for~$A_m^0$ in the environment shifted to~$z$, at cutoff~$m$. We apply~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} with one fixed small tolerance to this family on every grid cube of depth~$k\leq\theta N$, whose cutoff after rescaling to the unit cube is~$m=N-k$. There are at most~$C3^{dk}$ such cubes. We choose~$\theta>0$ so small that~$d\theta<c(1-\theta)/2$, where~$c$ is the exponent in~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response} at this tolerance; then the sum over these cubes of the exceptional probabilities is summable in~$N$. Hence, almost surely, for all sufficiently large~$N$, each of these finite-cutoff energies is bounded by~$C(1+Z(\Theta_{k,z}\omega))$, where~$Z$ is its limit. All the entries are nonnegative, and polarization recovers the coarse-grained matrices from this finite family.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We bound the coarse-grained matrices of the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$. For a cube~$q=z+3^{-k}\cu_0$, separating the scalar~$s_N(q)$ of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-local-normalizations} gives
\begin{equation*}
 A_N(z+3^{-k}y)=
 s_N(q)\exp\Bigl(\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr](z+3^{-k}y)
                -\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr](z)\Bigr)
 A_{N-k}^{0,\Theta_{k,z}\omega}(y)\,.
\end{equation*}
The deterministic ratio in~$s_N(q)$ and its inverse are at most~$e^{C\tau^2k}$. Hence the factors in front of $A_{N-k}^{0,\Theta_{k,z}\omega}$ and their inverses are bounded by
\begin{equation*}
 e^{C\tau^2k}\exp\Bigl(\Bigl|H(z)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}(z)\Bigr|
 +\operatorname{osc}_{q}\Bigl[H+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\g_{-j}\Bigr]\Bigr)\,,
\end{equation*}
whose~$L^p$ norm is at most~$C_pe^{C_p\gmcdelta^2 k}$, by the independence of the fields and the bound on their gradients weighted by their scales. The limits~$Z$ have the same moment bounds as their approximants. We choose~$\rho<s/4$, a moment order~$p$ with~$p\rho>2d$, and then~$\gmcdelta$ small enough. A union bound over all~$k$ and~$z$ gives a random~$K_\omega<\infty$ such that, for every grid cell of depth~$k$, the product of the expression in the last display and~$1+Z(\Theta_{k,z}\omega)$ is at most~$K_\omega3^{\rho k}$. Since both coarse-grained matrices are monotone in the coefficient, $a(q;A_N)+a_*^{-1}(q;A_N)\leq CK_\omega3^{\rho k}$ on the cells of depth at most~$\theta N$, with the same~$K_\omega$ for all sufficiently large~$N$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We bound the matrices of the cells of depth greater than~$\theta N$ and sum over all depths. For~$\theta N<k\leq N$, the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}, applied only to the matrices of the cells, gives the bound~$K_N3^{\rho k}$, where the moments of~$K_N$ of the finitely many orders fixed at the start of Section~\ref{sec:mfd-uniqueness} are bounded uniformly in~$N$. By the Borel--Cantelli lemma,~$K_N\leq3^{\xi N}$ for all large~$N$, for every fixed~$\xi>0$. The contribution of these cells to either sum defining~$\Lambda_{s,q}$ or~$\lambda_{s,q}$ is at most~$CK_N3^{-(s-\rho)\theta N}$, which is bounded if~$\xi<(s-\rho)\theta$. The cells below the smallest field scale contribute at most $C3^{-sN}(\sup_QA_N+\sup_QA_N^{-1})$, which is bounded almost surely after the fixed decrease of~$\gmcdelta$, by Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-extremes} and Markov's inequality. The cells of depth at most~$\theta N$ contribute at most $CK_\omega\sum_{k\geq0}3^{-(s-\rho)k}$. For~$q=1$, weighted Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the square of the sum of the square roots of the matrices by this sum of matrices; for~$q=2$, the definition is already a sum of matrices, with the smaller weight~$3^{-2sk}$. Finally, the coercivity bound and the bound~\eqref{eq:mfd-2} for H\"older boundary values follow from these bounds as in the proofs of Lemmas~\ref{mfd:lem-coercivity} and~\ref{mfd:lem-extension}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Regularity uniform in the cutoff]
\label{mfd:lem-as-regularity}
Let~$0<\alpha<1$ and~$d-1<t<d$ be strictly smaller than the exponents fixed in the moment estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source}. Then, for every fixed cube~$Q$, almost surely the estimates of Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-growth} and Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} with the exponents~$\alpha$ and~$t$ hold on~$Q$ with one finite random constant for all~$N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The regularity estimates follow from the iteration of Lemma~\ref{l.iteration.lemma.GMC} on the good events~$G(q)$ of the cells, defined in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches}. We show that, above the scale~$3^{-\theta N}$ with~$\theta>0$ small, these events can be controlled by limiting quantities that do not depend on~$N$ (Steps~1--5), and we use the moment bounds below~$3^{-\theta N}$ (Step~6).

\smallskip

\emph{Step 1.} We pass the conditions defining~$G(q)$ to the limit. Each coarse-grained matrix at scale~$n=N-k$, in the coordinates of~\eqref{eq:mfd-normalization}, that enters the conditions defining~$G(q)$ in Section~\ref{mfd:sec-branches} is a unit-cube energy of~$A_{N-k}^{0,\Theta_{k,z}\omega}$, in which the fields of scale larger than the cell side are absent. Hence the variational energy~$\mathcal J_n$ contains no ratio~$r_N(k)$. Using the limits of these unit-cube energies given by Proposition~\ref{mfd:prop-as-response-bank}, we define the limiting versions of the quantities~$\mathsf F$, $\mathsf P$, $\mathsf R$ and~$\mathbf G$ in these conditions. By Fatou's lemma their weighted tails satisfy the bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-band}, and sums over finite intervals of scales satisfy the bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-prefix-limit}.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 2.} We show that, almost surely for all large~$N$, the retained finite-cutoff energies are within a small relative error of their limits and the discarded tails are small. Fix~$\eta>0$ below the margins between the lower and upper thresholds of the continuous piecewise linear functions. We truncate each quantity in the conditions defining~$G(q)$ at relative depth~$\lfloor\theta'N\rfloor$, and we consider intervals of scales of length at most~$\lfloor\theta N\rfloor$ at all the centers needed in the regularity proof, including the nearest points on the faces and the centers of the initial balls of the iterations. There are at most $C N^C3^{C_d(\theta+\theta')N}$ retained unit-cube energies, all at cutoff at least~$(1-\theta-\theta')N-C$ after rescaling. By~\eqref{eq:mfd-summable-response}, the probability that one of them differs from its limit by more than the relative error~$\eta$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
 C_\eta N^C
 3^{C_d(\theta+\theta')N-c_\eta(1-\theta-\theta')N}\,.
\end{equation*}
The discarded tails, at finite cutoff or in the limit, have~$L^p$ norms at most~$C_p3^{-a\lfloor\theta'N\rfloor}$, so a union bound over the centers used in the conditions defining~$G(q)$ bounds the probability that one of them exceeds~$\eta$ by
\begin{equation*}
 C_\eta N^C3^{C_d\theta N-ap\theta'N}\,.
\end{equation*}
We choose~$\theta'$ so small that the first exponent is negative, and then~$\theta$ so small that both are. Both bounds are summable in~$N$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 3.} We compare the finite and the limiting sums over intervals of scales. The relative error of the unit-cube energies controls the continuous piecewise linear functions: polarization gives $|\mathcal J_N-\mathcal J_\infty|\leq C\eta(1+\mathcal J_\infty)$ on each retained normalized cell, and weighting gives the same inequality for the condition on~$\mathsf R$. A bounded continuous piecewise linear function of this quantity changes by at most~$C\eta$, and the square-root terms of the sums over intervals of scales change by at most~$C\sqrt\eta$. The terms of~$\mathbf G$ involving the fields~$\g_j$ at a retained depth coincide once their finite interval of field indices lies below~$N$, and their omitted tails satisfy the geometric bound of Step~2. Consequently, almost surely for all large~$N$, every retained sum over an interval of scales at finite cutoff is bounded by the corresponding limiting sum plus~$C\sqrt\eta$ per scale.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 4.} We bound, uniformly over the choices of center and initial radius, the number of scales after which the limiting sums over intervals of scales satisfy the thresholds. For a fixed choice~$\pi$ of center and initial radius, let~$B_\pi$ be the least integer after which all limiting sums over intervals of scales satisfy the stricter thresholds of the iteration. The bounds of Lemma~\ref{mfd:in-prefix} on the sums of the quantities~$\mathsf F$, $\mathsf P$, $\mathsf R$ and~$\mathbf G$ at a fixed center, summed over the lengths of the intervals of scales, give~$\P(B_\pi>b)\leq Ce^{-Ab}$, uniformly in~$\pi$. At target depth~$n$, the meshes of centers and initial radii used in the proof of Proposition~\ref{mfd:cor-neumann-source} involve at most~$C(n+1)^C3^{C_dn}$ choices of centers, initial radii and faces, edges and corners. We fix a small~$\xi_0>0$ before decreasing~$\gmcdelta$, and choose the rate~$A$ so that~$A\xi_0>C_d\log3+2$. A union bound over the choices~$\pi$ at depth~$n$ gives
\begin{equation*}
 \P\Bigl[\max_\pi B_\pi>\xi_0 n+b\Bigr]
 \leq C(n+1)^C3^{C_dn}e^{-A(\xi_0 n+b)}\,.
\end{equation*}
Summing over~$n$ shows that, almost surely, there is one finite~$B_\omega$ such that~$B_\pi\leq\xi_0n+B_\omega$ for every choice~$\pi$ at every depth~$n$.

\smallskip

\emph{Step 5.} We transfer these bounds to finite cutoffs and prove the estimates at radii~$r\geq 3^{-\theta N}$. We take~$\eta$ so small that the loss per scale of Step~3 is below the strict margin of the iteration. Then, for finite cutoffs and depths~$n\leq\theta N$, the same numbers~$B_\pi$ apply with slightly weaker thresholds. The estimate of Lemma~\ref{mfd:thm-fold} transfers the original field bounds to the reflected coefficients near faces, edges and corners. The reflection across faces is applied at most~$d+1$ times, and the numbers~$B_\pi$ of these applications add up, so their total contribution is bounded by a common random constant times~$3^{C\xi_0 n}$.

We next show that the averages~$b_i$ at the initial radius of each application are bounded by a random constant times a small negative power of the initial radius. Let~$\theta_0$ be the value of~$\theta$ in the proof of Lemma~\ref{mfd:lem-as-coarse}, so that the cells of depth at most~$\theta_0N$ were compared with their limits there, and decrease~$\theta$ to at most~$\theta_0/2$. For an initial radius at depth~$k\leq\theta N$, we split its coarse-grained sum at descendant depth~$\theta_0N$. For~$0<\rho<s$, the part with descendant depth at most~$\theta_0N$ is at most~$CK_\omega3^{\rho k}$, and the part with descendant depth greater than~$\theta_0N$ is at most
\begin{equation*}
 CK_N3^{\rho k}3^{-(s-\rho)(\theta_0N-k)}
 \leq C3^{\rho k}3^{(\xi-(s-\rho)\theta_0/2)N}\,,
